## Integration Test Surface Map: drain-cycle

**Report compiled from analysis of /Users/anton/src/drain-cycle**

### 1. Linear API Surface (HTTP GraphQL + MCP in spawned children)

- **Auth:** `LINEAR_API_KEY` environment variable via bearer token (line 50)
- **Operations called by orchestrator:**
  1. `current_cycle_id()` (line 73) — query for active cycle of "Personal" team
  2. `pending_issues(cycle_id)` (line 207) — fetch Todo/Backlog issues, labeling & inverse relations
  3. `get_issue(issue_id)` (line 262) — re-fetch issue state by id  
  4. `set_state(issue_id, state_name)` (line 284) — mutation to transition issue state (includes `_resolve_state_id` helper, line 309)

**Spawned `claude -p` child sessions:**
- **File:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/prompt.py` (lines 39–42)
- **Transport:** MCP tool calls invoked from agent prompt
- **Operations called by agent:**
  1. `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_comment` — post review-summary comment on issue
  2. `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` — transition issue to Done state

**Wrapper layer:**
- All GraphQL calls wrapped in `_post()` (line 38) with telemetry span named `linear.{operation}`, operation attribute set (line 49)

**Mock surface required:** Five distinct GraphQL operations (each with distinct variable shape):
- CurrentCycle query
- CyclePending query (issues with labels/inverse-relations flattened)
- Issue query (for post-spawn state check)
- WorkflowState query (state name → id resolution)
- IssueUpdate mutation (state transition)

---

### 2. Runtime / Orchestrator Structure

**Entry point:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/cli.py:main()` (line 54)
- Loads secrets from `~/.drain-cycle/.env` then repo-root `.env` (line 42)
- Delegates to `orchestrator.run(repos, limits)` (line 68)

**Cycle orchestrator:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`
- `run()` wrapper (line 111) → `_run()` body (line 124) inside `drain.cycle` span
- **Cycle pickup:** `linear.current_cycle_id()` (line 126), then `linear.pending_issues()` (line 130)
- **Per-issue execution:** Loop over `plan.order` (line 161), each issue via `_drain_one_issue()` (line 162)
  - **Pre-spawn:** Repo resolution (line 225), worktree creation & MCP config symlink (line 252), issue Todo→In Progress transition (line 263)
  - **Spawn:** `worker.run_issue()` (line 349) with streaming `claude -p` session
  - **Post-spawn:** State refresh (line 399), Done check (line 401), worktree teardown (line 409)
- **Retry/resume:** None in code; halt-on-not-Done policy means no retry within a run. A re-invocation of `drain-cycle` re-enters the cycle pickup and re-fetches pending issues (those in In Progress stay out until manually reverted).
- **Halt contracts:** Three per-issue paths (`_halt_message` line 43):
  1. Pre-spawn repo resolution fail (line 226)
  2. Pre-spawn setup fail (line 264)
  3. Worker per-issue cap breach (line 364) or not-Done exit (line 432)
- **Circuit breaker:** Cycle-wide token/cost/time check after each Done issue (line 179)

**Worker (spawned session):** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/worker.py`
- `run_issue()` (line 124) spawns `claude -p` with `start_new_session=True` (line 188)
- **Streaming:** `--verbose --output-format stream-json` flags (line 71)
- **Token accounting:** `_UsageAccumulator` threads the per-turn message-id-keyed usage (line 375), deduplicates by message.id (line 416)
- **Guardrails:** Token limit via poll loop (line 267), time limit via elapsed monotonic (line 298), cost via `--max-budget-usd` (line 172)
- **Kill on breach:** `os.killpg()` on process group (line 315), reaps grandchildren (MCP servers, sub-agents)

**Run log:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/runlog.py`
- Per-cycle JSON file at `~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<timestamp>.json` (line 144)
- Incremental append: `append_entry()` re-serializes full dict on every call (line 188)
- State persisted in `_persist()` (line 230): cycle totals, per-issue usage, halt reasons

### 4. Existing Test Suite

**Framework:** pytest; 5,774 lines across 26 test files (no conftest.py)

**Stubs/mocks strategy:** Monkeypatch-based function replacement on the module, not HTTP-level:
- Tests replace `linear._post` with fake functions returning canned dicts (example: `test_linear_pending_issues.py` lines 65–70)
- Tests replace `linear.current_cycle_id`, `linear.pending_issues`, `linear.get_issue` with in-process fakes (example: `test_orchestrator_iteration.py` lines 90–99)
- Spawned `claude -p` replaced with shell scripts that write markers to files (example: `test_orchestrator_iteration.py` line 22 comment)
- Real git init/worktree used in orchestrator tests for cheapest integration validation

**Test patterns:**
- Linear-focused tests (test_linear_*.py): stub `_post` return canned GraphQL response dicts, validate query shape & variable handling
- Orchestrator tests (test_orchestrator_*.py): stub Linear at the function level + spawn marker scripts, validate loop behavior & halt contracts
- No HTTP-level mocking; no mock server today

### 5. Configuration / Secrets / Environment

**API key loading (CLI):** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/cli.py:_load_secrets()` (line 33)
- Priority: shell env → `~/.drain-cycle/.env` → repo-root `.env` (line 42)
- `dotenv.load_dotenv(override=False)` means first source wins

**Hard-coded Linear endpoint:** `https://api.linear.app/graphql` (line 18 of linear.py)
- Not configurable; would need code change to redirect to mock

**Team scope:** Hard-coded to "Personal" team (line 19 of linear.py)
- All queries filter `team { name: { eq: $name } }` with `_TEAM_NAME = "Personal"`

**Spawned child process MCP:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`
- **Child invocation:** `["claude", "-p", "--dangerously-skip-permissions"]` (line 25)
- **MCP config inheritance:** Symlinks `.claude/` and `.mcp.json` from parent repo into worktree (line 257–259)
- **Default config paths:** `.claude`, `.mcp.json` (defined in `repos.py` line 34); configurable per repo via `repos.yml` key `worktree_config_paths`
- **Linear MCP in child:** Child reads its own `~/.drain-cycle/.env` (or `$HOME/.env`) at startup, so mock must intercept both parent and children unless you override env vars in the spawn command

**Limits config:** `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml` optional YAML (not required) (file `limits.py` line 97)
- Keys: `per_issue_tokens`, `per_issue_seconds`, `per_issue_cost_usd`, `cycle_tokens`, `cycle_seconds`, `cycle_cost_usd`
- Defaults: per-issue 8M tokens, 20 min, $15; cycle 30M tokens, 90 min, $60 (lines 87–92)

**Repo config:** `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml` required YAML with structure:
```yaml
repos:
  drain-cycle: /path/to/repo
worktree_config_paths: [".claude", ".mcp.json"]  # optional
```

---

### Load-Bearing File References

- **Linear entry point:** `linear.py:_post()` line 38 — all GraphQL funneled here; operation naming at line 49
- **Orchestrator cycle loop:** `orchestrator.py:_drain_one_issue()` line 196 — per-issue contract; halt paths lines 226, 264, 364, 432
- **Worker spawn & kill:** `worker.py:run_issue()` line 124; breach monitor line 267; process-group kill line 315
- **Token accumulation:** `worker.py:_UsageAccumulator.feed()` line 399; dedup by message.id line 416
- **Run log persistence:** `runlog.py:_persist()` line 230 — JSON written on every append
- **Secrets load order:** `cli.py:_load_secrets()` line 33
- **Spawn MCP symlink:** `orchestrator.py:_drain_one_issue()` line 252–259; config paths from `repos.py` line 34

The mock server must intercept: (1) parent `Linear` GraphQL calls via HTTP POST to `/graphql`, (2) child MCP `save_issue`/`save_comment` calls (as MCP tools invoked from spawned Claude processes). The child interception is the trickier constraint — either mock the MCP tool endpoint, or inject a modified `.mcp.json` into worktrees that redirects Linear to the mock.

Perfect! I have enough information now. Let me compile a comprehensive report on the failure modes, resume behaviors, and control-flow conditions.

## Behavior Surface Map: `drain-cycle` Failure Modes & Resume

Based on code exploration of the `drain-cycle` Python repo, here's the comprehensive behavior surface for integration testing:

### 1. **Retry Logic** — Presently Minimal
There is **no built-in retry logic** in the codebase. The worker (worker.py, line 124–232) spawns a `claude -p` session once per issue; if it fails, the issue halts. Linear API calls (`linear.py`, lines 38–70) are non-retrying: a single POST attempt with `timeout=30.0` (line 58); HTTP errors raise immediately (`raise_for_status` line 61), GraphQL errors raise immediately (line 69). This is deliberate: the orchestrator assumes transient Linear issues manifest as permanent halts (not reattempts).

### 2. **Resume / Checkpoint Behavior** — Run-Log State Artifact
A run log is the sole resumption mechanism:

- **Location**: `~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json` (runlog.py, line 115)
- **Persistence**: Incremental JSON written after each issue (runlog.py, lines 147–188); a crash mid-cycle leaves a well-formed file with every completed entry (runlog.py, line 96)
- **What survives across runs**: 
  - **In-flight** issues: Reverted to their pre-In-Progress state (orchestrator.py, lines 54–79, 370–374) so a re-run re-discovers them
  - **Completed** (Done): Final state in entry; next run's Linear query skips them (pending_issues filters by state type, line 226)
  - **Failed** (halt): Entry recorded with `final_linear_state` (not Done), plus `halt_reason` matching the stderr message (orchestrator.py, line 51)

- **How to resume**: Re-run `drain-cycle` against the same cycle; it queries Linear again and picks up `Todo`/`Backlog` issues. The halted worktree remains on disk (orchestrator.py, line 229, 282) for operator inspection but is not automatically cleaned.

### 3. **Cycle-Halt / Kill Conditions** — Two Paths
**Cycle-wide halts** exit without attempting further issues:
1. **Dependency-cycle detection** (orchestrator.py, lines 130–136): `linear.pending_issues` raises `DependencyCycleError` (linear.py, line 32) when the DAG of Todo/Backlog issues contains a cycle. Recorded in `cycle_halt_reason` (runlog.py, line 201).
2. **Per-issue cap breach, then subsequent cycle-cap breach** (orchestrator.py, lines 179–190): After each Done issue, `check_cycle` (limits.py, lines 156–170) compares cumulative tokens/cost/seconds against cycle limits. If crossed, halt and record the breach reason (no subsequent issues attempted).

**Per-issue halts** (return 1, preserve worktree, revert state):
- Repo-resolution failure (orchestrator.py, line 225–247): no `repo:` label, label misconfiguration, or resolved path missing
- Pre-spawn setup failure (worktree.add, linear.set_state) (orchestrator.py, lines 251–287)
- Per-issue cap breach after session spawn (token/time limit crossed by monitor loop) (orchestrator.py, lines 364–397)
- Session exits without reaching Done (orchestrator.py, lines 431–460): agent left issue in non-Done state

### 4. **Per-Issue Failure Modes** — Detection & Handling
Each is a non-halting error or a fatal halt:

| Failure | Detection | Outcome |
|---------|-----------|---------|
| **Worktree add fails** (dirty tree, missing main branch) | worktree.add raises (worktree.py:35) | Pre-spawn halt; run-log entry with `exit_code=-1`, `model=null`, `usage=null` |
| **Linear.set_state fails** (API outage, permissions) | orchestrator:263 try/except | Pre-spawn halt; no state revert attempted; planned path used |
| **Claude process spawn fails** | subprocess.Popen (worker.py:181) | Pre-spawn halt if Popen itself errors (e.g., command not found); caught by orchestrator's outer try/except |
| **Claude exits non-zero** | subprocess return code (worker.py:220) | Recorded but not inherently a halt; orchestrator checks `is_done` state in Linear (orchestrator.py:401) |
| **Per-issue token/time cap crossed** | monitor loop polls every 1s (worker.py:267–300), compares to limits | Worker returns `Breach` object; orchestrator SIGKILLs process group (worker.py:303), reverts state, halt |
| **Session killed mid-output** | reader thread timeout after SIGKILL (worker.py:216, _READER_JOIN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=5s) | Usage fields incomplete; `cost_usd`/`num_turns` may be `null` |
| **Revert set_state fails** | orchestrator:370 / 435 try/except | Halt-reason appends error; non-fatal, still exit 1 |

### 5. **Quota / Session-Hours Tracking** — Per-Cycle Caps
Limits (`limits.py`, lines 51–93) are per-issue and per-cycle:
- **Measurement**: Worker reconstructs usage from `stream-json` events, keyed by message ID (worker.py, lines 99–483)
- **Per-issue**: Token limit (default 8M) checked every poll interval; time limit (default 20 min) same; cost limit (default $15) passed to `claude` as `--max-budget-usd` (worker.py, line 172)
- **Per-cycle**: Checked after each Done issue (orchestrator.py:179); cumulative tokens (runlog.py:223), cost (line 218), wall-clock (line 211) versus caps. First breach halts.
- **When hit**: Breach is recorded in `cycle_halt_reason`, halt message printed, exit 1 (orchestrator.py:186–189)
- **Load source**: `limits.yml` at `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml` (limits.py:95–154); absent file uses defaults

### 6. **OpenTelemetry / Honeycomb Spans** — Trace Surface
Opt-in via `HONEYCOMB_API_KEY` env var (telemetry.py, lines 48–83):

**Span hierarchy** (root to leaf):
- `drain.cycle` (orchestrator.py:120) — attributes: `drain.cycle_id`, `drain.issues_planned`, `drain.issues_deferred`, `drain.outcome`
- `drain.issue` (orchestrator.py:216) — attributes: `issue.identifier`, `issue.title`, `issue.index`, `issue.total`, `issue.repo`, `issue.model`, `issue.exit_code`, `issue.is_done`, `issue.final_linear_state`
  - `linear.<operation>` (linear.py:48) — operations: `current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state`
    - `httpx` (auto-instrumented child spans for POST to Linear GraphQL endpoint)
  - `drain.worktree.add` / `drain.worktree.remove` (worktree.py:31, 76)
  - `drain.worker.session` (worker.py:166) — attributes: `worker.model`, `worker.exit_code`, `worker.duration_seconds`, `worker.tokens.cumulative`, `worker.tokens.peak_context`, `worker.num_turns`, `worker.cost_usd`, `worker.session_id`, `worker.is_error`
    - On breach: `worker.breach.scope`, `worker.breach.metric`, `worker.breach.limit`, `worker.breach.observed`

**Error marking** (telemetry.mark_error):
- `err-dependency-cycle`, `err-repo-resolution`, `err-setup-failed`, `err-per-issue-breach`, `err-issue-not-done`, `err-cycle-breach` (orchestrator.py)
- `err-worker-breach` (worker.py:264)
- `err-linear-no-api-key`, `err-linear-http`, `err-linear-graphql` (linear.py)

All error spans set `error=true` and `exception.slug=<slug>` for low-cardinality filtering.

---

This surface is extensive and deterministic: every failure path is captured into the run log, stderr, and (optionally) Honeycomb traces. A test suite can verify state reverts, halt messages, run-log shape, and trace attributes by mocking Linear + claude and observing the runlog artifact and telemetry output.
```

> AGENT

I have a solid map now. The mock has to intercept two surfaces:

- **Parent** — `linear.py` makes raw GraphQL POSTs to a hard-coded `https://api.linear.app/graphql` (5 ops: `current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state`).
- **Spawned children** — `claude -p` calls Linear MCP tools (`save_issue`, `save_comment`) via the `.mcp.json` that gets symlinked into the worktree.

Existing tests monkey-patch `linear._post` and replace `claude -p` with marker-touching shell scripts. The session/usage tracking flows from the child's `stream-json` output through `_UsageAccumulator` into the runlog at `~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<ts>.json`. No native retry — "resume" means re-running drain against the same cycle, where in-flight issues have been reverted to Todo. Two cycle-halt paths (`DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach) and four per-issue halt paths.

Before I write the plan, three architectural choices will materially shape the design:

          "description": "Each scenario is a pytest test that calls helper builders (`make_cycle(issues=[...], labels=[...])`, `script_child(transcript=...)`, `expect_runlog(...)`). Simple, debuggable, no DSL to learn. Best fit for the existing test style."
        },
        {
          "label": "YAML scenarios + a Python runner",
          "description": "Declarative YAML files describe issues, expected outcomes, injected failures. A Python harness loads and runs them. Easier to scan a list of scenarios, but adds a DSL layer and can't express conditional logic."
        },
        {
          "label": "Recorded fixtures (VCR-style)",
          "description": "Record real Linear responses, replay them. Realistic data but maintenance burden (re-record on schema drift) and awkward for injecting mid-call failures."
        }
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/.claude/plans/lets-design-plan-snug-tarjan.md",
  "content": "# Integration test suite: mock Linear + scripted child\n\n## Context\n\nToday the integration story for `drain-cycle` is \"use a real Linear cycle\". This makes anything beyond happy-path expensive to test: failure modes require deliberately broken issues, retries/resume require actually halting and restarting the drain, and usage/cycle-hours behaviour can only be exercised by running real Claude sessions. Unit tests cover individual modules but stop at the function boundary — monkey-patching `linear._post` and replacing `claude -p` with marker-touching shell scripts (`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22`, `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65`).\n\nThe gap is **scenario-level integration tests** that exercise the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, so we can validate:\n\n- **Best-case drains** — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions\n- **Per-issue failure paths** — every halt condition in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (repo resolution, worktree setup, `set_state` failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)\n- **Cycle-level halts** — `DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach propagation\n- **Resume semantics** — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo\n- **Session/usage tracking** — `_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches\n\nThe drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); \"retry\" in our context means **resume**, which is a re-invocation against the same cycle.\n\n## Workflow\n\nRecommended skill sequence:\n\n1. **`shape-design-doc`** — this plan is the equivalent artefact; promote to `docs/integration-test-architecture.md` (or an ADR) before building if more reviewers need it.\n2. **`shape-planning-and-task-breakdown`** — convert the *Slices* section below into Linear issues on a fresh initiative under the existing project.\n3. **`agent-skills:test-driven-development`** during build — each slice ships with a failing scenario before the supporting infra.\n4. **`agent-skills:code-review-and-quality`** per slice before commit, per `AGENTS.md`.\n\n## Approach\n\n**Parent-side HTTP mock + scripted child + in-process driver.** Three pillars:\n\n### Pillar 1 — `MockLinear`: an in-process fake GraphQL server\n\nA FastAPI/Starlette app bound to a random localhost port, started per-test via a pytest fixture. It speaks the five GraphQL operations `linear.py` actually uses (`current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state` / WorkflowState lookup) plus a small `IssueUpdate` mutation surface the scripted child can hit to flip an issue to Done.\n\nInternal state is a typed in-memory store: `Cycle`, `Issue`, `Label`, `WorkflowState`. The store records every call (operation, variables, timestamp) so tests can assert call sequence and counts.\n\nFault injection is **per-operation, per-call-index**: `mock.fail_after(op=\"set_state\", call=2, mode=\"http_500\")`. Modes include `http_500`, `http_429`, `graphql_error`, `malformed_json`, `slow_response(seconds)`, `connection_reset`. The drain has no retry, so \"fail\" here means \"this single call breaks\" — tests then verify the resulting halt path.\n\n**This requires one production change**: `linear.py:18` is hard-coded `https://api.linear.app/graphql`. Replace with `os.environ.get(\"LINEAR_API_URL\", \"https://api.linear.app/graphql\")` so tests can redirect. (No CLI flag — env var only.)\n\n### Pillar 2 — `fake_claude`: a programmable replacement for `claude -p`\n\nA small Python entrypoint (installed as a console script in tests/) that mimics `claude -p --dangerously-skip-permissions ...`. It:\n\n- Reads a **transcript fixture** named in an env var (e.g. `FAKE_CLAUDE_TRANSCRIPT=happy_done`).\n- Streams the fixture's `stream-json` events to stdout, with configurable inter-event delays so worker breach loops can fire deterministically.\n- Optionally POSTs to `$LINEAR_API_URL` (the mock) to transition the issue to Done — simulating what the real session's MCP tools would have done. Skipping the POST is how we test \"child exits 0 but issue not Done\" (`orchestrator.py:401`).\n- Exits with a configurable code.\n\nTranscript fixtures live in `tests/integration/transcripts/*.json` (e.g. `happy_done.json`, `breach_tokens.json`, `breach_time.json`, `dup_message_id.json`, `exits_without_done.json`). Reusable across scenarios.\n\nTests substitute it via `monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, \"_CLAUDE_CMD\", [str(fake_claude_path), ...])` or by patching `subprocess.Popen` at the worker boundary. The existing `_CLAUDE_CMD` constant in `orchestrator.py:25` is the natural seam.\n\n### Pillar 3 — `IntegrationHarness`: a fixture that drives `orchestrator.run()` in-process\n\nA pytest fixture that:\n\n- Creates a temp `HOME` so `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`, `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`, and `~/.drain-cycle/runs/` redirect to a tmpdir (set via `monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", ...)` plus whatever path-resolution helpers exist in `cli.py`/`runlog.py`).\n- Initialises one or more real git repos in tmpdir (the existing test pattern in `test_orchestrator_iteration.py`).\n- Writes a `repos.yml` mapping label names to those repo paths.\n- Starts `MockLinear`, populates it with the scenario's cycle/issues/labels.\n- Sets `LINEAR_API_URL`, `LINEAR_API_KEY=test`, `FAKE_CLAUDE_TRANSCRIPT=...` env vars.\n- Substitutes the claude command for `fake_claude`.\n- Calls `orchestrator.run(repos, limits)` directly and returns a `Result` object: exit code, runlog JSON, mock call log, final mock state.\n\nTests assert against `Result`. One scenario per `test_*` function. Helpers (`make_cycle`, `make_issue`, `expect_runlog`, `expect_calls`) keep scenarios short.\n\n## Production code changes required\n\nMinimal — every test-only seam piggybacks on something that already exists:\n\n1. **`drain_cycle/linear.py:18`** — `_GRAPHQL_URL` reads from `LINEAR_API_URL` env var, defaulting to the current constant. Single-line change.\n2. **`drain_cycle/runlog.py`** — confirm the run-log directory honours `HOME` (it should via `pathlib.Path.home()`); if it caches the path at import, add a small accessor so the harness can re-resolve after `monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", ...)`.\n3. **`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`** — `_CLAUDE_CMD` is already module-level; leave as-is, tests patch it.\n\nNo other production changes. No new abstraction layers, no `LinearClient` Protocol — the existing module-level functions are already easy enough to drive against an HTTP fake once the URL is configurable.\n\n## Scenario catalogue (initial coverage)\n\nGrouped by area; each is one test function. This is the target end-state, not a single slice — slices below build the infra and add scenarios incrementally.\n\n**Best case**\n- 3-issue dependency-ordered drain reaches Done in order; runlog totals correct\n- Mixed Todo + Backlog states picked up; correct planning\n- Single-issue drain\n- Empty cycle exits cleanly\n\n**Per-issue halts** (`orchestrator._drain_one_issue` paths)\n- Missing `repo:` label → halt + reason\n- `repo:` label resolves to nonexistent path → halt\n- Worktree `add` fails (dirty tree) → halt\n- `set_state` (Todo→In-Progress) fails with HTTP 500 → halt\n- Child exits 0 but issue not Done → halt + reason matches `final_linear_state`\n- Child exits non-zero → halt\n- Per-issue token cap exceeded mid-stream → breach kill, runlog records breach scope/metric/limit/observed\n- Per-issue time cap exceeded → time breach recorded\n- Revert `set_state` (In-Progress→Todo) fails after a halt → halt-reason concatenates\n\n**Cycle-level halts**\n- `DependencyCycleError` from `pending_issues` → halt before any issue attempted; `cycle_halt_reason` set; runlog otherwise empty\n- After two Done issues, cycle-cap breach detected → third issue not attempted; halt reason names cumulative metric\n\n**Resume**\n- Run halts on issue 2; re-run with the same cycle picks up issue 2 (mock has it back at Todo via the revert) and issue 3; second runlog file separate from first; final mock state correct\n\n**Session / usage tracking** (asserted against runlog + `_UsageAccumulator`)\n- Transcript with known token totals → runlog `usage.cumulative` and `peak_context` match\n- Duplicate `message.id` in transcript → counted once (worker dedup at `worker.py:416`)\n- `cost_usd`/`num_turns`/`session_id`/`is_error` from the `result` event populated\n- Cycle-level aggregates (`cycle_tokens_cumulative`, `cycle_cost_usd`, `cycle_duration_seconds`) are correct sums\n\n**Linear edge cases** (no native retry, so all of these halt)\n- 500, 429, GraphQL error, malformed JSON, slow-within-timeout (succeeds), slow-beyond-timeout (halts)\n\n**Out of scope for MVP**\n- Honeycomb/OTel span assertions (separate slice — needs fake OTLP collector; production code already wires spans cleanly per `drain_cycle/telemetry.py`)\n- Subprocess-based smoke suite (the in-process suite already proves the orchestrator; CLI wire-up is small and stable)\n- True MCP-server fake for children (the scripted-child + direct GraphQL POST simulates the contract sufficiently)\n\n## Slices (verifiable build order)\n\nEach slice ends with a passing scenario as proof, per agentic Principle 4. Each is small enough to ship in one session.\n\n1. **Production seam** — `LINEAR_API_URL` env var in `linear.py`; passing unit test that confirms override; default unchanged.\n2. **`MockLinear` skeleton** — server with `current_cycle` and `pending_issues` only; serves a hard-coded scenario; one scenario test wires it through `linear.py` and confirms the call lands on the mock.\n3. **`fake_claude` skeleton** — Python entrypoint that streams a static `happy_done` transcript and POSTs an `IssueUpdate` to the mock. First end-to-end happy-path scenario passes.\n4. **`IntegrationHarness` fixture** — tmpdir HOME, repos.yml, real git repo, mock startup. Refactor slice-3 scenario to use the harness.\n5. **Mock GraphQL completeness** — add `get_issue`, `set_state`, WorkflowState resolution; happy-path drain of 3 ordered issues passes.\n6. **Fault injection** — per-operation, per-call-index fault modes in mock; scenario covering `set_state` HTTP 500 halt.\n7. **Per-issue halt scenarios** — repo resolution failures, worktree-add failure, child non-zero exit, child exits without Done. One test each.\n8. **Breach scenarios** — transcript fixtures `breach_tokens`, `breach_time`; verify breach object recorded in runlog with correct scope/metric/limit/observed.\n9. **Cycle-level halt scenarios** — `DependencyCycleError` (mock returns cyclic graph), cycle-cap breach after two Done issues.\n10. **Resume scenario** — drive a halt, re-invoke orchestrator on same mock cycle, assert second runlog file and final state.\n11. **Usage-tracking scenarios** — duplicate-message-id transcript, cycle-aggregate sums, terminal `result` event fields.\n12. **Linear edge cases** — 429/malformed/slow-timeout halt scenarios.\n\nSlices 1–4 are the foundation; 5–12 are independent and can ship in any order once 4 is in. Each slice opens a Linear issue under the existing project; the slice is Done per `AGENTS.md` when reviewed, committed, pushed, and the Linear comment posted.\n\n## Critical files\n\nTo modify:\n- `drain_cycle/linear.py` — env-var override for GraphQL URL (slice 1)\n- `drain_cycle/runlog.py` — verify `HOME` re-resolution path (slice 4 if needed)\n\nTo create:\n- `tests/integration/conftest.py` — harness fixture\n- `tests/integration/mock_linear.py` — fake GraphQL server + in-memory store + fault injection\n- `tests/integration/fake_claude.py` — scripted child entrypoint\n- `tests/integration/transcripts/*.json` — stream-json fixtures\n- `tests/integration/test_happy_path.py`, `test_per_issue_halts.py`, `test_cycle_halts.py`, `test_resume.py`, `test_usage_tracking.py`, `test_linear_edges.py` — scenarios\n\nTo reuse from existing tests:\n- Real git-repo setup pattern in `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22-90`\n- Linear fixture style in `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65-70`\n- `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`\n\n## Verification\n\nEnd-to-end:\n- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -v` — all scenarios green, no real network calls, no `LINEAR_API_KEY` from the real Linear leaking in.\n- `uv run pytest --cov=drain_cycle/orchestrator --cov=drain_cycle/worker --cov=drain_cycle/runlog tests/integration/` — coverage on the orchestrator + worker + runlog modules increases meaningfully (target: every halt path in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` exercised at least once).\n- After each slice: `gh repo view` not required; this repo pushes to main directly, so the slice closes with `commit + push + Linear comment + Done`, per `AGENTS.md`.\n\nNegative checks during build:\n- `grep -r \"api.linear.app\" tests/` returns nothing (no accidental real-Linear calls in test fixtures).\n- The mock's call log shows every operation a scenario expects; tests fail loudly if an unexpected call lands.\n"
}

Each slice opens a Linear issue under the existing project; the slice is Done per `AGENTS.md` when reviewed, committed, pushed, and the Linear comment posted.\n\n## Critical files\n\nTo modify:\n- `drain_cycle/linear.py` — env-var override for GraphQL URL (slice 1)\n- `drain_cycle/runlog.py` — verify `HOME` re-resolution path (slice 4 if needed)\n\nTo create:\n- `tests/integration/conftest.py` — harness fixture\n- `tests/integration/mock_linear.py` — fake GraphQL server + in-memory store + fault injection\n- `tests/integration/fake_claude.py` — scripted child entrypoint\n- `tests/integration/transcripts/*.json` — stream-json fixtures\n- `tests/integration/test_happy_path.py`, `test_per_issue_halts.py`, `test_cycle_halts.py`, `test_resume.py`, `test_usage_tracking.py`, `test_linear_edges.py` — scenarios\n\nTo reuse from existing tests:\n- Real git-repo setup pattern in `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22-90`\n- Linear fixture style in `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65-70`\n- `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`\n\n## Verification\n\nEnd-to-end:\n- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -v` — all scenarios green, no real network calls, no `LINEAR_API_KEY` from the real Linear leaking in.\n- `uv run pytest --cov=drain_cycle/orchestrator --cov=drain_cycle/worker --cov=drain_cycle/runlog tests/integration/` — coverage on the orchestrator + worker + runlog modules increases meaningfully (target: every halt path in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` exercised at least once).\n- After each slice: `gh repo view` not required; this repo pushes to main directly, so the slice closes with `commit + push + Linear comment + Done`, per `AGENTS.md`.\n\nNegative checks during build:\n- `grep -r \"api.linear.app\" tests/` returns nothing (no accidental real-Linear calls in test fixtures).\n- The mock's call log shows every operation a scenario expects; tests fail loudly if an unexpected call lands.\n",

# Plan review: integration-test-suite-mock-linear

## Inputs
- **Appetite**: 12 slices (fixed cap)
- **Cynefin domain**: Complicated — known cause-effect with expertise
- **Tier**: **Full** — selected because appetite > 5 slices
- Fast-track gate: did not fire (new test infrastructure, not KTLO)

## B1 — Problem framing
PARTIAL. Plan opens with the problem ("today the integration story is 'use a real Linear cycle'…") — not solution-first. But no **measurable outcome target** is named (Product P1). *Falsifying condition: the plan would survive review without naming a quantitative or behavioural outcome.*

## B2 — Scope clarity

| Item | Verdict | Falsifying condition |
|---|---|---|
| `runlog.py` HOME re-resolution — "verify" implies possibly modify, not declared | PARTIAL | Slice 4 ships without a decision on whether `runlog` is modified or not |
| `cli.py` secrets behaviour when `~/.drain-cycle/.env` exists on test machine | PARTIAL | A test run on a developer's machine with real `LINEAR_API_KEY` in `~/.drain-cycle/.env` produces different behaviour than CI |
| Real git-repo setup (slice 4 says "real git repo") | OVERTURNED | Reuses an existing test pattern |
| "Fault injection" scenarios — modes listed, but no per-scenario expected state | SUSTAINED | Slice 6 ships without a runlog-snapshot + mock-state assertion contract |
| Existing git pattern reference | OVERTURNED | Pattern explicit |
| Transcript fixture authoring — no schema spec | SUSTAINED | Slice 3 ships without a documented fixture stencil |

## B3 — Assumptions + evidence quality

| Assumption | Confidence (Gilad) | 5-min test or owner | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| `_CLAUDE_CMD` is a module-level patch point | 7 | `grep -n _CLAUDE_CMD drain_cycle/` | OVERTURNED |
| `runlog` honours `pathlib.Path.home()` re-resolution | 3 | `grep -n "Path.home\|expanduser\|HOME" drain_cycle/runlog.py` | PARTIAL — verify in slice 1 |
| Scripted child POSTing GraphQL is contractually equivalent to MCP | 7 | Already verified — `orchestrator.py:401` checks `final_linear_state` only | OVERTURNED |
| `httpx` auto-instrumentation in tests won't pollute mock's call log or fail when no OTLP endpoint is set | 2 | Stand up FastAPI + import order check | SUSTAINED |
| Worker breach loop is deterministic enough to drive token-breaches from a transcript | 3 | Read `worker.py:267-300`; confirm token check is per-stream-event vs wall-clock | SUSTAINED |
| `MockLinear` random-port binding survives parallel test runners | 5 | pytest-xdist port-fixture pattern | PARTIAL |

## B4 — Dependencies
| Dependency | Owner | Capacity | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| FastAPI/Starlette as test dep | Self | Trivial | OVERTURNED |
| pytest, httpx | Already in repo | n/a | OVERTURNED |

No cross-team dependencies.

## B5 — Reversibility + ADR pairing
| One-way door | Alternatives in plan? | ADR committed? | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| Architecture: parent HTTP + scripted child vs. fake-MCP vs. skip-child | Mentioned in conversation (AskUserQuestion), not in plan body | "Promote to docs… or ADR" — not committed | SUSTAINED |
| `LINEAR_API_URL` env-var seam in `linear.py` | n/a — trivial | n/a | OVERTURNED |

*Falsifying condition: no file under `docs/adrs/` references this architecture after slice 4 ships.*

## B6 — Operability + success metrics
- Metrics: "all scenarios green" + qualitative coverage target — PARTIAL
- Alerts: n/a (test infrastructure)
- Rollback: implicit (revert the test files)
- Runbook: n/a
- Capacity headroom: n/a
- **User-visible outcome metric: ABSENT** — Product P1 not satisfied

*Falsifying condition: plan does not name a measurable outcome (e.g., "every new halt path added to `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` has an integration test before merge, enforced by …"). SUSTAINED.*

## B7 — Sequencing + capacity
OVERTURNED. Slices 1–4 foundation, 5–12 independent — named explicitly. Single FTE consistent with appetite.

## B8 — Pre-mortem (top 3, kill-switches for top 2)

1. **Breach scenarios are flaky** (slice 8). Worker's breach loop polls cumulative tokens from streaming events; driving a deterministic breach from a fake transcript requires the worker's loop to see events at the right rate. Real-world: passes locally, flakes in CI under load. **Likelihood: HIGH.**
   *Kill-switch:* before committing to slice 8, build one breach scenario as a spike at the end of slice 3 (fake_claude). If it can't be made deterministic in <2 hours of investigation, drop the token-breach scenario from MVP and keep only the time-breach one (wall-clock is deterministic).

2. **MockLinear becomes a parallel Linear implementation that drifts.** A real Linear schema change (new label-group semantics, new field) means production breaks while tests pass. **Likelihood: MEDIUM.**
   *Kill-switch:* add an `audit_linear_surface` test that fails CI when any new GraphQL operation is added to `linear.py` without a corresponding mock handler. Single grep-based test.

3. **In-process driver hides CLI/secrets bugs.** Harness skips `cli.main()`, so a regression in secrets-load order escapes. **Likelihood: LOW–MEDIUM.** No kill-switch needed if a single subprocess smoke test is added to MVP (it currently isn't).

## Recommendation

**REVISE** — six unresolved SUSTAINED verdicts. Quick-tier reversibility carve-out does not apply (Full tier).

### Conditions before APPROVE
1. **Outcome metric** — name what success looks like in the world (Product P1; B6).
2. **ADR commitment** — slice 1 produces `docs/adrs/NNN-integration-test-architecture.md` recording chosen approach + rejected alternatives (Universal A3; B5).
3. **Breach-determinism spike** — promoted into slice 3 with a kill-switch on slice 8 scope (B3 assumption 5; B8 #1).
4. **Per-scenario contract** — every scenario specifies expected runlog snapshot + mock final state (B2 #4).
5. **Transcript fixture spec** — slice 3 produces a documented stencil (B2 #6).
6. **httpx instrumentation check** — short spike in slice 2 to confirm `httpx` auto-instrumentation either no-ops without an OTLP endpoint or is suppressed in tests (B3 assumption 4).
7. **Subprocess smoke test** — single end-to-end CLI scenario added to MVP, not deferred (B8 #3).
8. **Schema-drift guard** — `audit_linear_surface` test added in slice 5 or earlier (B8 #2).

Updating the plan now to address each condition.

  "new_string": "The gap is **scenario-level integration tests** that exercise the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, so we can validate:\n\n- **Best-case drains** — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions\n- **Per-issue failure paths** — every halt condition in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (repo resolution, worktree setup, `set_state` failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)\n- **Cycle-level halts** — `DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach propagation\n- **Resume semantics** — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo\n- **Session/usage tracking** — `_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches\n\nThe drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); \"retry\" in our context means **resume**, which is a re-invocation against the same cycle.\n\n### Success criterion (outcome, not output)\n\nThe suite is \"working\" when **every halt path in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` and every cycle-halt path in `orchestrator._run` is exercised by at least one integration scenario, and a new halt path added to either function cannot be merged without an accompanying scenario**. Enforced by a CI gate (see *Verification*) that fails when the union of `mark_error` slugs emitted in production code is not a subset of the slugs observed in the test run's captured spans / runlog `halt_reason` strings. Behavioural outcome: when a future bug surfaces in the orchestrator's halt logic, time-to-reproduce drops from \"set up a real Linear cycle\" (~hours) to \"write a 20-line scenario\" (~minutes)."

Rejected because happy-path coverage is a hard requirement.\n- **Function-injection / `LinearClient` protocol.** Cheaper than HTTP, but doesn't exercise the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. Rejected.\n\nA short ADR — `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` — is a deliverable of slice 1 (see *Slices*). It records the chosen approach, the four rejected ones, and the conditions under which the choice should be revisited."

Helpers (`make_cycle`, `make_issue`, `expect_runlog`, `expect_calls`) keep scenarios short.\n\n**Per-scenario contract.** Every scenario specifies, at minimum:\n\n1. **Setup** — `Cycle`, `Issue[]`, `Label[]`, `WorkflowState[]`, repos.yml mapping, limits.yml overrides, transcript fixture name(s), any per-operation fault injection.\n2. **Expected exit code** — orchestrator return.\n3. **Expected runlog snapshot** — a dict the scenario asserts against `Result.runlog`. Use `dirty-equals` or a deep-diff helper so non-deterministic fields (timestamps, durations within tolerance, session ids) are matched loosely.\n4. **Expected final mock state** — the issue states the mock should end in (e.g. `{ \"ABA-1\": \"Done\", \"ABA-2\": \"Todo\" }` after a halt-on-issue-2).\n5. **Expected call sequence** — ordered list of `(operation, predicate-on-vars)` tuples the mock should have observed. Strict by default; `expect_calls(strict=False)` for tolerant cases.\n\nHelpers enforce the contract: a scenario that omits any of (3) (4) (5) fails a lint test at collection time."

One test each.\n8. **Breach scenarios** — transcript fixtures `breach_tokens`, `breach_time`; verify breach object recorded in runlog with correct scope/metric/limit/observed.\n9. **Cycle-level halt scenarios** — `DependencyCycleError` (mock returns cyclic graph), cycle-cap breach after two Done issues.\n10. **Resume scenario** — drive a halt, re-invoke orchestrator on same mock cycle, assert second runlog file and final state.\n11. **Usage-tracking scenarios** — duplicate-message-id transcript, cycle-aggregate sums, terminal `result` event fields.\n12. **Linear edge cases** — 429/malformed/slow-timeout halt scenarios.\n\nSlices 1–4 are the foundation; 5–12 are independent and can ship in any order once 4 is in. Each scenario carries the per-scenario contract (expected runlog snapshot + mock state + call sequence).\n7. **Per-issue halt scenarios** — repo resolution failures, worktree-add failure, child non-zero exit, child exits without Done. {
  "plan": "# Integration test suite: mock Linear + scripted child\n\n## Context\n\nToday the integration story for `drain-cycle` is \"use a real Linear cycle\". This makes anything beyond happy-path expensive to test: failure modes require deliberately broken issues, retries/resume require actually halting and restarting the drain, and usage/cycle-hours behaviour can only be exercised by running real Claude sessions. Unit tests cover individual modules but stop at the function boundary — monkey-patching `linear._post` and replacing `claude -p` with marker-touching shell scripts (`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22`, `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65`).\n\nThe gap is **scenario-level integration tests** that exercise the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, so we can validate:\n\n- **Best-case drains** — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions\n- **Per-issue failure paths** — every halt condition in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (repo resolution, worktree setup, `set_state` failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)\n- **Cycle-level halts** — `DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach propagation\n- **Resume semantics** — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo\n- **Session/usage tracking** — `_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches\n\nThe drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); \"retry\" in our context means **resume**, which is a re-invocation against the same cycle.\n\n### Success criterion (outcome, not output)\n\nThe suite is \"working\" when **every halt path in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` and every cycle-halt path in `orchestrator._run` is exercised by at least one integration scenario, and a new halt path added to either function cannot be merged without an accompanying scenario**. Enforced by a CI gate (see *Verification*) that fails when the union of `mark_error` slugs emitted in production code is not a subset of the slugs observed in the test run's captured spans / runlog `halt_reason` strings. Behavioural outcome: when a future bug surfaces in the orchestrator's halt logic, time-to-reproduce drops from \"set up a real Linear cycle\" (~hours) to \"write a 20-line scenario\" (~minutes).\n\n## Workflow\n\nRecommended skill sequence:\n\n1. **`shape-design-doc`** — this plan is the equivalent artefact; promote to `docs/integration-test-architecture.md` (or an ADR) before building if more reviewers need it.\n2. **`shape-planning-and-task-breakdown`** — convert the *Slices* section below into Linear issues on a fresh initiative under the existing project.\n3. **`agent-skills:test-driven-development`** during build — each slice ships with a failing scenario before the supporting infra.\n4. **`agent-skills:code-review-and-quality`** per slice before commit, per `AGENTS.md`.\n\n## Approach\n\n**Parent-side HTTP mock + scripted child + in-process driver.** Three pillars.\n\n**Alternatives considered and rejected** (recorded here so the ADR slice has a draft):\n\n- **Parent HTTP + fake MCP server.** Truer end-to-end on the child path, but doubles the mock surface — a real Linear MCP would need both protocol scaffolding and tool definitions for `save_issue`/`save_comment`. Rejected: the orchestrator's only post-spawn contract check is `final_linear_state == Done` (`orchestrator.py:401`), so scripting the child to POST GraphQL directly is contractually equivalent for less code.\n- **Skip child surface entirely.** Useful only for halt-path tests. Rejected because happy-path coverage is a hard requirement.\n- **Function-injection / `LinearClient` protocol.** Cheaper than HTTP, but doesn't exercise the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. Rejected: the existing module-level functions in `linear.py` are already easy to drive once `LINEAR_API_URL` is overridable.\n- **VCR-style recorded fixtures.** Realistic but awkward for failure injection and high maintenance under schema drift. Rejected.\n\nA short ADR — `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` — is a deliverable of slice 1 (see *Slices*). It records the chosen approach, the four rejected ones, and the conditions under which the choice should be revisited.\n\n### Pillar 1 — `MockLinear`: an in-process fake GraphQL server\n\nA FastAPI/Starlette app bound to a random localhost port, started per-test via a pytest fixture. It speaks the five GraphQL operations `linear.py` actually uses (`current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state` / WorkflowState lookup) plus a small `IssueUpdate` mutation surface the scripted child can hit to flip an issue to Done.\n\nInternal state is a typed in-memory store: `Cycle`, `Issue`, `Label`, `WorkflowState`. The store records every call (operation, variables, timestamp) so tests can assert call sequence and counts.\n\nFault injection is **per-operation, per-call-index**: `mock.fail_after(op=\"set_state\", call=2, mode=\"http_500\")`. Modes include `http_500`, `http_429`, `graphql_error`, `malformed_json`, `slow_response(seconds)`, `connection_reset`. The drain has no retry, so \"fail\" here means \"this single call breaks\" — tests then verify the resulting halt path.\n\n**This requires one production change**: `linear.py:18` is hard-coded `https://api.linear.app/graphql`. Replace with `os.environ.get(\"LINEAR_API_URL\", \"https://api.linear.app/graphql\")` so tests can redirect. (No CLI flag — env var only.)\n\n### Pillar 2 — `fake_claude`: a programmable replacement for `claude -p`\n\nA small Python entrypoint (installed as a console script in tests/) that mimics `claude -p --dangerously-skip-permissions ...`. It:\n\n- Reads a **transcript fixture** named in an env var (e.g. `FAKE_CLAUDE_TRANSCRIPT=happy_done`).\n- Streams the fixture's `stream-json` events to stdout, with configurable inter-event delays so worker breach loops can fire deterministically.\n- Optionally POSTs to `$LINEAR_API_URL` (the mock) to transition the issue to Done — simulating what the real session's MCP tools would have done. Skipping the POST is how we test \"child exits 0 but issue not Done\" (`orchestrator.py:401`).\n- Exits with a configurable code.\n\nTranscript fixtures live in `tests/integration/transcripts/*.json` (e.g. `happy_done.json`, `breach_tokens.json`, `breach_time.json`, `dup_message_id.json`, `exits_without_done.json`). Reusable across scenarios.\n\n**Fixture format** (documented in `tests/integration/transcripts/README.md`, deliverable of slice 3):\n\n```json\n{\n  \"exit_code\": 0,\n  \"post_to_linear\": { \"transition_to\": \"Done\" },\n  \"events\": [\n    { \"delay_ms\": 0,    \"event\": { \"type\": \"system\", \"subtype\": \"init\", \"session_id\": \"...\"} },\n    { \"delay_ms\": 50,   \"event\": { \"type\": \"assistant\", \"message\": { \"id\": \"msg_1\", \"usage\": {...} } } },\n    { \"delay_ms\": 100,  \"event\": { \"type\": \"result\", \"cost_usd\": 0.42, \"num_turns\": 3, \"is_error\": false } }\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n`delay_ms` is inter-event spacing for breach-loop determinism. The `event` payloads conform to the same `stream-json` shape `worker._UsageAccumulator.feed()` parses today (`worker.py:399`); the stencil is \"anything the accumulator dedupes on `message.id` and reads `cost_usd`/`num_turns`/`is_error`/`session_id` from the `result` event\".\n\nTests substitute it via `monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, \"_CLAUDE_CMD\", [str(fake_claude_path), ...])` or by patching `subprocess.Popen` at the worker boundary. The existing `_CLAUDE_CMD` constant in `orchestrator.py:25` is the natural seam.\n\n### Pillar 3 — `IntegrationHarness`: a fixture that drives `orchestrator.run()` in-process\n\nA pytest fixture that:\n\n- Creates a temp `HOME` so `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`, `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`, and `~/.drain-cycle/runs/` redirect to a tmpdir (set via `monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", ...)` plus whatever path-resolution helpers exist in `cli.py`/`runlog.py`).\n- Initialises one or more real git repos in tmpdir (the existing test pattern in `test_orchestrator_iteration.py`).\n- Writes a `repos.yml` mapping label names to those repo paths.\n- Starts `MockLinear`, populates it with the scenario's cycle/issues/labels.\n- Sets `LINEAR_API_URL`, `LINEAR_API_KEY=test`, `FAKE_CLAUDE_TRANSCRIPT=...` env vars.\n- Substitutes the claude command for `fake_claude`.\n- Calls `orchestrator.run(repos, limits)` directly and returns a `Result` object: exit code, runlog JSON, mock call log, final mock state.\n\nTests assert against `Result`. One scenario per `test_*` function. Helpers (`make_cycle`, `make_issue`, `expect_runlog`, `expect_calls`) keep scenarios short.\n\n**Per-scenario contract.** Every scenario specifies, at minimum:\n\n1. **Setup** — `Cycle`, `Issue[]`, `Label[]`, `WorkflowState[]`, repos.yml mapping, limits.yml overrides, transcript fixture name(s), any per-operation fault injection.\n2. **Expected exit code** — orchestrator return.\n3. **Expected runlog snapshot** — a dict the scenario asserts against `Result.runlog`. Use `dirty-equals` or a deep-diff helper so non-deterministic fields (timestamps, durations within tolerance, session ids) are matched loosely.\n4. **Expected final mock state** — the issue states the mock should end in (e.g. `{ \"ABA-1\": \"Done\", \"ABA-2\": \"Todo\" }` after a halt-on-issue-2).\n5. **Expected call sequence** — ordered list of `(operation, predicate-on-vars)` tuples the mock should have observed. Strict by default; `expect_calls(strict=False)` for tolerant cases.\n\nHelpers enforce the contract: a scenario that omits any of (3) (4) (5) fails a lint test at collection time.\n\n## Production code changes required\n\nMinimal — every test-only seam piggybacks on something that already exists:\n\n1. **`drain_cycle/linear.py:18`** — `_GRAPHQL_URL` reads from `LINEAR_API_URL` env var, defaulting to the current constant. Single-line change.\n2. **`drain_cycle/runlog.py`** — verify the run-log directory honours `HOME` re-resolution; slice 1 includes a `grep \"Path.home\\|expanduser\\|HOME\" drain_cycle/runlog.py` check. If the path is cached at module load, add a small accessor (e.g. `_run_log_dir()`) so the harness can re-resolve after `monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", ...)`.\n3. **`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`** — `_CLAUDE_CMD` is already module-level; verified by Explore. Tests patch it.\n\nNo other production changes. No new abstraction layers, no `LinearClient` Protocol — the existing module-level functions are already easy enough to drive against an HTTP fake once the URL is configurable.\n\n## Test-infra correctness checks (one-time pre-slice work)\n\nTwo small spikes the plan-review surfaced as risk; both must land before the slices that depend on them.\n\n- **httpx-instrumentation pollution** — drain-cycle wires httpx OTel auto-instrumentation in `telemetry.py`. Confirm that (a) when `HONEYCOMB_API_KEY` is unset, the instrumentation either no-ops or stays silent, and (b) tests don't accidentally export spans to a real OTLP endpoint. Land as part of slice 2; if not clean, set `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` in the harness fixture.\n- **Breach-loop determinism** — read `worker.py:267-300` to confirm whether token-breach detection is driven by per-stream-event cumulative reads (deterministic from a transcript) or by a wall-clock poll (potentially flaky under CI load). Land as a 30-minute spike at the end of slice 3 with a single token-breach scenario as the falsifying test. **Kill-switch**: if not deterministic in <2 hours, drop token-breach from MVP and keep only time-breach (wall-clock — deterministic). Record the spike outcome in the slice 3 commit message.\n\n## Scenario catalogue (initial coverage)\n\nGrouped by area; each is one test function. This is the target end-state, not a single slice — slices below build the infra and add scenarios incrementally.\n\n**Best case**\n- 3-issue dependency-ordered drain reaches Done in order; runlog totals correct\n- Mixed Todo + Backlog states picked up; correct planning\n- Single-issue drain\n- Empty cycle exits cleanly\n\n**Per-issue halts** (`orchestrator._drain_one_issue` paths)\n- Missing `repo:` label → halt + reason\n- `repo:` label resolves to nonexistent path → halt\n- Worktree `add` fails (dirty tree) → halt\n- `set_state` (Todo→In-Progress) fails with HTTP 500 → halt\n- Child exits 0 but issue not Done → halt + reason matches `final_linear_state`\n- Child exits non-zero → halt\n- Per-issue token cap exceeded mid-stream → breach kill, runlog records breach scope/metric/limit/observed\n- Per-issue time cap exceeded → time breach recorded\n- Revert `set_state` (In-Progress→Todo) fails after a halt → halt-reason concatenates\n\n**Cycle-level halts**\n- `DependencyCycleError` from `pending_issues` → halt before any issue attempted; `cycle_halt_reason` set; runlog otherwise empty\n- After two Done issues, cycle-cap breach detected → third issue not attempted; halt reason names cumulative metric\n\n**Resume**\n- Run halts on issue 2; re-run with the same cycle picks up issue 2 (mock has it back at Todo via the revert) and issue 3; second runlog file separate from first; final mock state correct\n\n**Session / usage tracking** (asserted against runlog + `_UsageAccumulator`)\n- Transcript with known token totals → runlog `usage.cumulative` and `peak_context` match\n- Duplicate `message.id` in transcript → counted once (worker dedup at `worker.py:416`)\n- `cost_usd`/`num_turns`/`session_id`/`is_error` from the `result` event populated\n- Cycle-level aggregates (`cycle_tokens_cumulative`, `cycle_cost_usd`, `cycle_duration_seconds`) are correct sums\n\n**Linear edge cases** (no native retry, so all of these halt)\n- 500, 429, GraphQL error, malformed JSON, slow-within-timeout (succeeds), slow-beyond-timeout (halts)\n\n**Out of scope for MVP**\n- Honeycomb/OTel span assertions (separate slice — needs fake OTLP collector; production code already wires spans cleanly per `drain_cycle/telemetry.py`)\n- True MCP-server fake for children (the scripted-child + direct GraphQL POST simulates the contract sufficiently)\n\n**In MVP, but explicitly small** — a single subprocess smoke test in slice 4 that spawns the real `drain-cycle` binary against the mock and asserts exit code + runlog presence. This is the only end-to-end coverage of `cli.py`'s secrets-load order and process exit-code path. Larger subprocess scenarios remain out of scope.\n\n## Slices (verifiable build order)\n\nEach slice ends with a passing scenario as proof, per agentic Principle 4. Each is small enough to ship in one session.\n\n1. **Production seam + ADR** — `LINEAR_API_URL` env var in `linear.py`; passing unit test that confirms override; default unchanged. Runlog `HOME` re-resolution verified (grep-check + accessor if needed). **Deliverable**: `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, the four rejected alternatives (see *Approach*), and revisit conditions (e.g. \"if the child contract grows beyond Done-state assertion, reconsider the MCP-fake option\").\n2. **`MockLinear` skeleton + instrumentation check** — server with `current_cycle` and `pending_issues` only; serves a hard-coded scenario; one scenario test wires it through `linear.py` and confirms the call lands on the mock. Verify httpx auto-instrumentation does not export spans during tests (set `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` in harness if needed).\n3. **`fake_claude` skeleton + transcript spec + breach spike** — Python entrypoint that streams a static `happy_done` transcript and POSTs an `IssueUpdate` to the mock. First end-to-end happy-path scenario passes. **Deliverable**: `tests/integration/transcripts/README.md` documenting the fixture format. **Spike at end of slice**: one token-breach scenario as a falsifying test; if not deterministic in 2h, drop token-breach from MVP and document the constraint in the README.\n4. **`IntegrationHarness` fixture + subprocess smoke** — tmpdir HOME, repos.yml, real git repo, mock startup. Refactor slice-3 scenario to use the harness. **Plus**: one subprocess test that spawns `drain-cycle` against the mock end-to-end, asserts exit code + runlog file exists.\n5. **Mock GraphQL completeness + drift guard** — add `get_issue`, `set_state`, WorkflowState resolution; happy-path drain of 3 ordered issues passes. **Plus**: `test_linear_surface_audit` that fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a corresponding handler in the mock — keeps the mock from silently lagging schema changes.\n6. **Fault injection** — per-operation, per-call-index fault modes in mock; scenario covering `set_state` HTTP 500 halt. Each scenario carries the per-scenario contract (expected runlog snapshot + mock state + call sequence).\n7. **Per-issue halt scenarios** — repo resolution failures, worktree-add failure, child non-zero exit, child exits without Done. One test each.\n8. **Breach scenarios** — `breach_time` first (deterministic, wall-clock); `breach_tokens` second only if slice 3's spike showed it can be made deterministic. Verify breach object recorded in runlog with correct scope/metric/limit/observed.\n9. **Cycle-level halt scenarios** — `DependencyCycleError` (mock returns cyclic graph), cycle-cap breach after two Done issues.\n10. **Resume scenario** — drive a halt, re-invoke orchestrator on same mock cycle, assert second runlog file and final state.\n11. **Usage-tracking scenarios** — duplicate-message-id transcript, cycle-aggregate sums, terminal `result` event fields.\n12. **Linear edge cases** — 429/malformed/slow-timeout halt scenarios.\n\nSlices 1–4 are the foundation; 5–12 are independent and can ship in any order once 4 is in. 1	# Integration test suite: mock Linear + scripted child
2	
3	## Context
4	
5	Today the integration story for `drain-cycle` is "use a real Linear cycle". This makes anything beyond happy-path expensive to test: failure modes require deliberately broken issues, retries/resume require actually halting and restarting the drain, and usage/cycle-hours behaviour can only be exercised by running real Claude sessions. Unit tests cover individual modules but stop at the function boundary — monkey-patching `linear._post` and replacing `claude -p` with marker-touching shell scripts (`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22`, `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65`).
6	
7	The gap is **scenario-level integration tests** that exercise the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, so we can validate:
8	
9	- **Best-case drains** — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions
10	- **Per-issue failure paths** — every halt condition in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (repo resolution, worktree setup, `set_state` failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)
11	- **Cycle-level halts** — `DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach propagation
12	- **Resume semantics** — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo
13	- **Session/usage tracking** — `_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches
14	
15	The drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); "retry" in our context means **resume**, which is a re-invocation against the same cycle.
16	
17	### Success criterion (outcome, not output)
18	
19	The suite is "working" when **every halt path in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` and every cycle-halt path in `orchestrator._run` is exercised by at least one integration scenario, and a new halt path added to either function cannot be merged without an accompanying scenario**. Enforced by a CI gate (see *Verification*) that fails when the union of `mark_error` slugs emitted in production code is not a subset of the slugs observed in the test run's captured spans / runlog `halt_reason` strings. Behavioural outcome: when a future bug surfaces in the orchestrator's halt logic, time-to-reproduce drops from "set up a real Linear cycle" (~hours) to "write a 20-line scenario" (~minutes).
20	
21	## Workflow
22	
23	Recommended skill sequence:
24	
25	1. **`shape-design-doc`** — this plan is the equivalent artefact; promote to `docs/integration-test-architecture.md` (or an ADR) before building if more reviewers need it.
26	2. **`shape-planning-and-task-breakdown`** — convert the *Slices* section below into Linear issues on a fresh initiative under the existing project.
27	3. **`agent-skills:test-driven-development`** during build — each slice ships with a failing scenario before the supporting infra.
28	4. **`agent-skills:code-review-and-quality`** per slice before commit, per `AGENTS.md`.
29	
30	## Approach
31	
32	**Parent-side HTTP mock + scripted child + in-process driver.** Three pillars.
33	
34	**Alternatives considered and rejected** (recorded here so the ADR slice has a draft):
35	
36	- **Parent HTTP + fake MCP server.** Truer end-to-end on the child path, but doubles the mock surface — a real Linear MCP would need both protocol scaffolding and tool definitions for `save_issue`/`save_comment`. Rejected: the orchestrator's only post-spawn contract check is `final_linear_state == Done` (`orchestrator.py:401`), so scripting the child to POST GraphQL directly is contractually equivalent for less code.
37	- **Skip child surface entirely.** Useful only for halt-path tests. Rejected because happy-path coverage is a hard requirement.
38	- **Function-injection / `LinearClient` protocol.** Cheaper than HTTP, but doesn't exercise the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. Rejected: the existing module-level functions in `linear.py` are already easy to drive once `LINEAR_API_URL` is overridable.
39	- **VCR-style recorded fixtures.** Realistic but awkward for failure injection and high maintenance under schema drift. Rejected.
40	
41	A short ADR — `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` — is a deliverable of slice 1 (see *Slices*). It records the chosen approach, the four rejected ones, and the conditions under which the choice should be revisited.
42	
43	### Pillar 1 — `MockLinear`: an in-process fake GraphQL server
44	
45	A FastAPI/Starlette app bound to a random localhost port, started per-test via a pytest fixture. It speaks the five GraphQL operations `linear.py` actually uses (`current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state` / WorkflowState lookup) plus a small `IssueUpdate` mutation surface the scripted child can hit to flip an issue to Done.
46	
47	Internal state is a typed in-memory store: `Cycle`, `Issue`, `Label`, `WorkflowState`. The store records every call (operation, variables, timestamp) so tests can assert call sequence and counts.
48	
49	Fault injection is **per-operation, per-call-index**: `mock.fail_after(op="set_state", call=2, mode="http_500")`. Modes include `http_500`, `http_429`, `graphql_error`, `malformed_json`, `slow_response(seconds)`, `connection_reset`. The drain has no retry, so "fail" here means "this single call breaks" — tests then verify the resulting halt path.
50	
51	**This requires one production change**: `linear.py:18` is hard-coded `https://api.linear.app/graphql`. Replace with `os.environ.get("LINEAR_API_URL", "https://api.linear.app/graphql")` so tests can redirect. (No CLI flag — env var only.)
52	
53	### Pillar 2 — `fake_claude`: a programmable replacement for `claude -p`
54	
55	A small Python entrypoint (installed as a console script in tests/) that mimics `claude -p --dangerously-skip-permissions ...`. It:
56	
57	- Reads a **transcript fixture** named in an env var (e.g. `FAKE_CLAUDE_TRANSCRIPT=happy_done`).
58	- Streams the fixture's `stream-json` events to stdout, with configurable inter-event delays so worker breach loops can fire deterministically.
59	- Optionally POSTs to `$LINEAR_API_URL` (the mock) to transition the issue to Done — simulating what the real session's MCP tools would have done. Skipping the POST is how we test "child exits 0 but issue not Done" (`orchestrator.py:401`).
60	- Exits with a configurable code.
61	
62	Transcript fixtures live in `tests/integration/transcripts/*.json` (e.g. `happy_done.json`, `breach_tokens.json`, `breach_time.json`, `dup_message_id.json`, `exits_without_done.json`). Reusable across scenarios.
63	
64	**Fixture format** (documented in `tests/integration/transcripts/README.md`, deliverable of slice 3):
65	
66	```json
67	{
68	  "exit_code": 0,
69	  "post_to_linear": { "transition_to": "Done" },
70	  "events": [
71	    { "delay_ms": 0,    "event": { "type": "system", "subtype": "init", "session_id": "..."} },
72	    { "delay_ms": 50,   "event": { "type": "assistant", "message": { "id": "msg_1", "usage": {...} } } },
73	    { "delay_ms": 100,  "event": { "type": "result", "cost_usd": 0.42, "num_turns": 3, "is_error": false } }
74	  ]
75	}
76	```
77	
78	`delay_ms` is inter-event spacing for breach-loop determinism. The `event` payloads conform to the same `stream-json` shape `worker._UsageAccumulator.feed()` parses today (`worker.py:399`); the stencil is "anything the accumulator dedupes on `message.id` and reads `cost_usd`/`num_turns`/`is_error`/`session_id` from the `result` event".
79	
80	Tests substitute it via `monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(fake_claude_path), ...])` or by patching `subprocess.Popen` at the worker boundary. The existing `_CLAUDE_CMD` constant in `orchestrator.py:25` is the natural seam.
81	
82	### Pillar 3 — `IntegrationHarness`: a fixture that drives `orchestrator.run()` in-process
83	
84	A pytest fixture that:
85	
86	- Creates a temp `HOME` so `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`, `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`, and `~/.drain-cycle/runs/` redirect to a tmpdir (set via `monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)` plus whatever path-resolution helpers exist in `cli.py`/`runlog.py`).
87	- Initialises one or more real git repos in tmpdir (the existing test pattern in `test_orchestrator_iteration.py`).
88	- Writes a `repos.yml` mapping label names to those repo paths.
89	- Starts `MockLinear`, populates it with the scenario's cycle/issues/labels.
90	- Sets `LINEAR_API_URL`, `LINEAR_API_KEY=test`, `FAKE_CLAUDE_TRANSCRIPT=...` env vars.
91	- Substitutes the claude command for `fake_claude`.
92	- Calls `orchestrator.run(repos, limits)` directly and returns a `Result` object: exit code, runlog JSON, mock call log, final mock state.
93	
94	Tests assert against `Result`. One scenario per `test_*` function. Helpers (`make_cycle`, `make_issue`, `expect_runlog`, `expect_calls`) keep scenarios short.
95	
96	**Per-scenario contract.** Every scenario specifies, at minimum:
97	
98	1. **Setup** — `Cycle`, `Issue[]`, `Label[]`, `WorkflowState[]`, repos.yml mapping, limits.yml overrides, transcript fixture name(s), any per-operation fault injection.
99	2. **Expected exit code** — orchestrator return.
100	3. **Expected runlog snapshot** — a dict the scenario asserts against `Result.runlog`. Use `dirty-equals` or a deep-diff helper so non-deterministic fields (timestamps, durations within tolerance, session ids) are matched loosely.
101	4. **Expected final mock state** — the issue states the mock should end in (e.g. `{ "ABA-1": "Done", "ABA-2": "Todo" }` after a halt-on-issue-2).
102	5. **Expected call sequence** — ordered list of `(operation, predicate-on-vars)` tuples the mock should have observed. Strict by default; `expect_calls(strict=False)` for tolerant cases.
103	
104	Helpers enforce the contract: a scenario that omits any of (3) (4) (5) fails a lint test at collection time.
105	
106	## Production code changes required
107	
108	Minimal — every test-only seam piggybacks on something that already exists:
109	
110	1. **`drain_cycle/linear.py:18`** — `_GRAPHQL_URL` reads from `LINEAR_API_URL` env var, defaulting to the current constant. Single-line change.
111	2. **`drain_cycle/runlog.py`** — verify the run-log directory honours `HOME` re-resolution; slice 1 includes a `grep "Path.home\|expanduser\|HOME" drain_cycle/runlog.py` check. If the path is cached at module load, add a small accessor (e.g. `_run_log_dir()`) so the harness can re-resolve after `monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)`.
112	3. **`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`** — `_CLAUDE_CMD` is already module-level; verified by Explore. Tests patch it.
113	
114	No other production changes. No new abstraction layers, no `LinearClient` Protocol — the existing module-level functions are already easy enough to drive against an HTTP fake once the URL is configurable.
115	
116	## Test-infra correctness checks (one-time pre-slice work)
117	
118	Two small spikes the plan-review surfaced as risk; both must land before the slices that depend on them.
119	
120	- **httpx-instrumentation pollution** — drain-cycle wires httpx OTel auto-instrumentation in `telemetry.py`. Confirm that (a) when `HONEYCOMB_API_KEY` is unset, the instrumentation either no-ops or stays silent, and (b) tests don't accidentally export spans to a real OTLP endpoint. Land as part of slice 2; if not clean, set `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` in the harness fixture.
121	- **Breach-loop determinism** — read `worker.py:267-300` to confirm whether token-breach detection is driven by per-stream-event cumulative reads (deterministic from a transcript) or by a wall-clock poll (potentially flaky under CI load). Land as a 30-minute spike at the end of slice 3 with a single token-breach scenario as the falsifying test. **Kill-switch**: if not deterministic in <2 hours, drop token-breach from MVP and keep only time-breach (wall-clock — deterministic). Record the spike outcome in the slice 3 commit message.
122	
123	## Scenario catalogue (initial coverage)
124	
125	Grouped by area; each is one test function. This is the target end-state, not a single slice — slices below build the infra and add scenarios incrementally.
126	
127	**Best case**
128	- 3-issue dependency-ordered drain reaches Done in order; runlog totals correct
129	- Mixed Todo + Backlog states picked up; correct planning
130	- Single-issue drain
131	- Empty cycle exits cleanly
132	
133	**Per-issue halts** (`orchestrator._drain_one_issue` paths)
134	- Missing `repo:` label → halt + reason
135	- `repo:` label resolves to nonexistent path → halt
136	- Worktree `add` fails (dirty tree) → halt
137	- `set_state` (Todo→In-Progress) fails with HTTP 500 → halt
138	- Child exits 0 but issue not Done → halt + reason matches `final_linear_state`
139	- Child exits non-zero → halt
140	- Per-issue token cap exceeded mid-stream → breach kill, runlog records breach scope/metric/limit/observed
141	- Per-issue time cap exceeded → time breach recorded
142	- Revert `set_state` (In-Progress→Todo) fails after a halt → halt-reason concatenates
143	
144	**Cycle-level halts**
145	- `DependencyCycleError` from `pending_issues` → halt before any issue attempted; `cycle_halt_reason` set; runlog otherwise empty
146	- After two Done issues, cycle-cap breach detected → third issue not attempted; halt reason names cumulative metric
147	
148	**Resume**
149	- Run halts on issue 2; re-run with the same cycle picks up issue 2 (mock has it back at Todo via the revert) and issue 3; second runlog file separate from first; final mock state correct
150	
151	**Session / usage tracking** (asserted against runlog + `_UsageAccumulator`)
152	- Transcript with known token totals → runlog `usage.cumulative` and `peak_context` match
153	- Duplicate `message.id` in transcript → counted once (worker dedup at `worker.py:416`)
154	- `cost_usd`/`num_turns`/`session_id`/`is_error` from the `result` event populated
155	- Cycle-level aggregates (`cycle_tokens_cumulative`, `cycle_cost_usd`, `cycle_duration_seconds`) are correct sums
156	
157	**Linear edge cases** (no native retry, so all of these halt)
158	- 500, 429, GraphQL error, malformed JSON, slow-within-timeout (succeeds), slow-beyond-timeout (halts)
159	
160	**Out of scope for MVP**
161	- Honeycomb/OTel span assertions (separate slice — needs fake OTLP collector; production code already wires spans cleanly per `drain_cycle/telemetry.py`)
162	- True MCP-server fake for children (the scripted-child + direct GraphQL POST simulates the contract sufficiently)
163	
164	**In MVP, but explicitly small** — a single subprocess smoke test in slice 4 that spawns the real `drain-cycle` binary against the mock and asserts exit code + runlog presence. This is the only end-to-end coverage of `cli.py`'s secrets-load order and process exit-code path. Larger subprocess scenarios remain out of scope.
165	
166	## Slices (verifiable build order)
167	
168	Each slice ends with a passing scenario as proof, per agentic Principle 4. Each is small enough to ship in one session.
169	
170	1. **Production seam + ADR** — `LINEAR_API_URL` env var in `linear.py`; passing unit test that confirms override; default unchanged. Runlog `HOME` re-resolution verified (grep-check + accessor if needed). **Deliverable**: `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, the four rejected alternatives (see *Approach*), and revisit conditions (e.g. "if the child contract grows beyond Done-state assertion, reconsider the MCP-fake option").
171	2. **`MockLinear` skeleton + instrumentation check** — server with `current_cycle` and `pending_issues` only; serves a hard-coded scenario; one scenario test wires it through `linear.py` and confirms the call lands on the mock. Verify httpx auto-instrumentation does not export spans during tests (set `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` in harness if needed).
172	3. **`fake_claude` skeleton + transcript spec + breach spike** — Python entrypoint that streams a static `happy_done` transcript and POSTs an `IssueUpdate` to the mock. First end-to-end happy-path scenario passes. **Deliverable**: `tests/integration/transcripts/README.md` documenting the fixture format. **Spike at end of slice**: one token-breach scenario as a falsifying test; if not deterministic in 2h, drop token-breach from MVP and document the constraint in the README.
173	4. **`IntegrationHarness` fixture + subprocess smoke** — tmpdir HOME, repos.yml, real git repo, mock startup. Refactor slice-3 scenario to use the harness. **Plus**: one subprocess test that spawns `drain-cycle` against the mock end-to-end, asserts exit code + runlog file exists.
174	5. **Mock GraphQL completeness + drift guard** — add `get_issue`, `set_state`, WorkflowState resolution; happy-path drain of 3 ordered issues passes. **Plus**: `test_linear_surface_audit` that fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a corresponding handler in the mock — keeps the mock from silently lagging schema changes.
175	6. **Fault injection** — per-operation, per-call-index fault modes in mock; scenario covering `set_state` HTTP 500 halt. Each scenario carries the per-scenario contract (expected runlog snapshot + mock state + call sequence).
176	7. **Per-issue halt scenarios** — repo resolution failures, worktree-add failure, child non-zero exit, child exits without Done. One test each.
177	8. **Breach scenarios** — `breach_time` first (deterministic, wall-clock); `breach_tokens` second only if slice 3's spike showed it can be made deterministic. Verify breach object recorded in runlog with correct scope/metric/limit/observed.
178	9. **Cycle-level halt scenarios** — `DependencyCycleError` (mock returns cyclic graph), cycle-cap breach after two Done issues.
179	10. **Resume scenario** — drive a halt, re-invoke orchestrator on same mock cycle, assert second runlog file and final state.
180	11. **Usage-tracking scenarios** — duplicate-message-id transcript, cycle-aggregate sums, terminal `result` event fields.
181	12. **Linear edge cases** — 429/malformed/slow-timeout halt scenarios.
182	
183	Slices 1–4 are the foundation; 5–12 are independent and can ship in any order once 4 is in. Each slice opens a Linear issue under the existing project; the slice is Done per `AGENTS.md` when reviewed, committed, pushed, and the Linear comment posted.
184	
185	## Critical files
186	
187	To modify:
188	- `drain_cycle/linear.py` — env-var override for GraphQL URL (slice 1)
