  "old_string": "Today the integration story for `drain-cycle` is \"use a real Linear cycle\". This makes anything beyond happy-path expensive to test: failure modes require deliberately broken issues, retries/resume require actually halting and restarting the drain, and usage/cycle-hours behaviour can only be exercised by running real Claude sessions. Unit tests cover individual modules but stop at the function boundary — monkey-patching `linear._post` and replacing `claude -p` with marker-touching shell scripts (`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22`, `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65`).\n\nThe gap is **scenario-level integration tests** that exercise the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, so we can validate:\n\n- **Best-case drains** — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions\n- **Per-issue failure paths** — every halt condition in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (repo resolution, worktree setup, `set_state` failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)\n- **Cycle-level halts** — `DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach propagation\n- **Resume semantics** — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo\n- **Session/usage tracking** — `_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches\n\nThe drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); \"retry\" in our context means **resume**, which is a re-invocation against the same cycle.\n\n### Success criterion (outcome, not output)\n\nThe suite is \"working\" when **every halt path in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` and every cycle-halt path in `orchestrator._run` is exercised by at least one integration scenario, and a new halt path added to either function cannot be merged without an accompanying scenario**. Enforced by a CI gate (see *Verification*) that fails when the union of `mark_error` slugs emitted in production code is not a subset of the slugs observed in the test run's captured spans / runlog `halt_reason` strings.   "new_string": "Today `drain-cycle` only tests against a real Linear cycle. Anything past happy-path becomes expensive: failure modes need deliberately broken issues, resume needs an actual halt-and-restart, and usage tracking needs real Claude sessions. Unit tests cover individual modules but stop at the function boundary — they monkey-patch `linear._post` and swap `claude -p` for marker-touching shell scripts (`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22`, `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65`).\n\nWe need **scenario-level integration tests** that drive the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, covering:\n\n- **Best-case drains** — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions\n- **Per-issue failure paths** — every halt condition in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (repo resolution, worktree setup, `set_state` failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)\n- **Cycle-level halts** — `DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach propagation\n- **Resume semantics** — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo\n- **Session/usage tracking** — `_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches\n\nThe drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); \"retry\" here means **resume** — a re-invocation against the same cycle.\n\n### Success criterion (outcome, not output)\n\nThe suite works when **every halt path in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` and every cycle-halt path in `orchestrator._run` has at least one integration scenario, and a new halt path cannot merge without one**. A CI gate enforces this (see *Verification*): it greps the production code for `mark_error` slugs, compares against the union of `exception.slug` and `halt_reason` strings observed in the test run, and fails on any gap.   "old_string": "**Parent-side HTTP mock + scripted child + in-process driver.** Three pillars.\n\n**Alternatives considered and rejected** (recorded here so the ADR slice has a draft):\n\n- **Parent HTTP + fake MCP server.** Truer end-to-end on the child path, but doubles the mock surface — a real Linear MCP would need both protocol scaffolding and tool definitions for `save_issue`/`save_comment`. Rejected: the orchestrator's only post-spawn contract check is `final_linear_state == Done` (`orchestrator.py:401`), so scripting the child to POST GraphQL directly is contractually equivalent for less code.\n- **Skip child surface entirely.** Useful only for halt-path tests. Rejected because happy-path coverage is a hard requirement.\n- **Function-injection / `LinearClient` protocol.** Cheaper than HTTP, but doesn't exercise the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. Rejected: the existing module-level functions in `linear.py` are already easy to drive once `LINEAR_API_URL` is overridable.\n- **VCR-style recorded fixtures.** Realistic but awkward for failure injection and high maintenance under schema drift. Rejected.\n\nA short ADR — `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` — is a deliverable of slice 1 (see *Slices*). It records the chosen approach, the four rejected ones, and the conditions under which the choice should be revisited.",
  "new_string": "**Parent-side HTTP mock + scripted child + in-process driver.** Three pillars.\n\n**Alternatives rejected** (draft for the ADR slice):\n\n- **Parent HTTP + fake MCP server.** Truer end-to-end on the child path, but doubles the mock surface — a real Linear MCP needs protocol scaffolding plus tool definitions for `save_issue` and `save_comment`. The orchestrator's only post-spawn check is `final_linear_state == Done` (`orchestrator.py:401`), so a scripted child that POSTs GraphQL directly satisfies the contract with less code.\n- **Skip child surface entirely.** Covers only halt paths. Happy-path coverage is required.\n- **Function-injection / `LinearClient` protocol.** Cheaper than HTTP but skips the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. The existing module-level functions in `linear.py` drive easily against an HTTP fake once `LINEAR_API_URL` is overridable.\n- **VCR-style recorded fixtures.** Realistic but awkward for failure injection, expensive to maintain under schema drift.\n\nSlice 1 produces a short ADR at `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, these four rejected alternatives, and the conditions under which we should revisit."
}

  "old_string": "### Pillar 1 — `MockLinear`: an in-process fake GraphQL server\n\nA FastAPI/Starlette app bound to a random localhost port, started per-test via a pytest fixture. It speaks the five GraphQL operations `linear.py` actually uses (`current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state` / WorkflowState lookup) plus a small `IssueUpdate` mutation surface the scripted child can hit to flip an issue to Done.\n\nInternal state is a typed in-memory store: `Cycle`, `Issue`, `Label`, `WorkflowState`. The store records every call (operation, variables, timestamp) so tests can assert call sequence and counts.\n\nFault injection is **per-operation, per-call-index**: `mock.fail_after(op=\"set_state\", call=2, mode=\"http_500\")`. Modes include `http_500`, `http_429`, `graphql_error`, `malformed_json`, `slow_response(seconds)`, `connection_reset`. The drain has no retry, so \"fail\" here means \"this single call breaks\" — tests then verify the resulting halt path.\n\n**This requires one production change**: `linear.py:18` is hard-coded `https://api.linear.app/graphql`. Replace with `os.environ.get(\"LINEAR_API_URL\", \"https://api.linear.app/graphql\")` so tests can redirect. (No CLI flag — env var only.)",
  "new_string": "### Pillar 1 — `MockLinear`: an in-process fake GraphQL server\n\nA FastAPI/Starlette app on a random localhost port, started per-test via a pytest fixture. It speaks the five GraphQL operations `linear.py` uses (`current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state` / WorkflowState lookup) plus an `IssueUpdate` mutation the scripted child hits to flip an issue to Done.\n\nState lives in a typed in-memory store: `Cycle`, `Issue`, `Label`, `WorkflowState`. The store records every call (operation, variables, timestamp) so tests can assert call sequence and counts.\n\nFault injection is **per-operation, per-call-index**: `mock.fail_after(op=\"set_state\", call=2, mode=\"http_500\")`. Modes: `http_500`, `http_429`, `graphql_error`, `malformed_json`, `slow_response(seconds)`, `connection_reset`. The drain has no retry, so \"fail\" means \"this single call breaks\" — tests then verify the halt path.\n\n**Production change required**: `linear.py:18` hard-codes `https://api.linear.app/graphql`. Replace with `os.environ.get(\"LINEAR_API_URL\", \"https://api.linear.app/graphql\")`. No CLI flag — env var only."
}

  "old_string": "### Pillar 2 — `fake_claude`: a programmable replacement for `claude -p`\n\nA small Python entrypoint (installed as a console script in tests/) that mimics `claude -p --dangerously-skip-permissions ...`. It:\n\n- Reads a **transcript fixture** named in an env var (e.g. `FAKE_CLAUDE_TRANSCRIPT=happy_done`).\n- Streams the fixture's `stream-json` events to stdout, with configurable inter-event delays so worker breach loops can fire deterministically.\n- Optionally POSTs to `$LINEAR_API_URL` (the mock) to transition the issue to Done — simulating what the real session's MCP tools would have done. Skipping the POST is how we test \"child exits 0 but issue not Done\" (`orchestrator.py:401`).\n- Exits with a configurable code.\n\nTranscript fixtures live in `tests/integration/transcripts/*.json` (e.g. `happy_done.json`, `breach_tokens.json`, `breach_time.json`, `dup_message_id.json`, `exits_without_done.json`). Reusable across scenarios.",
  "new_string": "### Pillar 2 — `fake_claude`: a programmable replacement for `claude -p`\n\nA Python entrypoint (installed as a console script in `tests/`) that mimics `claude -p --dangerously-skip-permissions ...`. To test \"child exits 0 but issue not Done\" (`orchestrator.py:401`), skip the POST.\n- Exits with a configurable code.\n\nTranscript fixtures live in `tests/integration/transcripts/*.json` (e.g. `happy_done.json`, `breach_tokens.json`, `breach_time.json`, `dup_message_id.json`, `exits_without_done.json`) and reuse across scenarios."

Tests patch it.\n\nNo new abstraction layers, no `LinearClient` Protocol — the existing module-level functions drive easily against an HTTP fake once the URL is configurable.\n\n## Test-infra correctness checks (one-time pre-slice work)\n\nTwo risks the plan-review surfaced. Both land before dependent slices.\n\n- **httpx-instrumentation pollution** — drain-cycle wires httpx OTel auto-instrumentation in `telemetry.py`. Confirm that (a) when `HONEYCOMB_API_KEY` is unset, the instrumentation no-ops or stays silent, and (b) tests do not export spans to a real OTLP endpoint. Lands in slice 2; if not clean, the harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true`.\n- **Breach-loop determinism** — read `worker.py:267-300` to confirm whether token-breach detection runs off per-stream-event cumulative reads (deterministic from a transcript) or off a wall-clock poll (potentially flaky under CI load). Lands as a 30-minute spike at the end of slice 3 with one token-breach scenario as the falsifying test. **Kill-switch**: if not deterministic in 2 hours, drop token-breach from MVP and keep only time-breach (wall-clock, deterministic). Record the spike outcome in the slice 3 commit message."

Strunk pass applied. Main changes:

```json
{
  "plan": "# Integration test suite: mock Linear + scripted child\n\n## Context\n\nToday `drain-cycle` only tests against a real Linear cycle. Anything past happy-path becomes expensive: failure modes need deliberately broken issues, resume needs an actual halt-and-restart, and usage tracking needs real Claude sessions. Unit tests cover individual modules but stop at the function boundary — they monkey-patch `linear._post` and swap `claude -p` for marker-touching shell scripts (`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22`, `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65`).\n\nWe need **scenario-level integration tests** that drive the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, covering:\n\n- **Best-case drains** — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions\n- **Per-issue failure paths** — every halt condition in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (repo resolution, worktree setup, `set_state` failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)\n- **Cycle-level halts** — `DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach propagation\n- **Resume semantics** — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo\n- **Session/usage tracking** — `_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches\n\nThe drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); \"retry\" here means **resume** — a re-invocation against the same cycle.\n\n### Success criterion (outcome, not output)\n\nThe suite works when **every halt path in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` and every cycle-halt path in `orchestrator._run` has at least one integration scenario, and a new halt path cannot merge without one**. A CI gate enforces this (see *Verification*): it greps the production code for `mark_error` slugs, compares against the union of `exception.slug` and `halt_reason` strings observed in the test run, and fails on any gap. Behavioural outcome: time-to-reproduce a future halt-logic bug drops from hours (build a real cycle) to minutes (write a 20-line scenario).\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. **`shape-design-doc`** — this plan is the artefact; promote to `docs/integration-test-architecture.md` (or an ADR) if more reviewers need it.\n2. **`shape-planning-and-task-breakdown`** — convert the *Slices* section into Linear issues on a fresh initiative under the existing project.\n3. **`agent-skills:test-driven-development`** during build — each slice ships a failing scenario before the supporting infra.\n4. **`agent-skills:code-review-and-quality`** per slice before commit, per `AGENTS.md`.\n\n## Approach\n\n**Parent-side HTTP mock + scripted child + in-process driver.** Three pillars.\n\n**Alternatives rejected** (draft for the ADR slice):\n\n- **Parent HTTP + fake MCP server.** Truer end-to-end on the child path, but doubles the mock surface — a real Linear MCP needs protocol scaffolding plus tool definitions for `save_issue` and `save_comment`. The orchestrator's only post-spawn check is `final_linear_state == Done` (`orchestrator.py:401`), so a scripted child that POSTs GraphQL directly satisfies the contract with less code.\n- **Skip child surface entirely.** Covers only halt paths. Happy-path coverage is required.\n- **Function-injection / `LinearClient` protocol.** Cheaper than HTTP but skips the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. The existing module-level functions in `linear.py` drive easily against an HTTP fake once `LINEAR_API_URL` is overridable.\n- **VCR-style recorded fixtures.** Realistic but awkward for failure injection, expensive to maintain under schema drift.\n\nSlice 1 produces a short ADR at `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, these four rejected alternatives, and the conditions under which we should revisit.\n\n### Pillar 1 — `MockLinear`: an in-process fake GraphQL server\n\nA FastAPI/Starlette app on a random localhost port, started per-test via a pytest fixture. It speaks the five GraphQL operations `linear.py` uses (`current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state` / WorkflowState lookup) plus an `IssueUpdate` mutation the scripted child hits to flip an issue to Done.\n\nState lives in a typed in-memory store: `Cycle`, `Issue`, `Label`, `WorkflowState`. The store records every call (operation, variables, timestamp) so tests can assert call sequence and counts.\n\nFault injection is **per-operation, per-call-index**: `mock.fail_after(op=\"set_state\", call=2, mode=\"http_500\")`. Modes: `http_500`, `http_429`, `graphql_error`, `malformed_json`, `slow_response(seconds)`, `connection_reset`. The drain has no retry, so \"fail\" means \"this single call breaks\" — tests then verify the halt path.\n\n**Production change required**: `linear.py:18` hard-codes `https://api.linear.app/graphql`. Replace with `os.environ.get(\"LINEAR_API_URL\", \"https://api.linear.app/graphql\")`. No CLI flag — env var only.\n\n### Pillar 2 — `fake_claude`: a programmable replacement for `claude -p`\n\nA Python entrypoint (installed as a console script in `tests/`) that mimics `claude -p --dangerously-skip-permissions ...`. It:\n\n- Reads a **transcript fixture** named in an env var (e.g. `FAKE_CLAUDE_TRANSCRIPT=happy_done`).\n- Streams the fixture's `stream-json` events to stdout with configurable inter-event delays, so worker breach loops fire deterministically.\n- Optionally POSTs to `$LINEAR_API_URL` (the mock) to transition the issue to Done — mimicking the real session's MCP tools. To test \"child exits 0 but issue not Done\" (`orchestrator.py:401`), skip the POST.\n- Exits with a configurable code.\n\nTranscript fixtures live in `tests/integration/transcripts/*.json` (e.g. `happy_done.json`, `breach_tokens.json`, `breach_time.json`, `dup_message_id.json`, `exits_without_done.json`) and reuse across scenarios.\n\n**Fixture format** (documented in `tests/integration/transcripts/README.md`, deliverable of slice 3):\n\n```json\n{\n  \"exit_code\": 0,\n  \"post_to_linear\": { \"transition_to\": \"Done\" },\n  \"events\": [\n    { \"delay_ms\": 0,    \"event\": { \"type\": \"system\", \"subtype\": \"init\", \"session_id\": \"...\"} },\n    { \"delay_ms\": 50,   \"event\": { \"type\": \"assistant\", \"message\": { \"id\": \"msg_1\", \"usage\": {...} } } },\n    { \"delay_ms\": 100,  \"event\": { \"type\": \"result\", \"cost_usd\": 0.42, \"num_turns\": 3, \"is_error\": false } }\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n`delay_ms` spaces events for breach-loop determinism. The `event` payloads match the `stream-json` shape `worker._UsageAccumulator.feed()` parses today (`worker.py:399`); the stencil is \"anything the accumulator dedupes on `message.id` and reads `cost_usd`, `num_turns`, `is_error`, `session_id` from the `result` event\".\n\nTests substitute via `monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, \"_CLAUDE_CMD\", [str(fake_claude_path), ...])` or by patching `subprocess.Popen` at the worker boundary. The module-level `_CLAUDE_CMD` in `orchestrator.py:25` is the natural seam.\n\n### Pillar 3 — `IntegrationHarness`: a fixture that drives `orchestrator.run()` in-process\n\nA pytest fixture that:\n\n- Creates a temp `HOME` so `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`, `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`, and `~/.drain-cycle/runs/` redirect to a tmpdir (`monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", ...)` plus any path-resolution helpers in `cli.py` / `runlog.py`).\n- Initialises one or more real git repos in tmpdir (existing pattern in `test_orchestrator_iteration.py`).\n- Writes a `repos.yml` mapping label names to those repo paths.\n- Starts `MockLinear` and populates it with the scenario's cycle, issues, and labels.\n- Sets `LINEAR_API_URL`, `LINEAR_API_KEY=test`, `FAKE_CLAUDE_TRANSCRIPT=...` env vars.\n- Swaps the claude command for `fake_claude`.\n- Calls `orchestrator.run(repos, limits)` directly and returns a `Result`: exit code, runlog JSON, mock call log, final mock state.\n\nTests assert against `Result`. One scenario per `test_*` function. Helpers (`make_cycle`, `make_issue`, `expect_runlog`, `expect_calls`) keep scenarios short.\n\n**Per-scenario contract.** Every scenario declares:\n\n1. **Setup** — `Cycle`, `Issue[]`, `Label[]`, `WorkflowState[]`, repos.yml mapping, limits.yml overrides, transcript fixture name(s), any fault injection.\n2. **Expected exit code** — orchestrator return.\n3. **Expected runlog snapshot** — a dict matched against `Result.runlog` via `dirty-equals` or a deep-diff helper, so timestamps, durations within tolerance, and session ids match loosely.\n4. **Expected final mock state** — the issue states the mock should end in (e.g. `{ \"ABA-1\": \"Done\", \"ABA-2\": \"Todo\" }` after a halt on issue 2).\n5. **Expected call sequence** — ordered `(operation, predicate-on-vars)` tuples the mock should have observed. Strict by default; `expect_calls(strict=False)` for tolerant cases.\n\nA collection-time lint test fails any scenario missing (3), (4), or (5).\n\n## Production code changes required\n\nMinimal. Every test-only seam reuses existing structure.\n\n1. **`drain_cycle/linear.py:18`** — `_GRAPHQL_URL` reads from `LINEAR_API_URL` env var, default unchanged. One-line change.\n2. **`drain_cycle/runlog.py`** — verify the run-log directory honours `HOME` re-resolution; slice 1 greps for `Path.home`, `expanduser`, and `HOME`. If the path caches at module load, add an accessor (e.g. `_run_log_dir()`) so the harness re-resolves after `monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", ...)`.\n3. **`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`** — `_CLAUDE_CMD` is already module-level (verified by Explore). Tests patch it.\n\nNo new abstraction layers, no `LinearClient` Protocol — the existing module-level functions drive easily against an HTTP fake once the URL is configurable.\n\n## Test-infra correctness checks (one-time pre-slice work)\n\nTwo risks the plan-review surfaced. Both land before dependent slices.\n\n- **httpx-instrumentation pollution** — drain-cycle wires httpx OTel auto-instrumentation in `telemetry.py`. Confirm that (a) when `HONEYCOMB_API_KEY` is unset, the instrumentation no-ops or stays silent, and (b) tests do not export spans to a real OTLP endpoint. Lands in slice 2; if not clean, the harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true`.\n- **Breach-loop determinism** — read `worker.py:267-300` to confirm whether token-breach detection runs off per-stream-event cumulative reads (deterministic from a transcript) or off a wall-clock poll (potentially flaky under CI load). Lands as a 30-minute spike at the end of slice 3 with one token-breach scenario as the falsifying test. **Kill-switch**: if not deterministic in 2 hours, drop token-breach from MVP and keep only time-breach (wall-clock, deterministic). Record the spike outcome in the slice 3 commit message.\n\n## Scenario catalogue (initial coverage)\n\nGrouped by area; one test function each. Target end-state, not a single slice — slices below build the infra and add scenarios incrementally.\n\n**Best case**\n- 3-issue dependency-ordered drain reaches Done in order; runlog totals correct\n- Mixed Todo + Backlog states picked up; correct planning\n- Single-issue drain\n- Empty cycle exits cleanly\n\n**Per-issue halts** (`orchestrator._drain_one_issue` paths)\n- Missing `repo:` label → halt + reason\n- `repo:` label resolves to nonexistent path → halt\n- Worktree `add` fails (dirty tree) → halt\n- `set_state` (Todo→In-Progress) fails with HTTP 500 → halt\n- Child exits 0 but issue not Done → halt + reason matches `final_linear_state`\n- Child exits non-zero → halt\n- Per-issue token cap exceeded mid-stream → breach kill, runlog records breach scope/metric/limit/observed\n- Per-issue time cap exceeded → time breach recorded\n- Revert `set_state` (In-Progress→Todo) fails after a halt → halt-reason concatenates\n\n**Cycle-level halts**\n- `DependencyCycleError` from `pending_issues` → halt before any issue attempted; `cycle_halt_reason` set; runlog otherwise empty\n- After two Done issues, cycle-cap breach detected → third issue not attempted; halt reason names cumulative metric\n\n**Resume**\n- Run halts on issue 2; re-run with the same cycle picks up issue 2 (mock has it back at Todo via the revert) and issue 3; second runlog file separate from first; final mock state correct\n\n**Session / usage tracking** (asserted against runlog + `_UsageAccumulator`)\n- Transcript with known token totals → runlog `usage.cumulative` and `peak_context` match\n- Duplicate `message.id` in transcript → counted once (worker dedup at `worker.py:416`)\n- `cost_usd`/`num_turns`/`session_id`/`is_error` from the `result` event populated\n- Cycle-level aggregates (`cycle_tokens_cumulative`, `cycle_cost_usd`, `cycle_duration_seconds`) are correct sums\n\n**Linear edge cases** (no native retry, so all of these halt)\n- 500, 429, GraphQL error, malformed JSON, slow-within-timeout (succeeds), slow-beyond-timeout (halts)\n\n**Out of scope for MVP**\n- Honeycomb/OTel span assertions (separate slice — needs a fake OTLP collector; production code wires spans cleanly per `drain_cycle/telemetry.py`)\n- True MCP-server fake for children (scripted-child + direct GraphQL POST satisfies the contract)\n\n**In MVP, bounded** — slice 4 ships one subprocess smoke test that spawns the real `drain-cycle` binary against the mock and asserts exit code plus runlog presence. This single test covers `cli.py`'s secrets-load order and process exit-code path. Larger subprocess scenarios stay out of scope.\n\n## Slices (verifiable build order)\n\nEach slice ships in one session and ends with a passing scenario.\n\n1. **Production seam + ADR** — `LINEAR_API_URL` env var in `linear.py`; unit test confirms override; default unchanged. Runlog `HOME` re-resolution verified (grep + accessor if needed). **Deliverable**: `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, the four rejected alternatives (see *Approach*), and revisit conditions (e.g. \"if the child contract grows beyond Done-state assertion, reconsider the MCP-fake option\").\n2. **`MockLinear` skeleton + instrumentation check** — server with `current_cycle` and `pending_issues` only, serving a hard-coded scenario; one test wires through `linear.py` and confirms the call lands on the mock. Verify httpx auto-instrumentation does not export spans during tests (set `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` in the harness if needed).\n3. **`fake_claude` skeleton + transcript spec + breach spike** — Python entrypoint streams a static `happy_done` transcript and POSTs an `IssueUpdate` to the mock; first end-to-end happy-path scenario passes. **Deliverable**: `tests/integration/transcripts/README.md` documenting the fixture format. **Spike at end of slice**: one token-breach scenario as a falsifying test; if not deterministic in 2h, drop token-breach from MVP and document the constraint in the README.\n4. **`IntegrationHarness` fixture + subprocess smoke** — tmpdir HOME, repos.yml, real git repo, mock startup; slice-3 scenario refactored onto the harness. **Plus**: one subprocess test spawns `drain-cycle` against the mock end-to-end and asserts exit code plus runlog file existence.\n5. **Mock GraphQL completeness + drift guard** — add `get_issue`, `set_state`, WorkflowState resolution; happy-path drain of 3 ordered issues passes. **Plus**: `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler — keeps the mock from lagging schema changes silently.\n6. **Fault injection** — per-operation, per-call-index fault modes in the mock; scenario covers `set_state` HTTP 500 halt. Each scenario carries the per-scenario contract (runlog snapshot, mock state, call sequence).\n7. **Per-issue halt scenarios** — repo-resolution failures, worktree-add failure, child non-zero exit, child exits without Done. One test each.\n8. **Breach scenarios** — `breach_time` first (wall-clock, deterministic); `breach_tokens` second, only if slice 3's spike proved determinism. Verify breach object in runlog with correct scope, metric, limit, observed.\n9. **Cycle-level halt scenarios** — `DependencyCycleError` (mock returns a cyclic graph); cycle-cap breach after two Done issues.\n10. **Resume scenario** — drive a halt, re-invoke orchestrator on the same mock cycle, assert second runlog file and final state.\n11. **Usage-tracking scenarios** — duplicate-message-id transcript, cycle-aggregate sums, terminal `result` event fields.\n12. **Linear edge cases** — 429, malformed JSON, slow-timeout halt scenarios.\n\nSlices 1–4 are the foundation; 5–12 are independent and ship in any order once 4 lands. Each slice opens a Linear issue under the existing project and closes per `AGENTS.md` (review → fix → commit + push → Linear summary comment → Done).\n\n## Critical files\n\nTo modify:\n- `drain_cycle/linear.py` — env-var override for GraphQL URL (slice 1)\n- `drain_cycle/runlog.py` — verify `HOME` re-resolution; add small accessor only if cached at module load (slice 1)\n\nTo create:\n- `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` — chosen approach + rejected alternatives + revisit conditions (slice 1)\n- `tests/integration/conftest.py` — harness fixture\n- `tests/integration/mock_linear.py` — fake GraphQL server + in-memory store + fault injection\n- `tests/integration/fake_claude.py` — scripted child entrypoint\n- `tests/integration/transcripts/README.md` — fixture format spec (slice 3)\n- `tests/integration/transcripts/*.json` — stream-json fixtures\n- `tests/integration/test_happy_path.py`, `test_per_issue_halts.py`, `test_cycle_halts.py`, `test_resume.py`, `test_usage_tracking.py`, `test_linear_edges.py`, `test_linear_surface_audit.py`, `test_subprocess_smoke.py` — scenarios\n\nTo reuse from existing tests:\n- Real git-repo setup pattern in `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22-90`\n- Linear fixture style in `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65-70`\n- `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`\n\n## Verification\n\nEnd-to-end:\n- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -v` — all scenarios green, no real network calls, no real `LINEAR_API_KEY` leaking in.\n- `uv run pytest --cov=drain_cycle/orchestrator --cov=drain_cycle/worker --cov=drain_cycle/runlog tests/integration/` — coverage on orchestrator, worker, and runlog rises meaningfully.\n- Each slice closes per `AGENTS.md`: review → fix → commit + push to main → Linear summary comment → Done.\n\n**Outcome-metric gate** (enforces the *Success criterion*):\n- `test_halt_coverage` greps `mark_error` slugs from `drain_cycle/**.py`, collects the union of `exception.slug` attributes (or `halt_reason` strings) observed across the integration run, and asserts equality. Fails CI when a new halt slug lands in production code without a scenario.\n\nNegative checks during build:\n- `grep -r \"api.linear.app\" tests/` returns nothing — no accidental real-Linear calls in fixtures.\n- The mock's call log records every operation a scenario expects; an unexpected call fails the test loudly.\n- `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler.\n",

> AGENT

That's a real architectural concern. If the spawned session is fake, what we've built is a sophisticated unit-test rig, not an integration test. Let me get the design right before going further.

If we keep `claude -p` real, three things change:

1. **Pillar 2 is no longer `fake_claude`.** It becomes a **Linear-MCP shim** — a tiny MCP server we register in a custom `.mcp.json` written into each worktree. It exposes `save_issue` and `save_comment`, and translates them to GraphQL against `MockLinear`. Same backing store as the parent's HTTP mock.
2. **No transcript fixtures.** Instead, each scenario writes an **issue body / prompt** the real claude session executes (e.g. "create `ABA-1.txt` with hello, then mark Done"; "loop and emit large output"; "exit without calling any tools").
3. **Determinism + cost.** Real claude is non-deterministic on numbers (tokens, turns, cost) but deterministic on outcomes (Done / not Done / breach). Tests use predicate assertions on numbers and exact assertions on outcomes. Breach scenarios get tight `per_issue_tokens` / `per_issue_seconds` caps. The suite splits: failure-mode scenarios that halt pre-spawn don't need claude at all; happy-path and breach scenarios do. Mark the costly subset `@pytest.mark.real_claude` and run nightly, not per-commit.

This is a meaningful redesign. Before I rewrite the plan, one decision will shape the rest:

The existing module-level functions in `linear.py` drive easily against an HTTP fake once `LINEAR_API_URL` is overridable.\n- **VCR-style recorded fixtures.** Realistic but awkward for failure injection, expensive to maintain under schema drift.\n\nSlice 1 produces a short ADR at `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, these four rejected alternatives, and the conditions under which we should revisit.",

Rejected because a scripted child stops being an integration test — it cannot validate the real session's usage stream, MCP tool calls, prompt-to-completion behaviour, or production OTel spans against actual data.\n- **Hybrid: real claude only on happy-path; scripted child for failure determinism.** Two patterns to maintain; weaker default signal; gives up real-claude coverage on usage-tracking edge cases.\n- **Function-injection / `LinearClient` protocol on the parent.** Cheaper than HTTP but skips the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. The existing module-level functions in `linear.py` drive easily against an HTTP fake once `LINEAR_API_URL` is overridable.\n- **VCR-style recorded fixtures.** Realistic but awkward for failure injection, expensive to maintain under schema drift.\n\nSlice 1 produces a short ADR at `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, these rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.\n\n### Cost, runtime, and determinism\n\nReal claude makes the suite costly, slow, and non-deterministic on numbers.\n\n- **Cost / runtime budget**: ~$0.05–$0.20 per spawning scenario, ~15–30 seconds per scenario. Full suite of ~30 scenarios: ~$1–$3 and ~15 minutes. Nightly job, not per-commit.\n- **Per-commit CI** runs only the pre-spawn halt scenarios (no claude spawned) — fast and deterministic.\n- **Determinism strategy** — split assertions:\n  - **Exact** — exit codes, final Linear state, halt reason class, runlog file presence, structural shape of runlog JSON, set of MCP tool calls observed.\n  - **Predicate** — `tokens > 0`, `0 < cost_usd < per_issue_cost_usd`, `num_turns >= 1`, `model is not None`. No exact values.\n  - **Tolerant snapshot** — `dirty-equals` matches keys and types, ignores values.\n- **Cost guard**: scenarios that spawn claude carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude`.   "old_string": "### Pillar 2 — `fake_claude`: a programmable replacement for `claude -p`\n\nA Python entrypoint (installed as a console script in `tests/`) that mimics `claude -p --dangerously-skip-permissions ...`. It:\n\n- Reads a **transcript fixture** named in an env var (e.g. `FAKE_CLAUDE_TRANSCRIPT=happy_done`).\n- Streams the fixture's `stream-json` events to stdout with configurable inter-event delays, so worker breach loops fire deterministically.\n- Optionally POSTs to `$LINEAR_API_URL` (the mock) to transition the issue to Done — mimicking the real session's MCP tools. To test \"child exits 0 but issue not Done\" (`orchestrator.py:401`), skip the POST.\n- Exits with a configurable code.\n\nTranscript fixtures live in `tests/integration/transcripts/*.json` (e.g. `happy_done.json`, `breach_tokens.json`, `breach_time.json`, `dup_message_id.json`, `exits_without_done.json`) and reuse across scenarios.\n\n**Fixture format** (documented in `tests/integration/transcripts/README.md`, deliverable of slice 3):\n\n```json\n{\n  \"exit_code\": 0,\n  \"post_to_linear\": { \"transition_to\": \"Done\" },\n  \"events\": [\n    { \"delay_ms\": 0,    \"event\": { \"type\": \"system\", \"subtype\": \"init\", \"session_id\": \"...\"} },\n    { \"delay_ms\": 50,   \"event\": { \"type\": \"assistant\", \"message\": { \"id\": \"msg_1\", \"usage\": {...} } } },\n    { \"delay_ms\": 100,  \"event\": { \"type\": \"result\", \"cost_usd\": 0.42, \"num_turns\": 3, \"is_error\": false } }\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n`delay_ms` spaces events for breach-loop determinism. The `event` payloads match the `stream-json` shape `worker._UsageAccumulator.feed()` parses today (`worker.py:399`); the stencil is \"anything the accumulator dedupes on `message.id` and reads `cost_usd`, `num_turns`, `is_error`, `session_id` from the `result` event\".\n\nTests substitute via `monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, \"_CLAUDE_CMD\", [str(fake_claude_path), ...])` or by patching `subprocess.Popen` at the worker boundary. The module-level `_CLAUDE_CMD` in `orchestrator.py:25` is the natural seam.",
  "new_string": "### Pillar 2 — `linear_mcp_shim`: a Linear MCP server backed by the mock\n\nA small Python stdio MCP server (built on the official `mcp` SDK) that exposes the two tools the drain-cycle prompt uses today: `save_issue` (state transitions) and `save_comment`. Each tool translates its call into a GraphQL POST against `MockLinear`, sharing the same backing store as the parent's HTTP surface.\n\nThe harness writes a custom `.mcp.json` into each test worktree registering the shim under the name `linear` (matching the existing tool prefix `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*`). The orchestrator already symlinks `.mcp.json` from the configured source into the worktree (`orchestrator.py:257-259`); the harness arranges for the test source to be the shim-configured file.\n\nThe shim records every tool call (tool name, arguments, timestamp) on the shared store, so scenarios assert against both parent GraphQL calls and child MCP calls through one call log.\n\n**Why an MCP shim and not just `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child env.** MCP is a separate protocol from raw GraphQL; the official Linear MCP server speaks MCP over stdio to claude and HTTPS to Linear. Setting `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child has no effect — there's no way to redirect the official server without forking it. A purpose-built shim is the smallest seam.\n\n### How scenarios steer claude\n\nScenarios drive child behaviour through three knobs the harness sets per-scenario:\n\n- **Issue body** — written into `MockLinear` and read by claude via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__get_issue`. The body is the natural prompt seam (e.g. \"create `<id>.txt` containing 'hello'; then mark this issue Done\"). Library lives in `tests/integration/prompts/*.md`.\n- **Per-issue limits** — `limits.yml` overrides (e.g. `per_issue_tokens=2000`, `per_issue_seconds=30`) used with verbose prompts to provoke breach scenarios deterministically.\n- **Claude flags** — the harness may inject `--max-turns 1` for \"exit without Done\" cases (claude exits before completing the workflow), via the `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `orchestrator.py:25`.\n\nThe drain-cycle prompt (`drain_cycle/prompt.py:39-42`) stays unchanged — same prompt the real drain uses."
}

  "new_string": "3. **Expected runlog assertions** — a list of (a) structural shape (keys, types) matched via `dirty-equals`, (b) predicate assertions on numeric fields (`tokens > 0`, `cost_usd < limits.per_issue_cost_usd`), (c) exact assertions on deterministic fields (`final_linear_state`, `halt_reason`).\n4. **Expected final mock state** — issue states the mock should end in (e.g. `{ \"ABA-1\": \"Done\", \"ABA-2\": \"Todo\" }` after a halt on issue 2).\n5. **Expected call sequence** — ordered `(surface, operation, predicate-on-args)` tuples across both parent GraphQL and child MCP calls. Strict by default; `expect_calls(strict=False)` for tolerant cases where claude's tool-call order varies between runs.\n\nA collection-time lint test fails any scenario missing (3), (4), or (5)."
}

> AGENT

Pillar 2 swap landed. Summary of the rewrite addressing the comment:

- **`fake_claude` is gone.** Real `claude -p` runs in every scenario that reaches spawn.
- **Pillar 2 is now a `linear_mcp_shim`** — a stdio MCP server that exposes `save_issue` and `save_comment`, translated to GraphQL against the same `MockLinear` store. The harness writes a custom `.mcp.json` into each worktree pointing claude at the shim instead of the real Linear MCP.
- **Scenarios steer claude through issue bodies + per-issue limits + claude flags**, not transcripts. Prompt library at `tests/integration/prompts/*.md`.
- **Cost / determinism strategy made explicit**: `@pytest.mark.real_claude` for spawning scenarios (nightly), pre-spawn halts run per-commit. Tests use exact assertions on outcomes, predicate assertions on numbers.
- **Slice plan re-shaped** — slice 3 now builds the MCP shim and runs the first real-claude drain end-to-end; slices 7/8 split pre-spawn (fast) from real-claude (costly); transcript spec dropped.
- **Cost guard added** — nightly job alerts on >$5 Anthropic spend.

```json
{
  "plan": "# Integration test suite: mock Linear + scripted child\n\n## Context\n\nToday `drain-cycle` only tests against a real Linear cycle. Anything past happy-path becomes expensive: failure modes need deliberately broken issues, resume needs an actual halt-and-restart, and usage tracking needs real Claude sessions. Unit tests cover individual modules but stop at the function boundary — they monkey-patch `linear._post` and swap `claude -p` for marker-touching shell scripts (`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22`, `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65`).\n\nWe need **scenario-level integration tests** that drive the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, covering:\n\n- **Best-case drains** — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions\n- **Per-issue failure paths** — every halt condition in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (repo resolution, worktree setup, `set_state` failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)\n- **Cycle-level halts** — `DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach propagation\n- **Resume semantics** — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo\n- **Session/usage tracking** — `_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches\n\nThe drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); \"retry\" here means **resume** — a re-invocation against the same cycle.\n\n### Success criterion (outcome, not output)\n\nThe suite works when **every halt path in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` and every cycle-halt path in `orchestrator._run` has at least one integration scenario, and a new halt path cannot merge without one**. A CI gate enforces this (see *Verification*): it greps the production code for `mark_error` slugs, compares against the union of `exception.slug` and `halt_reason` strings observed in the test run, and fails on any gap. Behavioural outcome: time-to-reproduce a future halt-logic bug drops from hours (build a real cycle) to minutes (write a 20-line scenario).\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. **`shape-design-doc`** — this plan is the artefact; promote to `docs/integration-test-architecture.md` (or an ADR) if more reviewers need it.\n2. **`shape-planning-and-task-breakdown`** — convert the *Slices* section into Linear issues on a fresh initiative under the existing project.\n3. **`agent-skills:test-driven-development`** during build — each slice ships a failing scenario before the supporting infra.\n4. **`agent-skills:code-review-and-quality`** per slice before commit, per `AGENTS.md`.\n\n## Approach\n\n**Parent HTTP mock + Linear-MCP shim + real `claude -p` + in-process driver.** The spawned session is real claude every time; only the Linear surface is mocked. Three pillars.\n\n**Alternatives rejected** (draft for the ADR slice):\n\n- **Scripted/fake `claude -p`.** Cheaper, faster, deterministic. Rejected because a scripted child stops being an integration test — it cannot validate the real session's usage stream, MCP tool calls, prompt-to-completion behaviour, or production OTel spans against actual data.\n- **Hybrid: real claude only on happy-path; scripted child for failure determinism.** Two patterns to maintain; weaker default signal; gives up real-claude coverage on usage-tracking edge cases.\n- **Function-injection / `LinearClient` protocol on the parent.** Cheaper than HTTP but skips the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. The existing module-level functions in `linear.py` drive easily against an HTTP fake once `LINEAR_API_URL` is overridable.\n- **VCR-style recorded fixtures.** Realistic but awkward for failure injection, expensive to maintain under schema drift.\n\nSlice 1 produces a short ADR at `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, these rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.\n\n### Cost, runtime, and determinism\n\nReal claude makes the suite costly, slow, and non-deterministic on numbers.\n\n- **Cost / runtime budget**: ~$0.05–$0.20 per spawning scenario, ~15–30 seconds per scenario. Full suite of ~30 scenarios: ~$1–$3 and ~15 minutes. Nightly job, not per-commit.\n- **Per-commit CI** runs only the pre-spawn halt scenarios (no claude spawned) — fast and deterministic.\n- **Determinism strategy** — split assertions:\n  - **Exact** — exit codes, final Linear state, halt reason class, runlog file presence, structural shape of runlog JSON, set of MCP tool calls observed.\n  - **Predicate** — `tokens > 0`, `0 < cost_usd < per_issue_cost_usd`, `num_turns >= 1`, `model is not None`. No exact values.\n  - **Tolerant snapshot** — `dirty-equals` matches keys and types, ignores values.\n- **Cost guard**: scenarios that spawn claude carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude`. Default `pytest` invocation excludes the marker; nightly job opts in via `pytest -m real_claude`. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` required.\n\n### Pillar 1 — `MockLinear`: an in-process fake GraphQL server\n\nA FastAPI/Starlette app on a random localhost port, started per-test via a pytest fixture. It speaks the five GraphQL operations `linear.py` uses (`current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state` / WorkflowState lookup) plus an `IssueUpdate` mutation the scripted child hits to flip an issue to Done.\n\nState lives in a typed in-memory store: `Cycle`, `Issue`, `Label`, `WorkflowState`. The store records every call (operation, variables, timestamp) so tests can assert call sequence and counts.\n\nFault injection is **per-operation, per-call-index**: `mock.fail_after(op=\"set_state\", call=2, mode=\"http_500\")`. Modes: `http_500`, `http_429`, `graphql_error`, `malformed_json`, `slow_response(seconds)`, `connection_reset`. The drain has no retry, so \"fail\" means \"this single call breaks\" — tests then verify the halt path.\n\n**Production change required**: `linear.py:18` hard-codes `https://api.linear.app/graphql`. Replace with `os.environ.get(\"LINEAR_API_URL\", \"https://api.linear.app/graphql\")`. No CLI flag — env var only.\n\n### Pillar 2 — `linear_mcp_shim`: a Linear MCP server backed by the mock\n\nA small Python stdio MCP server (built on the official `mcp` SDK) that exposes the two tools the drain-cycle prompt uses today: `save_issue` (state transitions) and `save_comment`. Each tool translates its call into a GraphQL POST against `MockLinear`, sharing the same backing store as the parent's HTTP surface.\n\nThe harness writes a custom `.mcp.json` into each test worktree registering the shim under the name `linear` (matching the existing tool prefix `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*`). The orchestrator already symlinks `.mcp.json` from the configured source into the worktree (`orchestrator.py:257-259`); the harness arranges for the test source to be the shim-configured file.\n\nThe shim records every tool call (tool name, arguments, timestamp) on the shared store, so scenarios assert against both parent GraphQL calls and child MCP calls through one call log.\n\n**Why an MCP shim and not just `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child env.** MCP is a separate protocol from raw GraphQL; the official Linear MCP server speaks MCP over stdio to claude and HTTPS to Linear. Setting `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child has no effect — there's no way to redirect the official server without forking it. A purpose-built shim is the smallest seam.\n\n### How scenarios steer claude\n\nScenarios drive child behaviour through three knobs the harness sets per-scenario:\n\n- **Issue body** — written into `MockLinear` and read by claude via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__get_issue`. The body is the natural prompt seam (e.g. \"create `<id>.txt` containing 'hello'; then mark this issue Done\"). Library lives in `tests/integration/prompts/*.md`.\n- **Per-issue limits** — `limits.yml` overrides (e.g. `per_issue_tokens=2000`, `per_issue_seconds=30`) used with verbose prompts to provoke breach scenarios deterministically.\n- **Claude flags** — the harness may inject `--max-turns 1` for \"exit without Done\" cases (claude exits before completing the workflow), via the `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `orchestrator.py:25`.\n\nThe drain-cycle prompt (`drain_cycle/prompt.py:39-42`) stays unchanged — same prompt the real drain uses.\n\n### Pillar 3 — `IntegrationHarness`: a fixture that drives `orchestrator.run()` in-process\n\nA pytest fixture that:\n\n- Creates a temp `HOME` so `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`, `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`, and `~/.drain-cycle/runs/` redirect to a tmpdir (`monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", ...)` plus any path-resolution helpers in `cli.py` / `runlog.py`).\n- Initialises one or more real git repos in tmpdir (existing pattern in `test_orchestrator_iteration.py`).\n- Writes a `repos.yml` mapping label names to those repo paths.\n- Writes a `.mcp.json` in each repo registering the Linear-MCP shim under `linear`.\n- Starts `MockLinear` and populates it with the scenario's cycle, issues (with prompt body), and labels.\n- Sets `LINEAR_API_URL`, `LINEAR_API_KEY=test`, `MOCK_LINEAR_URL=http://localhost:<port>`, `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` env vars. Verifies `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set for scenarios that spawn claude.\n- Optionally appends extra flags to `_CLAUDE_CMD` (e.g. `--max-turns 1`) for scenarios that need to coerce claude's behaviour.\n- Calls `orchestrator.run(repos, limits)` directly and returns a `Result`: exit code, runlog JSON, parent GraphQL call log, child MCP call log, final mock state.\n\nTests assert against `Result`. One scenario per `test_*` function. Helpers (`make_cycle`, `make_issue`, `expect_runlog`, `expect_calls`) keep scenarios short.\n\n**Per-scenario contract.** Every scenario declares:\n\n1. **Setup** — `Cycle`, `Issue[]`, `Label[]`, `WorkflowState[]`, repos.yml mapping, limits.yml overrides, transcript fixture name(s), any fault injection.\n2. **Expected exit code** — orchestrator return.\n3. **Expected runlog assertions** — a list of (a) structural shape (keys, types) matched via `dirty-equals`, (b) predicate assertions on numeric fields (`tokens > 0`, `cost_usd < limits.per_issue_cost_usd`), (c) exact assertions on deterministic fields (`final_linear_state`, `halt_reason`).\n4. **Expected final mock state** — issue states the mock should end in (e.g. `{ \"ABA-1\": \"Done\", \"ABA-2\": \"Todo\" }` after a halt on issue 2).\n5. **Expected call sequence** — ordered `(surface, operation, predicate-on-args)` tuples across both parent GraphQL and child MCP calls. Strict by default; `expect_calls(strict=False)` for tolerant cases where claude's tool-call order varies between runs.\n\nA collection-time lint test fails any scenario missing (3), (4), or (5).\n\n## Production code changes required\n\nMinimal. Every test-only seam reuses existing structure.\n\n1. **`drain_cycle/linear.py:18`** — `_GRAPHQL_URL` reads from `LINEAR_API_URL` env var, default unchanged. One-line change.\n2. **`drain_cycle/runlog.py`** — verify the run-log directory honours `HOME` re-resolution; slice 1 greps for `Path.home`, `expanduser`, and `HOME`. If the path caches at module load, add an accessor (e.g. `_run_log_dir()`) so the harness re-resolves after `monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", ...)`.\n3. **`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`** — `_CLAUDE_CMD` is already module-level (verified by Explore). Tests patch it.\n\nNo new abstraction layers, no `LinearClient` Protocol — the existing module-level functions drive easily against an HTTP fake once the URL is configurable.\n\n## Test-infra correctness checks (one-time pre-slice work)\n\nRisks the plan-review surfaced. Each lands before dependent slices.\n\n- **Telemetry isolation** — drain-cycle wires httpx OTel auto-instrumentation in `telemetry.py`. The harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` to keep tests from exporting spans. Confirmed in slice 2.\n- **MCP-shim correctness** — the shim must implement the same tool schemas the official Linear MCP exposes for `save_issue` and `save_comment`, otherwise claude's tool calls land with wrong shapes. Slice 3 spike: capture the official schemas (from the Linear MCP docs or by running the real one briefly), implement matching ones in the shim, verify a single happy-path drain reaches Done end-to-end. **Kill-switch**: if the schemas drift between Linear MCP versions, pin the shim to a captured snapshot and add a periodic check.\n- **Breach scenario reproducibility** — with real claude, breach scenarios depend on prompts that reliably exceed tight per-issue caps. Slice 8 spike: confirm a verbose prompt (e.g. \"write a 2000-line poem\") plus `per_issue_tokens=1000` reliably triggers token breach across 5 consecutive runs. **Kill-switch**: if not reliable, fall back to time-breach only (tight `per_issue_seconds` plus a \"sleep 60s\" prompt is robust because the breach loop polls wall-clock).\n\n## Scenario catalogue (initial coverage)\n\nGrouped by area; one test function each. Target end-state, not a single slice — slices below build the infra and add scenarios incrementally.\n\nScenarios that spawn `claude -p` carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude` (excluded from per-commit CI; run nightly). Scenarios that halt pre-spawn run on every commit.\n\n**Best case** *(real_claude)*\n- 3-issue dependency-ordered drain reaches Done in order; runlog totals positive and sum across issues; child MCP calls include `save_issue` (transition to Done) and `save_comment` (summary) per issue\n- Mixed Todo + Backlog states picked up; correct planning\n- Single-issue drain\n- Empty cycle exits cleanly *(no claude spawned)*\n\n**Per-issue halts** (`orchestrator._drain_one_issue` paths)\n- Missing `repo:` label → halt + reason *(no claude spawned)*\n- `repo:` label resolves to nonexistent path → halt *(no claude spawned)*\n- Worktree `add` fails (dirty tree) → halt *(no claude spawned)*\n- `set_state` (Todo→In-Progress) fails with HTTP 500 → halt *(no claude spawned)*\n- Child exits without setting Done *(real_claude; coerced via `--max-turns 1`)* → halt + reason matches `final_linear_state`\n- Child exits non-zero *(real_claude; provoked by an invalid prompt or a tool that errors)* → halt\n- Per-issue token cap exceeded mid-stream *(real_claude; verbose prompt + `per_issue_tokens=1000`)* → breach kill; runlog records breach scope/metric/limit/observed\n- Per-issue time cap exceeded *(real_claude; prompt \"wait 60s\" + `per_issue_seconds=10`)* → time breach recorded\n- Revert `set_state` (In-Progress→Todo) fails after a halt → halt-reason concatenates *(real_claude or pre-spawn)*\n\n**Cycle-level halts**\n- `DependencyCycleError` from `pending_issues` → halt before any issue attempted; `cycle_halt_reason` set; runlog otherwise empty *(no claude spawned)*\n- After two Done issues, cycle-cap breach detected → third issue not attempted; halt reason names cumulative metric *(real_claude)*\n\n**Resume** *(real_claude)*\n- Run halts on issue 2; re-run with the same cycle picks up issue 2 (mock has it back at Todo via the revert) and issue 3; second runlog file separate from first; final mock state correct\n\n**Session / usage tracking** *(real_claude; predicate assertions on numbers)*\n- Single-issue drain produces `usage.cumulative.input_tokens > 0`, `usage.cumulative.output_tokens > 0`, `peak_context > 0`, `cost_usd > 0`, `num_turns >= 1`, `session_id` non-empty, `is_error == false`, `model` non-empty\n- Multi-issue drain: cycle aggregates (`cycle_tokens_cumulative`, `cycle_cost_usd`, `cycle_duration_seconds`) equal the sum of per-issue values\n- Worker dedup invariant (`worker.py:416`) verified indirectly: re-emit a non-deterministic prompt and confirm `usage.cumulative` is monotonically non-decreasing within a turn; explicit dedup behaviour stays unit-tested\n\n**Linear edge cases** *(no claude spawned — parent halts before pre-spawn `set_state` succeeds)*\n- 500, 429, GraphQL error, malformed JSON, slow-within-timeout (drain proceeds), slow-beyond-timeout (halts)\n\n**Out of scope for MVP**\n- Honeycomb/OTel span assertions (separate slice — needs a fake OTLP collector; production code wires spans cleanly per `drain_cycle/telemetry.py`)\n- True MCP-server fake for children (scripted-child + direct GraphQL POST satisfies the contract)\n\n**In MVP, bounded** — slice 4 ships one subprocess smoke test that spawns the real `drain-cycle` binary against the mock and asserts exit code plus runlog presence. This single test covers `cli.py`'s secrets-load order and process exit-code path. Larger subprocess scenarios stay out of scope.\n\n**Note on the worker `_UsageAccumulator` dedup invariant** — the worker dedupes by `message.id` (`worker.py:416`). Real claude rarely emits duplicate ids in practice, so the dedup branch is exercised by existing unit tests, not by integration scenarios. Plan accordingly: keep the dedup unit test (`test_usage_accumulator_dedup.py` or equivalent); the integration suite asserts on monotonicity, not on dedup directly.\n\n## Slices (verifiable build order)\n\nEach slice ships in one session and ends with a passing scenario.\n\n1. **Production seam + ADR** — `LINEAR_API_URL` env var in `linear.py`; unit test confirms override; default unchanged. Runlog `HOME` re-resolution verified (grep + accessor if needed). **Deliverable**: `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.\n2. **`MockLinear` skeleton** — FastAPI app with `current_cycle` and `pending_issues` only; serves a hard-coded scenario; one unit test wires through `linear.py` and confirms the call lands on the mock. Harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true`.\n3. **`linear_mcp_shim` + first real-claude drain** — stdio MCP server implementing `save_issue` and `save_comment` against the mock; harness writes a `.mcp.json` registering it; first **real-claude** happy-path drain of one issue reaches Done end-to-end. Schemas captured from the official Linear MCP. Marked `@pytest.mark.real_claude`.\n4. **`IntegrationHarness` fixture + subprocess smoke** — tmpdir HOME, repos.yml, real git repo, `.mcp.json` writer, mock startup. Slice 3's scenario refactored onto the harness. **Plus**: one subprocess test spawns `drain-cycle` against the mock end-to-end and asserts exit code plus runlog file existence.\n5. **Mock GraphQL completeness + drift guard** — add `get_issue`, `set_state`, WorkflowState resolution; happy-path drain of 3 ordered issues passes. **Plus**: `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler.\n6. **Fault injection** — per-operation, per-call-index fault modes in the mock; scenario covers `set_state` HTTP 500 halt (pre-spawn, fast). Per-scenario contract enforced (runlog assertions, mock state, call sequence).\n7. **Pre-spawn halt scenarios** — repo-resolution failures, worktree-add failure, set_state 500. Fast, no claude.\n8. **Real-claude halt scenarios** — child exits without Done (via `--max-turns 1`); child non-zero exit (via invalid prompt). Marked `real_claude`.\n9. **Breach scenarios** — time-breach first (tight `per_issue_seconds` plus \"wait 60s\" prompt — robust). Token-breach gated on the slice-8 reproducibility spike. Both `real_claude`.\n10. **Cycle-level halt scenarios** — `DependencyCycleError` (no claude); cycle-cap breach after two Done issues (`real_claude`).\n11. **Resume scenario** — drive a halt, re-invoke orchestrator on the same mock cycle, assert second runlog file and final state. `real_claude`.\n12. **Usage-tracking + Linear edge-case scenarios** — usage predicates on a happy-path drain (`real_claude`); 429 / malformed / slow-timeout halts on the parent (no claude).\n\nSlices 1–4 are the foundation; 5–12 are independent and ship in any order once 4 lands. Each slice opens a Linear issue under the existing project and closes per `AGENTS.md` (review → fix → commit + push → Linear summary comment → Done).\n\n## Critical files\n\nTo modify:\n- `drain_cycle/linear.py` — env-var override for GraphQL URL (slice 1)\n- `drain_cycle/runlog.py` — verify `HOME` re-resolution; add small accessor only if cached at module load (slice 1)\n\nTo create:\n- `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` — chosen approach + rejected alternatives + revisit conditions (slice 1)\n- `tests/integration/conftest.py` — harness fixture, `real_claude` marker config\n- `tests/integration/mock_linear.py` — FastAPI GraphQL server + shared in-memory store + fault injection\n- `tests/integration/linear_mcp_shim.py` — stdio MCP server backed by the shared store\n- `tests/integration/prompts/*.md` — issue-body prompts (happy_path, exit_without_done, verbose, wait, etc.)\n- `tests/integration/test_happy_path.py`, `test_pre_spawn_halts.py`, `test_real_claude_halts.py`, `test_breach.py`, `test_cycle_halts.py`, `test_resume.py`, `test_usage_tracking.py`, `test_linear_edges.py`, `test_linear_surface_audit.py`, `test_subprocess_smoke.py` — scenarios\n\nTo reuse from existing tests:\n- Real git-repo setup pattern in `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22-90`\n- Linear fixture style in `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65-70`\n- `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`\n\n## Verification\n\nEnd-to-end:\n- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -v` — non-`real_claude` scenarios green; no real Linear network calls; no real `LINEAR_API_KEY` leaking in. Runs per commit.\n- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -m real_claude -v` — full suite green against a real Anthropic API key. Runs nightly; documented in CI or run-instructions.\n- `uv run pytest --cov=drain_cycle/orchestrator --cov=drain_cycle/worker --cov=drain_cycle/runlog tests/integration/` — coverage on orchestrator, worker, and runlog rises meaningfully.\n- Each slice closes per `AGENTS.md`: review → fix → commit + push to main → Linear summary comment → Done.\n\n**Outcome-metric gate** (enforces the *Success criterion*):\n- `test_halt_coverage` greps `mark_error` slugs from `drain_cycle/**.py`, collects the union of `exception.slug` attributes (or `halt_reason` strings) observed across the full integration run (including `real_claude`), and asserts equality. Fails when a new halt slug lands in production code without a scenario.\n\nNegative checks during build:\n- `grep -r \"api.linear.app\" tests/` returns nothing — no accidental real-Linear calls in fixtures.\n- The mock's call log (parent GraphQL + child MCP) records every operation a scenario expects; an unexpected call fails the test loudly.\n- `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler.\n- Cost budget guard: nightly job tracks total Anthropic spend per run and alerts if it exceeds $5 (catch a runaway loop scenario early).\n",
