```
1	# Integration test suite: mock Linear + scripted child
2	
3	## Context
4	
5	Today `drain-cycle` only tests against a real Linear cycle. Anything past happy-path becomes expensive: failure modes need deliberately broken issues, resume needs an actual halt-and-restart, and usage tracking needs real Claude sessions. Unit tests cover individual modules but stop at the function boundary — they monkey-patch `linear._post` and swap `claude -p` for marker-touching shell scripts (`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22`, `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65`).
6	
7	We need **scenario-level integration tests** that drive the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, covering:
8	
9	- **Best-case drains** — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions
10	- **Per-issue failure paths** — every halt condition in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (repo resolution, worktree setup, `set_state` failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)
11	- **Cycle-level halts** — `DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach propagation
12	- **Resume semantics** — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo
13	- **Session/usage tracking** — `_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches
14	
15	The drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); "retry" here means **resume** — a re-invocation against the same cycle.
16	
17	### Success criterion (outcome, not output)
18	
19	The suite works when **every halt path in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` and every cycle-halt path in `orchestrator._run` has at least one integration scenario, and a new halt path cannot merge without one**. A CI gate enforces this (see *Verification*): it greps the production code for `mark_error` slugs, compares against the union of `exception.slug` and `halt_reason` strings observed in the test run, and fails on any gap. Behavioural outcome: time-to-reproduce a future halt-logic bug drops from hours (build a real cycle) to minutes (write a 20-line scenario).
20	
21	## Workflow
22	
23	1. **`shape-design-doc`** — this plan is the artefact; promote to `docs/integration-test-architecture.md` (or an ADR) if more reviewers need it.
24	2. **`shape-planning-and-task-breakdown`** — convert the *Slices* section into Linear issues on a fresh initiative under the existing project.
25	3. **`agent-skills:test-driven-development`** during build — each slice ships a failing scenario before the supporting infra.
26	4. **`agent-skills:code-review-and-quality`** per slice before commit, per `AGENTS.md`.
27	
28	## Approach
29	
30	**Parent HTTP mock + Linear-MCP shim + real `claude -p` + in-process driver.** The spawned session is real claude every time; only the Linear surface is mocked. Three pillars.
31	
32	**Alternatives rejected** (draft for the ADR slice):
33	
34	- **Scripted/fake `claude -p`.** Cheaper, faster, deterministic. Rejected because a scripted child stops being an integration test — it cannot validate the real session's usage stream, MCP tool calls, prompt-to-completion behaviour, or production OTel spans against actual data.
35	- **Hybrid: real claude only on happy-path; scripted child for failure determinism.** Two patterns to maintain; weaker default signal; gives up real-claude coverage on usage-tracking edge cases.
36	- **Function-injection / `LinearClient` protocol on the parent.** Cheaper than HTTP but skips the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. The existing module-level functions in `linear.py` drive easily against an HTTP fake once `LINEAR_API_URL` is overridable.
37	- **VCR-style recorded fixtures.** Realistic but awkward for failure injection, expensive to maintain under schema drift.
38	
39	Slice 1 produces a short ADR at `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, these rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.
40	
41	### Cost, runtime, and determinism
42	
43	Real claude makes the suite costly, slow, and non-deterministic on numbers.
44	
45	- **Cost / runtime budget**: ~$0.05–$0.20 per spawning scenario, ~15–30 seconds per scenario. Full suite of ~30 scenarios: ~$1–$3 and ~15 minutes. Nightly job, not per-commit.
46	- **Per-commit CI** runs only the pre-spawn halt scenarios (no claude spawned) — fast and deterministic.
47	- **Determinism strategy** — split assertions:
48	  - **Exact** — exit codes, final Linear state, halt reason class, runlog file presence, structural shape of runlog JSON, set of MCP tool calls observed.
49	  - **Predicate** — `tokens > 0`, `0 < cost_usd < per_issue_cost_usd`, `num_turns >= 1`, `model is not None`. No exact values.
50	  - **Tolerant snapshot** — `dirty-equals` matches keys and types, ignores values.
51	- **Cost guard**: scenarios that spawn claude carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude`. Default `pytest` invocation excludes the marker; nightly job opts in via `pytest -m real_claude`. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` required.
52	
53	### Pillar 1 — `MockLinear`: an in-process fake GraphQL server
54	
55	A FastAPI/Starlette app on a random localhost port, started per-test via a pytest fixture. It speaks the five GraphQL operations `linear.py` uses (`current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state` / WorkflowState lookup) plus an `IssueUpdate` mutation the scripted child hits to flip an issue to Done.
56	
57	State lives in a typed in-memory store: `Cycle`, `Issue`, `Label`, `WorkflowState`. The store records every call (operation, variables, timestamp) so tests can assert call sequence and counts.
58	
59	Fault injection is **per-operation, per-call-index**: `mock.fail_after(op="set_state", call=2, mode="http_500")`. Modes: `http_500`, `http_429`, `graphql_error`, `malformed_json`, `slow_response(seconds)`, `connection_reset`. The drain has no retry, so "fail" means "this single call breaks" — tests then verify the halt path.
60	
61	**Production change required**: `linear.py:18` hard-codes `https://api.linear.app/graphql`. Replace with `os.environ.get("LINEAR_API_URL", "https://api.linear.app/graphql")`. No CLI flag — env var only.
62	
63	### Pillar 2 — `linear_mcp_shim`: a Linear MCP server backed by the mock
64	
65	A small Python stdio MCP server (built on the official `mcp` SDK) that exposes the two tools the drain-cycle prompt uses today: `save_issue` (state transitions) and `save_comment`. Each tool translates its call into a GraphQL POST against `MockLinear`, sharing the same backing store as the parent's HTTP surface.
66	
67	The harness writes a custom `.mcp.json` into each test worktree registering the shim under the name `linear` (matching the existing tool prefix `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*`). The orchestrator already symlinks `.mcp.json` from the configured source into the worktree (`orchestrator.py:257-259`); the harness arranges for the test source to be the shim-configured file.
68	
69	The shim records every tool call (tool name, arguments, timestamp) on the shared store, so scenarios assert against both parent GraphQL calls and child MCP calls through one call log.
70	
71	**Why an MCP shim and not just `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child env.** MCP is a separate protocol from raw GraphQL; the official Linear MCP server speaks MCP over stdio to claude and HTTPS to Linear. Setting `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child has no effect — there's no way to redirect the official server without forking it. A purpose-built shim is the smallest seam.
72	
73	### How scenarios steer claude
74	
75	Scenarios drive child behaviour through three knobs the harness sets per-scenario:
76	
77	- **Issue body** — written into `MockLinear` and read by claude via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__get_issue`. The body is the natural prompt seam (e.g. "create `<id>.txt` containing 'hello'; then mark this issue Done"). Library lives in `tests/integration/prompts/*.md`.
78	- **Per-issue limits** — `limits.yml` overrides (e.g. `per_issue_tokens=2000`, `per_issue_seconds=30`) used with verbose prompts to provoke breach scenarios deterministically.
79	- **Claude flags** — the harness may inject `--max-turns 1` for "exit without Done" cases (claude exits before completing the workflow), via the `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `orchestrator.py:25`.
80	
81	The drain-cycle prompt (`drain_cycle/prompt.py:39-42`) stays unchanged — same prompt the real drain uses.
82	
83	### Pillar 3 — `IntegrationHarness`: a fixture that drives `orchestrator.run()` in-process
84	
85	A pytest fixture that:
86	
87	- Creates a temp `HOME` so `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`, `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`, and `~/.drain-cycle/runs/` redirect to a tmpdir (`monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)` plus any path-resolution helpers in `cli.py` / `runlog.py`).
88	- Initialises one or more real git repos in tmpdir (existing pattern in `test_orchestrator_iteration.py`).
89	- Writes a `repos.yml` mapping label names to those repo paths.
90	- Writes a `.mcp.json` in each repo registering the Linear-MCP shim under `linear`.
91	- Starts `MockLinear` and populates it with the scenario's cycle, issues (with prompt body), and labels.
92	- Sets `LINEAR_API_URL`, `LINEAR_API_KEY=test`, `MOCK_LINEAR_URL=http://localhost:<port>`, `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` env vars. Verifies `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set for scenarios that spawn claude.
93	- Optionally appends extra flags to `_CLAUDE_CMD` (e.g. `--max-turns 1`) for scenarios that need to coerce claude's behaviour.
94	- Calls `orchestrator.run(repos, limits)` directly and returns a `Result`: exit code, runlog JSON, parent GraphQL call log, child MCP call log, final mock state.
95	
96	Tests assert against `Result`. One scenario per `test_*` function. Helpers (`make_cycle`, `make_issue`, `expect_runlog`, `expect_calls`) keep scenarios short.
97	
98	**Per-scenario contract.** Every scenario declares:
99	
100	1. **Setup** — `Cycle`, `Issue[]`, `Label[]`, `WorkflowState[]`, repos.yml mapping, limits.yml overrides, transcript fixture name(s), any fault injection.
101	2. **Expected exit code** — orchestrator return.
102	3. **Expected runlog assertions** — a list of (a) structural shape (keys, types) matched via `dirty-equals`, (b) predicate assertions on numeric fields (`tokens > 0`, `cost_usd < limits.per_issue_cost_usd`), (c) exact assertions on deterministic fields (`final_linear_state`, `halt_reason`).
103	4. **Expected final mock state** — issue states the mock should end in (e.g. `{ "ABA-1": "Done", "ABA-2": "Todo" }` after a halt on issue 2).
104	5. **Expected call sequence** — ordered `(surface, operation, predicate-on-args)` tuples across both parent GraphQL and child MCP calls. Strict by default; `expect_calls(strict=False)` for tolerant cases where claude's tool-call order varies between runs.
105	
106	A collection-time lint test fails any scenario missing (3), (4), or (5).
107	
108	## Production code changes required
109	
110	Minimal. Every test-only seam reuses existing structure.
111	
112	1. **`drain_cycle/linear.py:18`** — `_GRAPHQL_URL` reads from `LINEAR_API_URL` env var, default unchanged. One-line change.
113	2. **`drain_cycle/runlog.py`** — verify the run-log directory honours `HOME` re-resolution; slice 1 greps for `Path.home`, `expanduser`, and `HOME`. If the path caches at module load, add an accessor (e.g. `_run_log_dir()`) so the harness re-resolves after `monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)`.
114	3. **`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`** — `_CLAUDE_CMD` is already module-level (verified by Explore). Tests patch it.
115	
116	No new abstraction layers, no `LinearClient` Protocol — the existing module-level functions drive easily against an HTTP fake once the URL is configurable.
117	
118	## Test-infra correctness checks (one-time pre-slice work)
119	
120	Risks the plan-review surfaced. Each lands before dependent slices.
121	
122	- **Telemetry isolation** — drain-cycle wires httpx OTel auto-instrumentation in `telemetry.py`. The harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` to keep tests from exporting spans. Confirmed in slice 2.
123	- **MCP-shim correctness** — the shim must implement the same tool schemas the official Linear MCP exposes for `save_issue` and `save_comment`, otherwise claude's tool calls land with wrong shapes. Slice 3 spike: capture the official schemas (from the Linear MCP docs or by running the real one briefly), implement matching ones in the shim, verify a single happy-path drain reaches Done end-to-end. **Kill-switch**: if the schemas drift between Linear MCP versions, pin the shim to a captured snapshot and add a periodic check.
124	- **Breach scenario reproducibility** — with real claude, breach scenarios depend on prompts that reliably exceed tight per-issue caps. Slice 8 spike: confirm a verbose prompt (e.g. "write a 2000-line poem") plus `per_issue_tokens=1000` reliably triggers token breach across 5 consecutive runs. **Kill-switch**: if not reliable, fall back to time-breach only (tight `per_issue_seconds` plus a "sleep 60s" prompt is robust because the breach loop polls wall-clock).
125	
126	## Scenario catalogue (initial coverage)
127	
128	Grouped by area; one test function each. Target end-state, not a single slice — slices below build the infra and add scenarios incrementally.
129	
130	Scenarios that spawn `claude -p` carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude` (excluded from per-commit CI; run nightly). Scenarios that halt pre-spawn run on every commit.
131	
132	**Best case** *(real_claude)*
133	- 3-issue dependency-ordered drain reaches Done in order; runlog totals positive and sum across issues; child MCP calls include `save_issue` (transition to Done) and `save_comment` (summary) per issue
134	- Mixed Todo + Backlog states picked up; correct planning
135	- Single-issue drain
136	- Empty cycle exits cleanly *(no claude spawned)*
137	
138	**Per-issue halts** (`orchestrator._drain_one_issue` paths)
139	- Missing `repo:` label → halt + reason *(no claude spawned)*
140	- `repo:` label resolves to nonexistent path → halt *(no claude spawned)*
141	- Worktree `add` fails (dirty tree) → halt *(no claude spawned)*
142	- `set_state` (Todo→In-Progress) fails with HTTP 500 → halt *(no claude spawned)*
143	- Child exits without setting Done *(real_claude; coerced via `--max-turns 1`)* → halt + reason matches `final_linear_state`
144	- Child exits non-zero *(real_claude; provoked by an invalid prompt or a tool that errors)* → halt
145	- Per-issue token cap exceeded mid-stream *(real_claude; verbose prompt + `per_issue_tokens=1000`)* → breach kill; runlog records breach scope/metric/limit/observed
146	- Per-issue time cap exceeded *(real_claude; prompt "wait 60s" + `per_issue_seconds=10`)* → time breach recorded
147	- Revert `set_state` (In-Progress→Todo) fails after a halt → halt-reason concatenates *(real_claude or pre-spawn)*
148	
149	**Cycle-level halts**
150	- `DependencyCycleError` from `pending_issues` → halt before any issue attempted; `cycle_halt_reason` set; runlog otherwise empty *(no claude spawned)*
151	- After two Done issues, cycle-cap breach detected → third issue not attempted; halt reason names cumulative metric *(real_claude)*
152	
153	**Resume** *(real_claude)*
154	- Run halts on issue 2; re-run with the same cycle picks up issue 2 (mock has it back at Todo via the revert) and issue 3; second runlog file separate from first; final mock state correct
155	
156	**Session / usage tracking** *(real_claude; predicate assertions on numbers)*
157	- Single-issue drain produces `usage.cumulative.input_tokens > 0`, `usage.cumulative.output_tokens > 0`, `peak_context > 0`, `cost_usd > 0`, `num_turns >= 1`, `session_id` non-empty, `is_error == false`, `model` non-empty
158	- Multi-issue drain: cycle aggregates (`cycle_tokens_cumulative`, `cycle_cost_usd`, `cycle_duration_seconds`) equal the sum of per-issue values
159	- Worker dedup invariant (`worker.py:416`) verified indirectly: re-emit a non-deterministic prompt and confirm `usage.cumulative` is monotonically non-decreasing within a turn; explicit dedup behaviour stays unit-tested
160	
161	**Linear edge cases** *(no claude spawned — parent halts before pre-spawn `set_state` succeeds)*
162	- 500, 429, GraphQL error, malformed JSON, slow-within-timeout (drain proceeds), slow-beyond-timeout (halts)
163	
164	**Out of scope for MVP**
165	- Honeycomb/OTel span assertions (separate slice — needs a fake OTLP collector; production code wires spans cleanly per `drain_cycle/telemetry.py`)
166	- True MCP-server fake for children (scripted-child + direct GraphQL POST satisfies the contract)
167	
168	**In MVP, bounded** — slice 4 ships one subprocess smoke test that spawns the real `drain-cycle` binary against the mock and asserts exit code plus runlog presence. This single test covers `cli.py`'s secrets-load order and process exit-code path. Larger subprocess scenarios stay out of scope.
169	
170	**Note on the worker `_UsageAccumulator` dedup invariant** — the worker dedupes by `message.id` (`worker.py:416`). Real claude rarely emits duplicate ids in practice, so the dedup branch is exercised by existing unit tests, not by integration scenarios. Plan accordingly: keep the dedup unit test (`test_usage_accumulator_dedup.py` or equivalent); the integration suite asserts on monotonicity, not on dedup directly.
171	
172	## Slices (verifiable build order)
173	
174	Each slice ships in one session and ends with a passing scenario.
175	
176	1. **Production seam + ADR** — `LINEAR_API_URL` env var in `linear.py`; unit test confirms override; default unchanged. Runlog `HOME` re-resolution verified (grep + accessor if needed). **Deliverable**: `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.
177	2. **`MockLinear` skeleton** — FastAPI app with `current_cycle` and `pending_issues` only; serves a hard-coded scenario; one unit test wires through `linear.py` and confirms the call lands on the mock. Harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true`.
178	3. **`linear_mcp_shim` + first real-claude drain** — stdio MCP server implementing `save_issue` and `save_comment` against the mock; harness writes a `.mcp.json` registering it; first **real-claude** happy-path drain of one issue reaches Done end-to-end. Schemas captured from the official Linear MCP. Marked `@pytest.mark.real_claude`.
179	4. **`IntegrationHarness` fixture + subprocess smoke** — tmpdir HOME, repos.yml, real git repo, `.mcp.json` writer, mock startup. Slice 3's scenario refactored onto the harness. **Plus**: one subprocess test spawns `drain-cycle` against the mock end-to-end and asserts exit code plus runlog file existence.
180	5. **Mock GraphQL completeness + drift guard** — add `get_issue`, `set_state`, WorkflowState resolution; happy-path drain of 3 ordered issues passes. **Plus**: `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler.
181	6. **Fault injection** — per-operation, per-call-index fault modes in the mock; scenario covers `set_state` HTTP 500 halt (pre-spawn, fast). Per-scenario contract enforced (runlog assertions, mock state, call sequence).
182	7. **Pre-spawn halt scenarios** — repo-resolution failures, worktree-add failure, set_state 500. Fast, no claude.
183	8. **Real-claude halt scenarios** — child exits without Done (via `--max-turns 1`); child non-zero exit (via invalid prompt). Marked `real_claude`.
184	9. **Breach scenarios** — time-breach first (tight `per_issue_seconds` plus "wait 60s" prompt — robust). Token-breach gated on the slice-8 reproducibility spike. Both `real_claude`.
185	10. **Cycle-level halt scenarios** — `DependencyCycleError` (no claude); cycle-cap breach after two Done issues (`real_claude`).
186	11. **Resume scenario** — drive a halt, re-invoke orchestrator on the same mock cycle, assert second runlog file and final state. `real_claude`.
187	12. **Usage-tracking + Linear edge-case scenarios** — usage predicates on a happy-path drain (`real_claude`); 429 / malformed / slow-timeout halts on the parent (no claude).
188	
189	Slices 1–4 are the foundation; 5–12 are independent and ship in any order once 4 lands. Each slice opens a Linear issue under the existing project and closes per `AGENTS.md` (review → fix → commit + push → Linear summary comment → Done).
190	
191	## Critical files
192	
193	To modify:
194	- `drain_cycle/linear.py` — env-var override for GraphQL URL (slice 1)
195	- `drain_cycle/runlog.py` — verify `HOME` re-resolution; add small accessor only if cached at module load (slice 1)
196	
197	To create:
198	- `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` — chosen approach + rejected alternatives + revisit conditions (slice 1)
199	- `tests/integration/conftest.py` — harness fixture, `real_claude` marker config
200	- `tests/integration/mock_linear.py` — FastAPI GraphQL server + shared in-memory store + fault injection
201	- `tests/integration/linear_mcp_shim.py` — stdio MCP server backed by the shared store
202	- `tests/integration/prompts/*.md` — issue-body prompts (happy_path, exit_without_done, verbose, wait, etc.)
203	- `tests/integration/test_happy_path.py`, `test_pre_spawn_halts.py`, `test_real_claude_halts.py`, `test_breach.py`, `test_cycle_halts.py`, `test_resume.py`, `test_usage_tracking.py`, `test_linear_edges.py`, `test_linear_surface_audit.py`, `test_subprocess_smoke.py` — scenarios
204	
205	To reuse from existing tests:
206	- Real git-repo setup pattern in `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22-90`
207	- Linear fixture style in `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65-70`
208	- `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`
209	
210	## Verification
211	
212	End-to-end:
213	- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -v` — non-`real_claude` scenarios green; no real Linear network calls; no real `LINEAR_API_KEY` leaking in. Runs per commit.
214	- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -m real_claude -v` — full suite green against a real Anthropic API key. Runs nightly; documented in CI or run-instructions.
215	- `uv run pytest --cov=drain_cycle/orchestrator --cov=drain_cycle/worker --cov=drain_cycle/runlog tests/integration/` — coverage on orchestrator, worker, and runlog rises meaningfully.
216	- Each slice closes per `AGENTS.md`: review → fix → commit + push to main → Linear summary comment → Done.
217	
218	**Outcome-metric gate** (enforces the *Success criterion*):
219	- `test_halt_coverage` greps `mark_error` slugs from `drain_cycle/**.py`, collects the union of `exception.slug` attributes (or `halt_reason` strings) observed across the full integration run (including `real_claude`), and asserts equality. Fails when a new halt slug lands in production code without a scenario.
220	
221	Negative checks during build:
222	- `grep -r "api.linear.app" tests/` returns nothing — no accidental real-Linear calls in fixtures.
223	- The mock's call log (parent GraphQL + child MCP) records every operation a scenario expects; an unexpected call fails the test loudly.
224	- `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler.
225	- Cost budget guard: nightly job tracks total Anthropic spend per run and alerts if it exceeds $5 (catch a runaway loop scenario early).
226	
```

```json
  "old_string": "The drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); \"retry\" here means **resume** — a re-invocation against the same cycle.\n\n### Success criterion (outcome, not output)\n\nThe suite works when **every halt path in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` and every cycle-halt path in `orchestrator._run` has at least one integration scenario, and a new halt path cannot merge without one**. A CI gate enforces this (see *Verification*): it greps the production code for `mark_error` slugs, compares against the union of `exception.slug` and `halt_reason` strings observed in the test run, and fails on any gap. Behavioural outcome: time-to-reproduce a future halt-logic bug drops from hours (build a real cycle) to minutes (write a 20-line scenario).\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. **`shape-design-doc`** — this plan is the artefact; promote to `docs/integration-test-architecture.md` (or an ADR) if more reviewers need it.\n2. **`shape-planning-and-task-breakdown`** — convert the *Slices* section into Linear issues on a fresh initiative under the existing project.\n3. **`agent-skills:test-driven-development`** during build — each slice ships a failing scenario before the supporting infra.\n4. **`agent-skills:code-review-and-quality`** per slice before commit, per `AGENTS.md`.",
  "new_string": "The drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); \"retry\" here means **resume** — a re-invocation against the same cycle.\n\n### Success criteria (outcomes)\n\n- Core happy-path scenarios (dependency-ordered drain, single-issue drain, empty cycle) each have an integration test that exercises them end-to-end and passes locally.\n- Every known per-issue halt path in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` and every cycle-level halt path in `orchestrator._run` has at least one integration scenario that triggers it and asserts the halt reason.\n- A local pytest assertion (`test_halt_coverage`) fails when production code grows a new `mark_error` slug without a covering scenario — caught before commit, not after.\n- Reproducing a future halt-logic bug takes minutes (write a short scenario) rather than hours (build a real Linear cycle).\n- Session/usage tracking (`_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates, per-issue breach detection) has predicate coverage on a real-claude drain.\n\n### Linear initiative (six-field skeleton)\n\nThis plan is the design doc for a Linear initiative. `/shape-initiative` (see *Workflow*) is the gate that creates the project and runs the six-field check; the skeleton below feeds that step.\n\n```\nGoal:           For drain-cycle (single-user, personal product), we want to make\n                failure-mode and resume behaviour cheap to test by replacing the\n                \"need a real Linear cycle\" prerequisite with an in-process mock\n                + Linear-MCP shim, while still spawning real claude.\nKey results:    1. committed — every halt slug in drain_cycle/**.py (parsed\n                   from mark_error call sites) appears in the integration run's\n                   observed halt slugs; test_halt_coverage asserts equality.\n                   baseline: 0 halt slugs covered by integration tests today\n                   target:   100% of present halt slugs covered, gap-detector green\n                   window:   by end of initiative\n                   source:   tests/integration/test_halt_coverage.py\n                2. committed — a real-claude happy-path drain of 3 dependency-\n                   ordered issues reaches Done end-to-end against MockLinear +\n                   linear_mcp_shim, with predicate assertions on usage totals\n                   green.\n                   baseline: no such test exists\n                   target:   tests/integration/test_happy_path.py passing\n                   window:   by end of initiative\n                   source:   pytest -m real_claude tests/integration/test_happy_path.py\n                3. committed — resume scenario passes: drive a mid-cycle halt,\n                   re-invoke orchestrator, observe pickup of reverted issue and\n                   completion of remainder.\n                   baseline: no such test exists\n                   target:   tests/integration/test_resume.py passing\n                   window:   by end of initiative\n                   source:   pytest -m real_claude tests/integration/test_resume.py\n                4. aspirational — default `uv run pytest tests/integration/`\n                   completes in < 60 seconds with no real Anthropic spend (pre-\n                   spawn halts only); the `real_claude` slice completes in <\n                   20 minutes and < $5.\n                   baseline: n/a\n                   target:   measured wall-clock + cost printed at suite end\n                   window:   by end of initiative\n                   source:   suite run output; manual review\nAffected repos: drain-cycle\nAppetite:       ~12 issues (one per slice; see Slices section)\nKill condition: After the slice-3 spike, the linear_mcp_shim cannot produce a\n                schema that claude accepts for save_issue / save_comment\n                without forking the official Linear MCP — at which point real-\n                claude scenarios are not feasible and the initiative pivots to\n                a hybrid (scripted child for failure paths) or is parked.\nProject type:   2 (personal product)\n```\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. **`/shape-initiative`** — create the Linear project (initiative) with this plan promoted to `docs/integration-test-architecture.md` and linked from the project description. The six-field skeleton above is the input; `/shape-initiative` runs the 11-rule verification rubric before the project is created.\n2. **`shape-planning-and-task-breakdown`** — convert the *Slices* section into Linear issues under the new initiative; assign slices 1–4 to the current cycle, 5–12 to the backlog.\n3. **`agent-skills:test-driven-development`** during build — each slice ships a failing scenario before the supporting infra.\n4. **`agent-skills:code-review-and-quality`** per slice before commit, per `AGENTS.md`."
}
```

manual review\nAffected repos: drain-cycle\nAppetite:       ~12 issues (one per slice; see Slices section)\nKill condition: After the slice-3 spike, the linear_mcp_shim cannot produce a\n                schema that claude accepts for save_issue / save_comment\n                without forking the official Linear MCP — at which point real-\n                claude scenarios are not feasible and the initiative pivots to\n                a hybrid (scripted child for failure paths) or is parked.\nProject type:   2 (personal product)\n```",

A new scenario plugs\n                   into the harness, runs against the in-process mock + Linear-\n                   MCP shim, and passes or fails fast on the developer's\n                   laptop.\n                   baseline: every failure scenario today needs a hand-built\n                             real Linear cycle and ~hours of setup\n                   target:   any new failure scenario is a ~20-line addition\n                             that runs in under a minute locally\n                   window:   by end of initiative\n                   source:   tests/integration/conftest.py + at least one\n                             example new scenario added in < 30 minutes\n\n                2. committed — Every failure path drain-cycle knows about today\n                   is covered. {
```json
  "plan": "# Integration test suite: mock Linear + scripted child\n\n## What this document is\n\nThis is the design doc for a Linear initiative. Its job is to:\n\n1. Create the Linear project (initiative) via `/shape-initiative`.\n2. Be copied into that project as the linked design doc.\n3. Be sliced — via `shape-planning-and-task-breakdown` — into Linear issues that future cycles pick up.\n\nIt is **not** an execution plan. How each slice gets built happens later, when the cycle picks that issue up.\n\n## Context\n\nToday `drain-cycle` only tests against a real Linear cycle. Anything past happy-path becomes expensive: failure modes need deliberately broken issues, resume needs an actual halt-and-restart, and usage tracking needs real Claude sessions. Unit tests cover individual modules but stop at the function boundary — they monkey-patch `linear._post` and swap `claude -p` for marker-touching shell scripts (`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22`, `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65`).\n\nWe need **scenario-level integration tests** that drive the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, covering:\n\n- **Best-case drains** — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions\n- **Per-issue failure paths** — every halt condition in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (repo resolution, worktree setup, `set_state` failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)\n- **Cycle-level halts** — `DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach propagation\n- **Resume semantics** — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo\n- **Session/usage tracking** — `_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches\n\nThe drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); \"retry\" here means **resume** — a re-invocation against the same cycle.\n\n### Linear initiative (six-field skeleton)\n\nThe skeleton below feeds `/shape-initiative` (see *Workflow*), which runs the 11-rule rubric and creates the project.\n\n```\nGoal:           Make drain-cycle's failure modes and resume behaviour cheap to\n                test, so that adding a test for a new scenario no longer needs\n                a real Linear cycle and the existing failure paths gain a\n                trusted safety net.\n\nKey results:    1. committed — Adding a test for a new drain-cycle failure mode\n                   no longer needs a real Linear cycle. A new scenario plugs\n                   into the harness, runs against the in-process mock + Linear-\n                   MCP shim, and passes or fails fast on the developer's\n                   laptop.\n                   baseline: every failure scenario today needs a hand-built\n                             real Linear cycle and ~hours of setup\n                   target:   any new failure scenario is a ~20-line addition\n                             that runs in under a minute locally\n                   window:   by end of initiative\n                   source:   tests/integration/conftest.py + at least one\n                             example new scenario added in < 30 minutes\n\n                2. committed — Every failure path drain-cycle knows about today\n                   is covered. The suite triggers each one, confirms the right\n                   halt reason, and refuses to let a new untested failure mode\n                   slip into production.\n                   baseline: no failure paths covered by integration tests\n                   target:   every present halt path covered; the halt-coverage\n                             check is green and fails loudly on a gap\n                   window:   by end of initiative\n                   source:   tests/integration/test_halt_coverage.py\n\n                3. committed — A full real-claude happy-path drain works end-\n                   to-end against the mock, exercising usage tracking, cycle\n                   reporting, and resume-after-halt under realistic\n                   conditions.\n                   baseline: happy-path, resume, and usage tracking only\n                             validated by manual smoke runs against real\n                             Linear\n                   target:   the real-claude scenarios for happy-path, resume,\n                             and usage tracking all pass\n                   window:   by end of initiative\n                   source:   pytest -m real_claude tests/integration/ -v\n\nAffected repos: drain-cycle\n\nAppetite:       ~12 issues (one per slice; see Slices section)\n\nKill condition: After the linear_mcp_shim spike, the shim cannot produce a\n                schema that claude accepts for save_issue / save_comment\n                without forking the official Linear MCP — at which point the\n                real-claude scenarios are not feasible and the initiative\n                pivots to a hybrid (scripted child for failure paths) or\n                parks.\n\nProject type:   2 (personal product)\n```\n\nThe three Key Results above are the success criteria for the initiative. The rest of this document explains *how* the work is shaped to deliver them.\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. **`/shape-initiative`** — create the Linear project (initiative) with this plan promoted to `docs/integration-test-architecture.md` and linked from the project description. The six-field skeleton above is the input; `/shape-initiative` runs the 11-rule verification rubric before the project is created.\n2. **`shape-planning-and-task-breakdown`** — convert the *Slices* section into Linear issues under the new initiative; assign slices 1–4 to the current cycle, 5–12 to the backlog.\n3. **`agent-skills:test-driven-development`** during build — each slice ships a failing scenario before the supporting infra.\n4. **`agent-skills:code-review-and-quality`** per slice before commit, per `AGENTS.md`.\n\n## Approach\n\n**Parent HTTP mock + Linear-MCP shim + real `claude -p` + in-process driver.** The spawned session is real claude every time; only the Linear surface is mocked. Three pillars.\n\n**Alternatives rejected** (draft for the ADR slice):\n\n- **Scripted/fake `claude -p`.** Cheaper, faster, deterministic. Rejected because a scripted child stops being an integration test — it cannot validate the real session's usage stream, MCP tool calls, prompt-to-completion behaviour, or production OTel spans against actual data.\n- **Hybrid: real claude only on happy-path; scripted child for failure determinism.** Two patterns to maintain; weaker default signal; gives up real-claude coverage on usage-tracking edge cases.\n- **Function-injection / `LinearClient` protocol on the parent.** Cheaper than HTTP but skips the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. The existing module-level functions in `linear.py` drive easily against an HTTP fake once `LINEAR_API_URL` is overridable.\n- **VCR-style recorded fixtures.** Realistic but awkward for failure injection, expensive to maintain under schema drift.\n\nSlice 1 produces a short ADR at `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, these rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.\n\n### Cost, runtime, and determinism\n\nReal claude makes the suite costly, slow, and non-deterministic on numbers.\n\n- **Cost / runtime budget**: ~$0.05–$0.20 per spawning scenario, ~15–30 seconds per scenario. Full suite of ~30 scenarios: ~$1–$3 and ~15 minutes. Run on demand, not as part of the default suite.\n- **Default `pytest` run** covers only the pre-spawn halt scenarios (no claude spawned) — fast and deterministic, suitable for repeated local invocation while iterating.\n- **Determinism strategy** — split assertions:\n  - **Exact** — exit codes, final Linear state, halt reason class, runlog file presence, structural shape of runlog JSON, set of MCP tool calls observed.\n  - **Predicate** — `tokens > 0`, `0 < cost_usd < per_issue_cost_usd`, `num_turns >= 1`, `model is not None`. No exact values.\n  - **Tolerant snapshot** — `dirty-equals` matches keys and types, ignores values.\n- **Cost guard**: scenarios that spawn claude carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude`. Default `pytest` invocation excludes the marker; the `real_claude` slice opts in via `pytest -m real_claude` and runs on demand. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` required.\n\n### Pillar 1 — `MockLinear`: an in-process fake GraphQL server\n\nA FastAPI/Starlette app on a random localhost port, started per-test via a pytest fixture. It speaks the five GraphQL operations `linear.py` uses (`current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state` / WorkflowState lookup) plus an `IssueUpdate` mutation the scripted child hits to flip an issue to Done.\n\nState lives in a typed in-memory store: `Cycle`, `Issue`, `Label`, `WorkflowState`. The store records every call (operation, variables, timestamp) so tests can assert call sequence and counts.\n\nFault injection is **per-operation, per-call-index**: `mock.fail_after(op=\"set_state\", call=2, mode=\"http_500\")`. Modes: `http_500`, `http_429`, `graphql_error`, `malformed_json`, `slow_response(seconds)`, `connection_reset`. The drain has no retry, so \"fail\" means \"this single call breaks\" — tests then verify the halt path.\n\n**Production change required**: `linear.py:18` hard-codes `https://api.linear.app/graphql`. Replace with `os.environ.get(\"LINEAR_API_URL\", \"https://api.linear.app/graphql\")`. No CLI flag — env var only.\n\n### Pillar 2 — `linear_mcp_shim`: a Linear MCP server backed by the mock\n\nA small Python stdio MCP server (built on the official `mcp` SDK) that exposes the two tools the drain-cycle prompt uses today: `save_issue` (state transitions) and `save_comment`. Each tool translates its call into a GraphQL POST against `MockLinear`, sharing the same backing store as the parent's HTTP surface.\n\nThe harness writes a custom `.mcp.json` into each test worktree registering the shim under the name `linear` (matching the existing tool prefix `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*`). The orchestrator already symlinks `.mcp.json` from the configured source into the worktree (`orchestrator.py:257-259`); the harness arranges for the test source to be the shim-configured file.\n\nThe shim records every tool call (tool name, arguments, timestamp) on the shared store, so scenarios assert against both parent GraphQL calls and child MCP calls through one call log.\n\n**Why an MCP shim and not just `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child env.** MCP is a separate protocol from raw GraphQL; the official Linear MCP server speaks MCP over stdio to claude and HTTPS to Linear. Setting `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child has no effect — there's no way to redirect the official server without forking it. A purpose-built shim is the smallest seam.\n\n### How scenarios steer claude\n\nScenarios drive child behaviour through three knobs the harness sets per-scenario:\n\n- **Issue body** — written into `MockLinear` and read by claude via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__get_issue`. The body is the natural prompt seam (e.g. \"create `<id>.txt` containing 'hello'; then mark this issue Done\"). Library lives in `tests/integration/prompts/*.md`.\n- **Per-issue limits** — `limits.yml` overrides (e.g. `per_issue_tokens=2000`, `per_issue_seconds=30`) used with verbose prompts to provoke breach scenarios deterministically.\n- **Claude flags** — the harness may inject `--max-turns 1` for \"exit without Done\" cases (claude exits before completing the workflow), via the `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `orchestrator.py:25`.\n\nThe drain-cycle prompt (`drain_cycle/prompt.py:39-42`) stays unchanged — same prompt the real drain uses.\n\n### Pillar 3 — `IntegrationHarness`: a fixture that drives `orchestrator.run()` in-process\n\nA pytest fixture that:\n\n- Creates a temp `HOME` so `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`, `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`, and `~/.drain-cycle/runs/` redirect to a tmpdir (`monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", ...)` plus any path-resolution helpers in `cli.py` / `runlog.py`).\n- Initialises one or more real git repos in tmpdir (existing pattern in `test_orchestrator_iteration.py`).\n- Writes a `repos.yml` mapping label names to those repo paths.\n- Writes a `.mcp.json` in each repo registering the Linear-MCP shim under `linear`.\n- Starts `MockLinear` and populates it with the scenario's cycle, issues (with prompt body), and labels.\n- Sets `LINEAR_API_URL`, `LINEAR_API_KEY=test`, `MOCK_LINEAR_URL=http://localhost:<port>`, `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` env vars. Verifies `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set for scenarios that spawn claude.\n- Optionally appends extra flags to `_CLAUDE_CMD` (e.g. `--max-turns 1`) for scenarios that need to coerce claude's behaviour.\n- Calls `orchestrator.run(repos, limits)` directly and returns a `Result`: exit code, runlog JSON, parent GraphQL call log, child MCP call log, final mock state.\n\nTests assert against `Result`. One scenario per `test_*` function. Helpers (`make_cycle`, `make_issue`, `expect_runlog`, `expect_calls`) keep scenarios short.\n\n**Per-scenario contract.** Every scenario declares:\n\n1. **Setup** — `Cycle`, `Issue[]`, `Label[]`, `WorkflowState[]`, repos.yml mapping, limits.yml overrides, transcript fixture name(s), any fault injection.\n2. **Expected exit code** — orchestrator return.\n3. **Expected runlog assertions** — a list of (a) structural shape (keys, types) matched via `dirty-equals`, (b) predicate assertions on numeric fields (`tokens > 0`, `cost_usd < limits.per_issue_cost_usd`), (c) exact assertions on deterministic fields (`final_linear_state`, `halt_reason`).\n4. **Expected final mock state** — issue states the mock should end in (e.g. `{ \"ABA-1\": \"Done\", \"ABA-2\": \"Todo\" }` after a halt on issue 2).\n5. **Expected call sequence** — ordered `(surface, operation, predicate-on-args)` tuples across both parent GraphQL and child MCP calls. Strict by default; `expect_calls(strict=False)` for tolerant cases where claude's tool-call order varies between runs.\n\nA collection-time lint test fails any scenario missing (3), (4), or (5).\n\n## Production code changes required\n\nMinimal. Every test-only seam reuses existing structure.\n\n1. **`drain_cycle/linear.py:18`** — `_GRAPHQL_URL` reads from `LINEAR_API_URL` env var, default unchanged. One-line change.\n2. **`drain_cycle/runlog.py`** — verify the run-log directory honours `HOME` re-resolution; slice 1 greps for `Path.home`, `expanduser`, and `HOME`. ## Approved Plan (edited by user):
```
```
# Integration test suite: mock Linear + scripted child

## What this document is

This is the design doc for a Linear initiative. Its job is to:

1. Create the Linear project (initiative) via `/shape-initiative`.
2. Be copied into that project as the linked design doc.
3. Be sliced — via `shape-planning-and-task-breakdown` — into Linear issues that future cycles pick up.

It is **not** an execution plan. How each slice gets built happens later, when the cycle picks that issue up.

## Context

Today `drain-cycle` only tests against a real Linear cycle. Anything past happy-path becomes expensive: failure modes need deliberately broken issues, resume needs an actual halt-and-restart, and usage tracking needs real Claude sessions. Unit tests cover individual modules but stop at the function boundary — they monkey-patch `linear._post` and swap `claude -p` for marker-touching shell scripts (`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22`, `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65`).

We need **scenario-level integration tests** that drive the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, covering:

- **Best-case drains** — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions
- **Per-issue failure paths** — every halt condition in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (repo resolution, worktree setup, `set_state` failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)
- **Cycle-level halts** — `DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach propagation
- **Resume semantics** — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo
- **Session/usage tracking** — `_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches

The drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); "retry" here means **resume** — a re-invocation against the same cycle.

### Linear initiative (six-field skeleton)

The skeleton below feeds `/shape-initiative` (see *Workflow*), which runs the 11-rule rubric and creates the project.

```
Goal:           Make drain-cycle's failure modes and resume behaviour cheap to
                test, so that adding a test for a new scenario no longer needs
                a real Linear cycle and the existing failure paths gain a
                trusted safety net.

Key results:    1. committed — Adding a test for a new drain-cycle failure mode
                   no longer needs a real Linear cycle. A new scenario plugs
                   into the harness, runs against the in-process mock + Linear-
                   MCP shim, and passes or fails fast on the developer's
                   laptop.
                   baseline: every failure scenario today needs a hand-built
                             real Linear cycle and ~hours of setup
                   target:   any new failure scenario is a ~20-line addition
                             that runs in under a minute locally
                   window:   by end of initiative
                   source:   tests/integration/conftest.py + at least one
                             example new scenario added in < 30 minutes

                2. committed — Every failure path drain-cycle knows about today
                   is covered. The suite triggers each one, confirms the right
                   halt reason, and refuses to let a new untested failure mode
                   slip into production.
                   baseline: no failure paths covered by integration tests
                   target:   every present halt path covered; the halt-coverage
                             check is green and fails loudly on a gap
                   window:   by end of initiative
                   source:   tests/integration/test_halt_coverage.py

                3. committed — A full real-claude happy-path drain works end-
                   to-end against the mock, exercising usage tracking, cycle
                   reporting, and resume-after-halt under realistic
                   conditions.
                   baseline: happy-path, resume, and usage tracking only
                             validated by manual smoke runs against real
                             Linear
                   target:   the real-claude scenarios for happy-path, resume,
                             and usage tracking all pass
                   window:   by end of initiative
                   source:   pytest -m real_claude tests/integration/ -v

Affected repos: drain-cycle

Appetite:       ~12 issues (one per slice; see Slices section)

Kill condition: After the linear_mcp_shim spike, the shim cannot produce a
                schema that claude accepts for save_issue / save_comment
                without forking the official Linear MCP — at which point the
                real-claude scenarios are not feasible and the initiative
                pivots to a hybrid (scripted child for failure paths) or
                parks.

Project type:   2 (personal product)
```

The three Key Results above are the success criteria for the initiative. The rest of this document explains *how* the work is shaped to deliver them.

## Workflow

1. **`/shape-initiative`** — create the Linear project (initiative) with this plan promoted to `docs/integration-test-architecture.md` and linked from the project description. The six-field skeleton above is the input; `/shape-initiative` runs the 11-rule verification rubric before the project is created.
2. **`shape-planning-and-task-breakdown`** — convert the *Slices* section into Linear issues under the new initiative; assign slices 1–4 to the current cycle, 5–12 to the backlog.
3. **`agent-skills:test-driven-development`** during build — each slice ships a failing scenario before the supporting infra.
4. **`agent-skills:code-review-and-quality`** per slice before commit, per `AGENTS.md`.

## Approach

**Parent HTTP mock + Linear-MCP shim + real `claude -p` + in-process driver.** The spawned session is real claude every time; only the Linear surface is mocked. Three pillars.

**Alternatives rejected** (draft for the ADR slice):

- **Scripted/fake `claude -p`.** Cheaper, faster, deterministic. Rejected because a scripted child stops being an integration test — it cannot validate the real session's usage stream, MCP tool calls, prompt-to-completion behaviour, or production OTel spans against actual data.
- **Hybrid: real claude only on happy-path; scripted child for failure determinism.** Two patterns to maintain; weaker default signal; gives up real-claude coverage on usage-tracking edge cases.
- **Function-injection / `LinearClient` protocol on the parent.** Cheaper than HTTP but skips the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. The existing module-level functions in `linear.py` drive easily against an HTTP fake once `LINEAR_API_URL` is overridable.
- **VCR-style recorded fixtures.** Realistic but awkward for failure injection, expensive to maintain under schema drift.

Slice 1 produces a short ADR at `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, these rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.

### Cost, runtime, and determinism

Real claude makes the suite costly, slow, and non-deterministic on numbers.

- **Cost / runtime budget**: ~$0.05–$0.20 per spawning scenario, ~15–30 seconds per scenario. Full suite of ~30 scenarios: ~$1–$3 and ~15 minutes. Run on demand, not as part of the default suite.
- **Default `pytest` run** covers only the pre-spawn halt scenarios (no claude spawned) — fast and deterministic, suitable for repeated local invocation while iterating.
- **Determinism strategy** — split assertions:
  - **Exact** — exit codes, final Linear state, halt reason class, runlog file presence, structural shape of runlog JSON, set of MCP tool calls observed.
  - **Predicate** — `tokens > 0`, `0 < cost_usd < per_issue_cost_usd`, `num_turns >= 1`, `model is not None`. No exact values.
  - **Tolerant snapshot** — `dirty-equals` matches keys and types, ignores values.
- **Cost guard**: scenarios that spawn claude carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude`. Default `pytest` invocation excludes the marker; the `real_claude` slice opts in via `pytest -m real_claude` and runs on demand. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` required.

### Pillar 1 — `MockLinear`: an in-process fake GraphQL server

A FastAPI/Starlette app on a random localhost port, started per-test via a pytest fixture. It speaks the five GraphQL operations `linear.py` uses (`current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state` / WorkflowState lookup) plus an `IssueUpdate` mutation the scripted child hits to flip an issue to Done.

State lives in a typed in-memory store: `Cycle`, `Issue`, `Label`, `WorkflowState`. The store records every call (operation, variables, timestamp) so tests can assert call sequence and counts.

Fault injection is **per-operation, per-call-index**: `mock.fail_after(op="set_state", call=2, mode="http_500")`. Modes: `http_500`, `http_429`, `graphql_error`, `malformed_json`, `slow_response(seconds)`, `connection_reset`. The drain has no retry, so "fail" means "this single call breaks" — tests then verify the halt path.

**Production change required**: `linear.py:18` hard-codes `https://api.linear.app/graphql`. Replace with `os.environ.get("LINEAR_API_URL", "https://api.linear.app/graphql")`. No CLI flag — env var only.

### Pillar 2 — `linear_mcp_shim`: a Linear MCP server backed by the mock

A small Python stdio MCP server (built on the official `mcp` SDK) that exposes the two tools the drain-cycle prompt uses today: `save_issue` (state transitions) and `save_comment`. Each tool translates its call into a GraphQL POST against `MockLinear`, sharing the same backing store as the parent's HTTP surface.

The harness writes a custom `.mcp.json` into each test worktree registering the shim under the name `linear` (matching the existing tool prefix `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*`). The orchestrator already symlinks `.mcp.json` from the configured source into the worktree (`orchestrator.py:257-259`); the harness arranges for the test source to be the shim-configured file.

The shim records every tool call (tool name, arguments, timestamp) on the shared store, so scenarios assert against both parent GraphQL calls and child MCP calls through one call log.

**Why an MCP shim and not just `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child env.** MCP is a separate protocol from raw GraphQL; the official Linear MCP server speaks MCP over stdio to claude and HTTPS to Linear. Setting `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child has no effect — there's no way to redirect the official server without forking it. A purpose-built shim is the smallest seam.

### How scenarios steer claude

Scenarios drive child behaviour through three knobs the harness sets per-scenario:

- **Issue body** — written into `MockLinear` and read by claude via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__get_issue`. The body is the natural prompt seam (e.g. "create `<id>.txt` containing 'hello'; then mark this issue Done"). Library lives in `tests/integration/prompts/*.md`.
- **Per-issue limits** — `limits.yml` overrides (e.g. `per_issue_tokens=2000`, `per_issue_seconds=30`) used with verbose prompts to provoke breach scenarios deterministically.
- **Claude flags** — the harness may inject `--max-turns 1` for "exit without Done" cases (claude exits before completing the workflow), via the `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `orchestrator.py:25`.

The drain-cycle prompt (`drain_cycle/prompt.py:39-42`) stays unchanged — same prompt the real drain uses.

### Pillar 3 — `IntegrationHarness`: a fixture that drives `orchestrator.run()` in-process

A pytest fixture that:

- Creates a temp `HOME` so `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`, `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`, and `~/.drain-cycle/runs/` redirect to a tmpdir (`monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)` plus any path-resolution helpers in `cli.py` / `runlog.py`).
- Initialises one or more real git repos in tmpdir (existing pattern in `test_orchestrator_iteration.py`).
- Writes a `repos.yml` mapping label names to those repo paths.
- Writes a `.mcp.json` in each repo registering the Linear-MCP shim under `linear`.
- Starts `MockLinear` and populates it with the scenario's cycle, issues (with prompt body), and labels.
- Sets `LINEAR_API_URL`, `LINEAR_API_KEY=test`, `MOCK_LINEAR_URL=http://localhost:<port>`, `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` env vars. Verifies `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set for scenarios that spawn claude.
- Optionally appends extra flags to `_CLAUDE_CMD` (e.g. `--max-turns 1`) for scenarios that need to coerce claude's behaviour.
- Calls `orchestrator.run(repos, limits)` directly and returns a `Result`: exit code, runlog JSON, parent GraphQL call log, child MCP call log, final mock state.

Tests assert against `Result`. One scenario per `test_*` function. Helpers (`make_cycle`, `make_issue`, `expect_runlog`, `expect_calls`) keep scenarios short.

**Per-scenario contract.** Every scenario declares:

1. **Setup** — `Cycle`, `Issue[]`, `Label[]`, `WorkflowState[]`, repos.yml mapping, limits.yml overrides, transcript fixture name(s), any fault injection.
2. **Expected exit code** — orchestrator return.
3. **Expected runlog assertions** — a list of (a) structural shape (keys, types) matched via `dirty-equals`, (b) predicate assertions on numeric fields (`tokens > 0`, `cost_usd < limits.per_issue_cost_usd`), (c) exact assertions on deterministic fields (`final_linear_state`, `halt_reason`).
4. **Expected final mock state** — issue states the mock should end in (e.g. `{ "ABA-1": "Done", "ABA-2": "Todo" }` after a halt on issue 2).
5. **Expected call sequence** — ordered `(surface, operation, predicate-on-args)` tuples across both parent GraphQL and child MCP calls. Strict by default; `expect_calls(strict=False)` for tolerant cases where claude's tool-call order varies between runs.

A collection-time lint test fails any scenario missing (3), (4), or (5).

## Production code changes required

Minimal. Every test-only seam reuses existing structure.

1. **`drain_cycle/linear.py:18`** — `_GRAPHQL_URL` reads from `LINEAR_API_URL` env var, default unchanged. One-line change.
2. **`drain_cycle/runlog.py`** — verify the run-log directory honours `HOME` re-resolution; slice 1 greps for `Path.home`, `expanduser`, and `HOME`. If the path caches at module load, add an accessor (e.g. `_run_log_dir()`) so the harness re-resolves after `monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)`.
3. **`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`** — `_CLAUDE_CMD` is already module-level (verified by Explore). Tests patch it.

No new abstraction layers, no `LinearClient` Protocol — the existing module-level functions drive easily against an HTTP fake once the URL is configurable.

## Test-infra correctness checks (one-time pre-slice work)

Risks the plan-review surfaced. Each lands before dependent slices.

- **Telemetry isolation** — drain-cycle wires httpx OTel auto-instrumentation in `telemetry.py`. The harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` to keep tests from exporting spans. Confirmed in slice 2.
- **MCP-shim correctness** — the shim must implement the same tool schemas the official Linear MCP exposes for `save_issue` and `save_comment`, otherwise claude's tool calls land with wrong shapes. Slice 3 spike: capture the official schemas (from the Linear MCP docs or by running the real one briefly), implement matching ones in the shim, verify a single happy-path drain reaches Done end-to-end. **Kill-switch**: if the schemas drift between Linear MCP versions, pin the shim to a captured snapshot and add a periodic check.
- **Breach scenario reproducibility** — with real claude, breach scenarios depend on prompts that reliably exceed tight per-issue caps. Slice 8 spike: confirm a verbose prompt (e.g. "write a 2000-line poem") plus `per_issue_tokens=1000` reliably triggers token breach across 5 consecutive runs. **Kill-switch**: if not reliable, fall back to time-breach only (tight `per_issue_seconds` plus a "sleep 60s" prompt is robust because the breach loop polls wall-clock).

## Scenario catalogue (initial coverage)

Grouped by area; one test function each. Target end-state, not a single slice — slices below build the infra and add scenarios incrementally.

Scenarios that spawn `claude -p` carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude` (excluded from the default `pytest` run; opt-in via `-m real_claude`). Scenarios that halt pre-spawn run on every default invocation.

**Best case** *(real_claude)*
- 3-issue dependency-ordered drain reaches Done in order; runlog totals positive and sum across issues; child MCP calls include `save_issue` (transition to Done) and `save_comment` (summary) per issue
- Mixed Todo + Backlog states picked up; correct planning
- Single-issue drain
- Empty cycle exits cleanly *(no claude spawned)*

**Per-issue halts** (`orchestrator._drain_one_issue` paths)
- Missing `repo:` label → halt + reason *(no claude spawned)*
- `repo:` label resolves to nonexistent path → halt *(no claude spawned)*
- Worktree `add` fails (dirty tree) → halt *(no claude spawned)*
- `set_state` (Todo→In-Progress) fails with HTTP 500 → halt *(no claude spawned)*
- Child exits without setting Done *(real_claude; coerced via `--max-turns 1`)* → halt + reason matches `final_linear_state`
- Child exits non-zero *(real_claude; provoked by an invalid prompt or a tool that errors)* → halt
- Per-issue token cap exceeded mid-stream *(real_claude; verbose prompt + `per_issue_tokens=1000`)* → breach kill; runlog records breach scope/metric/limit/observed
- Per-issue time cap exceeded *(real_claude; prompt "wait 60s" + `per_issue_seconds=10`)* → time breach recorded
- Revert `set_state` (In-Progress→Todo) fails after a halt → halt-reason concatenates *(real_claude or pre-spawn)*

**Cycle-level halts**
- `DependencyCycleError` from `pending_issues` → halt before any issue attempted; `cycle_halt_reason` set; runlog otherwise empty *(no claude spawned)*
- After two Done issues, cycle-cap breach detected → third issue not attempted; halt reason names cumulative metric *(real_claude)*

**Resume** *(real_claude)*
- Run halts on issue 2; re-run with the same cycle picks up issue 2 (mock has it back at Todo via the revert) and issue 3; second runlog file separate from first; final mock state correct

**Session / usage tracking** *(real_claude; predicate assertions on numbers)*
- Single-issue drain produces `usage.cumulative.input_tokens > 0`, `usage.cumulative.output_tokens > 0`, `peak_context > 0`, `cost_usd > 0`, `num_turns >= 1`, `session_id` non-empty, `is_error == false`, `model` non-empty
- Multi-issue drain: cycle aggregates (`cycle_tokens_cumulative`, `cycle_cost_usd`, `cycle_duration_seconds`) equal the sum of per-issue values
- Worker dedup invariant (`worker.py:416`) verified indirectly: re-emit a non-deterministic prompt and confirm `usage.cumulative` is monotonically non-decreasing within a turn; explicit dedup behaviour stays unit-tested

**Linear edge cases** *(no claude spawned — parent halts before pre-spawn `set_state` succeeds)*
- 500, 429, GraphQL error, malformed JSON, slow-within-timeout (drain proceeds), slow-beyond-timeout (halts)

**Out of scope for MVP**
- Honeycomb/OTel span assertions (separate slice — needs a fake OTLP collector; production code wires spans cleanly per `drain_cycle/telemetry.py`)
- True MCP-server fake for children (scripted-child + direct GraphQL POST satisfies the contract)

**In MVP, bounded** — slice 4 ships one subprocess smoke test that spawns the real `drain-cycle` binary against the mock and asserts exit code plus runlog presence. This single test covers `cli.py`'s secrets-load order and process exit-code path. Larger subprocess scenarios stay out of scope.

**Note on the worker `_UsageAccumulator` dedup invariant** — the worker dedupes by `message.id` (`worker.py:416`). Real claude rarely emits duplicate ids in practice, so the dedup branch is exercised by existing unit tests, not by integration scenarios. Plan accordingly: keep the dedup unit test (`test_usage_accumulator_dedup.py` or equivalent); the integration suite asserts on monotonicity, not on dedup directly.

## Slices (verifiable build order)

Each slice ships in one session and ends with a passing scenario.

1. **Production seam + ADR** — `LINEAR_API_URL` env var in `linear.py`; unit test confirms override; default unchanged. Runlog `HOME` re-resolution verified (grep + accessor if needed). **Deliverable**: `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.
2. **`MockLinear` skeleton** — FastAPI app with `current_cycle` and `pending_issues` only; serves a hard-coded scenario; one unit test wires through `linear.py` and confirms the call lands on the mock. Harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true`.
3. **`linear_mcp_shim` + first real-claude drain** — stdio MCP server implementing `save_issue` and `save_comment` against the mock; harness writes a `.mcp.json` registering it; first **real-claude** happy-path drain of one issue reaches Done end-to-end. Schemas captured from the official Linear MCP. Marked `@pytest.mark.real_claude`.
4. **`IntegrationHarness` fixture + subprocess smoke** — tmpdir HOME, repos.yml, real git repo, `.mcp.json` writer, mock startup. Slice 3's scenario refactored onto the harness. **Plus**: one subprocess test spawns `drain-cycle` against the mock end-to-end and asserts exit code plus runlog file existence.
5. **Mock GraphQL completeness + drift guard** — add `get_issue`, `set_state`, WorkflowState resolution; happy-path drain of 3 ordered issues passes. **Plus**: `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler.
6. **Fault injection** — per-operation, per-call-index fault modes in the mock; scenario covers `set_state` HTTP 500 halt (pre-spawn, fast). Per-scenario contract enforced (runlog assertions, mock state, call sequence).
7. **Pre-spawn halt scenarios** — repo-resolution failures, worktree-add failure, set_state 500. Fast, no claude.
8. **Real-claude halt scenarios** — child exits without Done (via `--max-turns 1`); child non-zero exit (via invalid prompt). Marked `real_claude`.
9. **Breach scenarios** — time-breach first (tight `per_issue_seconds` plus "wait 60s" prompt — robust). Token-breach gated on the slice-8 reproducibility spike. Both `real_claude`.
10. **Cycle-level halt scenarios** — `DependencyCycleError` (no claude); cycle-cap breach after two Done issues (`real_claude`).
11. **Resume scenario** — drive a halt, re-invoke orchestrator on the same mock cycle, assert second runlog file and final state. `real_claude`.
12. **Usage-tracking + Linear edge-case scenarios** — usage predicates on a happy-path drain (`real_claude`); 429 / malformed / slow-timeout halts on the parent (no claude).

Slices 1–4 are the foundation; 5–12 are independent and ship in any order once 4 lands. Each slice opens a Linear issue under the existing project and closes per `AGENTS.md` (review → fix → commit + push → Linear summary comment → Done).

## Critical files

To modify:
- `drain_cycle/linear.py` — env-var override for GraphQL URL (slice 1)
- `drain_cycle/runlog.py` — verify `HOME` re-resolution; add small accessor only if cached at module load (slice 1)

To create:
- `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` — chosen approach + rejected alternatives + revisit conditions (slice 1)
- `tests/integration/conftest.py` — harness fixture, `real_claude` marker config
- `tests/integration/mock_linear.py` — FastAPI GraphQL server + shared in-memory store + fault injection
- `tests/integration/linear_mcp_shim.py` — stdio MCP server backed by the shared store
- `tests/integration/prompts/*.md` — issue-body prompts (happy_path, exit_without_done, verbose, wait, etc.)
- `tests/integration/test_happy_path.py`, `test_pre_spawn_halts.py`, `test_real_claude_halts.py`, `test_breach.py`, `test_cycle_halts.py`, `test_resume.py`, `test_usage_tracking.py`, `test_linear_edges.py`, `test_linear_surface_audit.py`, `test_subprocess_smoke.py` — scenarios

To reuse from existing tests:
- Real git-repo setup pattern in `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22-90`
- Linear fixture style in `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65-70`
- `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`

## Verification

End-to-end:
- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -v` — non-`real_claude` scenarios green; no real Linear network calls; no real `LINEAR_API_KEY` leaking in. The default local invocation.
- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -m real_claude -v` — full suite green against a real Anthropic API key. Run on demand; instructions live in `tests/integration/README.md`.
- `uv run pytest --cov=drain_cycle/orchestrator --cov=drain_cycle/worker --cov=drain_cycle/runlog tests/integration/` — coverage on orchestrator, worker, and runlog rises meaningfully.
- Each slice closes per `AGENTS.md`: review → fix → commit + push to main → Linear summary comment → Done.

**Halt-coverage assertion** (enforces KR 2):
- `test_halt_coverage` greps `mark_error` slugs from `drain_cycle/**.py`, collects the union of `exception.slug` attributes (or `halt_reason` strings) observed across the full integration run (including `real_claude`), and asserts equality. Fails locally when a new halt slug lands in production code without a covering scenario.

Negative checks during build:
- `grep -r "api.linear.app" tests/` returns nothing — no accidental real-Linear calls in fixtures.
- The mock's call log (parent GraphQL + child MCP) records every operation a scenario expects; an unexpected call fails the test loudly.
- `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler.
- Cost budget guard: the `real_claude` suite prints total Anthropic spend at the end of each run; manual review flags runs over $5 (catch a runaway loop scenario early).

```

The skill's referenced files (`references/initiative-types.md`, `kr-quality-templates.md`) aren't shipped — the SKILL.md body has the Type 2 defaults inline, so I'll work from those.

**Type 2 (personal product) default KR mix**: correctness (lagging) + no-silent-failure (committed) + maintenance-burden (committed). The plan's three KRs map cleanly to that mix:

- KR1 (cheap to add tests) → **maintenance-burden** dimension
- KR2 (halt-coverage gate) → **discipline / no-silent-failure** dimension
- KR3 (real-claude end-to-end) → **correctness** dimension

Below is the shaped draft with `(commit)` tags, `bet/foundation/bet` roles, grader-backed `how we'll know` fields, and Layer 1/2 audit footers. I also propose tightening the project **name** — the current title (`Integration test suite: mock Linear + scripted child`) opens with the solution; rubric rule 3 prefers a goal/problem framing.

**Proposed project name**: `Test drain-cycle failure modes and resume without a real Linear cycle`

**Goal:** For the drain-cycle operator (single-user, personal product), make failure modes and resume behaviour cheap to test, so that adding a test for a new scenario no longer needs a real Linear cycle and the existing failure paths gain a trusted safety net.

**Key results:**

**KR1 (commit)** — Adding a test for a new drain-cycle failure mode no longer needs a real Linear cycle. A new scenario plugs into the harness, runs against the in-process mock + Linear-MCP shim, and passes or fails fast on the developer's laptop. *(bet)*
- baseline: every failure scenario today needs a hand-built real Linear cycle and ~hours of setup
- target: a fresh failure scenario added on top of the merged harness is ≤ 30 lines of test code and runs to a verdict in under 60 seconds locally
- measured over: by cycle close, demonstrated on one example scenario added after slice 4 ships
- how we'll know: `uv run pytest tests/integration/<new_scenario>.py` exits 0 in < 60s; `git diff --stat` on the new scenario shows ≤ 30 lines added; the example scenario lives at `tests/integration/test_example_new_scenario.py`

*Layer 1: maintenance · Layer 2: structural cap + revisit gate*

**KR2 (commit)** — Every failure path drain-cycle knows about today is covered. The suite triggers each one, confirms the right halt reason, and refuses to let a new untested failure mode slip into production. *(foundation)*
- baseline: 0 of the present `mark_error` slugs in `drain_cycle/**.py` have a covering integration scenario
- target: 100% of present halt slugs have a covering scenario; `test_halt_coverage` fails loudly on any new uncovered slug
- measured over: by cycle close, and continuously thereafter on every suite run
- how we'll know: `uv run pytest tests/integration/test_halt_coverage.py` is green; the same command run after deliberately adding an uncovered `mark_error("synthetic_halt", ...)` to `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` returns a failing test that names the missing slug

*Layer 1: discipline · Layer 2: artefact-exists + sections-complete*

**KR3 (commit)** — A real-claude happy-path drain works end-to-end against the mock, exercising usage tracking, cycle reporting, and resume-after-halt under realistic conditions. *(bet)*
- baseline: happy-path, resume, and usage tracking are only validated by manual smoke runs against real Linear
- target: real-claude scenarios for happy-path, resume, and usage tracking all pass against `MockLinear` + `linear_mcp_shim` with predicate assertions on usage totals green
- measured over: by cycle close
- how we'll know: `uv run pytest -m real_claude tests/integration/test_happy_path.py tests/integration/test_resume.py tests/integration/test_usage_tracking.py -v` exits 0; the runlog under tmpdir contains non-zero token / cost totals; the resume scenario produces a distinct second runlog file

*Layer 1: correctness · Layer 2: golden-path test*

*Dimensions: maintenance / discipline / correctness — all distinct per rule 5b.*

**Affected repos:** drain-cycle

**Appetite:** ~12 issues (one per slice; foundation slices 1–4, independent slices 5–12)

**Kill condition:** After the `linear_mcp_shim` spike (slice 3), the shim cannot produce a schema that claude accepts for `save_issue` / `save_comment` without forking the official Linear MCP — at which point real-claude scenarios are not feasible and the initiative either pivots to a hybrid (scripted child for failure paths) or parks.

**Project type:** 2 (personal product)

the same command run after deliberately adding an uncovered `mark_error(\"synthetic_halt\", ...)` to `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` returns a failing test that names the missing slug\n\n*Layer 1: discipline · Layer 2: artefact-exists + sections-complete*\n\n**KR3 (commit)** — A real-claude happy-path drain works end-to-end against the mock, exercising usage tracking, cycle reporting, and resume-after-halt under realistic conditions. *(bet)*\n- baseline: happy-path, resume, and usage tracking are only validated by manual smoke runs against real Linear\n- target: real-claude scenarios for happy-path, resume, and usage tracking all pass against `MockLinear` + `linear_mcp_shim` with predicate assertions on usage totals green\n- measured over: by cycle close\n- how we'll know: `uv run pytest -m real_claude tests/integration/test_happy_path.py tests/integration/test_resume.py tests/integration/test_usage_tracking.py -v` exits 0; the runlog under tmpdir contains non-zero token / cost totals; the resume scenario produces a distinct second runlog file\n\n*Layer 1: correctness · Layer 2: golden-path test*\n\n*Dimensions: maintenance / discipline / correctness — all distinct per rule 5b.*\n\n**Affected repos:** drain-cycle\n\n**Appetite:** ~12 issues (one per slice; foundation slices 1–4, independent slices 5–12)\n\n**Kill condition:** After the `linear_mcp_shim` spike (slice 3), the shim cannot produce a schema that claude accepts for `save_issue` / `save_comment` without forking the official Linear MCP — at which point real-claude scenarios are not feasible and the initiative either pivots to a hybrid (scripted child for failure paths) or parks.\n\n**Project type:** 2 (personal product)\n\n---\n\n**Design doc:** This project description is the OKR header. The full design doc (approach, three pillars, scenario catalogue, slices, verification) lives at `docs/integration-test-architecture.md` in the drain-cycle repo and is the input to `shape-planning-and-task-breakdown` when slicing this initiative into issues."
```json
}
```
