1	# Draft Linear initiative — multi-agent collaboration for correctness
2	
3	**Status:** draft for review (Step 5 of `initiative-shape`). Linear project NOT created yet — pending user confirmation (Step 6) and the 11-rule verification rubric (Step 6.5).
4	
5	**How to use this doc:** leave inline feedback on any section. The KRs (especially KR3) and KR1 grading details are the primary review surfaces. Section IDs in headings make it easy to reference points.
6	
7	---
8	
9	## §1 Raw idea (Step 1)
10	
11	drain-cycle evolves from "drain a Linear cycle unattended" toward a **batch/unattended driver for an ecosystem of small composable primitives** (skills + MCPs), with Linear tickets as the work queue. The operator failure mode it addresses: **single complex tickets that can't be sliced further, where the implementing agent forgets details or under-verifies**. The bet is multi-agent collaboration across three surfaces — input refinement (pre-implementation ticket critique), output verification (fresh-context critic of the diff before Done), and PR feedback loop (autonomous response to human review on Graphite stacks). Skills + MCPs are composable primitives the worker invokes; ticket-driven and manual modes share the same toolkit. Subscription auth only (Claude Code / Codex CLI). Correctness > throughput.
12	
13	**Scope of this initiative:** input refinement + output verification + the contract mechanism that binds them. PR feedback loop is **explicitly deferred** to a sibling initiative — different trigger problem (when does the agent know there's new feedback?), different blast radius (amending pushed code).
14	
15	## §2 Problem-or-solution gate (Step 2)
16	
17	**Passes.** The problem is named and specific: *"single complex tickets that can't be sliced further, where the implementing agent forgets details or under-verifies."* The three surfaces are proposed solutions; the underlying operator-impacting failure is articulated.
18	
19	## §3 Project type (Step 2.5)
20	
21	**Type 2 — personal product, single-user.** Confirmed.
22	
23	Rationale: drain-cycle is described in `AGENTS.md` as "Python, single-user, personal product"; the artefact being evolved is the product itself; any new skills/MCPs are primitives drain-cycle invokes, not the headline deliverable; one user (you), so growth/MAU KRs are categorically inapplicable per Type 2 playbook.
24	
25	## §4 Negative outcome if not solved
26	
27	Complex Linear tickets that can't be sliced further get drained today, the worker self-asserts Done on exit (per design decision §1), and the produced diff routinely misses stated AC or skips thoroughness checks. The current contract has no answer for *"the agent thought it finished but missed half the requirements."* The operator finds out at PR review, manually re-invokes claude or fixes by hand, and the unattended-drain promise breaks on exactly the tickets where it matters most.
28	
29	## §5 Affected repos
30	
31	- **`drain-cycle`** — primary and only. Worker integration, run-log schema, `flow:` label mechanism, `drain-cycle grade` extensions, supersession of design decision §1.
32	
33	Existing skills (`/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`, `/code-review-and-quality`) are reused as-is; no new skills authored in `agent-skills-shaper` as part of this initiative.
34	
35	## §6 Goal and Key results
36	
37	The KRs below are written in plain English — a non-pack-author should be able to grade either one in 30 seconds. Rubric annotations (Layer 1 / Layer 2 dimensions) live in italic audit-footers under each KR; the dimensions summary is at the end of §6.
38	
39	### §6.0 Goal
40

{
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/docs/integration-test-architecture.md",
  "content": "# Integration test architecture\n\n**Linear initiative:** [Test drain-cycle failure modes and resume without a real Linear cycle](https://linear.app/ababushkin/project/test-drain-cycle-failure-modes-and-resume-without-a-real-linear-cycle-910b954af13f)\n\nThe OKR (Goal + Key Results + appetite + kill condition) is on the Linear project. This doc is the design — what gets built, why, in what order — and is the input to `shape-planning-and-task-breakdown` when the initiative is sliced into issues.\n\n## Context\n\nToday `drain-cycle` only tests against a real Linear cycle. Anything past happy-path becomes expensive: failure modes need deliberately broken issues, resume needs an actual halt-and-restart, and usage tracking needs real Claude sessions. Unit tests cover individual modules but stop at the function boundary — they monkey-patch `linear._post` and swap `claude -p` for marker-touching shell scripts (`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22`, `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65`).\n\nWe need **scenario-level integration tests** that drive the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, covering:\n\n- **Best-case drains** — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions\n- **Per-issue failure paths** — every halt condition in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (repo resolution, worktree setup, `set_state` failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)\n- **Cycle-level halts** — `DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach propagation\n- **Resume semantics** — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo\n- **Session/usage tracking** — `_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches\n\nThe drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); \"retry\" here means **resume** — a re-invocation against the same cycle.\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. **`/shape-initiative`** — done. Linear project created with the six-field OKR (linked above).\n2. **`shape-planning-and-task-breakdown`** — convert the *Slices* section below into Linear issues under the new initiative; assign slices 1–4 to the current cycle, 5–12 to the backlog.\n3. **`agent-skills:test-driven-development`** during build — each slice ships a failing scenario before the supporting infra.\n4. **`agent-skills:code-review-and-quality`** per slice before commit, per `AGENTS.md`.\n\n## Approach\n\n**Parent HTTP mock + Linear-MCP shim + real `claude -p` + in-process driver.** The spawned session is real claude every time; only the Linear surface is mocked. Three pillars.\n\n**Alternatives rejected** (draft for the ADR slice):\n\n- **Scripted/fake `claude -p`.** Cheaper, faster, deterministic. Rejected because a scripted child stops being an integration test — it cannot validate the real session's usage stream, MCP tool calls, prompt-to-completion behaviour, or production OTel spans against actual data.\n- **Hybrid: real claude only on happy-path; scripted child for failure determinism.** Two patterns to maintain; weaker default signal; gives up real-claude coverage on usage-tracking edge cases.\n- **Function-injection / `LinearClient` protocol on the parent.** Cheaper than HTTP but skips the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. The existing module-level functions in `linear.py` drive easily against an HTTP fake once `LINEAR_API_URL` is overridable.\n- **VCR-style recorded fixtures.** Realistic but awkward for failure injection, expensive to maintain under schema drift.\n\nSlice 1 produces a short ADR at `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, these rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.\n\n### Cost, runtime, and determinism\n\nReal claude makes the suite costly, slow, and non-deterministic on numbers.\n\n- **Cost / runtime budget**: ~$0.05–$0.20 per spawning scenario, ~15–30 seconds per scenario. Full suite of ~30 scenarios: ~$1–$3 and ~15 minutes. Run on demand, not as part of the default suite.\n- **Default `pytest` run** covers only the pre-spawn halt scenarios (no claude spawned) — fast and deterministic, suitable for repeated local invocation while iterating.\n- **Determinism strategy** — split assertions:\n  - **Exact** — exit codes, final Linear state, halt reason class, runlog file presence, structural shape of runlog JSON, set of MCP tool calls observed.\n  - **Predicate** — `tokens > 0`, `0 < cost_usd < per_issue_cost_usd`, `num_turns >= 1`, `model is not None`. No exact values.\n  - **Tolerant snapshot** — `dirty-equals` matches keys and types, ignores values.\n- **Cost guard**: scenarios that spawn claude carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude`. Default `pytest` invocation excludes the marker; the `real_claude` slice opts in via `pytest -m real_claude` and runs on demand. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` required.\n\n### Pillar 1 — `MockLinear`: an in-process fake GraphQL server\n\nA FastAPI/Starlette app on a random localhost port, started per-test via a pytest fixture. It speaks the five GraphQL operations `linear.py` uses (`current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state` / WorkflowState lookup) plus an `IssueUpdate` mutation the scripted child hits to flip an issue to Done.\n\nState lives in a typed in-memory store: `Cycle`, `Issue`, `Label`, `WorkflowState`. The store records every call (operation, variables, timestamp) so tests can assert call sequence and counts.\n\nFault injection is **per-operation, per-call-index**: `mock.fail_after(op=\"set_state\", call=2, mode=\"http_500\")`. Modes: `http_500`, `http_429`, `graphql_error`, `malformed_json`, `slow_response(seconds)`, `connection_reset`. The drain has no retry, so \"fail\" means \"this single call breaks\" — tests then verify the halt path.\n\n**Production change required**: `linear.py:18` hard-codes `https://api.linear.app/graphql`. Replace with `os.environ.get(\"LINEAR_API_URL\", \"https://api.linear.app/graphql\")`. No CLI flag — env var only.\n\n### Pillar 2 — `linear_mcp_shim`: a Linear MCP server backed by the mock\n\nA small Python stdio MCP server (built on the official `mcp` SDK) that exposes the two tools the drain-cycle prompt uses today: `save_issue` (state transitions) and `save_comment`. Each tool translates its call into a GraphQL POST against `MockLinear`, sharing the same backing store as the parent's HTTP surface.\n\nThe harness writes a custom `.mcp.json` into each test worktree registering the shim under the name `linear` (matching the existing tool prefix `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*`). The orchestrator already symlinks `.mcp.json` from the configured source into the worktree (`orchestrator.py:257-259`); the harness arranges for the test source to be the shim-configured file.\n\nThe shim records every tool call (tool name, arguments, timestamp) on the shared store, so scenarios assert against both parent GraphQL calls and child MCP calls through one call log.\n\n**Why an MCP shim and not just `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child env.** MCP is a separate protocol from raw GraphQL; the official Linear MCP server speaks MCP over stdio to claude and HTTPS to Linear. Setting `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child has no effect — there's no way to redirect the official server without forking it. A purpose-built shim is the smallest seam.\n\n### How scenarios steer claude\n\nScenarios drive child behaviour through three knobs the harness sets per-scenario:\n\n- **Issue body** — written into `MockLinear` and read by claude via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__get_issue`. The body is the natural prompt seam (e.g. \"create `<id>.txt` containing 'hello'; then mark this issue Done\"). Library lives in `tests/integration/prompts/*.md`.\n- **Per-issue limits** — `limits.yml` overrides (e.g. `per_issue_tokens=2000`, `per_issue_seconds=30`) used with verbose prompts to provoke breach scenarios deterministically.\n- **Claude flags** — the harness may inject `--max-turns 1` for \"exit without Done\" cases (claude exits before completing the workflow), via the `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `orchestrator.py:25`.\n\nThe drain-cycle prompt (`drain_cycle/prompt.py:39-42`) stays unchanged — same prompt the real drain uses.\n\n### Pillar 3 — `IntegrationHarness`: a fixture that drives `orchestrator.run()` in-process\n\nA pytest fixture that:\n\n- Creates a temp `HOME` so `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`, `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`, and `~/.drain-cycle/runs/` redirect to a tmpdir (`monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", ...)` plus any path-resolution helpers in `cli.py` / `runlog.py`).\n- Initialises one or more real git repos in tmpdir (existing pattern in `test_orchestrator_iteration.py`).\n- Writes a `repos.yml` mapping label names to those repo paths.\n- Writes a `.mcp.json` in each repo registering the Linear-MCP shim under `linear`.\n- Starts `MockLinear` and populates it with the scenario's cycle, issues (with prompt body), and labels.\n- Sets `LINEAR_API_URL`, `LINEAR_API_KEY=test`, `MOCK_LINEAR_URL=http://localhost:<port>`, `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` env vars. Verifies `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set for scenarios that spawn claude.\n- Optionally appends extra flags to `_CLAUDE_CMD` (e.g. `--max-turns 1`) for scenarios that need to coerce claude's behaviour.\n- Calls `orchestrator.run(repos, limits)` directly and returns a `Result`: exit code, runlog JSON, parent GraphQL call log, child MCP call log, final mock state.\n\nTests assert against `Result`. One scenario per `test_*` function. Helpers (`make_cycle`, `make_issue`, `expect_runlog`, `expect_calls`) keep scenarios short.\n\n**Per-scenario contract.** Every scenario declares:\n\n1. **Setup** — `Cycle`, `Issue[]`, `Label[]`, `WorkflowState[]`, repos.yml mapping, limits.yml overrides, transcript fixture name(s), any fault injection.\n2. **Expected exit code** — orchestrator return.\n3. **Expected runlog assertions** — a list of (a) structural shape (keys, types) matched via `dirty-equals`, (b) predicate assertions on numeric fields (`tokens > 0`, `cost_usd < limits.per_issue_cost_usd`), (c) exact assertions on deterministic fields (`final_linear_state`, `halt_reason`).\n4. **Expected final mock state** — issue states the mock should end in (e.g. `{ \"ABA-1\": \"Done\", \"ABA-2\": \"Todo\" }` after a halt on issue 2).\n5. **Expected call sequence** — ordered `(surface, operation, predicate-on-args)` tuples across both parent GraphQL and child MCP calls. Strict by default; `expect_calls(strict=False)` for tolerant cases where claude's tool-call order varies between runs.\n\nA collection-time lint test fails any scenario missing (3), (4), or (5).\n\n## Production code changes required\n\nMinimal. Every test-only seam reuses existing structure.\n\n1. **`drain_cycle/linear.py:18`** — `_GRAPHQL_URL` reads from `LINEAR_API_URL` env var, default unchanged. One-line change.\n2. **`drain_cycle/runlog.py`** — verify the run-log directory honours `HOME` re-resolution; slice 1 greps for `Path.home`, `expanduser`, and `HOME`. If the path caches at module load, add an accessor (e.g. `_run_log_dir()`) so the harness re-resolves after `monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", ...)`.\n3. **`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`** — `_CLAUDE_CMD` is already module-level. Tests patch it.\n\nNo new abstraction layers, no `LinearClient` Protocol — the existing module-level functions drive easily against an HTTP fake once the URL is configurable.\n\n## Test-infra correctness checks (one-time pre-slice work)\n\nRisks the plan-review surfaced. Each lands before dependent slices.\n\n- **Telemetry isolation** — drain-cycle wires httpx OTel auto-instrumentation in `telemetry.py`. The harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` to keep tests from exporting spans. Confirmed in slice 2.\n- **MCP-shim correctness** — the shim must implement the same tool schemas the official Linear MCP exposes for `save_issue` and `save_comment`, otherwise claude's tool calls land with wrong shapes. Slice 3 spike: capture the official schemas (from the Linear MCP docs or by running the real one briefly), implement matching ones in the shim, verify a single happy-path drain reaches Done end-to-end. **Kill-switch**: if the schemas drift between Linear MCP versions, pin the shim to a captured snapshot and add a periodic check.\n- **Breach scenario reproducibility** — with real claude, breach scenarios depend on prompts that reliably exceed tight per-issue caps. Slice 8 spike: confirm a verbose prompt (e.g. \"write a 2000-line poem\") plus `per_issue_tokens=1000` reliably triggers token breach across 5 consecutive runs. **Kill-switch**: if not reliable, fall back to time-breach only (tight `per_issue_seconds` plus a \"sleep 60s\" prompt is robust because the breach loop polls wall-clock).\n\n## Scenario catalogue (initial coverage)\n\nGrouped by area; one test function each. Target end-state, not a single slice — slices below build the infra and add scenarios incrementally.\n\nScenarios that spawn `claude -p` carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude` (excluded from the default `pytest` run; opt-in via `-m real_claude`). Scenarios that halt pre-spawn run on every default invocation.\n\n**Best case** *(real_claude)*\n- 3-issue dependency-ordered drain reaches Done in order; runlog totals positive and sum across issues; child MCP calls include `save_issue` (transition to Done) and `save_comment` (summary) per issue\n- Mixed Todo + Backlog states picked up; correct planning\n- Single-issue drain\n- Empty cycle exits cleanly *(no claude spawned)*\n\n**Per-issue halts** (`orchestrator._drain_one_issue` paths)\n- Missing `repo:` label → halt + reason *(no claude spawned)*\n- `repo:` label resolves to nonexistent path → halt *(no claude spawned)*\n- Worktree `add` fails (dirty tree) → halt *(no claude spawned)*\n- `set_state` (Todo→In-Progress) fails with HTTP 500 → halt *(no claude spawned)*\n- Child exits without setting Done *(real_claude; coerced via `--max-turns 1`)* → halt + reason matches `final_linear_state`\n- Child exits non-zero *(real_claude; provoked by an invalid prompt or a tool that errors)* → halt\n- Per-issue token cap exceeded mid-stream *(real_claude; verbose prompt + `per_issue_tokens=1000`)* → breach kill; runlog records breach scope/metric/limit/observed\n- Per-issue time cap exceeded *(real_claude; prompt \"wait 60s\" + `per_issue_seconds=10`)* → time breach recorded\n- Revert `set_state` (In-Progress→Todo) fails after a halt → halt-reason concatenates *(real_claude or pre-spawn)*\n\n**Cycle-level halts**\n- `DependencyCycleError` from `pending_issues` → halt before any issue attempted; `cycle_halt_reason` set; runlog otherwise empty *(no claude spawned)*\n- After two Done issues, cycle-cap breach detected → third issue not attempted; halt reason names cumulative metric *(real_claude)*\n\n**Resume** *(real_claude)*\n- Run halts on issue 2; re-run with the same cycle picks up issue 2 (mock has it back at Todo via the revert) and issue 3; second runlog file separate from first; final mock state correct\n\n**Session / usage tracking** *(real_claude; predicate assertions on numbers)*\n- Single-issue drain produces `usage.cumulative.input_tokens > 0`, `usage.cumulative.output_tokens > 0`, `peak_context > 0`, `cost_usd > 0`, `num_turns >= 1`, `session_id` non-empty, `is_error == false`, `model` non-empty\n- Multi-issue drain: cycle aggregates (`cycle_tokens_cumulative`, `cycle_cost_usd`, `cycle_duration_seconds`) equal the sum of per-issue values\n- Worker dedup invariant (`worker.py:416`) verified indirectly: re-emit a non-deterministic prompt and confirm `usage.cumulative` is monotonically non-decreasing within a turn; explicit dedup behaviour stays unit-tested\n\n**Linear edge cases** *(no claude spawned — parent halts before pre-spawn `set_state` succeeds)*\n- 500, 429, GraphQL error, malformed JSON, slow-within-timeout (drain proceeds), slow-beyond-timeout (halts)\n\n**Out of scope for MVP**\n- Honeycomb/OTel span assertions (separate slice — needs a fake OTLP collector; production code wires spans cleanly per `drain_cycle/telemetry.py`)\n- True MCP-server fake for children (scripted-child + direct GraphQL POST satisfies the contract)\n\n**In MVP, bounded** — slice 4 ships one subprocess smoke test that spawns the real `drain-cycle` binary against the mock and asserts exit code plus runlog presence. This single test covers `cli.py`'s secrets-load order and process exit-code path. Larger subprocess scenarios stay out of scope.\n\n**Note on the worker `_UsageAccumulator` dedup invariant** — the worker dedupes by `message.id` (`worker.py:416`). Real claude rarely emits duplicate ids in practice, so the dedup branch is exercised by existing unit tests, not by integration scenarios. Plan accordingly: keep the dedup unit test (`test_usage_accumulator_dedup.py` or equivalent); the integration suite asserts on monotonicity, not on dedup directly.\n\n## Slices (verifiable build order)\n\nEach slice ships in one session and ends with a passing scenario.\n\n1. **Production seam + ADR** — `LINEAR_API_URL` env var in `linear.py`; unit test confirms override; default unchanged. Runlog `HOME` re-resolution verified (grep + accessor if needed). **Deliverable**: `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.\n2. **`MockLinear` skeleton** — FastAPI app with `current_cycle` and `pending_issues` only; serves a hard-coded scenario; one unit test wires through `linear.py` and confirms the call lands on the mock. Harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true`.\n3. **`linear_mcp_shim` + first real-claude drain** — stdio MCP server implementing `save_issue` and `save_comment` against the mock; harness writes a `.mcp.json` registering it; first **real-claude** happy-path drain of one issue reaches Done end-to-end. Schemas captured from the official Linear MCP. Marked `@pytest.mark.real_claude`.\n4. **`IntegrationHarness` fixture + subprocess smoke** — tmpdir HOME, repos.yml, real git repo, `.mcp.json` writer, mock startup. Slice 3's scenario refactored onto the harness. **Plus**: one subprocess test spawns `drain-cycle` against the mock end-to-end and asserts exit code plus runlog file existence.\n5. **Mock GraphQL completeness + drift guard** — add `get_issue`, `set_state`, WorkflowState resolution; happy-path drain of 3 ordered issues passes. **Plus**: `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler.\n6. **Fault injection** — per-operation, per-call-index fault modes in the mock; scenario covers `set_state` HTTP 500 halt (pre-spawn, fast). Per-scenario contract enforced (runlog assertions, mock state, call sequence).\n7. **Pre-spawn halt scenarios** — repo-resolution failures, worktree-add failure, set_state 500. Fast, no claude.\n8. **Real-claude halt scenarios** — child exits without Done (via `--max-turns 1`); child non-zero exit (via invalid prompt). Marked `real_claude`.\n9. **Breach scenarios** — time-breach first (tight `per_issue_seconds` plus \"wait 60s\" prompt — robust). Token-breach gated on the slice-8 reproducibility spike. Both `real_claude`.\n10. **Cycle-level halt scenarios** — `DependencyCycleError` (no claude); cycle-cap breach after two Done issues (`real_claude`).\n11. **Resume scenario** — drive a halt, re-invoke orchestrator on the same mock cycle, assert second runlog file and final state. `real_claude`.\n12. **Usage-tracking + Linear edge-case scenarios** — usage predicates on a happy-path drain (`real_claude`); 429 / malformed / slow-timeout halts on the parent (no claude).\n\nSlices 1–4 are the foundation; 5–12 are independent and ship in any order once 4 lands. Each slice opens a Linear issue under the initiative and closes per `AGENTS.md` (review → fix → commit + push → Linear summary comment → Done).\n\n## Critical files\n\nTo modify:\n- `drain_cycle/linear.py` — env-var override for GraphQL URL (slice 1)\n- `drain_cycle/runlog.py` — verify `HOME` re-resolution; add small accessor only if cached at module load (slice 1)\n\nTo create:\n- `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` — chosen approach + rejected alternatives + revisit conditions (slice 1)\n- `tests/integration/conftest.py` — harness fixture, `real_claude` marker config\n- `tests/integration/mock_linear.py` — FastAPI GraphQL server + shared in-memory store + fault injection\n- `tests/integration/linear_mcp_shim.py` — stdio MCP server backed by the shared store\n- `tests/integration/prompts/*.md` — issue-body prompts (happy_path, exit_without_done, verbose, wait, etc.)\n- `tests/integration/test_happy_path.py`, `test_pre_spawn_halts.py`, `test_real_claude_halts.py`, `test_breach.py`, `test_cycle_halts.py`, `test_resume.py`, `test_usage_tracking.py`, `test_linear_edges.py`, `test_linear_surface_audit.py`, `test_subprocess_smoke.py` — scenarios\n\nTo reuse from existing tests:\n- Real git-repo setup pattern in `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22-90`\n- Linear fixture style in `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65-70`\n- `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`\n\n## Verification\n\nEnd-to-end:\n- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -v` — non-`real_claude` scenarios green; no real Linear network calls; no real `LINEAR_API_KEY` leaking in. The default local invocation.\n- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -m real_claude -v` — full suite green against a real Anthropic API key. Run on demand; instructions live in `tests/integration/README.md`.\n- `uv run pytest --cov=drain_cycle/orchestrator --cov=drain_cycle/worker --cov=drain_cycle/runlog tests/integration/` — coverage on orchestrator, worker, and runlog rises meaningfully.\n- Each slice closes per `AGENTS.md`: review → fix → commit + push to main → Linear summary comment → Done.\n\n**Halt-coverage assertion** (enforces KR 2):\n- `test_halt_coverage` greps `mark_error` slugs from `drain_cycle/**.py`, collects the union of `exception.slug` attributes (or `halt_reason` strings) observed across the full integration run (including `real_claude`), and asserts equality. Fails locally when a new halt slug lands in production code without a covering scenario.\n\nNegative checks during build:\n- `grep -r \"api.linear.app\" tests/` returns nothing — no accidental real-Linear calls in fixtures.\n- The mock's call log (parent GraphQL + child MCP) records every operation a scenario expects; an unexpected call fails the test loudly.\n- `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler.\n- Cost budget guard: the `real_claude` suite prints total Anthropic spend at the end of each run; manual review flags runs over $5 (catch a runaway loop scenario early).\n"
}

> AGENT

Six visuals carry the most weight; the first three are non-negotiable, the rest are nice-to-haves. Ordered by the question they answer.

**1. System context diagram — "what's mocked, what's real"** *(tech lead)*
The single most important picture. Three boxes (parent orchestrator, MockLinear, real `claude -p` child) with two arrows from the child (MCP stdio → `linear_mcp_shim` → MockLinear; subprocess) and one arrow from the parent (GraphQL → MockLinear). Annotate the shared in-memory store. This is the visual that answers "why an MCP shim and not just `LINEAR_API_URL` in child env?" — the third-pillar paragraph in §Pillar 2 takes 200 words; the diagram takes one glance.

**2. Sequence diagram of one happy-path scenario** *(tech lead)*
Lifelines: `IntegrationHarness`, `orchestrator`, `MockLinear`, `linear_mcp_shim`, `claude -p`. Shows: setup → `pending_issues` → spawn → child reads body via MCP `get_issue` → child writes `save_issue(Done)` → harness asserts on call log + runlog. Proves the seam closes end-to-end. Replaces the prose in §How scenarios steer claude.

**3. Failure-mode coverage matrix** *(manager + tech lead)*
A table: rows = halt paths from `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` and cycle-level halts; columns = `[scenario name | spawns claude? | mark | covered?]`. This is the visual form of KR2 ("every failure path covered") and makes the halt-coverage guard tangible. A manager can scan it in 30 seconds and ask "what about X?" — exactly the review you want.

**4. Cost/runtime split** *(manager)*
Two side-by-side bars: default `pytest` run (~0 cost, seconds, runs on every save) vs `pytest -m real_claude` (~$1–$3, ~15 min, run on demand). Makes the cost-guard rule legible without a paragraph of caveats.

**5. Slice dependency graph** *(manager + tech lead)*
Slices 1→2→3→4 in a chain; 5–12 as a fan-out from 4. Shows commit shape: four foundational slices land sequentially, then eight independent slices can ship in any order. Replaces the appetite-claim prose ("~12 issues") with something a reviewer can actually push back on.

**6. Per-scenario contract card** *(team)*
A small filled-in example of the five-field contract (Setup / Exit code / Runlog assertions / Final mock state / Call sequence) for `test_set_state_500_halt`. Onboarding aid more than approval aid — but if you want the team to actually adopt the pattern post-merge, this is the one that does it.

**What I'd skip:** a class diagram of the mock store (overkill), a Gantt of slices (slices aren't calendar-boxed), an ADR decision tree (one paragraph in slice 1's ADR is enough).

**Where to put them in the doc:**
- Diagram 1 → top of *Approach*, before the three pillars are introduced.
- Diagram 2 → end of *Pillar 2*, where the shim's role gets fuzzy in prose.
- Matrix 3 → top of *Scenario catalogue*, so the catalogue reads as filling in the matrix.
- Bar 4 → *Cost, runtime, and determinism* (currently three paragraphs; could be one paragraph + the bar).
- Graph 5 → top of *Slices*.
- Card 6 → inside *Per-scenario contract*, as a worked example.

If you want, I can sketch any of these in mermaid in the design doc itself — diagrams 1, 2, and 5 render cleanly in mermaid; matrix 3 is just a table; bar 4 is honestly fine as a table too. Which ones first?

1	# Integration test suite: mock Linear + scripted child
2	
3	## What this document is
4	
5	This is the design doc for a Linear initiative. Its job is to:
6	
7	1. Create the Linear project (initiative) via `/shape-initiative`.
8	2. Be copied into that project as the linked design doc.
9	3. Be sliced — via `shape-planning-and-task-breakdown` — into Linear issues that future cycles pick up.
10	
11	It is **not** an execution plan. How each slice gets built happens later, when the cycle picks that issue up.
12	
13	## Context
14	
15	Today `drain-cycle` only tests against a real Linear cycle. Anything past happy-path becomes expensive: failure modes need deliberately broken issues, resume needs an actual halt-and-restart, and usage tracking needs real Claude sessions. Unit tests cover individual modules but stop at the function boundary — they monkey-patch `linear._post` and swap `claude -p` for marker-touching shell scripts (`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22`, `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65`).
16	
17	We need **scenario-level integration tests** that drive the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, covering:
18	
19	- **Best-case drains** — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions
20	- **Per-issue failure paths** — every halt condition in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (repo resolution, worktree setup, `set_state` failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)
21	- **Cycle-level halts** — `DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach propagation
22	- **Resume semantics** — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo
23	- **Session/usage tracking** — `_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches
24	
25	The drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); "retry" here means **resume** — a re-invocation against the same cycle.
26	
27	### Linear initiative (six-field skeleton)
28	
29	The skeleton below feeds `/shape-initiative` (see *Workflow*), which runs the 11-rule rubric and creates the project.
30	
31	```
32	Goal:           Make drain-cycle's failure modes and resume behaviour cheap to
33	                test, so that adding a test for a new scenario no longer needs
34	                a real Linear cycle and the existing failure paths gain a
35	                trusted safety net.
36	
37	Key results:    1. committed — Adding a test for a new drain-cycle failure mode
38	                   no longer needs a real Linear cycle. A new scenario plugs
39	                   into the harness, runs against the in-process mock + Linear-
40	                   MCP shim, and passes or fails fast on the developer's
41	                   laptop.
42	                   baseline: every failure scenario today needs a hand-built
43	                             real Linear cycle and ~hours of setup
44	                   target:   any new failure scenario is a ~20-line addition
45	                             that runs in under a minute locally
46	                   window:   by end of initiative
47	                   source:   tests/integration/conftest.py + at least one
48	                             example new scenario added in < 30 minutes
49	
50	                2. committed — Every failure path drain-cycle knows about today
51	                   is covered. The suite triggers each one, confirms the right
52	                   halt reason, and refuses to let a new untested failure mode
53	                   slip into production.
54	                   baseline: no failure paths covered by integration tests
55	                   target:   every present halt path covered; the halt-coverage
56	                             check is green and fails loudly on a gap
57	                   window:   by end of initiative
58	                   source:   tests/integration/test_halt_coverage.py
59	
60	                3. committed — A full real-claude happy-path drain works end-
61	                   to-end against the mock, exercising usage tracking, cycle
62	                   reporting, and resume-after-halt under realistic
63	                   conditions.
64	                   baseline: happy-path, resume, and usage tracking only
65	                             validated by manual smoke runs against real
66	                             Linear
67	                   target:   the real-claude scenarios for happy-path, resume,
68	                             and usage tracking all pass
69	                   window:   by end of initiative
70	                   source:   pytest -m real_claude tests/integration/ -v
71	
72	Affected repos: drain-cycle
73	
74	Appetite:       ~12 issues (one per slice; see Slices section)
75	
76	Kill condition: After the linear_mcp_shim spike, the shim cannot produce a
77	                schema that claude accepts for save_issue / save_comment
78	                without forking the official Linear MCP — at which point the
79	                real-claude scenarios are not feasible and the initiative
80	                pivots to a hybrid (scripted child for failure paths) or
81	                parks.
82	
83	Project type:   2 (personal product)
84	```
85	
86	The three Key Results above are the success criteria for the initiative. The rest of this document explains *how* the work is shaped to deliver them.
87	
88	## Workflow
89	
90	1. **`/shape-initiative`** — create the Linear project (initiative) with this plan promoted to `docs/integration-test-architecture.md` and linked from the project description. The six-field skeleton above is the input; `/shape-initiative` runs the 11-rule verification rubric before the project is created.
91	2. **`shape-planning-and-task-breakdown`** — convert the *Slices* section into Linear issues under the new initiative; assign slices 1–4 to the current cycle, 5–12 to the backlog.
92	3. **`agent-skills:test-driven-development`** during build — each slice ships a failing scenario before the supporting infra.
93	4. **`agent-skills:code-review-and-quality`** per slice before commit, per `AGENTS.md`.
94	
95	## Approach
96	
97	**Parent HTTP mock + Linear-MCP shim + real `claude -p` + in-process driver.** The spawned session is real claude every time; only the Linear surface is mocked. Three pillars.
98	
99	**Alternatives rejected** (draft for the ADR slice):
100	
101	- **Scripted/fake `claude -p`.** Cheaper, faster, deterministic. Rejected because a scripted child stops being an integration test — it cannot validate the real session's usage stream, MCP tool calls, prompt-to-completion behaviour, or production OTel spans against actual data.
102	- **Hybrid: real claude only on happy-path; scripted child for failure determinism.** Two patterns to maintain; weaker default signal; gives up real-claude coverage on usage-tracking edge cases.
103	- **Function-injection / `LinearClient` protocol on the parent.** Cheaper than HTTP but skips the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. The existing module-level functions in `linear.py` drive easily against an HTTP fake once `LINEAR_API_URL` is overridable.
104	- **VCR-style recorded fixtures.** Realistic but awkward for failure injection, expensive to maintain under schema drift.
105	
106	Slice 1 produces a short ADR at `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, these rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.
107	
108	### Cost, runtime, and determinism
109	
110	Real claude makes the suite costly, slow, and non-deterministic on numbers.
111	
112	- **Cost / runtime budget**: ~$0.05–$0.20 per spawning scenario, ~15–30 seconds per scenario. Full suite of ~30 scenarios: ~$1–$3 and ~15 minutes. Run on demand, not as part of the default suite.
113	- **Default `pytest` run** covers only the pre-spawn halt scenarios (no claude spawned) — fast and deterministic, suitable for repeated local invocation while iterating.
114	- **Determinism strategy** — split assertions:
115	  - **Exact** — exit codes, final Linear state, halt reason class, runlog file presence, structural shape of runlog JSON, set of MCP tool calls observed.
116	  - **Predicate** — `tokens > 0`, `0 < cost_usd < per_issue_cost_usd`, `num_turns >= 1`, `model is not None`. No exact values.
117	  - **Tolerant snapshot** — `dirty-equals` matches keys and types, ignores values.
118	- **Cost guard**: scenarios that spawn claude carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude`. Default `pytest` invocation excludes the marker; the `real_claude` slice opts in via `pytest -m real_claude` and runs on demand. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` required.
119	
120	### Pillar 1 — `MockLinear`: an in-process fake GraphQL server
121	
122	A FastAPI/Starlette app on a random localhost port, started per-test via a pytest fixture. It speaks the five GraphQL operations `linear.py` uses (`current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state` / WorkflowState lookup) plus an `IssueUpdate` mutation the scripted child hits to flip an issue to Done.
123	
124	State lives in a typed in-memory store: `Cycle`, `Issue`, `Label`, `WorkflowState`. The store records every call (operation, variables, timestamp) so tests can assert call sequence and counts.
125	
126	Fault injection is **per-operation, per-call-index**: `mock.fail_after(op="set_state", call=2, mode="http_500")`. Modes: `http_500`, `http_429`, `graphql_error`, `malformed_json`, `slow_response(seconds)`, `connection_reset`. The drain has no retry, so "fail" means "this single call breaks" — tests then verify the halt path.
127	
128	**Production change required**: `linear.py:18` hard-codes `https://api.linear.app/graphql`. Replace with `os.environ.get("LINEAR_API_URL", "https://api.linear.app/graphql")`. No CLI flag — env var only.
129	
130	### Pillar 2 — `linear_mcp_shim`: a Linear MCP server backed by the mock
131	
132	A small Python stdio MCP server (built on the official `mcp` SDK) that exposes the two tools the drain-cycle prompt uses today: `save_issue` (state transitions) and `save_comment`. Each tool translates its call into a GraphQL POST against `MockLinear`, sharing the same backing store as the parent's HTTP surface.
133	
134	The harness writes a custom `.mcp.json` into each test worktree registering the shim under the name `linear` (matching the existing tool prefix `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*`). The orchestrator already symlinks `.mcp.json` from the configured source into the worktree (`orchestrator.py:257-259`); the harness arranges for the test source to be the shim-configured file.
135	
136	The shim records every tool call (tool name, arguments, timestamp) on the shared store, so scenarios assert against both parent GraphQL calls and child MCP calls through one call log.
137	
138	**Why an MCP shim and not just `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child env.** MCP is a separate protocol from raw GraphQL; the official Linear MCP server speaks MCP over stdio to claude and HTTPS to Linear. Setting `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child has no effect — there's no way to redirect the official server without forking it. A purpose-built shim is the smallest seam.
139	
140	### How scenarios steer claude
141	
142	Scenarios drive child behaviour through three knobs the harness sets per-scenario:
143	
144	- **Issue body** — written into `MockLinear` and read by claude via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__get_issue`. The body is the natural prompt seam (e.g. "create `<id>.txt` containing 'hello'; then mark this issue Done"). Library lives in `tests/integration/prompts/*.md`.
145	- **Per-issue limits** — `limits.yml` overrides (e.g. `per_issue_tokens=2000`, `per_issue_seconds=30`) used with verbose prompts to provoke breach scenarios deterministically.
146	- **Claude flags** — the harness may inject `--max-turns 1` for "exit without Done" cases (claude exits before completing the workflow), via the `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `orchestrator.py:25`.
147	
148	The drain-cycle prompt (`drain_cycle/prompt.py:39-42`) stays unchanged — same prompt the real drain uses.
149	
150	### Pillar 3 — `IntegrationHarness`: a fixture that drives `orchestrator.run()` in-process
151	
152	A pytest fixture that:
153	
154	- Creates a temp `HOME` so `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`, `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`, and `~/.drain-cycle/runs/` redirect to a tmpdir (`monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)` plus any path-resolution helpers in `cli.py` / `runlog.py`).
155	- Initialises one or more real git repos in tmpdir (existing pattern in `test_orchestrator_iteration.py`).
156	- Writes a `repos.yml` mapping label names to those repo paths.
157	- Writes a `.mcp.json` in each repo registering the Linear-MCP shim under `linear`.
158	- Starts `MockLinear` and populates it with the scenario's cycle, issues (with prompt body), and labels.
159	- Sets `LINEAR_API_URL`, `LINEAR_API_KEY=test`, `MOCK_LINEAR_URL=http://localhost:<port>`, `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` env vars. Verifies `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set for scenarios that spawn claude.
160	- Optionally appends extra flags to `_CLAUDE_CMD` (e.g. `--max-turns 1`) for scenarios that need to coerce claude's behaviour.
161	- Calls `orchestrator.run(repos, limits)` directly and returns a `Result`: exit code, runlog JSON, parent GraphQL call log, child MCP call log, final mock state.
162	
163	Tests assert against `Result`. One scenario per `test_*` function. Helpers (`make_cycle`, `make_issue`, `expect_runlog`, `expect_calls`) keep scenarios short.
164	
165	**Per-scenario contract.** Every scenario declares:
166	
167	1. **Setup** — `Cycle`, `Issue[]`, `Label[]`, `WorkflowState[]`, repos.yml mapping, limits.yml overrides, transcript fixture name(s), any fault injection.
168	2. **Expected exit code** — orchestrator return.
169	3. **Expected runlog assertions** — a list of (a) structural shape (keys, types) matched via `dirty-equals`, (b) predicate assertions on numeric fields (`tokens > 0`, `cost_usd < limits.per_issue_cost_usd`), (c) exact assertions on deterministic fields (`final_linear_state`, `halt_reason`).
170	4. **Expected final mock state** — issue states the mock should end in (e.g. `{ "ABA-1": "Done", "ABA-2": "Todo" }` after a halt on issue 2).
171	5. **Expected call sequence** — ordered `(surface, operation, predicate-on-args)` tuples across both parent GraphQL and child MCP calls. Strict by default; `expect_calls(strict=False)` for tolerant cases where claude's tool-call order varies between runs.
172	
173	A collection-time lint test fails any scenario missing (3), (4), or (5).
174	
175	## Production code changes required
176	
177	Minimal. Every test-only seam reuses existing structure.
178	
179	1. **`drain_cycle/linear.py:18`** — `_GRAPHQL_URL` reads from `LINEAR_API_URL` env var, default unchanged. One-line change.
180	2. **`drain_cycle/runlog.py`** — verify the run-log directory honours `HOME` re-resolution; slice 1 greps for `Path.home`, `expanduser`, and `HOME`. If the path caches at module load, add an accessor (e.g. `_run_log_dir()`) so the harness re-resolves after `monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)`.
181	3. **`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`** — `_CLAUDE_CMD` is already module-level (verified by Explore). Tests patch it.
182	
183	No new abstraction layers, no `LinearClient` Protocol — the existing module-level functions drive easily against an HTTP fake once the URL is configurable.
184	
185	## Test-infra correctness checks (one-time pre-slice work)
186	
187	Risks the plan-review surfaced. Each lands before dependent slices.
188	
189	- **Telemetry isolation** — drain-cycle wires httpx OTel auto-instrumentation in `telemetry.py`. The harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` to keep tests from exporting spans. Confirmed in slice 2.
190	- **MCP-shim correctness** — the shim must implement the same tool schemas the official Linear MCP exposes for `save_issue` and `save_comment`, otherwise claude's tool calls land with wrong shapes. Slice 3 spike: capture the official schemas (from the Linear MCP docs or by running the real one briefly), implement matching ones in the shim, verify a single happy-path drain reaches Done end-to-end. **Kill-switch**: if the schemas drift between Linear MCP versions, pin the shim to a captured snapshot and add a periodic check.
191	- **Breach scenario reproducibility** — with real claude, breach scenarios depend on prompts that reliably exceed tight per-issue caps. Slice 8 spike: confirm a verbose prompt (e.g. "write a 2000-line poem") plus `per_issue_tokens=1000` reliably triggers token breach across 5 consecutive runs. **Kill-switch**: if not reliable, fall back to time-breach only (tight `per_issue_seconds` plus a "sleep 60s" prompt is robust because the breach loop polls wall-clock).
192	
193	## Scenario catalogue (initial coverage)
194	
195	Grouped by area; one test function each. Target end-state, not a single slice — slices below build the infra and add scenarios incrementally.
196	
197	Scenarios that spawn `claude -p` carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude` (excluded from the default `pytest` run; opt-in via `-m real_claude`). Scenarios that halt pre-spawn run on every default invocation.
198	
199	**Best case** *(real_claude)*
200	- 3-issue dependency-ordered drain reaches Done in order; runlog totals positive and sum across issues; child MCP calls include `save_issue` (transition to Done) and `save_comment` (summary) per issue
201	- Mixed Todo + Backlog states picked up; correct planning
202	- Single-issue drain
203	- Empty cycle exits cleanly *(no claude spawned)*
204	
205	**Per-issue halts** (`orchestrator._drain_one_issue` paths)
206	- Missing `repo:` label → halt + reason *(no claude spawned)*
207	- `repo:` label resolves to nonexistent path → halt *(no claude spawned)*
208	- Worktree `add` fails (dirty tree) → halt *(no claude spawned)*
209	- `set_state` (Todo→In-Progress) fails with HTTP 500 → halt *(no claude spawned)*
210	- Child exits without setting Done *(real_claude; coerced via `--max-turns 1`)* → halt + reason matches `final_linear_state`
211	- Child exits non-zero *(real_claude; provoked by an invalid prompt or a tool that errors)* → halt
212	- Per-issue token cap exceeded mid-stream *(real_claude; verbose prompt + `per_issue_tokens=1000`)* → breach kill; runlog records breach scope/metric/limit/observed
213	- Per-issue time cap exceeded *(real_claude; prompt "wait 60s" + `per_issue_seconds=10`)* → time breach recorded
214	- Revert `set_state` (In-Progress→Todo) fails after a halt → halt-reason concatenates *(real_claude or pre-spawn)*
215	
216	**Cycle-level halts**
217	- `DependencyCycleError` from `pending_issues` → halt before any issue attempted; `cycle_halt_reason` set; runlog otherwise empty *(no claude spawned)*
218	- After two Done issues, cycle-cap breach detected → third issue not attempted; halt reason names cumulative metric *(real_claude)*
219	
220	**Resume** *(real_claude)*
221	- Run halts on issue 2; re-run with the same cycle picks up issue 2 (mock has it back at Todo via the revert) and issue 3; second runlog file separate from first; final mock state correct
222	
223	**Session / usage tracking** *(real_claude; predicate assertions on numbers)*
224	- Single-issue drain produces `usage.cumulative.input_tokens > 0`, `usage.cumulative.output_tokens > 0`, `peak_context > 0`, `cost_usd > 0`, `num_turns >= 1`, `session_id` non-empty, `is_error == false`, `model` non-empty
225	- Multi-issue drain: cycle aggregates (`cycle_tokens_cumulative`, `cycle_cost_usd`, `cycle_duration_seconds`) equal the sum of per-issue values
226	- Worker dedup invariant (`worker.py:416`) verified indirectly: re-emit a non-deterministic prompt and confirm `usage.cumulative` is monotonically non-decreasing within a turn; explicit dedup behaviour stays unit-tested
227	
228	**Linear edge cases** *(no claude spawned — parent halts before pre-spawn `set_state` succeeds)*
229	- 500, 429, GraphQL error, malformed JSON, slow-within-timeout (drain proceeds), slow-beyond-timeout (halts)
230	
231	**Out of scope for MVP**
232	- Honeycomb/OTel span assertions (separate slice — needs a fake OTLP collector; production code wires spans cleanly per `drain_cycle/telemetry.py`)
233	- True MCP-server fake for children (scripted-child + direct GraphQL POST satisfies the contract)
234	
235	**In MVP, bounded** — slice 4 ships one subprocess smoke test that spawns the real `drain-cycle` binary against the mock and asserts exit code plus runlog presence. This single test covers `cli.py`'s secrets-load order and process exit-code path. Larger subprocess scenarios stay out of scope.
236	
237	**Note on the worker `_UsageAccumulator` dedup invariant** — the worker dedupes by `message.id` (`worker.py:416`). Real claude rarely emits duplicate ids in practice, so the dedup branch is exercised by existing unit tests, not by integration scenarios. Plan accordingly: keep the dedup unit test (`test_usage_accumulator_dedup.py` or equivalent); the integration suite asserts on monotonicity, not on dedup directly.
238	
239	## Slices (verifiable build order)
240	
241	Each slice ships in one session and ends with a passing scenario.
242	
243	1. **Production seam + ADR** — `LINEAR_API_URL` env var in `linear.py`; unit test confirms override; default unchanged. Runlog `HOME` re-resolution verified (grep + accessor if needed). **Deliverable**: `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.
244	2. **`MockLinear` skeleton** — FastAPI app with `current_cycle` and `pending_issues` only; serves a hard-coded scenario; one unit test wires through `linear.py` and confirms the call lands on the mock. Harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true`.
245	3. **`linear_mcp_shim` + first real-claude drain** — stdio MCP server implementing `save_issue` and `save_comment` against the mock; harness writes a `.mcp.json` registering it; first **real-claude** happy-path drain of one issue reaches Done end-to-end. Schemas captured from the official Linear MCP. Marked `@pytest.mark.real_claude`.
246	4. **`IntegrationHarness` fixture + subprocess smoke** — tmpdir HOME, repos.yml, real git repo, `.mcp.json` writer, mock startup. Slice 3's scenario refactored onto the harness. **Plus**: one subprocess test spawns `drain-cycle` against the mock end-to-end and asserts exit code plus runlog file existence.
247	5. **Mock GraphQL completeness + drift guard** — add `get_issue`, `set_state`, WorkflowState resolution; happy-path drain of 3 ordered issues passes. **Plus**: `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler.
248	6. **Fault injection** — per-operation, per-call-index fault modes in the mock; scenario covers `set_state` HTTP 500 halt (pre-spawn, fast). Per-scenario contract enforced (runlog assertions, mock state, call sequence).
249	7. **Pre-spawn halt scenarios** — repo-resolution failures, worktree-add failure, set_state 500. Fast, no claude.
250	8. **Real-claude halt scenarios** — child exits without Done (via `--max-turns 1`); child non-zero exit (via invalid prompt). Marked `real_claude`.
251	9. **Breach scenarios** — time-breach first (tight `per_issue_seconds` plus "wait 60s" prompt — robust). Token-breach gated on the slice-8 reproducibility spike. Both `real_claude`.
252	10. **Cycle-level halt scenarios** — `DependencyCycleError` (no claude); cycle-cap breach after two Done issues (`real_claude`).
253	11. **Resume scenario** — drive a halt, re-invoke orchestrator on the same mock cycle, assert second runlog file and final state. `real_claude`.
254	12. **Usage-tracking + Linear edge-case scenarios** — usage predicates on a happy-path drain (`real_claude`); 429 / malformed / slow-timeout halts on the parent (no claude).
255	
256	Slices 1–4 are the foundation; 5–12 are independent and ship in any order once 4 lands. Each slice opens a Linear issue under the existing project and closes per `AGENTS.md` (review → fix → commit + push → Linear summary comment → Done).
257

277	## Verification
278	
279	End-to-end:
280	- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -v` — non-`real_claude` scenarios green; no real Linear network calls; no real `LINEAR_API_KEY` leaking in. The default local invocation.
281	- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -m real_claude -v` — full suite green against a real Anthropic API key. Run on demand; instructions live in `tests/integration/README.md`.
282	- `uv run pytest --cov=drain_cycle/orchestrator --cov=drain_cycle/worker --cov=drain_cycle/runlog tests/integration/` — coverage on orchestrator, worker, and runlog rises meaningfully.
283	- Each slice closes per `AGENTS.md`: review → fix → commit + push to main → Linear summary comment → Done.
284	
285	**Halt-coverage assertion** (enforces KR 2):
286	- `test_halt_coverage` greps `mark_error` slugs from `drain_cycle/**.py`, collects the union of `exception.slug` attributes (or `halt_reason` strings) observed across the full integration run (including `real_claude`), and asserts equality. Fails locally when a new halt slug lands in production code without a covering scenario.
287	
288	Negative checks during build:
289	- `grep -r "api.linear.app" tests/` returns nothing — no accidental real-Linear calls in fixtures.
290	- The mock's call log (parent GraphQL + child MCP) records every operation a scenario expects; an unexpected call fails the test loudly.
291	- `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler.
292	- Cost budget guard: the `real_claude` suite prints total Anthropic spend at the end of each run; manual review flags runs over $5 (catch a runaway loop scenario early).

1	# Integration test architecture
2	
3	**Linear initiative:** [Test drain-cycle failure modes and resume without a real Linear cycle](https://linear.app/ababushkin/project/test-drain-cycle-failure-modes-and-resume-without-a-real-linear-cycle-910b954af13f)
4	
5	The OKR (Goal + Key Results + appetite + kill condition) is on the Linear project. This doc is the design — what gets built, why, in what order — and is the input to `shape-planning-and-task-breakdown` when the initiative is sliced into issues.
6	
7	## Context
8	
9	Today `drain-cycle` only tests against a real Linear cycle. Anything past happy-path becomes expensive: failure modes need deliberately broken issues, resume needs an actual halt-and-restart, and usage tracking needs real Claude sessions. Unit tests cover individual modules but stop at the function boundary — they monkey-patch `linear._post` and swap `claude -p` for marker-touching shell scripts (`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22`, `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65`).
10	
11	We need **scenario-level integration tests** that drive the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, covering:
12	
13	- **Best-case drains** — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions
14	- **Per-issue failure paths** — every halt condition in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (repo resolution, worktree setup, `set_state` failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)
15	- **Cycle-level halts** — `DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach propagation
16	- **Resume semantics** — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo
17	- **Session/usage tracking** — `_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches
18	
19	The drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); "retry" here means **resume** — a re-invocation against the same cycle.
20	
21	## Workflow
22	
23	1. **`/shape-initiative`** — done. Linear project created with the six-field OKR (linked above).
24	2. **`shape-planning-and-task-breakdown`** — convert the *Slices* section below into Linear issues under the new initiative; assign slices 1–4 to the current cycle, 5–12 to the backlog.
25	3. **`agent-skills:test-driven-development`** during build — each slice ships a failing scenario before the supporting infra.
26	4. **`agent-skills:code-review-and-quality`** per slice before commit, per `AGENTS.md`.
27	
28	## Approach
29	
30	**Parent HTTP mock + Linear-MCP shim + real `claude -p` + in-process driver.** The spawned session is real claude every time; only the Linear surface is mocked. Three pillars.
31	
32	**Alternatives rejected** (draft for the ADR slice):
33	
34	- **Scripted/fake `claude -p`.** Cheaper, faster, deterministic. Rejected because a scripted child stops being an integration test — it cannot validate the real session's usage stream, MCP tool calls, prompt-to-completion behaviour, or production OTel spans against actual data.
35	- **Hybrid: real claude only on happy-path; scripted child for failure determinism.** Two patterns to maintain; weaker default signal; gives up real-claude coverage on usage-tracking edge cases.
36	- **Function-injection / `LinearClient` protocol on the parent.** Cheaper than HTTP but skips the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. The existing module-level functions in `linear.py` drive easily against an HTTP fake once `LINEAR_API_URL` is overridable.
37	- **VCR-style recorded fixtures.** Realistic but awkward for failure injection, expensive to maintain under schema drift.
38	
39	Slice 1 produces a short ADR at `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, these rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.
40	
41	### Cost, runtime, and determinism
42	
43	Real claude makes the suite costly, slow, and non-deterministic on numbers.
44	
45	- **Cost / runtime budget**: ~$0.05–$0.20 per spawning scenario, ~15–30 seconds per scenario. Full suite of ~30 scenarios: ~$1–$3 and ~15 minutes. Run on demand, not as part of the default suite.
46	- **Default `pytest` run** covers only the pre-spawn halt scenarios (no claude spawned) — fast and deterministic, suitable for repeated local invocation while iterating.
47	- **Determinism strategy** — split assertions:
48	  - **Exact** — exit codes, final Linear state, halt reason class, runlog file presence, structural shape of runlog JSON, set of MCP tool calls observed.
49	  - **Predicate** — `tokens > 0`, `0 < cost_usd < per_issue_cost_usd`, `num_turns >= 1`, `model is not None`. No exact values.
50	  - **Tolerant snapshot** — `dirty-equals` matches keys and types, ignores values.
51	- **Cost guard**: scenarios that spawn claude carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude`. Default `pytest` invocation excludes the marker; the `real_claude` slice opts in via `pytest -m real_claude` and runs on demand. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` required.
52	
53	### Pillar 1 — `MockLinear`: an in-process fake GraphQL server
54	
55	A FastAPI/Starlette app on a random localhost port, started per-test via a pytest fixture. It speaks the five GraphQL operations `linear.py` uses (`current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state` / WorkflowState lookup) plus an `IssueUpdate` mutation the scripted child hits to flip an issue to Done.
56	
57	State lives in a typed in-memory store: `Cycle`, `Issue`, `Label`, `WorkflowState`. The store records every call (operation, variables, timestamp) so tests can assert call sequence and counts.
58	
59	Fault injection is **per-operation, per-call-index**: `mock.fail_after(op="set_state", call=2, mode="http_500")`. Modes: `http_500`, `http_429`, `graphql_error`, `malformed_json`, `slow_response(seconds)`, `connection_reset`. The drain has no retry, so "fail" means "this single call breaks" — tests then verify the halt path.
60	
61	**Production change required**: `linear.py:18` hard-codes `https://api.linear.app/graphql`. Replace with `os.environ.get("LINEAR_API_URL", "https://api.linear.app/graphql")`. No CLI flag — env var only.
62	
63	### Pillar 2 — `linear_mcp_shim`: a Linear MCP server backed by the mock
64	
65	A small Python stdio MCP server (built on the official `mcp` SDK) that exposes the two tools the drain-cycle prompt uses today: `save_issue` (state transitions) and `save_comment`. Each tool translates its call into a GraphQL POST against `MockLinear`, sharing the same backing store as the parent's HTTP surface.
66	
67	The harness writes a custom `.mcp.json` into each test worktree registering the shim under the name `linear` (matching the existing tool prefix `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*`). The orchestrator already symlinks `.mcp.json` from the configured source into the worktree (`orchestrator.py:257-259`); the harness arranges for the test source to be the shim-configured file.
68	
69	The shim records every tool call (tool name, arguments, timestamp) on the shared store, so scenarios assert against both parent GraphQL calls and child MCP calls through one call log.
70	
71	**Why an MCP shim and not just `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child env.** MCP is a separate protocol from raw GraphQL; the official Linear MCP server speaks MCP over stdio to claude and HTTPS to Linear. Setting `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child has no effect — there's no way to redirect the official server without forking it. A purpose-built shim is the smallest seam.
72	
73	### How scenarios steer claude
74	
75	Scenarios drive child behaviour through three knobs the harness sets per-scenario:
76	
77	- **Issue body** — written into `MockLinear` and read by claude via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__get_issue`. The body is the natural prompt seam (e.g. "create `<id>.txt` containing 'hello'; then mark this issue Done"). Library lives in `tests/integration/prompts/*.md`.
78	- **Per-issue limits** — `limits.yml` overrides (e.g. `per_issue_tokens=2000`, `per_issue_seconds=30`) used with verbose prompts to provoke breach scenarios deterministically.
79	- **Claude flags** — the harness may inject `--max-turns 1` for "exit without Done" cases (claude exits before completing the workflow), via the `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `orchestrator.py:25`.
80	
81	The drain-cycle prompt (`drain_cycle/prompt.py:39-42`) stays unchanged — same prompt the real drain uses.
82	
83	### Pillar 3 — `IntegrationHarness`: a fixture that drives `orchestrator.run()` in-process
84	
85	A pytest fixture that:
86	
87	- Creates a temp `HOME` so `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`, `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`, and `~/.drain-cycle/runs/` redirect to a tmpdir (`monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)` plus any path-resolution helpers in `cli.py` / `runlog.py`).
88	- Initialises one or more real git repos in tmpdir (existing pattern in `test_orchestrator_iteration.py`).
89	- Writes a `repos.yml` mapping label names to those repo paths.
90	- Writes a `.mcp.json` in each repo registering the Linear-MCP shim under `linear`.
91	- Starts `MockLinear` and populates it with the scenario's cycle, issues (with prompt body), and labels.
92	- Sets `LINEAR_API_URL`, `LINEAR_API_KEY=test`, `MOCK_LINEAR_URL=http://localhost:<port>`, `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` env vars. Verifies `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set for scenarios that spawn claude.
93	- Optionally appends extra flags to `_CLAUDE_CMD` (e.g. `--max-turns 1`) for scenarios that need to coerce claude's behaviour.
94	- Calls `orchestrator.run(repos, limits)` directly and returns a `Result`: exit code, runlog JSON, parent GraphQL call log, child MCP call log, final mock state.
95	
96	Tests assert against `Result`. One scenario per `test_*` function. Helpers (`make_cycle`, `make_issue`, `expect_runlog`, `expect_calls`) keep scenarios short.
97	
98	**Per-scenario contract.** Every scenario declares:
99	
100	1. **Setup** — `Cycle`, `Issue[]`, `Label[]`, `WorkflowState[]`, repos.yml mapping, limits.yml overrides, transcript fixture name(s), any fault injection.
101	2. **Expected exit code** — orchestrator return.
102	3. **Expected runlog assertions** — a list of (a) structural shape (keys, types) matched via `dirty-equals`, (b) predicate assertions on numeric fields (`tokens > 0`, `cost_usd < limits.per_issue_cost_usd`), (c) exact assertions on deterministic fields (`final_linear_state`, `halt_reason`).
103	4. **Expected final mock state** — issue states the mock should end in (e.g. `{ "ABA-1": "Done", "ABA-2": "Todo" }` after a halt on issue 2).
104	5. **Expected call sequence** — ordered `(surface, operation, predicate-on-args)` tuples across both parent GraphQL and child MCP calls. Strict by default; `expect_calls(strict=False)` for tolerant cases where claude's tool-call order varies between runs.
105	
106	A collection-time lint test fails any scenario missing (3), (4), or (5).
107	
108	## Production code changes required
109	
110	Minimal. Every test-only seam reuses existing structure.
111	
112	1. **`drain_cycle/linear.py:18`** — `_GRAPHQL_URL` reads from `LINEAR_API_URL` env var, default unchanged. One-line change.
113	2. **`drain_cycle/runlog.py`** — verify the run-log directory honours `HOME` re-resolution; slice 1 greps for `Path.home`, `expanduser`, and `HOME`. If the path caches at module load, add an accessor (e.g. `_run_log_dir()`) so the harness re-resolves after `monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)`.
114	3. **`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`** — `_CLAUDE_CMD` is already module-level. Tests patch it.
115	
116	No new abstraction layers, no `LinearClient` Protocol — the existing module-level functions drive easily against an HTTP fake once the URL is configurable.
117	
118	## Test-infra correctness checks (one-time pre-slice work)
119	
120	Risks the plan-review surfaced. Each lands before dependent slices.
121	
122	- **Telemetry isolation** — drain-cycle wires httpx OTel auto-instrumentation in `telemetry.py`. The harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` to keep tests from exporting spans. Confirmed in slice 2.
123	- **MCP-shim correctness** — the shim must implement the same tool schemas the official Linear MCP exposes for `save_issue` and `save_comment`, otherwise claude's tool calls land with wrong shapes. Slice 3 spike: capture the official schemas (from the Linear MCP docs or by running the real one briefly), implement matching ones in the shim, verify a single happy-path drain reaches Done end-to-end. **Kill-switch**: if the schemas drift between Linear MCP versions, pin the shim to a captured snapshot and add a periodic check.
124	- **Breach scenario reproducibility** — with real claude, breach scenarios depend on prompts that reliably exceed tight per-issue caps. Slice 8 spike: confirm a verbose prompt (e.g. "write a 2000-line poem") plus `per_issue_tokens=1000` reliably triggers token breach across 5 consecutive runs. **Kill-switch**: if not reliable, fall back to time-breach only (tight `per_issue_seconds` plus a "sleep 60s" prompt is robust because the breach loop polls wall-clock).
125	
126	## Scenario catalogue (initial coverage)
127	
128	Grouped by area; one test function each. Target end-state, not a single slice — slices below build the infra and add scenarios incrementally.
129	
130	Scenarios that spawn `claude -p` carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude` (excluded from the default `pytest` run; opt-in via `-m real_claude`). Scenarios that halt pre-spawn run on every default invocation.
131	
132	**Best case** *(real_claude)*
133	- 3-issue dependency-ordered drain reaches Done in order; runlog totals positive and sum across issues; child MCP calls include `save_issue` (transition to Done) and `save_comment` (summary) per issue
134	- Mixed Todo + Backlog states picked up; correct planning
135	- Single-issue drain
136	- Empty cycle exits cleanly *(no claude spawned)*
137	
138	**Per-issue halts** (`orchestrator._drain_one_issue` paths)
139	- Missing `repo:` label → halt + reason *(no claude spawned)*
140	- `repo:` label resolves to nonexistent path → halt *(no claude spawned)*
141	- Worktree `add` fails (dirty tree) → halt *(no claude spawned)*
142	- `set_state` (Todo→In-Progress) fails with HTTP 500 → halt *(no claude spawned)*
143	- Child exits without setting Done *(real_claude; coerced via `--max-turns 1`)* → halt + reason matches `final_linear_state`
144	- Child exits non-zero *(real_claude; provoked by an invalid prompt or a tool that errors)* → halt
145	- Per-issue token cap exceeded mid-stream *(real_claude; verbose prompt + `per_issue_tokens=1000`)* → breach kill; runlog records breach scope/metric/limit/observed
146	- Per-issue time cap exceeded *(real_claude; prompt "wait 60s" + `per_issue_seconds=10`)* → time breach recorded
147	- Revert `set_state` (In-Progress→Todo) fails after a halt → halt-reason concatenates *(real_claude or pre-spawn)*
148	
149	**Cycle-level halts**
150	- `DependencyCycleError` from `pending_issues` → halt before any issue attempted; `cycle_halt_reason` set; runlog otherwise empty *(no claude spawned)*
151	- After two Done issues, cycle-cap breach detected → third issue not attempted; halt reason names cumulative metric *(real_claude)*
152	
153	**Resume** *(real_claude)*
154	- Run halts on issue 2; re-run with the same cycle picks up issue 2 (mock has it back at Todo via the revert) and issue 3; second runlog file separate from first; final mock state correct
155	
156	**Session / usage tracking** *(real_claude; predicate assertions on numbers)*
157	- Single-issue drain produces `usage.cumulative.input_tokens > 0`, `usage.cumulative.output_tokens > 0`, `peak_context > 0`, `cost_usd > 0`, `num_turns >= 1`, `session_id` non-empty, `is_error == false`, `model` non-empty
158	- Multi-issue drain: cycle aggregates (`cycle_tokens_cumulative`, `cycle_cost_usd`, `cycle_duration_seconds`) equal the sum of per-issue values
159	- Worker dedup invariant (`worker.py:416`) verified indirectly: re-emit a non-deterministic prompt and confirm `usage.cumulative` is monotonically non-decreasing within a turn; explicit dedup behaviour stays unit-tested
160	
161	**Linear edge cases** *(no claude spawned — parent halts before pre-spawn `set_state` succeeds)*
162	- 500, 429, GraphQL error, malformed JSON, slow-within-timeout (drain proceeds), slow-beyond-timeout (halts)
163	
164	**Out of scope for MVP**
165	- Honeycomb/OTel span assertions (separate slice — needs a fake OTLP collector; production code wires spans cleanly per `drain_cycle/telemetry.py`)
166	- True MCP-server fake for children (scripted-child + direct GraphQL POST satisfies the contract)
167	
168	**In MVP, bounded** — slice 4 ships one subprocess smoke test that spawns the real `drain-cycle` binary against the mock and asserts exit code plus runlog presence. This single test covers `cli.py`'s secrets-load order and process exit-code path. Larger subprocess scenarios stay out of scope.
169	
170	**Note on the worker `_UsageAccumulator` dedup invariant** — the worker dedupes by `message.id` (`worker.py:416`). Real claude rarely emits duplicate ids in practice, so the dedup branch is exercised by existing unit tests, not by integration scenarios. Plan accordingly: keep the dedup unit test (`test_usage_accumulator_dedup.py` or equivalent); the integration suite asserts on monotonicity, not on dedup directly.
171	
172	## Slices (verifiable build order)
173	
174	Each slice ships in one session and ends with a passing scenario.
175	
176	1. **Production seam + ADR** — `LINEAR_API_URL` env var in `linear.py`; unit test confirms override; default unchanged. Runlog `HOME` re-resolution verified (grep + accessor if needed). **Deliverable**: `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.
177	2. **`MockLinear` skeleton** — FastAPI app with `current_cycle` and `pending_issues` only; serves a hard-coded scenario; one unit test wires through `linear.py` and confirms the call lands on the mock. Harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true`.
178	3. **`linear_mcp_shim` + first real-claude drain** — stdio MCP server implementing `save_issue` and `save_comment` against the mock; harness writes a `.mcp.json` registering it; first **real-claude** happy-path drain of one issue reaches Done end-to-end. Schemas captured from the official Linear MCP. Marked `@pytest.mark.real_claude`.
179	4. **`IntegrationHarness` fixture + subprocess smoke** — tmpdir HOME, repos.yml, real git repo, `.mcp.json` writer, mock startup. Slice 3's scenario refactored onto the harness. **Plus**: one subprocess test spawns `drain-cycle` against the mock end-to-end and asserts exit code plus runlog file existence.
180	5. **Mock GraphQL completeness + drift guard** — add `get_issue`, `set_state`, WorkflowState resolution; happy-path drain of 3 ordered issues passes. **Plus**: `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler.
181	6. **Fault injection** — per-operation, per-call-index fault modes in the mock; scenario covers `set_state` HTTP 500 halt (pre-spawn, fast). Per-scenario contract enforced (runlog assertions, mock state, call sequence).
182	7. **Pre-spawn halt scenarios** — repo-resolution failures, worktree-add failure, set_state 500. Fast, no claude.
183	8. **Real-claude halt scenarios** — child exits without Done (via `--max-turns 1`); child non-zero exit (via invalid prompt). Marked `real_claude`.
184	9. **Breach scenarios** — time-breach first (tight `per_issue_seconds` plus "wait 60s" prompt — robust). Token-breach gated on the slice-8 reproducibility spike. Both `real_claude`.
185	10. **Cycle-level halt scenarios** — `DependencyCycleError` (no claude); cycle-cap breach after two Done issues (`real_claude`).
186	11. **Resume scenario** — drive a halt, re-invoke orchestrator on the same mock cycle, assert second runlog file and final state. `real_claude`.
187	12. **Usage-tracking + Linear edge-case scenarios** — usage predicates on a happy-path drain (`real_claude`); 429 / malformed / slow-timeout halts on the parent (no claude).
188	
189	Slices 1–4 are the foundation; 5–12 are independent and ship in any order once 4 lands. Each slice opens a Linear issue under the initiative and closes per `AGENTS.md` (review → fix → commit + push → Linear summary comment → Done).
190	
191	## Critical files
192	
193	To modify:
194	- `drain_cycle/linear.py` — env-var override for GraphQL URL (slice 1)
195	- `drain_cycle/runlog.py` — verify `HOME` re-resolution; add small accessor only if cached at module load (slice 1)
196	
197	To create:
198	- `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` — chosen approach + rejected alternatives + revisit conditions (slice 1)
199	- `tests/integration/conftest.py` — harness fixture, `real_claude` marker config
200	- `tests/integration/mock_linear.py` — FastAPI GraphQL server + shared in-memory store + fault injection
201	- `tests/integration/linear_mcp_shim.py` — stdio MCP server backed by the shared store
202	- `tests/integration/prompts/*.md` — issue-body prompts (happy_path, exit_without_done, verbose, wait, etc.)
203	- `tests/integration/test_happy_path.py`, `test_pre_spawn_halts.py`, `test_real_claude_halts.py`, `test_breach.py`, `test_cycle_halts.py`, `test_resume.py`, `test_usage_tracking.py`, `test_linear_edges.py`, `test_linear_surface_audit.py`, `test_subprocess_smoke.py` — scenarios
204	
205	To reuse from existing tests:
206	- Real git-repo setup pattern in `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22-90`
207	- Linear fixture style in `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65-70`
208	- `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`
209	
210	## Verification
211	
212	End-to-end:
213	- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -v` — non-`real_claude` scenarios green; no real Linear network calls; no real `LINEAR_API_KEY` leaking in. The default local invocation.
214	- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -m real_claude -v` — full suite green against a real Anthropic API key. Run on demand; instructions live in `tests/integration/README.md`.
215	- `uv run pytest --cov=drain_cycle/orchestrator --cov=drain_cycle/worker --cov=drain_cycle/runlog tests/integration/` — coverage on orchestrator, worker, and runlog rises meaningfully.
216	- Each slice closes per `AGENTS.md`: review → fix → commit + push to main → Linear summary comment → Done.
217	
218	**Halt-coverage assertion** (enforces KR 2):
219	- `test_halt_coverage` greps `mark_error` slugs from `drain_cycle/**.py`, collects the union of `exception.slug` attributes (or `halt_reason` strings) observed across the full integration run (including `real_claude`), and asserts equality. Fails locally when a new halt slug lands in production code without a covering scenario.
220	
221	Negative checks during build:
222	- `grep -r "api.linear.app" tests/` returns nothing — no accidental real-Linear calls in fixtures.
223	- The mock's call log (parent GraphQL + child MCP) records every operation a scenario expects; an unexpected call fails the test loudly.
224	- `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler.
225	- Cost budget guard: the `real_claude` suite prints total Anthropic spend at the end of each run; manual review flags runs over $5 (catch a runaway loop scenario early).
