```
30	---
31	
32	# Render HTML
33	
34	## Purpose
35	
36	This skill converts a markdown artefact — design doc, task list, ADR, roadmap, postmortem — into one self-contained HTML file written next to the source as `<source>.html`. The deliverable is for humans about to review or sign off on the artefact, not for archival or publication. It is the operational form of agentic Principle 7 (memory lives in artefacts): the doc itself, made navigable and legible, is the persistent record reviewers and future agents read. And it is a lever on universal Principle 1 (shipped is not done; observed is done): better review UX raises the rate at which review actually happens, which raises the rate at which outcomes get observed.
37	
38	## When to use
39	
40	- The user asks to "render", "convert to HTML", "make this reviewable", or "share this doc" and points at a markdown file.
41	- A markdown artefact is about to go to a human reviewer for design review, code review prep, ADR sign-off, or stakeholder alignment.
42	- The doc is long enough that navigation matters (more than ~3 H2 sections) or contains structure that flattens badly in markdown previewers.
43	
44	## When not to use
45	
46	- **Drafting** — write the markdown first. HTML rendering is for finished or near-finished artefacts, not works in progress. The output is regenerated each time the source changes; rendering mid-draft burns cycles for no review benefit.
47	- **Single-screen documents** — a 30-line note doesn't earn the overhead of a TOC and shell. Send the markdown.
48	- **Documents the reviewer will edit** — HTML round-trip back to markdown is not supported. If reviewers want to suggest edits, send the markdown or use `/crit`.
49	- **Multi-document sites** — one source in, one file out. For a site, use a static-site generator.
50	
51	## Inputs
52	
53	- Path to a single `.md` file with at least one H1 and structured H2/H3 sections.
54	- Optional: prior `<source>.html` exists alongside the source (triggers overwrite confirmation).
55	
56	## Outputs
57	
58	- A single HTML file written to `<source>.html` — same directory as the source, same basename, `.html` extension.
59	- The file is self-contained: inline `<style>`, inline `<script>`, no `<link rel="stylesheet">`, no `<script src=>`, no remote images. Opens correctly via `file://` with the network offline.
60	- Size budget: ≤ ~300KB for typical inputs; hard cap 500KB.
61	
62	## Workflow
63	
64	**Step 1 — Source check [GATE]**
65	Read the source markdown. Confirm it has at least one H1 and at least one H2. If not, stop and report: "render-html expects structured markdown — source has no H1/H2." Do not invent structure to make the doc renderable. The skill is a renderer, not a restructurer.
66	
67	**Step 2 — Overwrite check [GATE]**
68	Compute the output path: replace the source's `.md` extension with `.html`. If a file already exists at that path, call `Read(<output-path>, limit=1)` immediately — this satisfies the Write tool's precondition that the file must be read before it can be overwritten, and it costs only one line of tokens. Then prompt the user for confirmation before proceeding: "`<output-path>` exists. Overwrite? (y/n)". Do not silently overwrite. Do not append timestamps or version suffixes — re-rendering must be idempotent so diffs are meaningful. If the user declines, stop.
69	
70	**Step 3 — Inventory headings**
71	Walk the markdown and collect every H2 and H3 in document order. Generate a stable slug for each (kebab-case from the heading text, deduplicated with `-2`, `-3` if needed). These become both anchor IDs in the body and the TOC entries in the sidebar. H1 is the document title — it goes in the page header, not the TOC.
72	
73	**Step 4 — Convert body**
74	Walk the markdown section by section (H2-delimited). For each section, read `<skill-base>/references/html-patterns.md` and check the patterns in declared order — first match wins. If a pattern matches, emit its HTML template and append its CSS rules to the collected pattern-CSS buffer. If nothing matches, render the section as straightforward prose using standard CommonMark semantics (H2/H3 with anchor IDs from Step 3, paragraphs, lists, blockquotes, fenced code blocks preserving language hints, inline code, tables, links). Prose is the fallback, not the exception — false-positive pattern matches degrade reviewer trust faster than missing treatments.
75	
76	**Step 5 — Compose the page**
77	Read the HTML shell from `<skill-base>/references/html-skeleton.md`. Substitute the tokens defined there: `{{title}}` (H1 from the source), `{{source-path}}`, `{{render-date}}` (ISO date only, no time, so same-day re-renders produce no diff), `{{toc-entries}}` (one `<li class="h2|h3">` per entry from Step 3), and `{{body-html}}` (the converted body from Step 4). Append the pattern-CSS buffer from Step 4 to the end of the inline `<style>` block — patterns add new selectors prefixed `p-`, never overriding the prose styles.
78	
79	**Step 6 — Self-containment check [GATE]**
80	Before writing the file, verify the composed HTML contains zero `<link rel="stylesheet">`, zero `<script src=>`, and no `<img src="http...">` or `<img src="//...">`. SVG and base64 data URIs are allowed; remote URLs are not. If any external reference is present, fix the shell rather than ship the violation.
81	
82	**Step 7 — Write and report**
83	Write the file to `<source>.html`. Report the output path, file size, and the count of H2/H3 entries in the TOC. Tell the user: "Open the file in your browser to verify TOC navigation and that no console errors appear." This handoff is required — the skill cannot observe the rendered page itself (universal Principle 1: the agent has shipped, the user observes).
84	
85	## Artefact template
86	
87	The HTML shell — `<!doctype html>` template, inline CSS, scrollspy JS, and the substitution-token contract (`{{title}}`, `{{source-path}}`, `{{render-date}}`, `{{toc-entries}}`, `{{body-html}}`) — lives in `references/html-skeleton.md`, bundled with this skill. Read it in Step 5 using the `Base directory for this skill` path shown in the skill context header: `<skill-base>/references/html-skeleton.md`. The shell is not duplicated here so a single edit to the skeleton updates both this skill and any future pattern-aware extensions of it.
88	
89	## Common rationalisations
90	
91	| Rationalisation | Rebuttal |
92	|---|---|
93	| "The markdown previewer is fine — they can just read it on GitHub." | GitHub flattens side-by-side alternatives into stacked blocks, hides long task lists in scroll, and gives no in-doc navigation for 40-section design docs. The reviewer experience is the bottleneck on outcome-observation (universal Principle 1); a renderer optimised for review removes it. |
94	| "I'll add a CDN link for the syntax highlighter / icon font / chart library." | The self-containment rule is load-bearing. Reviewers open these files via `file://`, attach them to emails, store them in private wikis. A broken CDN reference produces a silently-degraded page that the reviewer doesn't know to distrust. Inline or omit. |
95	| "Let me append a timestamp to the filename so I don't overwrite." | Idempotent re-rendering is the property that makes the output diffable and the source the canonical record. Versioned filenames produce orphaned copies. Prompt before overwriting (Step 2); do not invent new names. |
96	| "The source has no headings yet — let me invent some structure to make it renderable." | The skill is a renderer, not a restructurer. Inventing headings creates artefacts that look authoritative but were never agreed. If the source isn't structured, fix the source first. |
97	| "I'll skip the TOC for short docs to save space." | The TOC is the navigation contract reviewers learn to expect. Suppressing it inconsistently trains reviewers to scroll-hunt every doc. If a doc is short enough that the TOC is useless, the doc is short enough that the skill doesn't apply (see When not to use). |
98	
99	## Red flags
100	
101	- Reference files read from a relative path instead of `<skill-base>/references/` — causes silent degradation when invoked cross-project.
102	- Output contains `<link rel="stylesheet">`, `<script src=>`, or remote `<img>` URLs.
103	- File written to a path other than `<source>.html` (no timestamps, no version suffixes, no other directories).
104	- Existing output silently overwritten without prompt.
105	- TOC entries do not match the H2/H3 headings in the body.
106	- Body markdown silently dropped (e.g., a fenced code block missing) rather than rendered.
107	- File size above 500KB.
108	- HTML claims to render structure not present in the source.
109	
110	## Verification / exit criteria
111	
112	The skill has run correctly when:
113	
114	1. `<source>.html` exists in the same directory as the source.
115	2. Opening the file via `file://` in a browser produces no console errors or warnings.
116	3. The DevTools Network panel shows zero network requests after load.
117	4. Every H2 and H3 in the source appears as a TOC entry and is reachable by clicking.
118	5. The page renders correctly with the network disabled.
119	6. File size is below 500KB.
120	7. Re-running the skill against the same source (after declining overwrite) leaves the existing file untouched; accepting overwrite produces an HTML whose diff against the previous render is limited to the provenance line.
121	8. The user has been told to open the file and confirm — the skill has not declared the render verified on its own (universal Principle 1).
122	
123	## References
124	
125	- `<skill-base>/references/html-skeleton.md` — the self-contained HTML shell, substitution tokens, and the hard self-containment rule (bundled with this skill)
126	- `<skill-base>/references/html-patterns.md` — the pattern catalogue (alternatives, timelines, callouts, kanban, diffs, chips) consulted in Step 4 (bundled with this skill)
127	- `rules/eng-principles-agentic.md` — P7 (memory lives in artefacts)
128	- `rules/eng-principles-universal.md` — P1 (shipped is not done; observed is done)
129	- Thariq Shihipar — "HTML effectiveness" (https://thariqs.github.io/html-effectiveness/) — case for HTML as a substrate for spatial review content
130	- `docs/skill-anatomy.md` — frontmatter and section-order contract
131	- `docs/tasks/render-html.md` — task plan; later iterations build the pattern catalogue at `skills/render-html/references/html-patterns.md`
132	
```

1	---
2	name: html-patterns
3	description: >
4	  Pattern catalogue for the render-html skill. Each entry maps a recognisable
5	  markdown shape — found in implementation plans and design docs — to a
6	  product-UI HTML treatment: section-header tags, hover cards, decision
7	  panels, summary strips, SVG diagrams, mockup frames, code panels, risk

13	---
14	
15	# HTML pattern catalogue
16	
17	The render-html skill walks the markdown body section by section. For each section, it checks the patterns below in order. The **first match wins** — if no pattern matches, the section renders with the shell's prose defaults (which are themselves styled — see `html-skeleton.md`).
18	
19	Every pattern is defined by:
20	
21	1. **Detect** — the markdown signal that triggers the treatment. Cheap to check; conservative (false-negative is fine, false-positive is bad — wrongly-treated content is harder to debug than untreated).
22	2. **HTML** — the output markup. All class names are prefixed `p-` so they don't collide with the shell's utilities.
23	3. **CSS** — additions appended to the shell's inline `<style>` block. The shell's prose styles remain the fallback; pattern CSS only adds, never overrides.
24	
25	The hard self-containment rule from `html-skeleton.md` applies to every pattern: no external CSS, no external JS, no remote images. Inline SVG is preferred for visual elements. Colour tokens come from the shell's `:root` palette (`--accent`, `--ok`, `--warn`, `--info`, `--danger`, each with a `-soft` variant) — patterns must not introduce ad-hoc hex values.
26	
27	---
28	
29	## P1 — Alternatives considered → side-by-side cards
30	
31	**Detect.** A section whose heading text matches `/alternatives?( considered)?/i`, OR a section containing two-to-four sibling H3s each followed by a bulleted list whose first item starts with `Pros:` or `Cons:` (case-insensitive). Maximum four columns — beyond that, fall back to prose (columns become unreadable). **Does not match** ADR `Rationale` sections whose options use bold-paragraph headers (`**Why X:**`) rather than H3 — those go to P11.
32	
33	**HTML.** Each alternative becomes a hover-state card in an auto-fit grid; pros/cons/cost/reversibility render as a small definition list inside.
34	
35	```html
36	<div class="p-alt-grid">
37	  <article class="p-alt-card">
38	    <header><h3>Alternative A — short name</h3><span class="p-alt-verdict p-alt-verdict-chosen">Chosen</span></header>
39	    <dl>
40	      <dt>Pros</dt><dd><ul><li>…</li></ul></dd>
41	      <dt>Cons</dt><dd><ul><li>…</li></ul></dd>
42	      <dt>Cost</dt><dd>…</dd>
43	      <dt>Reversibility</dt><dd>…</dd>
44	    </dl>
45	  </article>
46	  <!-- repeat per alternative -->
47	</div>
48	```
49	
50	The verdict pill (`Chosen` / `Rejected` / em-dash) is optional — apply only if the source explicitly marks one option as chosen.
51	
52	**CSS.**
53	
54	```css
55	.p-alt-grid { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(260px, 1fr)); gap: 16px; margin: 20px 0; }
56	.p-alt-card { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 10px; padding: 18px 20px; transition: border-color 0.15s, transform 0.15s; }
57	.p-alt-card:hover { border-color: var(--accent); }
58	.p-alt-card > header { display: flex; justify-content: space-between; align-items: baseline; gap: 8px; margin-bottom: 12px; }
59	.p-alt-card h3 { margin: 0; font-size: 16px; font-weight: 700; color: var(--fg); }
60	.p-alt-card dl { margin: 0; }
61	.p-alt-card dt { font-size: 12px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.06em; color: var(--muted); margin-top: 12px; font-weight: 700; }
62	.p-alt-card dd { margin: 4px 0 0; font-size: 15px; color: var(--fg-soft); line-height: 1.55; }
63	.p-alt-card ul { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 20px; }
64	.p-alt-verdict { font: 700 12px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.04em; text-transform: uppercase; padding: 4px 8px; border-radius: 4px; }
65	.p-alt-verdict-chosen { background: var(--ok-soft); color: var(--ok); }
66	.p-alt-verdict-rejected { background: var(--rule-soft); color: var(--muted); }
67	```
68	
69	**Accessibility.** Each card is an `<article>` with a unique `<h3>`; cards appear in DOM order so screen readers read them sequentially. The verdict pill text carries the meaning; colour is supplemental.
70	
71	---
72	
73	## P2 — Task list with dependencies → vertical timeline
74	
75	**Detect.** A section containing an ordered list (or sequence of H3s named `Task N — title` / `Step N`) where two or more entries mention "depends on", "after", "blocks", or "blocked by" in their first paragraph. Pure task lists without dependency language stay as ordinary ordered lists.
76	
77	**HTML.** Three-column row per task: optional `when` (date/sprint label), dot-and-line rail, body. The rail column is always present so the timeline reads as a connected sequence; the `when` column is omitted across the whole timeline if no task has scheduling info.
78	
79	```html
80	<ol class="p-timeline">
81	  <li class="p-task" id="task-1">
82	    <div class="p-task-when">Week 1 · Mon–Tue</div>
83	    <div class="p-task-rail" aria-hidden="true">
84	      <span class="p-task-dot p-task-dot-done"></span>
85	      <span class="p-task-line"></span>
86	    </div>
87	    <div class="p-task-body">
88	      <h3>Schema &amp; API contract</h3>
89	      <p>New <code>comments</code> and <code>comment_reads</code> tables, migrations, tRPC router stubs. Contract reviewed before anything else lands.</p>
90	      <p class="p-task-deps">Depends on: <em>none</em></p>
91	      <div class="p-task-tags">
92	        <span class="p-task-tag">packages/db</span>
93	        <span class="p-task-tag">packages/api</span>
94	        <span class="p-task-tag">migration 0042</span>
95	      </div>
96	    </div>
97	  </li>
98	  <!-- repeat per task; final <li> drops the trailing line -->
99	</ol>
100	```
101	
102	The dot has two states: default (`p-task-dot`, hollow ring in `--accent`) and done (`p-task-dot-done`, solid `--ok`). The line is omitted on the final task. Tag chips are optional — emit them when the source mentions concrete packages, components, file paths, or migration IDs.
103	
104	**CSS.**
105	
106	```css
107	.p-timeline { list-style: none; padding: 0; margin: 24px 0; }
108	.p-task { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 132px 28px 1fr; gap: 0 18px; }
109	.p-task[data-no-when] { grid-template-columns: 28px 1fr; }
110	.p-task-when { text-align: right; font: 12.5px ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; color: var(--muted); padding-top: 6px; }
111	.p-task-rail { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; }
112	.p-task-dot { width: 14px; height: 14px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--bg); border: 3px solid var(--accent); margin-top: 6px; flex-shrink: 0; }
113	.p-task-dot-done { background: var(--ok); border-color: var(--ok); }
114	.p-task-line { width: 2px; flex: 1; background: var(--rule); margin: 4px 0; }
115	.p-task:last-child .p-task-line { display: none; }
116	.p-task-body { padding-bottom: 28px; }
117	.p-task-body h3 { margin: 0 0 6px; font-size: 17px; font-weight: 600; color: var(--fg); }
118	.p-task-body p { font-size: 14.5px; color: var(--fg-soft); margin: 0 0 10px; line-height: 1.55; max-width: 65ch; }
119	.p-task-deps { color: var(--muted); font-size: 13.5px; }
120	.p-task-deps a { color: var(--accent); text-decoration: none; }
121	.p-task-deps a:hover { text-decoration: underline; }
122	.p-task-tags { display: flex; gap: 8px; flex-wrap: wrap; margin-top: 8px; }
123	.p-task-tag { font: 11.5px ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; background: var(--rule-soft); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 6px; padding: 3px 8px; color: var(--fg-soft); }
124	@media (max-width: 720px) { .p-task, .p-task[data-no-when] { grid-template-columns: 22px 1fr; } .p-task-when { display: none; } }
125	```
126	
127	**Accessibility.** Still an `<ol>` — screen readers announce the task count and order. Dependency phrases become inline links (`<a href="#task-2">Task 2</a>`); rail (`p-task-rail`) is `aria-hidden`. The `when` label is plain text and reads as part of each task.
128	
129	---
130	
131	## P3 — Design-doc blocks → tagged H2 + accent panel
132	
133	**Detect.** H2 headings whose text matches one of: `Problem`, `Context`, `Constraints`, `Decision` (or `Recommended approach`), `Consequences`, `Open questions`. Apply per matching H2 — sibling non-matching H2s render with the shell's plain section header.
134	
135	**HTML.** The H2 picks up a small tag pill matching the section type. For `Decision`, the body wraps in a **decision panel** (gradient-filled, indigo border, with a `→ We will` label). Other matches use a thin coloured left-border on the section body but otherwise inherit prose defaults — the type-tag does the visual work.
136	
137	```html
138	<section class="s p-block p-block-decision" id="decision">
139	  <header>
140	    <span class="ix">02</span>
141	    <h2>Decision <span class="p-block-tag p-block-tag-decision">Chosen</span></h2>
142	  </header>
143	  <div class="p-decision-panel">
144	    <span class="p-decision-label">→ We will</span>
145	    Build as a Ruby on Rails 8 monolith using Rails defaults wherever possible.
146	  </div>
147	</section>
148	```
149	
150	```html
151	<section class="s p-block p-block-context" id="context">
152	  <header>
153	    <span class="ix">01</span>
154	    <h2>Context <span class="p-block-tag p-block-tag-context">Background</span></h2>
155	  </header>
156	  <p>…</p>
157	</section>
158	```
159	
160	Tag-label mapping: `Problem` → `Problem` (danger), `Context` → `Background` (muted), `Constraints` → `Limits` (warn), `Decision` → `Chosen` (info / accent), `Consequences` → `Outcomes` (ok), `Open questions` → `Open` (accent-soft).
161	
162	**CSS.**
163	
164	```css
165	.p-block-tag { margin-left: 12px; font: 700 12px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.05em; text-transform: uppercase; vertical-align: 3px; padding: 4px 8px; border-radius: 4px; }
166	.p-block-tag-problem    { background: var(--danger-soft); color: var(--danger); }
167	.p-block-tag-context    { background: var(--rule-soft);   color: var(--muted); }
168	.p-block-tag-constraints{ background: var(--warn-soft);   color: var(--warn); }
169	.p-block-tag-decision   { background: var(--info-soft);   color: var(--info); }
170	.p-block-tag-consequences{ background: var(--ok-soft);    color: var(--ok); }
171	.p-block-tag-open-questions { background: var(--accent-soft); color: var(--accent); }
172	
173	.p-decision-panel {
174	  background: linear-gradient(180deg, var(--info-soft), transparent 80%);
175	  border: 1px solid var(--info); border-radius: 12px;
176	  padding: 22px 24px; margin: 14px 0;
177	  font-size: 18px; line-height: 1.5; color: var(--fg); font-weight: 500;
178	}
179	.p-decision-panel .p-decision-label { display: block; font: 700 13px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.08em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--info); margin-bottom: 10px; }
180	```
181	
182	**Accessibility.** Colour is supplemental; the tag text carries the meaning. The decision panel's label (`→ We will`) is a real text node, not a pseudo-element, so screen readers announce it.
183	
184	**Sub-buckets inside `Consequences`.** When a `Consequences` section's body contains H3s (or bold-paragraph headings) named `Positive`, `Negative`, `Neutral` / `Open`, render each as a coloured sub-card rather than as nested H3s:
185	
186	```html
187	<div class="p-conseq-grid">
188	  <div class="p-conseq p-conseq-positive"><h4>Positive</h4><ul>…</ul></div>
189	  <div class="p-conseq p-conseq-negative"><h4>Negative</h4><ul>…</ul></div>
190	  <div class="p-conseq p-conseq-neutral"><h4>Neutral</h4><ul>…</ul></div>
191	</div>
192	```
193	
194	```css
195	.p-conseq-grid { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(240px, 1fr)); gap: 14px; margin: 20px 0; }
196	.p-conseq { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-left-width: 4px; border-radius: 8px; padding: 14px 16px; }
197	.p-conseq h4 { margin: 0 0 10px; font: 700 12px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.06em; text-transform: uppercase; }
198	.p-conseq-positive { border-left-color: var(--ok);     } .p-conseq-positive h4 { color: var(--ok); }
199	.p-conseq-negative { border-left-color: var(--danger); } .p-conseq-negative h4 { color: var(--danger); }
200	.p-conseq-neutral  { border-left-color: var(--muted);  } .p-conseq-neutral h4  { color: var(--muted); }
201	```
202	
203	This is a P3 extension, not a separate pattern — the parent section is still `p-block-consequences`.
204	
205	**Consequences as checklist (when flat).** If the `Consequences` body is a single flat bullet list with no sub-buckets, render each bullet as a checklist-card row:
206	
207	```html
208	<div class="p-conseq-list">
209	  <div class="p-conseq-item"><span class="p-conseq-check">→</span><span>All code lives in one repo and deploys as one unit</span></div>
210	</div>
211	```
212	
213	```css
214	.p-conseq-list { display: grid; gap: 10px; margin: 14px 0; }
215	.p-conseq-item { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 32px 1fr; gap: 14px; align-items: start; padding: 14px 16px; background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 8px; font-size: 15px; line-height: 1.55; color: var(--fg); }
216	.p-conseq-check { width: 24px; height: 24px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--ok-soft); color: var(--ok); display: grid; place-items: center; font-size: 14px; font-weight: 700; }
217	```
218	
219	---
220	
221	## P4 — NFRs table → highlighted numeric table
222	
223	**Detect.** Markdown table inside a section whose heading matches `/non-functional|NFRs?/i`, OR any table whose header row contains one of: `Target`, `SLO`, `p95`, `p99`, `Latency`, `Throughput`, `Availability`, `Budget`.
224	
225	**HTML.** Standard `<table>` with `class="p-nfr"`; cells that parse as numbers (optionally with units `ms`, `s`, `%`, `/s`, `KB`, `MB`) get `class="p-num"`.
226	
227	```html
228	<table class="p-nfr">
229	  <thead><tr><th>Metric</th><th>Target</th><th>Current</th></tr></thead>
230	  <tbody>
231	    <tr><td>p95 latency</td><td class="p-num">200 ms</td><td class="p-num">340 ms</td></tr>
232	  </tbody>
233	</table>
234	```
235	
236	**CSS.**
237	
238	```css
239	.p-nfr td.p-num { font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; text-align: right; white-space: nowrap; font-size: 14px; }
240	.p-nfr tbody tr:hover { background: var(--rule-soft); }
241	```
242	
243	The shell already styles `<table>` cleanly; P4 only adds numeric-cell alignment + hover. **Accessibility.** Standard table semantics; `<thead>`/`<tbody>` preserved.
244	
245	---
246	
247	## P5 — Open questions → left-accent decision cards
248	
249	**Detect.** Section heading matches `/open questions?|outstanding questions?|to decide|decisions? to make/i`. Body is either a bullet list (one item per question) or a sequence of bold-paragraph headers (`**Do we allow editing?**`) each followed by a short paragraph of context. Optional trailing metadata `(decide with: design, before slice 2)` or `(owner: @anton, due: 2026-06-01)` lifts into a footer line.
250	
251	**HTML.** Each question becomes a vertically-stacked card with a clay accent on the left, a bold question, a context paragraph, and an optional owner/deadline footer in mono.
252	
253	```html
254	<div class="p-questions">
255	  <div class="p-q">
256	    <div class="p-q-title">Do we allow editing, or only delete-and-repost?</div>
257	    <div class="p-q-body">Editing needs an <code>edited_at</code> column and an "edited" affordance. Delete-and-repost is simpler but loses the reply anchor. Leaning toward delete-only for v1.</div>
258	    <div class="p-q-owner">Decide with · design, before slice 2</div>
259	  </div>
260	  <div class="p-q">
261	    <div class="p-q-title">Email digest cadence when a user has the app closed</div>
262	    <div class="p-q-body">Immediate-per-mention will be noisy. Proposal: batch on a 15-minute window, collapse to one email per task, and respect quiet hours from the existing settings table.</div>
263	    <div class="p-q-owner">Decide with · platform, before slice 4</div>
264	  </div>
265	</div>
266	```
267	
268	The `p-q-owner` footer is optional. When metadata isn't present in the source, omit the `<div>` entirely rather than render an empty footer.
269	
270	**CSS.**
271	
272	```css
273	.p-questions { display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 14px; margin: 20px 0; max-width: 820px; }
274	.p-q { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-left: 4px solid var(--accent); border-radius: 10px; padding: 16px 20px; }
275	.p-q-title { font-weight: 600; font-size: 15.5px; color: var(--fg); margin-bottom: 6px; }
276	.p-q-body { font-size: 14px; color: var(--fg-soft); line-height: 1.55; }
277	.p-q-body code { font-size: 0.92em; padding: 1px 5px; background: var(--rule-soft); border-radius: 3px; }
278	.p-q-owner { font: 11.5px ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; color: var(--muted); margin-top: 10px; }
279	```
280	
281	**Accessibility.** Plain text hierarchy (title → body → owner). The accent border is decorative — the bold title carries the visual weight and reads cleanly on screen readers.
282	
283	---
284	
285	## P6 — Diff / before-after → two-column annotated diff
286	
287	**Detect.** Two adjacent fenced code blocks within the same H3 / paragraph where the first is labelled (in info string or preceding line) `before`, `was`, `old`, `current`, and the second `after`, `now`, `new`, `proposed`. Also: any code block with info string `diff` renders inline with `+`/`-` line colouring.
288	
289	**HTML (two-column).**
290	
291	```html
292	<div class="p-diff">
293	  <div class="p-diff-side p-diff-before">
294	    <header>Before</header>
295	    <pre><code>…</code></pre>
296	  </div>
297	  <div class="p-diff-side p-diff-after">
298	    <header>After</header>
299	    <pre><code>…</code></pre>
300	  </div>
301	</div>
302	```
303	
304	**HTML (inline diff).** Standard `<pre><code class="language-diff">` with each line wrapped: `<span class="p-diff-add">+ …</span>`, `<span class="p-diff-del">- …</span>`.
305	
306	**CSS.**
307	
308	```css
309	.p-diff { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1fr 1fr; gap: 14px; margin: 20px 0; }
310	.p-diff-side header { font: 700 12px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.06em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--muted); margin-bottom: 8px; }
311	.p-diff-before pre { border-left: 3px solid var(--danger); }
312	.p-diff-after  pre { border-left: 3px solid var(--ok); }
313	.p-diff-add { background: var(--ok-soft); color: var(--ok); display: block; }
314	.p-diff-del { background: var(--danger-soft); color: var(--danger); display: block; }
315	@media (max-width: 720px) { .p-diff { grid-template-columns: 1fr; } }
316	```
317	
318	**Accessibility.** Headers (`Before` / `After`) carry the meaning; colour stripe is supplemental.
319	
320	---
321	
322	<!-- P7 (Now/Next/Later kanban) and P8 (ICE scores) are out of scope for v1
323	     — they belong to a future render-roadmap skill. P-numbers are not reused;
324	     downstream patterns keep P9–P12 so cross-references stay stable. -->
325	
326	---
327	
328	## P9 — YAML frontmatter → metadata grid (lifted into doc head)
329	
330	**Detect.** Source markdown begins with a YAML frontmatter block delimited by `---` on its own line, before the H1. Common in ADRs and design docs (fields like `id`, `status`, `date`, `authors`, `linear`, `supersedes`).
331	
332	**HTML.** Frontmatter fields **lift into the shell's `{{meta-grid}}` slot** under the doc head — they do not render as a separate block in the body. The shell's `.meta-grid` styles already cover the visual treatment; P9 only specifies the *lifting* behaviour. The `status` field also lifts into `{{header-pills}}` via the P10 pill treatment.
333	
334	```html
335	<!-- inside the shell's <dl class="meta-grid"> -->
336	<dt>id</dt>      <dd>ADR-engagement-modifier-constants</dd>
337	<dt>authors</dt> <dd>Anton Babushkin</dd>
338	<dt>linear</dt>  <dd><a href="https://linear.app/…">ABA-110</a></dd>
339	<dt>supersedes</dt><dd>—</dd>
340	<dt>source</dt>  <dd>docs/adrs/engagement-modifier-constants.md</dd>
341	<dt>rendered</dt><dd>2026-05-17</dd>
342	```
343	
344	`source` and `rendered` are always included; frontmatter fields fold in alphabetically after them, except `status` (which becomes a header pill, not a grid row) and `date` (which becomes a date pill in the header). **Do not drop frontmatter on the floor** — readers use it to anchor the doc.
345	
346	**Accessibility.** Definition-list semantics; screen readers announce each key-value pair.
347	
348	---
349	
350	## P10 — Status / state values → coloured pill
351	
352	**Detect.** A frontmatter `status:` field, OR a top-of-doc bold line matching `/\*\*Status:\*\*\s+(\w+)/i`, OR a single-cell `Status` column in a table. Recognised values (case-insensitive): `accepted`, `proposed`, `draft`, `rejected`, `superseded`, `deprecated`, `flagged`, `ok`.
353	
354	**HTML.** When detected from frontmatter or top-of-doc bold line, the pill **lifts into the shell's `{{header-pills}}` slot** (using the shell's `.pill` utility — no new CSS needed):
355	
356	```html
357	<span class="pill pill-accepted"><span class="dot"></span>Accepted</span>
358	```
359	
360	When detected inside a table cell, render in-place:
361	
362	```html
363	<td><span class="pill pill-accepted"><span class="dot"></span>Accepted</span></td>
364	```
365	
366	The shell already defines `.pill-accepted`, `.pill-proposed`, `.pill-rejected`, `.pill-superseded`, etc. P10 adds no new CSS — it specifies the *detection + placement* contract. **Accessibility.** Label text carries the meaning; colour is supplemental.
367	
368	---
369	
370	## P11 — Rationale "Why X" blocks → hover-state cards
371	
372	**Detect.** A section heading matches `/rationale/i` AND its body contains two-or-more bold-paragraph headers (`**Why X:**` / `**Why not Y:**`) each followed by a bullet list. Common in ADRs that consider multiple alternatives without dedicated H3s per option.
373	
374	**HTML.** Each bold paragraph becomes a card heading; the following list is the card body. Cards stack in a single-column grid (one per row) so each option gets full line-length for its bullets, but pick up an indigo border on hover and a directional chevron in the title — `✓` for `Why X`, `✗` for `Why not Y`.
375	
376	```html
377	<div class="p-why-grid">
378	  <div class="p-why-card p-why-pro">
379	    <h3><span class="p-why-chev">✓</span>Why Rails</h3>
380	    <ul><li>…</li></ul>
381	  </div>
382	  <div class="p-why-card p-why-con">
383	    <h3><span class="p-why-chev">✗</span>Why not a separate frontend</h3>
384	    <ul><li>…</li></ul>
385	  </div>
386	</div>
387	```
388	
389	**CSS.**
390	
391	```css
392	.p-why-grid { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1fr; gap: 14px; margin: 20px 0; }
393	.p-why-card { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 10px; padding: 18px 22px; transition: border-color 0.15s; }
394	.p-why-card:hover { border-color: var(--accent); }
395	.p-why-card h3 { margin: 0 0 14px; font-size: 16px; font-weight: 700; display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 10px; color: var(--fg); }
396	.p-why-chev { font-size: 18px; line-height: 1; font-weight: 700; }
397	.p-why-pro .p-why-chev { color: var(--ok); }
398	.p-why-con .p-why-chev { color: var(--warn); }
399	.p-why-card ul { margin: 0; padding: 0; list-style: none; }
400	.p-why-card li { position: relative; padding: 6px 0 6px 20px; font-size: 15px; color: var(--fg-soft); line-height: 1.6; }
401	.p-why-card li::before { content: ""; position: absolute; left: 4px; top: 15px; width: 5px; height: 5px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--muted); }
402	```
403	
404	**Precedence.** P11 ranks below P1 (Alternatives). When both could match (`Rationale` section whose options also have `Pros:`/`Cons:`), P1's side-by-side card grid is the stronger treatment.
405	
406	**Accessibility.** `<h3>` keeps headings in the document outline; lists keep list semantics; chevron is a real text node (not pseudo-content) so screen readers announce it.
407	
408	---
409	
410	## P12 — Labelled rubric cards (C1, C2, FR3 …) → repeated sub-field cards
411	
412	**Detect.** Two or more sibling H3s whose text matches `/^([A-Z]+\d+)\s+[—–-]\s+/` (e.g. `C1 — Deadband: ±2%`, `FR3 — Idempotent writes`, `OQ5 — …`), AND each section repeats the same set of bold-paragraph sub-fields (e.g. **Reasoning**, **Alternative considered**, **Revision trigger**). The identifier prefix (`C`, `FR`, `OQ`, `NFR`, `ADR`) is rendered as a pill on the card header.
413	
414	**HTML.**
415	
416	```html
417	<div class="p-rubric" id="c1">
418	  <h3><span class="p-rubric-id">C1</span> Deadband: ±2% YoY</h3>
419	  <div class="p-rubric-formula">If <code>abs(yoy_change) &lt; 0.02</code>, set <code>direction = 0</code> …</div>
420	  <h4>Reasoning</h4><p>…</p>
421	  <h4>Alternative considered</h4><p>…</p>
422	  <h4>Revision trigger</h4><p>…</p>
423	</div>
424	```
425	
426	The first paragraph (often a one-line formal definition or formula) becomes `p-rubric-formula`. Subsequent bold-paragraph sub-fields become `<h4>` headings inside the card.
427	
428	**CSS.**
429	
430	```css
431	.p-rubric { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-left: 4px solid var(--accent); border-radius: 10px; padding: 18px 22px; margin: 20px 0; transition: border-color 0.15s; }
432	.p-rubric:hover { border-color: var(--accent); }
433	.p-rubric h3 { margin: 0 0 10px; display: flex; gap: 12px; align-items: baseline; font-size: 17px; font-weight: 700; color: var(--fg); }
434	.p-rubric-id { font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; font-size: 13px; padding: 4px 9px; background: var(--accent); color: white; border-radius: 4px; letter-spacing: 0.02em; font-weight: 700; }
435	.p-rubric h4 { font: 700 12px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.06em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--muted); margin: 16px 0 6px; }
436	.p-rubric-formula { background: var(--accent-soft); border-left: 3px solid var(--accent); padding: 12px 16px; margin: 12px 0; font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; font-size: 14px; line-height: 1.55; border-radius: 0 6px 6px 0; color: var(--fg); }
437	.p-rubric p { margin: 0 0 10px; font-size: 15px; line-height: 1.6; color: var(--fg-soft); }
438	```
439	
440	**Why a dedicated pattern.** Plain-prose rendering of a 6-card ADR section (C1–C6 with Reasoning / Alternative / Revision trigger each) produces a wall of repeating H3+H4 that the reader has to mentally segment. The card boundary plus the identifier pill makes "which constant am I reading" answerable at a glance.
441	
442	**Accessibility.** Heading levels (`<h3>` outer, `<h4>` inner) preserve the document outline; the card boundary is decorative.
443	
444	---
445	
446	## P13 — Summary strip → top-of-doc KPI cells
447	
448	**Detect.** The first content block after the doc header is a short bullet list or definition list of 3–6 key:value pairs where keys are short labels (`Effort`, `Surfaces touched`, `New tables`, `Feature flag`, `Owner`, `Decision deadline`, `Cost`, `Status`) and values are short atomic phrases (a duration, a count, a flag name, a date, a dollar figure). Also matches when the source uses a YAML-frontmatter-style block under a `## Summary` or `## At a glance` heading. **Does not apply** when the same shape appears mid-doc — only the first such block lifts into the summary strip; subsequent ones render as ordinary `<dl>`s.
449	
450	**HTML.** Lifts into a 4-up grid directly under the doc head (above the first `<section>`):
451	
452	```html
453	<div class="p-summary">
454	  <div class="p-summary-cell"><div class="p-summary-k">Effort</div><div class="p-summary-v p-summary-v-accent">~2 weeks</div></div>
455	  <div class="p-summary-cell"><div class="p-summary-k">Surfaces touched</div><div class="p-summary-v">3 packages</div></div>
456	  <div class="p-summary-cell"><div class="p-summary-k">New tables</div><div class="p-summary-v">2</div></div>
457	  <div class="p-summary-cell"><div class="p-summary-k">Feature flag</div><div class="p-summary-v">task_comments_v1</div></div>
458	</div>
459	```
460	
461	The first cell's value gets `p-summary-v-accent` when it carries the headline number (effort, cost, deadline) — at most one per strip. Strip auto-collapses to 2-up at narrow widths.
462	
463	**CSS.**
464	
465	```css
466	.p-summary { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(4, 1fr); gap: 14px; margin: 0 0 32px; }
467	.p-summary-cell { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 10px; padding: 16px 18px; }
468	.p-summary-k { font: 700 11px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.06em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--muted); margin-bottom: 8px; }
469	.p-summary-v { font-size: 17px; font-weight: 600; color: var(--fg); line-height: 1.3; }
470	.p-summary-v-accent { color: var(--accent); }
471	@media (max-width: 720px) { .p-summary { grid-template-columns: repeat(2, 1fr); } }
472	```
473	
474	**Accessibility.** Each cell is plain text; label and value are independent text nodes. **Why a dedicated pattern.** A plan or design doc that opens with "this will take two weeks, touch three packages, and ship behind `task_comments_v1`" gives every subsequent reader an anchor for "is this proportionate?" before they hit the first detail.
475	
476	---
477	
478	## P14 — Data flow / architecture → inline SVG diagram
479	
480	**Detect.** A section heading matches `/data flow|architecture|sequence|system( context)?|fan[- ]?out|pipeline/i` AND its body contains EITHER (a) a fenced code block tagged ` ```svg ` containing literal `<svg>` markup, OR (b) a markdown description of nodes-and-edges that the agent has already converted to SVG during conversion. The skill **does not** auto-generate SVG from prose — agents author the SVG explicitly when they identify a diagram-worthy concept.
481	
482	**HTML.** The SVG is wrapped in a framed panel with a caption beneath. Solid lines and dashed lines carry semantic meaning by convention; the caption explains the convention used.
483	
484	```html
485	<div class="p-diagram">
486	  <svg viewBox="0 0 860 340" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" role="img" aria-label="Comment write path and realtime fan-out">
487	    <defs>
488	      <marker id="arr" viewBox="0 0 10 10" refX="9" refY="5" markerWidth="7" markerHeight="7" orient="auto-start-reverse">
489	        <path d="M0,0 L10,5 L0,10z" fill="currentColor"/>
490	      </marker>
491	      <marker id="arr-accent" viewBox="0 0 10 10" refX="9" refY="5" markerWidth="7" markerHeight="7" orient="auto-start-reverse">
492	        <path d="M0,0 L10,5 L0,10z" fill="var(--accent)"/>
493	      </marker>
494	    </defs>
495	    <!-- Node template -->
496	    <g class="p-node">
497	      <rect x="20" y="20" width="180" height="54" rx="10"/>
498	      <text x="110" y="43" text-anchor="middle" class="p-node-title">&lt;Composer&gt;</text>
499	      <text x="110" y="60" text-anchor="middle" class="p-node-sub">apps/web</text>
500	    </g>
501	    <!-- Edges: stroke="currentColor" = solid request path; class="p-edge-async" = dashed realtime path -->
502	    <path d="M110 74 L110 150" marker-end="url(#arr)" class="p-edge"/>
503	    <path d="M520 177 L660 177" marker-end="url(#arr-accent)" class="p-edge-async"/>
504	    <!-- Edge label -->
505	    <text x="240" y="170" class="p-edge-label">mutate</text>
506	  </svg>
507	  <p class="p-diagram-caption">Solid = request/response. Dashed = realtime fan-out. The composer never waits on the dashed path.</p>
508	</div>
509	```
510	
511	**CSS.**
512	
513	```css
514	.p-diagram { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 12px; padding: 24px; margin: 20px 0; overflow-x: auto; }
515	.p-diagram svg { display: block; min-width: 100%; max-width: 100%; height: auto; color: var(--muted); }
516	.p-diagram .p-node rect { fill: var(--bg); stroke: var(--rule); stroke-width: 1.5; }
517	.p-diagram .p-node-title { font: 600 12px ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; fill: var(--fg); }
518	.p-diagram .p-node-sub { font: 10.5px ui-monospace, monospace; fill: var(--muted); }
519	.p-diagram .p-edge { stroke: currentColor; stroke-width: 1.5; fill: none; }
520	.p-diagram .p-edge-async { stroke: var(--accent); stroke-width: 1.5; fill: none; stroke-dasharray: 5 4; }
521	.p-diagram .p-edge-label { font: 10.5px ui-monospace, monospace; fill: var(--muted); }
522	.p-diagram-caption { font-size: 13.5px; color: var(--muted); margin: 12px 0 0; line-height: 1.55; }
523	```
524	
525	**Authoring guidance for the agent.** Use the four node-classes (`p-node` for default, `p-node p-node-emphasis` for the persistence layer or any single "focus" box — fills with `var(--fg)` background and `var(--bg)` text). Limit to ~7 nodes and ~10 edges; if the diagram needs more, split it. Edge labels must be ≤ 24 chars; longer explanations go in the caption.
526	
527	**Accessibility.** `<svg role="img" aria-label="…">` provides a short description; the caption beneath provides the long description. Text inside the SVG is selectable. **No remote fonts** — the SVG inherits the page font stack.
528	
529	---
530	
531	## P15 — Mockup frame → labelled UI sketch
532	
533	**Detect.** Section heading matches `/mockup|wireframe|sketch|prototype/i` AND body contains 1–3 sub-blocks separated by H3s or bold paragraph headers that look like artefact labels (`A · Thread inside a task card`, `Variant 1`, `Empty state`). Mockup contents can be either (a) inline HTML the agent has authored to approximate the UI, or (b) an inline SVG sketch.
534	
535	**HTML.** Each mockup is a framed card with a small label bar and a body area. Two-up grid by default; collapses to single column at narrow widths.
536	
537	```html
538	<div class="p-mocks">
539	  <figure class="p-mock">
540	    <figcaption class="p-mock-label">A · Thread inside a task card</figcaption>
541	    <div class="p-mock-body">
542	      <!-- agent-authored UI sketch goes here -->
543	    </div>
544	  </figure>
545	  <figure class="p-mock">
546	    <figcaption class="p-mock-label">B · Sidebar unread digest</figcaption>
547	    <div class="p-mock-body"> … </div>
548	  </figure>
549	</div>
550	```
551	
552	**CSS.**
553	
554	```css
555	.p-mocks { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(320px, 1fr)); gap: 20px; margin: 20px 0; }
556	.p-mock { margin: 0; background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 12px; overflow: hidden; }
557	.p-mock-label { padding: 10px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); background: var(--rule-soft); font: 700 12px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.06em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--muted); }
558	.p-mock-body { padding: 20px; background: var(--bg); }
559	```
560	
561	**Authoring guidance.** Mockups are deliberately sketchy — they exist to align reviewers on layout, not to look pixel-final. Real UI primitives the agent can lean on: rounded `<div>`s for cards, small circular `<div>`s for avatars (initials inside), dashed-border divs for "image goes here," `<input disabled>` for form fields. The aim is "we agree on the shape," not "this is the final design."
562	
563	**Accessibility.** `<figure>` + `<figcaption>` keeps the label tied to the mockup for screen readers. Mockup bodies should be ARIA-`role="presentation"` if they contain non-functional interactive-looking elements (a `<button>` that doesn't do anything is worse than a styled `<div>`).
564	
565	---
566	
567	## P16 — Code panel → file-labelled dark block
568	
569	**Detect.** Any fenced code block that has BOTH (a) an info string naming the language AND (b) a preceding line or paragraph that names a file path (`apps/web/hooks/useAddComment.ts`, `packages/db/migrations/0042_comments.sql`). The file path is detected as a relative-path-looking token containing at least one `/` and ending in a recognised extension. Plain fenced blocks without a file label fall through to the shell's default `<pre>` styling. The P6 two-column diff treatment takes precedence when both apply.
570	
571	**HTML.**
572	
573	```html
574	<figure class="p-code">
575	  <figcaption class="p-code-file">apps/web/hooks/useAddComment.ts</figcaption>
576	  <pre class="p-code-body"><code class="language-ts">…</code></pre>
577	</figure>
578	```
579	
580	When two or more code panels appear in the same section back-to-back, they render in a 2-up grid so reviewers can scan them in parallel (e.g. migration + hook):
581	
582	```html
583	<div class="p-code-grid">
584	  <figure class="p-code"> … </figure>
585	  <figure class="p-code"> … </figure>
586	</div>
587	```
588	
589	**CSS.**
590	
591	```css
592	.p-code { margin: 16px 0; display: flex; flex-direction: column; }
593	.p-code-file { font: 12px ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; color: var(--muted); margin-bottom: 8px; }
594	.p-code-body { background: var(--fg); color: #e8e6de; border-radius: 12px; padding: 18px 20px; overflow-x: auto; margin: 0; }
595	.p-code-body code { font: 12.5px/1.65 ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; color: inherit; background: transparent; padding: 0; }
596	.p-code-body .tok-kw  { color: var(--accent); }
597	.p-code-body .tok-str { color: var(--ok); }
598	.p-code-body .tok-cm  { color: var(--muted); font-style: italic; }
599	.p-code-body .tok-fn  { color: #c9b98a; }
600	.p-code-grid { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1fr 1fr; gap: 20px; margin: 16px 0; }
601	@media (max-width: 900px) { .p-code-grid { grid-template-columns: 1fr; } }
602	```
603	
604	**Token wrapping.** The agent applies four span classes inline (`tok-kw` for keywords, `tok-str` for string literals, `tok-cm` for comments, `tok-fn` for function/identifier names). Don't attempt full lexer-grade highlighting — the four tokens are enough to give the panel hierarchy without misleading the reader. If the agent isn't confident about a token, leave it un-spanned (the default body colour reads cleanly).
605	
606	**Accessibility.** `<figure>` + `<figcaption>` ties the file path to the code. Dark background + light text passes AA at 14.5:1 (`#e8e6de` on `var(--fg)` `#0a0a0f`).
607	
608	---
609	
610	## P17 — Risks & mitigations → severity-tagged table
611	
612	**Detect.** Section heading matches `/risks?( ?(and|&) ?mitigations?)?|threats?|failure modes?/i` AND body is a markdown table with at least these columns (case-insensitive, partial match OK): `Risk` (or `Description`), `Severity` (or `Sev` / `Likelihood`), `Mitigation` (or `Response`). An optional `Owner` column is preserved. Severity values are matched against `high|med(ium)?|low|critical` (case-insensitive).
613	
614	**HTML.** Three- or four-column grid (not a real `<table>`, because we want the severity cell to be a chip in a narrow centred column). Borders form the visual table.
615	
616	```html
617	<div class="p-risks">
618	  <div class="p-risks-row p-risks-head">
619	    <div>Risk</div><div>Sev</div><div>Mitigation</div>
620	  </div>
621	  <div class="p-risks-row">
622	    <div>Realtime duplicate: socket append races with HTTP response.</div>
623	    <div><span class="p-sev p-sev-high">HIGH</span></div>
624	    <div>Dedupe on server-assigned <code>id</code>; temp rows filtered on reconcile.</div>
625	  </div>
626	  <!-- repeat rows -->
627	</div>
628	```
629	
630	**CSS.**
631	
632	```css
633	.p-risks { border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 12px; overflow: hidden; background: var(--surface); margin: 20px 0; }
634	.p-risks-row { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1.6fr 80px 1.6fr; }
635	.p-risks-row + .p-risks-row { border-top: 1px solid var(--rule); }
636	.p-risks-row > div { padding: 14px 18px; font-size: 14px; line-height: 1.55; color: var(--fg-soft); }
637	.p-risks-row > div + div { border-left: 1px solid var(--rule); }
638	.p-risks-head > div { background: var(--rule-soft); color: var(--fg); font: 700 12px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.05em; text-transform: uppercase; padding: 12px 18px; }
639	.p-sev { display: inline-block; font: 700 11px/1 ui-monospace, monospace; padding: 4px 9px; border-radius: 6px; letter-spacing: 0.04em; }
640	.p-sev-high     { background: var(--danger-soft); color: var(--danger); }
641	.p-sev-critical { background: var(--danger);      color: white; }
642	.p-sev-med      { background: var(--warn-soft);   color: var(--warn); }
643	.p-sev-low      { background: var(--ok-soft);     color: var(--ok); }
644	@media (max-width: 720px) {
645	  .p-risks-row { grid-template-columns: 1fr; }
646	  .p-risks-row > div + div { border-left: none; border-top: 1px dashed var(--rule); }
647	  .p-risks-head { display: none; }
648	}
649	```
650	
651	**Optional owner column.** When the source table has an Owner column, switch the grid to `1.5fr 80px 1.5fr 120px` and add a fourth `<div>` per row. Owner renders as plain text or a `pill pill-meta` if it looks like a handle (`@anton`).
652	
653	**Accessibility.** Severity label text (`HIGH` / `MED` / `LOW`) carries the meaning; colour is supplemental. The grid uses `<div>`s rather than `<table>` because the severity column is intentionally narrow and centre-aligned — a real table here forces awkward column-width compromises. Screen readers still announce each row's content in order; for users who require table semantics, the source markdown table is the canonical form.
654	
655	---
656	
657	## P18 — Segment-mix horizontal stack bar
658	
659	**Detect.** Section H2 matches `/business architecture|segment mix|revenue mix/i` AND the section contains a markdown table whose first column header matches `Segment` (case-insensitive) and second column header contains `%`, `share`, or `mix`. Pattern fires on the table; the bar inserts immediately before it, and the table renders below as the canonical legend.
660	
661	**HTML.** Parse each data row's second-column value (format `~XX%` or `XX%`); normalise all shares to sum to exactly 100%. Render a flex-row container where each segment is a coloured `<div>` with inline `style="width:N%"`. Include a `<span class="p-seg-bar-lbl">` only on segments with normalised share ≥8% (first significant word of the segment name). Wrap in a `<div role="img" aria-label="Segment mix: [name] N%, …">`. Six colour slots `.p-seg-s0`–`.p-seg-s5` cycle through the shell's primary tokens; use the first N slots for N segments.
662	
663	```html
664	<div class="p-seg-bar-fig" role="img" aria-label="Segment mix: EUV systems 50%, Advanced DUV 25%, Mature DUV 10%, Installed Base Management 15%">
665	  <div class="p-seg-bar">
666	    <div class="p-seg-s0" style="width:50%" title="EUV systems ~50%">
667	      <span class="p-seg-bar-lbl">EUV</span>
668	    </div>
669	    <div class="p-seg-s1" style="width:25%" title="Advanced DUV ~25%">
670	      <span class="p-seg-bar-lbl">Adv DUV</span>
671	    </div>
672	    <div class="p-seg-s2" style="width:10%" title="Mature DUV ~10%">
673	      <span class="p-seg-bar-lbl">Mature</span>
674	    </div>
675	    <div class="p-seg-s3" style="width:15%" title="Installed Base Management ~15%">
676	      <span class="p-seg-bar-lbl">IBM</span>
677	    </div>
678	  </div>
679	</div>
680	<!-- source table renders immediately below as canonical legend -->
681	```
682	
683	**CSS.**
684	
685	```css
686	.p-seg-bar-fig { margin: 0.5rem 0 0.25rem; }
687	.p-seg-bar { display: flex; height: 36px; border-radius: 4px; overflow: hidden; gap: 1px; background: var(--rule); }
688	.p-seg-s0 { background: var(--accent);  flex-shrink: 0; display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; overflow: hidden; }
689	.p-seg-s1 { background: var(--info);    flex-shrink: 0; display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; overflow: hidden; }
690	.p-seg-s2 { background: var(--warn);    flex-shrink: 0; display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; overflow: hidden; }
691	.p-seg-s3 { background: var(--ok);      flex-shrink: 0; display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; overflow: hidden; }
692	.p-seg-s4 { background: var(--danger);  flex-shrink: 0; overflow: hidden; }
693	.p-seg-s5 { background: var(--muted);   flex-shrink: 0; overflow: hidden; }
694	.p-seg-bar-lbl { color: white; font-size: 0.68rem; font-weight: 700; white-space: nowrap; overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis; padding: 0 6px; }
695	```
696	
697	**Accessibility.** The `role="img" aria-label` on the wrapper div provides a text summary for screen readers; segment divs are presentational. The source table that follows is the canonical data record.
698	
699	**When to use.** Playbook §1 "Business Architecture" segment-mix tables. Fires on the first `Segment`-headed table in the section. Does not fire on tables whose second column is growth rate, margin, or another non-share metric.
700	
701	---
702	
703	## P19 — Normalised-margin position-band strip
704	
705	**Detect.** Three concurrent conditions must all be true: (1) section H2 matches `/(capex|AI investment) cycle|cycle position|normalised.?margin/i`; (2) the section body contains a sentence with one of `floor`, `ceiling`, `sits between`, or `anchor` AND at least two `%` values; (3) the two or more `%` values are within 300 characters of the triggering keyword. If any condition fails, fall through to prose — DCF prose is dense with `%` figures that are not margin anchors.
706	
707	**Extract.** From the matching sentence: parse the lower bound (floor `%`), the upper bound (ceiling `%`), and the base anchor `%`. These three values drive the visual. Tick position = `(base − floor) / (ceiling − floor) × 100`.
708	
709	**HTML.**
710	```html
711	<figure class="p-band-fig" role="img"
712	  aria-label="Normalised-margin range: floor ~{{floor}}%, base ~{{base}}%, ceiling ~{{ceiling}}%">
713	  <div class="p-band-row">
714	    <span class="p-band-ep">~{{floor}}%<br><span class="p-band-ep-lbl">floor</span></span>
715	    <div class="p-band-track">
716	      <div class="p-band-tick" style="left:{{tick_pct}}%">
717	        <span class="p-band-tick-lbl">~{{base}}%</span>
718	      </div>
719	    </div>
720	    <span class="p-band-ep p-band-ep-r">~{{ceiling}}%<br><span class="p-band-ep-lbl">ceiling</span></span>
721	  </div>
722	</figure>
723	```
724	
725	**CSS.**
726	```css
727	.p-band-fig { margin: 0.5rem 0 0.75rem; }
728	.p-band-row { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 0.75rem; }
729	.p-band-ep { font-size: 0.75rem; color: var(--muted); text-align: center; white-space: nowrap; min-width: 2.5rem; line-height: 1.3; }
730	.p-band-ep-r { text-align: right; }
731	.p-band-ep-lbl { font-size: 0.65rem; }
732	.p-band-track { flex: 1; height: 12px; border-radius: 6px; background: linear-gradient(to right, var(--danger-soft), var(--warn-soft), var(--ok-soft)); position: relative; }
733	.p-band-tick { position: absolute; top: -4px; width: 3px; height: 20px; background: var(--fg); border-radius: 2px; transform: translateX(-50%); }
734	.p-band-tick-lbl { position: absolute; bottom: 24px; left: 50%; transform: translateX(-50%); font-size: 0.7rem; font-weight: 700; color: var(--fg); white-space: nowrap; background: var(--bg); padding: 0 2px; }
735	```
736	
737	**Accessibility.** The `<figure>` carries a full `aria-label` with all three values in plain text. The gradient is decorative — floor, base, and ceiling values are announced verbatim by the label.
738	
739	**When to use.** Playbook §2 sections that establish a floor-to-ceiling margin range with a named base anchor. Both GOOG §2 ("AI Investment Cycle Position") and ASML §2 ("Capex-Cycle Position") match. Do not fire on general prose that mentions `%` without an explicit floor/ceiling/base structure — the three-condition gate handles this.
740	
741	---
742	
743	## P20 — Catalyst probability × IV-impact mini-bars
744	
745	**Detect.** Section H2 matches `/catalysts?/i` AND the section contains a markdown table with headers matching `Catalyst`, `Probability`, and `IV impact` (case-insensitive, partial match on each header).
746	
747	**HTML.** Render the standard 4-column table augmented with inline mini-bar `<div>`s below the text value in two cells per row. Probability bar: `width = parsed_probability_percent%` (e.g., `~35%` → `width:35%`). IV-impact bar: `width = min(midpoint_of_range, 30) / 30 × 100%` — midpoint is the average of the two endpoint values in a range like `−10–20%` (using absolute values, e.g. midpoint = 15); a 30%-absolute range fills the bar fully. IV bar class: `p-cat-bar-pos` if range starts with `+`; `p-cat-bar-neg` if it starts with `−` or `-`. Direction cell becomes a pill: text containing `positive` → `.p-cat-dir-pos`; `negative` → `.p-cat-dir-neg`; `mixed`, `neutral`, or `modest` → `.p-cat-dir-mixed`.

## Approved Plan (edited by user):
# Add architecture visuals to integration-test design doc + render to HTML

## Context

`docs/integration-test-architecture.md` exists and is the design doc for the Linear initiative ([project](https://linear.app/ababushkin/project/test-drain-cycle-failure-modes-and-resume-without-a-real-linear-cycle-910b954af13f)). It's about to go to a team / tech lead / manager for review-and-approval.

Two design choices in the doc carry most of the review risk and are hardest to convey in prose:

1. **The seam choice** — real `claude -p` + mocked Linear, glued by an MCP shim that translates child MCP calls into GraphQL against the same in-memory store the parent hits over HTTP. The §Pillar 2 paragraph "Why an MCP shim and not just `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child env" takes ~200 words to explain something a system-context diagram explains in one glance.
2. **The seam closes end-to-end** — that an end-to-end happy-path drain actually flows through the mock both at the parent boundary and through the child's MCP. A sequence diagram is the cheapest proof.

A third visual — the **failure-mode coverage matrix** — turns KR 2 ("every failure path covered") from a sentence into something a reviewer can scan and push back on ("what about X?").

After the three visuals land, render the whole doc to HTML via `/render-html` so the reviewer experience matches a product OKR review rather than a GitHub markdown blob.

The user explicitly chose architecture-focused visuals; cost-split, slice-dependency graph, and per-scenario-contract card are deferred — sequencing/slice mechanics are already clear from the prose.

## Approach

Three edits to `docs/integration-test-architecture.md` and one render. Sections are added; no existing sections are rewritten.

### Edit 1 — System context diagram (new H2 "System context")

Placement: between §Context and §Workflow.

Heading: `## System context` — matches P14's detector (`/system( context)?|architecture/i`), so render-html wraps the inline SVG in `.p-diagram` with the caption styled.

Inline SVG content (authored in the markdown as a ```svg fenced block):

- **Nodes** (~7):
  - `IntegrationHarness` (pytest fixture)
  - `orchestrator.run()` (parent, real)
  - `claude -p` (child, real, spawned per issue)
  - `MockLinear` (FastAPI, mocked)
  - `linear_mcp_shim` (stdio MCP, mocked)
  - `in-memory store` (Cycle / Issue / Label / WorkflowState + call log)
- **Boundary**: a dashed group around `MockLinear` + `linear_mcp_shim` + `in-memory store` labelled "MOCKED". Everything else is real.
- **Edges**:
  - Harness → MockLinear: `populate scenario`
  - Harness → orchestrator: `run(repos, limits)`
  - orchestrator → MockLinear: `GraphQL: current_cycle, pending_issues, set_state, get_issue, resolve_state` (solid)
  - orchestrator → claude -p: `subprocess.spawn` (solid)
  - claude -p → linear_mcp_shim: `MCP stdio: save_issue, save_comment, get_issue` (solid, accent colour)
  - linear_mcp_shim → MockLinear: `GraphQL POST` (solid)
  - both `MockLinear` and `linear_mcp_shim` write into `in-memory store` (thin lines)

Caption: "Solid = control flow. Dashed boundary = mocked. Real `claude -p` is the only path that exercises the production MCP-tool surface; the shim is the smallest seam that lets us mock Linear without forking the official MCP."

### Edit 2 — Happy-path sequence diagram (new H3 inside §Pillar 2)

Placement: at the end of §Pillar 2 (`### Pillar 2 — linear_mcp_shim …`), where the "Why an MCP shim" prose paragraph currently sits. The sequence diagram is the visual answer to that paragraph; the paragraph stays as the textual answer above it.

Heading: `### Happy-path sequence` — placed inside an existing P14-matching parent doesn't matter; P14 also detects on `/sequence/i` at H2/H3 level (P14 detector is per-section, not depth-restricted).

Inline SVG content (authored as a ```svg fenced block; ~8–10 messages, vertical lifelines):

- **Lifelines** (5, left to right): `Harness`, `orchestrator`, `MockLinear`, `claude -p`, `linear_mcp_shim`
- **Messages** (top to bottom):
  1. Harness → MockLinear: `populate(Cycle, Issue, Label)`
  2. Harness → orchestrator: `run(repos, limits)`
  3. orchestrator → MockLinear: `pending_issues` → `[Issue]`
  4. orchestrator → MockLinear: `set_state(Todo → In-Progress)`
  5. orchestrator → claude -p: `spawn`
  6. claude -p → linear_mcp_shim: `get_issue(id)` (dashed = MCP)
  7. linear_mcp_shim → MockLinear: `GraphQL get_issue`
  8. claude -p → linear_mcp_shim: `save_issue(Done)` + `save_comment(...)`
  9. linear_mcp_shim → MockLinear: `GraphQL set_state(Done)`, `comment_create`
  10. orchestrator → MockLinear: `get_issue` (verify Done) → orchestrator returns `Result`

Caption: "End-to-end happy path. The parent (left) hits `MockLinear` directly over HTTP; the child (right) hits it indirectly via `linear_mcp_shim` over stdio MCP. Both write into the same in-memory store, so scenarios assert on one unified call log."

### Edit 3 — Failure-mode coverage matrix (new H2 "Failure-mode coverage")

Placement: at the top of §Scenario catalogue, before the existing groupings (Best case / Per-issue halts / etc.). The catalogue then reads as filling in the matrix.

Heading: `## Failure-mode coverage`

Body: an introductory sentence ("The matrix below names every halt path in the production code and the scenario that covers it. KR 2's halt-coverage guard fails the suite when a new halt slug lands without a matching row.") then a markdown table:

| Halt path | Source | Scenario | Spawns claude? | Slice |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Missing `repo:` label | `orchestrator.py` repo-resolve | `test_pre_spawn_halts::test_missing_repo_label` | no | 7 |
| `repo:` label → nonexistent path | `orchestrator.py` repo-resolve | `test_pre_spawn_halts::test_unknown_repo_path` | no | 7 |
| Worktree `add` fails | `orchestrator.py` worktree | `test_pre_spawn_halts::test_dirty_worktree` | no | 7 |
| `set_state` (Todo→In-Progress) HTTP 500 | `linear.py` set_state | `test_pre_spawn_halts::test_set_state_500` | no | 6 |
| Child exits without setting Done | `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` | `test_real_claude_halts::test_not_done_exit` | yes (`--max-turns 1`) | 8 |
| Child exits non-zero | `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` | `test_real_claude_halts::test_child_nonzero` | yes | 8 |
| Per-issue token cap exceeded | `worker.py` breach loop | `test_breach::test_token_cap` | yes (verbose prompt) | 9 |
| Per-issue time cap exceeded | `worker.py` breach loop | `test_breach::test_time_cap` | yes (`wait 60s` prompt) | 9 |
| Revert `set_state` (In-Progress→Todo) fails after halt | `orchestrator.py` revert | `test_real_claude_halts::test_revert_failure` | either | 8 |
| `DependencyCycleError` from `pending_issues` | `linear.py` pending_issues | `test_cycle_halts::test_dep_cycle` | no | 10 |
| Cycle-cap breach after N Done | `worker.py` cycle accumulator | `test_cycle_halts::test_cycle_cap` | yes | 10 |
| Linear 429 / malformed JSON / slow-beyond-timeout | `linear._post` | `test_linear_edges::*` | no | 12 |

The table is intentionally plain markdown — render-html's shell styles tables cleanly out of the box (no pattern needed). Cells with `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*` tool names use inline code formatting.

Footnote under the table: "Source paths are illustrative — the halt-coverage guard (`test_halt_coverage`) reads them from `mark_error` slugs in `drain_cycle/**.py` at suite time. A new halt slug with no matching row fails the suite."

### Edit 4 — Render to HTML

Run `/render-html docs/integration-test-architecture.md`. The skill writes `docs/integration-test-architecture.html` next to the source, self-contained, with:

- P14 fires on §System context → SVG diagram wrapped in `.p-diagram` with caption.
- P14 fires on §Pillar 2's `### Happy-path sequence` H3 → second SVG wrapped likewise. (P14 detects per-section, not just H2 — confirmed in `html-patterns.md`.)
- §Failure-mode coverage table → shell-default `<table>` styling.
- TOC sidebar includes "System context", "Failure-mode coverage" as new top-level H2 entries.

Open the file in the browser, verify TOC navigation works and both SVGs render. Skill workflow already requires the user-facing handoff sentence ("Open the file in your browser to verify TOC navigation and that no console errors appear.").

## Critical files

To modify:
- `docs/integration-test-architecture.md` — three new sections (System context, Happy-path sequence inside §Pillar 2, Failure-mode coverage at top of §Scenario catalogue). No existing prose is rewritten; the additions are insertions.

To create (by `/render-html`, not by hand):
- `docs/integration-test-architecture.html` — self-contained HTML render.

## Authoring notes for the SVGs

- Use `viewBox="0 0 860 340"` and the node/edge classes from P14 (`p-node`, `p-edge`, `p-edge-async`, `p-node-emphasis`). Don't introduce custom hex colours — colours come from the shell's `:root` palette (`--accent`, `--ok`, `--muted`, etc.). Verified via `references/html-patterns.md` P14.
- Cap each diagram at ~7 nodes / ~10 edges per P14's authoring guidance. Both diagrams hit that.
- Edge labels ≤ 24 chars; longer text goes in the caption.
- Each `<svg>` carries `role="img"` and a short `aria-label`; the caption beneath is the long description.

## Verification

End-to-end:
- `cat docs/integration-test-architecture.md | grep -c '```svg'` returns `2` — both SVG blocks land.
- `cat docs/integration-test-architecture.md | grep -c '^## '` increases by 2 (System context + Failure-mode coverage).
- `/render-html docs/integration-test-architecture.md` runs without warnings.
- `docs/integration-test-architecture.html` exists; size under 500KB (P14's hard cap from the skill).
- Open in browser:
  - Network panel: zero requests after load (self-containment rule).
  - Both SVGs render visibly with arrows + labels.
  - TOC sidebar lists the two new H2s.
  - Failure-mode-coverage table is legible at desktop and narrow widths.
- Original markdown is still review-readable (no skill-only structure that breaks on GitHub).

No production code changes. No tests added. No commits — the doc + HTML are local artefacts the user reviews before deciding to commit.

1	# Integration test architecture
2	
3	**Linear initiative:** [Test drain-cycle failure modes and resume without a real Linear cycle](https://linear.app/ababushkin/project/test-drain-cycle-failure-modes-and-resume-without-a-real-linear-cycle-910b954af13f)
4	
5	The OKR (Goal + Key Results + appetite + kill condition) is on the Linear project. This doc is the design — what gets built, why, in what order — and is the input to `shape-planning-and-task-breakdown` when the initiative is sliced into issues.
6	
7	## Context
8	
9	Today `drain-cycle` only tests against a real Linear cycle. Anything past happy-path becomes expensive: failure modes need deliberately broken issues, resume needs an actual halt-and-restart, and usage tracking needs real Claude sessions. Unit tests cover individual modules but stop at the function boundary — they monkey-patch `linear._post` and swap `claude -p` for marker-touching shell scripts (`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22`, `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65`).
10	
11	We need **scenario-level integration tests** that drive the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, covering:
12	
13	- **Best-case drains** — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions
14	- **Per-issue failure paths** — every halt condition in `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` (repo resolution, worktree setup, `set_state` failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)
15	- **Cycle-level halts** — `DependencyCycleError`, cycle-cap breach propagation
16	- **Resume semantics** — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo
17	- **Session/usage tracking** — `_UsageAccumulator` totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches
18	
19	The drain has no native retry (deliberate, per `docs/design-decisions.md`); "retry" here means **resume** — a re-invocation against the same cycle.
20	
21	## System context
22	
23	The seam choice in one picture: real `claude -p`, mocked Linear, glued by an MCP shim that translates child MCP calls into GraphQL against the same in-memory store the parent hits over HTTP.

96	
97	Solid = control / data flow over HTTP or subprocess. Dashed (accent) = MCP stdio between the child and the shim. The dashed boundary marks what's mocked: every byte that would otherwise hit `api.linear.app` is intercepted, but `claude -p` itself is the production binary every run. Both the parent's HTTP calls and the child's MCP calls land in one shared store, so scenarios assert against one unified call log.
98	
99	## Workflow
100	
101	1. **`/shape-initiative`** — done. Linear project created with the six-field OKR (linked above).
102	2. **`shape-planning-and-task-breakdown`** — convert the *Slices* section below into Linear issues under the new initiative; assign slices 1–4 to the current cycle, 5–12 to the backlog.
103	3. **`agent-skills:test-driven-development`** during build — each slice ships a failing scenario before the supporting infra.
104	4. **`agent-skills:code-review-and-quality`** per slice before commit, per `AGENTS.md`.
105	
106	## Approach
107	
108	**Parent HTTP mock + Linear-MCP shim + real `claude -p` + in-process driver.** The spawned session is real claude every time; only the Linear surface is mocked. Three pillars.
109	
110	**Alternatives rejected** (draft for the ADR slice):
111	
112	- **Scripted/fake `claude -p`.** Cheaper, faster, deterministic. Rejected because a scripted child stops being an integration test — it cannot validate the real session's usage stream, MCP tool calls, prompt-to-completion behaviour, or production OTel spans against actual data.
113	- **Hybrid: real claude only on happy-path; scripted child for failure determinism.** Two patterns to maintain; weaker default signal; gives up real-claude coverage on usage-tracking edge cases.
114	- **Function-injection / `LinearClient` protocol on the parent.** Cheaper than HTTP but skips the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. The existing module-level functions in `linear.py` drive easily against an HTTP fake once `LINEAR_API_URL` is overridable.
115	- **VCR-style recorded fixtures.** Realistic but awkward for failure injection, expensive to maintain under schema drift.
116	
117	Slice 1 produces a short ADR at `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, these rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.
118	
119	### Cost, runtime, and determinism
120	
121	Real claude makes the suite costly, slow, and non-deterministic on numbers.
122	
123	- **Cost / runtime budget**: ~$0.05–$0.20 per spawning scenario, ~15–30 seconds per scenario. Full suite of ~30 scenarios: ~$1–$3 and ~15 minutes. Run on demand, not as part of the default suite.
124	- **Default `pytest` run** covers only the pre-spawn halt scenarios (no claude spawned) — fast and deterministic, suitable for repeated local invocation while iterating.
125	- **Determinism strategy** — split assertions:
126	  - **Exact** — exit codes, final Linear state, halt reason class, runlog file presence, structural shape of runlog JSON, set of MCP tool calls observed.
127	  - **Predicate** — `tokens > 0`, `0 < cost_usd < per_issue_cost_usd`, `num_turns >= 1`, `model is not None`. No exact values.
128	  - **Tolerant snapshot** — `dirty-equals` matches keys and types, ignores values.
129	- **Cost guard**: scenarios that spawn claude carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude`. Default `pytest` invocation excludes the marker; the `real_claude` slice opts in via `pytest -m real_claude` and runs on demand. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` required.
130	
131	### Pillar 1 — `MockLinear`: an in-process fake GraphQL server
132	
133	A FastAPI/Starlette app on a random localhost port, started per-test via a pytest fixture. It speaks the five GraphQL operations `linear.py` uses (`current_cycle`, `pending_issues`, `get_issue`, `set_state`, `resolve_state` / WorkflowState lookup) plus an `IssueUpdate` mutation the scripted child hits to flip an issue to Done.
134	
135	State lives in a typed in-memory store: `Cycle`, `Issue`, `Label`, `WorkflowState`. The store records every call (operation, variables, timestamp) so tests can assert call sequence and counts.
136	
137	Fault injection is **per-operation, per-call-index**: `mock.fail_after(op="set_state", call=2, mode="http_500")`. Modes: `http_500`, `http_429`, `graphql_error`, `malformed_json`, `slow_response(seconds)`, `connection_reset`. The drain has no retry, so "fail" means "this single call breaks" — tests then verify the halt path.
138	
139	**Production change required**: `linear.py:18` hard-codes `https://api.linear.app/graphql`. Replace with `os.environ.get("LINEAR_API_URL", "https://api.linear.app/graphql")`. No CLI flag — env var only.
140	
141	### Pillar 2 — `linear_mcp_shim`: a Linear MCP server backed by the mock
142	
143	A small Python stdio MCP server (built on the official `mcp` SDK) that exposes the two tools the drain-cycle prompt uses today: `save_issue` (state transitions) and `save_comment`. Each tool translates its call into a GraphQL POST against `MockLinear`, sharing the same backing store as the parent's HTTP surface.
144	
145	The harness writes a custom `.mcp.json` into each test worktree registering the shim under the name `linear` (matching the existing tool prefix `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*`). The orchestrator already symlinks `.mcp.json` from the configured source into the worktree (`orchestrator.py:257-259`); the harness arranges for the test source to be the shim-configured file.
146	
147	The shim records every tool call (tool name, arguments, timestamp) on the shared store, so scenarios assert against both parent GraphQL calls and child MCP calls through one call log.
148	
149	**Why an MCP shim and not just `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child env.** MCP is a separate protocol from raw GraphQL; the official Linear MCP server speaks MCP over stdio to claude and HTTPS to Linear. Setting `LINEAR_API_URL` in the child has no effect — there's no way to redirect the official server without forking it. A purpose-built shim is the smallest seam.
150	
151	#### Happy-path sequence
