224	
225	The parent (left half) talks to `MockLinear` directly over HTTP. The child (right half) reaches the same store indirectly, via `linear_mcp_shim` over MCP stdio. Both write into the in-memory store, so the test's call log records the full conversation across both surfaces — the parent's `pending_issues` / `set_state` calls and the child's `save_issue` / `save_comment` calls interleave in one ordered list that scenarios assert against.
226	
227	### How scenarios steer claude
228	
229	Scenarios drive child behaviour through three knobs the harness sets per-scenario:
230	
231	- **Issue body** — written into `MockLinear` and read by claude via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__get_issue`. The body is the natural prompt seam (e.g. "create `<id>.txt` containing 'hello'; then mark this issue Done"). Library lives in `tests/integration/prompts/*.md`.
232	- **Per-issue limits** — `limits.yml` overrides (e.g. `per_issue_tokens=2000`, `per_issue_seconds=30`) used with verbose prompts to provoke breach scenarios deterministically.
233	- **Claude flags** — the harness may inject `--max-turns 1` for "exit without Done" cases (claude exits before completing the workflow), via the `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `orchestrator.py:25`.
234	
235	The drain-cycle prompt (`drain_cycle/prompt.py:39-42`) stays unchanged — same prompt the real drain uses.
236	
237	### Pillar 3 — `IntegrationHarness`: a fixture that drives `orchestrator.run()` in-process
238	
239	A pytest fixture that:
240	
241	- Creates a temp `HOME` so `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`, `~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml`, and `~/.drain-cycle/runs/` redirect to a tmpdir (`monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)` plus any path-resolution helpers in `cli.py` / `runlog.py`).
242	- Initialises one or more real git repos in tmpdir (existing pattern in `test_orchestrator_iteration.py`).
243	- Writes a `repos.yml` mapping label names to those repo paths.
244	- Writes a `.mcp.json` in each repo registering the Linear-MCP shim under `linear`.
245	- Starts `MockLinear` and populates it with the scenario's cycle, issues (with prompt body), and labels.
246	- Sets `LINEAR_API_URL`, `LINEAR_API_KEY=test`, `MOCK_LINEAR_URL=http://localhost:<port>`, `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` env vars. Verifies `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set for scenarios that spawn claude.
247	- Optionally appends extra flags to `_CLAUDE_CMD` (e.g. `--max-turns 1`) for scenarios that need to coerce claude's behaviour.
248	- Calls `orchestrator.run(repos, limits)` directly and returns a `Result`: exit code, runlog JSON, parent GraphQL call log, child MCP call log, final mock state.
249	
250	Tests assert against `Result`. One scenario per `test_*` function. Helpers (`make_cycle`, `make_issue`, `expect_runlog`, `expect_calls`) keep scenarios short.
251	
252	**Per-scenario contract.** Every scenario declares:
253	
254	1. **Setup** — `Cycle`, `Issue[]`, `Label[]`, `WorkflowState[]`, repos.yml mapping, limits.yml overrides, transcript fixture name(s), any fault injection.
255	2. **Expected exit code** — orchestrator return.
256	3. **Expected runlog assertions** — a list of (a) structural shape (keys, types) matched via `dirty-equals`, (b) predicate assertions on numeric fields (`tokens > 0`, `cost_usd < limits.per_issue_cost_usd`), (c) exact assertions on deterministic fields (`final_linear_state`, `halt_reason`).
257	4. **Expected final mock state** — issue states the mock should end in (e.g. `{ "ABA-1": "Done", "ABA-2": "Todo" }` after a halt on issue 2).
258	5. **Expected call sequence** — ordered `(surface, operation, predicate-on-args)` tuples across both parent GraphQL and child MCP calls. Strict by default; `expect_calls(strict=False)` for tolerant cases where claude's tool-call order varies between runs.
259	
260	A collection-time lint test fails any scenario missing (3), (4), or (5).
261	
262	## Production code changes required
263	
264	Minimal. Every test-only seam reuses existing structure.
265	
266	1. **`drain_cycle/linear.py:18`** — `_GRAPHQL_URL` reads from `LINEAR_API_URL` env var, default unchanged. One-line change.
267	2. **`drain_cycle/runlog.py`** — verify the run-log directory honours `HOME` re-resolution; slice 1 greps for `Path.home`, `expanduser`, and `HOME`. If the path caches at module load, add an accessor (e.g. `_run_log_dir()`) so the harness re-resolves after `monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)`.
268	3. **`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`** — `_CLAUDE_CMD` is already module-level. Tests patch it.
269	
270	No new abstraction layers, no `LinearClient` Protocol — the existing module-level functions drive easily against an HTTP fake once the URL is configurable.
271	
272	## Test-infra correctness checks (one-time pre-slice work)
273	
274	Risks the plan-review surfaced. Each lands before dependent slices.
275	
276	- **Telemetry isolation** — drain-cycle wires httpx OTel auto-instrumentation in `telemetry.py`. The harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` to keep tests from exporting spans. Confirmed in slice 2.
277	- **MCP-shim correctness** — the shim must implement the same tool schemas the official Linear MCP exposes for `save_issue` and `save_comment`, otherwise claude's tool calls land with wrong shapes. Slice 3 spike: capture the official schemas (from the Linear MCP docs or by running the real one briefly), implement matching ones in the shim, verify a single happy-path drain reaches Done end-to-end. **Kill-switch**: if the schemas drift between Linear MCP versions, pin the shim to a captured snapshot and add a periodic check.
278	- **Breach scenario reproducibility** — with real claude, breach scenarios depend on prompts that reliably exceed tight per-issue caps. Slice 8 spike: confirm a verbose prompt (e.g. "write a 2000-line poem") plus `per_issue_tokens=1000` reliably triggers token breach across 5 consecutive runs. **Kill-switch**: if not reliable, fall back to time-breach only (tight `per_issue_seconds` plus a "sleep 60s" prompt is robust because the breach loop polls wall-clock).
279	
280	## Failure-mode coverage
281	
282	The matrix below names every halt path in the production code and the scenario that covers it. KR 2's halt-coverage guard fails the suite when a new halt slug lands without a matching row.
283	
284	| Halt path | Source | Scenario | Spawns claude? | Slice |
285	|---|---|---|---|---|
286	| Missing `repo:` label | `orchestrator.py` repo-resolve | `test_pre_spawn_halts::test_missing_repo_label` | no | 7 |
287	| `repo:` label → nonexistent path | `orchestrator.py` repo-resolve | `test_pre_spawn_halts::test_unknown_repo_path` | no | 7 |
288	| Worktree `add` fails | `orchestrator.py` worktree | `test_pre_spawn_halts::test_dirty_worktree` | no | 7 |
289	| `set_state` (Todo→In-Progress) HTTP 500 | `linear.py` set_state | `test_pre_spawn_halts::test_set_state_500` | no | 6 |
290	| Child exits without setting Done | `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` | `test_real_claude_halts::test_not_done_exit` | yes (`--max-turns 1`) | 8 |
291	| Child exits non-zero | `orchestrator._drain_one_issue` | `test_real_claude_halts::test_child_nonzero` | yes | 8 |
292	| Per-issue token cap exceeded | `worker.py` breach loop | `test_breach::test_token_cap` | yes (verbose prompt) | 9 |
293	| Per-issue time cap exceeded | `worker.py` breach loop | `test_breach::test_time_cap` | yes (`wait 60s` prompt) | 9 |
294	| Revert `set_state` (In-Progress→Todo) fails after halt | `orchestrator.py` revert | `test_real_claude_halts::test_revert_failure` | either | 8 |
295	| `DependencyCycleError` from `pending_issues` | `linear.py` pending_issues | `test_cycle_halts::test_dep_cycle` | no | 10 |
296	| Cycle-cap breach after N Done | `worker.py` cycle accumulator | `test_cycle_halts::test_cycle_cap` | yes | 10 |
297	| Linear 429 / malformed JSON / slow-beyond-timeout | `linear._post` | `test_linear_edges::*` | no | 12 |
298	
299	Source paths are illustrative — the halt-coverage guard (`test_halt_coverage`) reads them from `mark_error` slugs in `drain_cycle/**.py` at suite time. A new halt slug with no matching row fails the suite loudly.
300	
301	## Scenario catalogue (initial coverage)
302	
303	Grouped by area; one test function each. Target end-state, not a single slice — slices below build the infra and add scenarios incrementally.
304	
305	Scenarios that spawn `claude -p` carry `@pytest.mark.real_claude` (excluded from the default `pytest` run; opt-in via `-m real_claude`). Scenarios that halt pre-spawn run on every default invocation.
306	
307	**Best case** *(real_claude)*
308	- 3-issue dependency-ordered drain reaches Done in order; runlog totals positive and sum across issues; child MCP calls include `save_issue` (transition to Done) and `save_comment` (summary) per issue
309	- Mixed Todo + Backlog states picked up; correct planning
310	- Single-issue drain
311	- Empty cycle exits cleanly *(no claude spawned)*
312	
313	**Per-issue halts** (`orchestrator._drain_one_issue` paths)
314	- Missing `repo:` label → halt + reason *(no claude spawned)*
315	- `repo:` label resolves to nonexistent path → halt *(no claude spawned)*
316	- Worktree `add` fails (dirty tree) → halt *(no claude spawned)*
317	- `set_state` (Todo→In-Progress) fails with HTTP 500 → halt *(no claude spawned)*
318	- Child exits without setting Done *(real_claude; coerced via `--max-turns 1`)* → halt + reason matches `final_linear_state`
319	- Child exits non-zero *(real_claude; provoked by an invalid prompt or a tool that errors)* → halt
320	- Per-issue token cap exceeded mid-stream *(real_claude; verbose prompt + `per_issue_tokens=1000`)* → breach kill; runlog records breach scope/metric/limit/observed
321	- Per-issue time cap exceeded *(real_claude; prompt "wait 60s" + `per_issue_seconds=10`)* → time breach recorded
322	- Revert `set_state` (In-Progress→Todo) fails after a halt → halt-reason concatenates *(real_claude or pre-spawn)*
323	
324	**Cycle-level halts**
325	- `DependencyCycleError` from `pending_issues` → halt before any issue attempted; `cycle_halt_reason` set; runlog otherwise empty *(no claude spawned)*
326	- After two Done issues, cycle-cap breach detected → third issue not attempted; halt reason names cumulative metric *(real_claude)*
327	
328	**Resume** *(real_claude)*
329	- Run halts on issue 2; re-run with the same cycle picks up issue 2 (mock has it back at Todo via the revert) and issue 3; second runlog file separate from first; final mock state correct
330	
331	**Session / usage tracking** *(real_claude; predicate assertions on numbers)*
332	- Single-issue drain produces `usage.cumulative.input_tokens > 0`, `usage.cumulative.output_tokens > 0`, `peak_context > 0`, `cost_usd > 0`, `num_turns >= 1`, `session_id` non-empty, `is_error == false`, `model` non-empty
333	- Multi-issue drain: cycle aggregates (`cycle_tokens_cumulative`, `cycle_cost_usd`, `cycle_duration_seconds`) equal the sum of per-issue values
334	- Worker dedup invariant (`worker.py:416`) verified indirectly: re-emit a non-deterministic prompt and confirm `usage.cumulative` is monotonically non-decreasing within a turn; explicit dedup behaviour stays unit-tested
335	
336	**Linear edge cases** *(no claude spawned — parent halts before pre-spawn `set_state` succeeds)*
337	- 500, 429, GraphQL error, malformed JSON, slow-within-timeout (drain proceeds), slow-beyond-timeout (halts)
338	
339	**Out of scope for MVP**
340	- Honeycomb/OTel span assertions (separate slice — needs a fake OTLP collector; production code wires spans cleanly per `drain_cycle/telemetry.py`)
341	- True MCP-server fake for children (scripted-child + direct GraphQL POST satisfies the contract)
342	
343	**In MVP, bounded** — slice 4 ships one subprocess smoke test that spawns the real `drain-cycle` binary against the mock and asserts exit code plus runlog presence. This single test covers `cli.py`'s secrets-load order and process exit-code path. Larger subprocess scenarios stay out of scope.
344	
345	**Note on the worker `_UsageAccumulator` dedup invariant** — the worker dedupes by `message.id` (`worker.py:416`). Real claude rarely emits duplicate ids in practice, so the dedup branch is exercised by existing unit tests, not by integration scenarios. Plan accordingly: keep the dedup unit test (`test_usage_accumulator_dedup.py` or equivalent); the integration suite asserts on monotonicity, not on dedup directly.
346	
347	## Slices (verifiable build order)
348	
349	Each slice ships in one session and ends with a passing scenario.
350	
351	1. **Production seam + ADR** — `LINEAR_API_URL` env var in `linear.py`; unit test confirms override; default unchanged. Runlog `HOME` re-resolution verified (grep + accessor if needed). **Deliverable**: `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` recording the chosen approach, rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.
352	2. **`MockLinear` skeleton** — FastAPI app with `current_cycle` and `pending_issues` only; serves a hard-coded scenario; one unit test wires through `linear.py` and confirms the call lands on the mock. Harness sets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true`.
353	3. **`linear_mcp_shim` + first real-claude drain** — stdio MCP server implementing `save_issue` and `save_comment` against the mock; harness writes a `.mcp.json` registering it; first **real-claude** happy-path drain of one issue reaches Done end-to-end. Schemas captured from the official Linear MCP. Marked `@pytest.mark.real_claude`.
354	4. **`IntegrationHarness` fixture + subprocess smoke** — tmpdir HOME, repos.yml, real git repo, `.mcp.json` writer, mock startup. Slice 3's scenario refactored onto the harness. **Plus**: one subprocess test spawns `drain-cycle` against the mock end-to-end and asserts exit code plus runlog file existence.
355	5. **Mock GraphQL completeness + drift guard** — add `get_issue`, `set_state`, WorkflowState resolution; happy-path drain of 3 ordered issues passes. **Plus**: `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler.
356	6. **Fault injection** — per-operation, per-call-index fault modes in the mock; scenario covers `set_state` HTTP 500 halt (pre-spawn, fast). Per-scenario contract enforced (runlog assertions, mock state, call sequence).
357	7. **Pre-spawn halt scenarios** — repo-resolution failures, worktree-add failure, set_state 500. Fast, no claude.
358	8. **Real-claude halt scenarios** — child exits without Done (via `--max-turns 1`); child non-zero exit (via invalid prompt). Marked `real_claude`.
359	9. **Breach scenarios** — time-breach first (tight `per_issue_seconds` plus "wait 60s" prompt — robust). Token-breach gated on the slice-8 reproducibility spike. Both `real_claude`.
360	10. **Cycle-level halt scenarios** — `DependencyCycleError` (no claude); cycle-cap breach after two Done issues (`real_claude`).
361	11. **Resume scenario** — drive a halt, re-invoke orchestrator on the same mock cycle, assert second runlog file and final state. `real_claude`.
362	12. **Usage-tracking + Linear edge-case scenarios** — usage predicates on a happy-path drain (`real_claude`); 429 / malformed / slow-timeout halts on the parent (no claude).
363	
364	Slices 1–4 are the foundation; 5–12 are independent and ship in any order once 4 lands. Each slice opens a Linear issue under the initiative and closes per `AGENTS.md` (review → fix → commit + push → Linear summary comment → Done).
365	
366	## Critical files
367	
368	To modify:
369	- `drain_cycle/linear.py` — env-var override for GraphQL URL (slice 1)
370	- `drain_cycle/runlog.py` — verify `HOME` re-resolution; add small accessor only if cached at module load (slice 1)
371	
372	To create:
373	- `docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md` — chosen approach + rejected alternatives + revisit conditions (slice 1)
374	- `tests/integration/conftest.py` — harness fixture, `real_claude` marker config
375	- `tests/integration/mock_linear.py` — FastAPI GraphQL server + shared in-memory store + fault injection
376	- `tests/integration/linear_mcp_shim.py` — stdio MCP server backed by the shared store
377	- `tests/integration/prompts/*.md` — issue-body prompts (happy_path, exit_without_done, verbose, wait, etc.)
378	- `tests/integration/test_happy_path.py`, `test_pre_spawn_halts.py`, `test_real_claude_halts.py`, `test_breach.py`, `test_cycle_halts.py`, `test_resume.py`, `test_usage_tracking.py`, `test_linear_edges.py`, `test_linear_surface_audit.py`, `test_subprocess_smoke.py` — scenarios
379	
380	To reuse from existing tests:
381	- Real git-repo setup pattern in `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22-90`
382	- Linear fixture style in `tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65-70`
383	- `_CLAUDE_CMD` patch point at `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25`
384	
385	## Verification
386	
387	End-to-end:
388	- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -v` — non-`real_claude` scenarios green; no real Linear network calls; no real `LINEAR_API_KEY` leaking in. The default local invocation.
389	- `uv run pytest tests/integration/ -m real_claude -v` — full suite green against a real Anthropic API key. Run on demand; instructions live in `tests/integration/README.md`.
390	- `uv run pytest --cov=drain_cycle/orchestrator --cov=drain_cycle/worker --cov=drain_cycle/runlog tests/integration/` — coverage on orchestrator, worker, and runlog rises meaningfully.
391	- Each slice closes per `AGENTS.md`: review → fix → commit + push to main → Linear summary comment → Done.
392	
393	**Halt-coverage assertion** (enforces KR 2):
394	- `test_halt_coverage` greps `mark_error` slugs from `drain_cycle/**.py`, collects the union of `exception.slug` attributes (or `halt_reason` strings) observed across the full integration run (including `real_claude`), and asserts equality. Fails locally when a new halt slug lands in production code without a covering scenario.
395	
396	Negative checks during build:
397	- `grep -r "api.linear.app" tests/` returns nothing — no accidental real-Linear calls in fixtures.
398	- The mock's call log (parent GraphQL + child MCP) records every operation a scenario expects; an unexpected call fails the test loudly.
399	- `test_linear_surface_audit` fails when any `_post(operation=...)` call site in `linear.py` lacks a mock handler.
400	- Cost budget guard: the `real_claude` suite prints total Anthropic spend at the end of each run; manual review flags runs over $5 (catch a runaway loop scenario early).

1	---
2	name: html-skeleton
3	description: >
4	  Self-contained HTML shell used by the render-html skill. Defines the single-file
5	  document template — the product-UI visual system (Inter sans, indigo accent,
6	  numbered sidebar nav with scrollspy, pill + metadata-grid utilities, section
7	  headers with index + tag, dark-mode auto, print stylesheet) — plus the inline
8	  scrollspy JS and the hard self-containment rule.
9	type: reference
10	cited_by:
11	  - skills/render-html/SKILL.md
12	---
13	
14	# HTML skeleton
15	
16	The render-html skill wraps every markdown body in the shell below. The shell is the **only** mechanism by which output styling and navigation arrive — no external CSS, no external JS, no remote images. That rule is load-bearing for the skill's "opens via `file://` with no network" guarantee; reviewers attach these files to emails and store them in private wikis where CDN URLs silently break.
17	
18	The visual language is **product-UI**: Inter (system fallback), tight type, indigo accent, sidebar with numbered nav and scrollspy, status pills and metadata grids as first-class. Reads as a Linear/Stripe spec page rather than a markdown previewer. Pattern treatments (P1–P12) extend this language; they don't introduce a competing one.
19	
20	## The hard rule
21	
22	Anything injected into the rendered output must satisfy all three:
23	
24	- **No `<link rel="stylesheet">`.** All CSS lives in the inline `<style>` block.
25	- **No `<script src=...>`.** All JS lives in the inline `<script>` block at the end of `<body>`.
26	- **No remote `<img>` URLs.** Inline SVG is preferred; base64 data URIs are allowed when an SVG is impractical. Remote URLs (`http:`, `https:`, protocol-relative `//`) are not.
27	
28	If a future pattern needs richer interactivity (charts, diagrams, syntax highlighting), inline the minimal library or hand-write the SVG. Adding a CDN is not an option.
29	
30	## Substitution tokens
31	
32	| Token | Source | Notes |
33	|---|---|---|
34	| `{{title}}` | The source markdown's first H1 | Goes in both `<title>` and the page `<h1>`. |
35	| `{{breadcrumb}}` | Inferred from path (e.g. `docs/adr/001-...` → `Architecture · ADRs · ADR-001`) or empty | Optional; render empty `<div>` if nothing meaningful to show. |
36	| `{{header-pills}}` | Status pill (P10) + identifier pill + date pill, in that order | Rendered into the `.pill-row` slot. Empty if none apply. |
37	| `{{meta-grid}}` | Definition-list rows for `Source`, `Rendered`, plus any frontmatter fields after P9 lifts them | The grid is always rendered; if only `Source` and `Rendered` apply, it shows two rows. |
38	| `{{toc-entries}}` | One `<li>` per H2 (and indented H3), with anchor href to the heading's slug | H1 is the title, not a TOC entry. Slugs are kebab-case, deduplicated `-2`, `-3`. |
39	| `{{status-block}}` | One-line aside under "Status" kicker in the sidebar (e.g. "Accepted 31 Mar 2026. No superseding ADR.") | Empty `<div>` if no status info. |
40	| `{{body-html}}` | The markdown body converted to HTML, with H2/H3 carrying `id="<slug>"` and wrapped per `html-patterns.md` | Pattern markup substitutes here. |
41	| `{{source-path}}` | Path of the input `.md` file, relative to the repo root if possible | Shown in the metadata grid and footer. |
42	| `{{render-date}}` | ISO date (YYYY-MM-DD) of the render | Provenance only — re-renders on the same day produce no diff. |
43	
44	## The shell
45	
46	```html

48	<html lang="en">
49	<head>
50	<meta charset="utf-8">
51	<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">
52	<title>{{title}}</title>
53	<style>

158	
159	  /* ---------- Section header (utility for pattern + prose alike) ---------- */
160	  section.s { margin: 56px 0; scroll-margin-top: 20px; }
161	  section.s > header { display: flex; align-items: baseline; gap: 12px; margin-bottom: 18px; flex-wrap: wrap; }
162	  section.s .ix { font: 700 12px/1 ui-monospace, monospace; color: var(--muted); padding: 4px 8px; border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 4px; flex-shrink: 0; }
163	  main h2 { font-size: 22px; line-height: 1.3; margin: 0; letter-spacing: -0.01em; font-weight: 700; color: var(--fg); }
164	  main h3 { font-size: 17px; line-height: 1.4; margin: 28px 0 10px; font-weight: 650; color: var(--fg); }
165	
166	  /* ---------- Prose defaults ---------- */
167	  /* Body paragraphs use --fg-soft (13:1 on --bg) — easier on the eye than
168	     pure --fg but still well above AA. Lists inherit. */
169	  main p { margin: 0 0 16px; color: var(--fg-soft); }
170	  main ul, main ol { margin: 0 0 16px; padding-left: 26px; color: var(--fg-soft); }
171	  main li { margin: 6px 0; }
172	  main pre { background: var(--rule-soft); padding: 16px 18px; border-radius: 8px; overflow-x: auto; font-size: 14px; line-height: 1.6; border: 1px solid var(--rule); }
173	  main code { font-size: 0.92em; padding: 2px 6px; background: var(--rule-soft); border-radius: 4px; color: var(--fg); }
174	  main pre code { padding: 0; background: transparent; font-size: 1em; }
175	  main blockquote { margin: 18px 0; padding: 10px 18px; border-left: 4px solid var(--accent); color: var(--fg-soft); background: var(--accent-soft); border-radius: 0 6px 6px 0; }
176	  main table { border-collapse: collapse; margin: 16px 0; width: 100%; font-size: 14.5px; }
177	  main th, main td { border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); padding: 10px 14px; text-align: left; }
178	  main th { background: var(--rule-soft); font-weight: 700; color: var(--fg); font-size: 13px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.04em; }
179	  main hr { border: none; border-top: 1px solid var(--rule); margin: 40px 0; }
180	
181	  /* ---------- Footer ---------- */
182	  footer { grid-column: 1 / -1; padding: 24px 56px; color: var(--muted); font-size: 13px; border-top: 1px solid var(--rule); }
183	  footer code { background: transparent; padding: 0; color: var(--fg-soft); }
184	
185	  /* ---------- Print ---------- */
186	  @media print {
187	    aside { display: none; }
188	    .layout { grid-template-columns: 1fr; }
189	    main { padding: 24px; max-width: none; }
190	  }
191	
192	  /* ---------- Responsive ---------- */
193	  @media (max-width: 880px) {
194	    .layout { grid-template-columns: 1fr; }
195	    aside { position: static; height: auto; border-right: none; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); padding: 20px; }
196	    aside nav ol { display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap; gap: 4px; }
197	    aside .kicker { margin-top: 16px; }
198	    main { padding: 28px 24px 60px; }
199	  }
200	</style>
201	</head>
202	<body>
203	<div class="layout">
204	  <aside>
205	    <div class="brand"><span class="dot"></span><span>{{breadcrumb-short}}</span></div>
206	    <p class="kicker">On this page</p>
207	    <nav aria-label="Table of contents">
208	      <ol>
209	        {{toc-entries}}
210	      </ol>
211	    </nav>
212	    <p class="kicker">Status</p>
213	    <div class="status-block">{{status-block}}</div>
214	  </aside>
215	
216	  <main>
217	    <div class="doc-head">
218	      <div class="breadcrumbs">{{breadcrumb}}</div>
219	      <h1>{{title}}</h1>
220	      <div class="pill-row">{{header-pills}}</div>
221	      <dl class="meta-grid">{{meta-grid}}</dl>
222	    </div>
223	
224	    {{body-html}}
225	  </main>
226	
227	  <footer>Rendered from <code>{{source-path}}</code> on {{render-date}} · regenerate to update.</footer>
228	</div>
229	
230	<script>
231	  // Scrollspy: highlight the TOC entry for the section currently in view.
232	  const links = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('aside nav a'));
233	  const targets = links.map(a => document.getElementById(a.getAttribute('href').slice(1))).filter(Boolean);
234	  const byId = new Map(targets.map((el, i) => [el.id, links[i]]));
235	  const io = new IntersectionObserver(entries => {
236	    for (const e of entries) {
237	      if (e.isIntersecting) {
238	        links.forEach(a => a.classList.remove('active'));
239	        byId.get(e.target.id)?.classList.add('active');
240	      }
241	    }
242	  }, { rootMargin: '-30% 0px -60% 0px' });
243	  targets.forEach(t => io.observe(t));
244	</script>
245	</body>
246	</html>
247	```
248	
249	## Design notes
250	
251	- **Inter via system fallback** — the font stack starts with `"Inter"` for environments where it's installed; otherwise falls back to `ui-sans-serif` / `system-ui`. The `font-feature-settings` lines (`cv11`, `ss01`, `ss03`) activate Inter's curly-l, single-storey-a, and rounded variants when Inter is present; they're harmless no-ops otherwise. **No web-font load** — that would violate the hard rule.
252	- **Indigo accent** — `#5b4cff` (light) / `#8b7cff` (dark). Used for active nav, links, decision callouts (via P3), and primary card borders on hover. Soft variant `--accent-soft` for fills.
253	- **Semantic colour tokens** — `--ok`, `--warn`, `--info`, `--danger`, each with a soft companion for low-contrast fills. Pills, callouts, and chips draw from this palette consistently; pattern authors should not introduce ad-hoc hex values.
254	- **Sidebar is the primary nav** — 260px sticky column, numbered entries (`01`, `02`, …), scrollspy-highlighted. Brand block at the top is the doc family ("Nestl · ADRs", "PDE · Roadmap"). Status block at the bottom is the one-line state line.
255	- **Doc head is structured** — breadcrumbs, H1, pill row, metadata grid. Even minimal docs show source + render date in the metadata grid, so provenance is always visible without footer-hunting.
256	- **Section headers carry index + h2** — every body section wraps in `<section class="s"><header><span class="ix">01</span><h2>…</h2></header>…</section>`. Patterns that attach colour tags (P3) append a `.tag` span inside the `<h2>`.
257	- **Prose defaults are styled** — even a section that matches no pattern still gets readable type, table styling, blockquote, pre/code treatment. The fallback is "well-typeset doc," not "raw HTML."
258	- **Print** — sidebar hidden, main expands. Reviewers print-to-PDF for offline annotation get usable output.
259	- **Responsive** — below 880px the sidebar collapses above the body and nav becomes a horizontal pill list. Skill is desktop-first but mobile shouldn't be broken.
260	- **Dark mode** — `prefers-color-scheme: dark` swaps every variable. No toggle UI (out of scope).
261	
262	## When pattern treatments arrive
263	
264	Pattern markup substitutes into `{{body-html}}` — the shell itself does not change. Pattern CSS appends to the inline `<style>` block via additions, not replacements. Every pattern class is prefixed `p-`; the shell uses no `p-` classes, so collisions are impossible. The fallback (well-typeset prose with index + h2) is the default for any section the catalogue doesn't classify.
265	
```

779	**CSS.**
780	
781	```css
782	.p-cat-table { width: 100%; border-collapse: collapse; font-size: 0.875rem; }
783	.p-cat-table th, .p-cat-table td { padding: 0.5rem 0.65rem; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); vertical-align: top; }
784	.p-cat-table th { font-size: 0.72rem; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.05em; color: var(--muted); font-weight: 600; border-bottom: 2px solid var(--rule); white-space: nowrap; }
785	.p-cat-bar-track { height: 4px; background: var(--rule); border-radius: 2px; margin-top: 5px; overflow: hidden; }
786	.p-cat-bar-fill  { height: 100%; border-radius: 2px; }
787	.p-cat-bar-prob  { background: var(--accent); }
788	.p-cat-bar-pos   { background: var(--ok); }
789	.p-cat-bar-neg   { background: var(--danger); }
790	.p-cat-bar-mixed { background: var(--warn); }
791	.p-cat-dir { display: inline-block; padding: 0.15rem 0.5rem; border-radius: 4px; font-size: 0.75rem; font-weight: 600; }
792	.p-cat-dir-pos   { background: var(--ok-soft);    color: var(--ok); }
793	.p-cat-dir-neg   { background: var(--danger-soft); color: var(--danger); }
794	.p-cat-dir-mixed { background: var(--warn-soft);   color: var(--warn); }
795	```
796	
797	**Accessibility.** Table semantic structure (`<table>/<thead>/<tbody>`) is preserved. Mini-bar `<div>`s are presentational; the text value in each cell already carries the numeric content. Direction pills are text spans; colour is supplemental.
798	
799	**When to use.** Playbook §3 "Active Catalysts" tables with the `Catalyst | Probability | IV impact | Direction` header shape. Does not fire on catalyst sections with a different table structure; those fall through to standard prose table rendering.

802	
803	## P21 — Sell-side disagreement axes → bull/bear/NVS cards
804	
805	**Detect.** Section H2 matches `/sell[- ]side disagreement|disagreement axes/i` AND the section body contains at least one H3 followed by both a `**Bull:**` and a `**Bear:**` marker (case-insensitive) within that H3's scope.
806	
807	**HTML.** Any introductory paragraph before the first axis H3 renders as plain prose. Each H3 becomes a `.p-axis-card`. The card header carries the axis title and the `**The question:**` paragraph (with the `**The question:**` bold prefix stripped). The card body has a two-column grid for Bull and Bear views, and a full-width Noise-vs-Structural strip if present. The list-item prefixes `- **Bull:**`, `- **Bear:**`, and `- **Noise vs. structural:**` are stripped; their inline content fills the respective blocks.
808	
809	```html
810	<p>The real arguments the sell-side is having…</p>
811	
812	<div class="p-axis-card">
813	  <div class="p-axis-head">
814	    <h3 id="axis-1">1. Capex-cycle trough timing</h3>
815	    <p class="p-axis-q">Does the capex cycle trough in H2 2026…</p>
816	  </div>
817	  <div class="p-axis-body">
818	    <div class="p-axis-views">
819	      <div class="p-axis-bull">
820	        <div class="p-axis-view-label">Bull</div>
821	        TSMC has sustained its 2025 and 2026 capex guidance…
822	      </div>
823	      <div class="p-axis-bear">
824	        <div class="p-axis-view-label">Bear</div>
825	        Memory oversupply is slower-than-consensus…
826	      </div>
827	    </div>
828	    <div class="p-axis-nvs">
829	      <div class="p-axis-nvs-label">Noise vs. Structural</div>
830	      A single quarter of order intake recovery is noise…
831	    </div>
832	  </div>
833	</div>
834	<!-- one .p-axis-card per H3 axis -->
835	```
836	
837	Omit `.p-axis-nvs` if the H3 has no `**Noise vs. structural:**` block. H3 anchors use the same slug rules as Step 3 of the render-html workflow.
838	
839	**CSS.**
840	
841	```css
842	.p-axis-card { border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 8px; margin: 1.25rem 0; overflow: hidden; }
843	.p-axis-head { padding: 0.8rem 1rem; background: var(--surface); border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); }
844	.p-axis-head h3 { margin: 0; font-size: 1rem; scroll-margin-top: 1.5rem; }
845	.p-axis-q { font-size: 0.85rem; color: var(--muted); font-style: italic; margin: 0.25rem 0 0; }
846	.p-axis-body { padding: 1rem; }
847	.p-axis-views { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1fr 1fr; gap: 0.65rem; margin-bottom: 0.65rem; }
848	.p-axis-bull, .p-axis-bear { border-radius: 6px; padding: 0.75rem; font-size: 0.84rem; line-height: 1.6; }
849	.p-axis-bull { background: var(--ok-soft); border: 1px solid var(--ok); }
850	.p-axis-bear { background: var(--danger-soft); border: 1px solid var(--danger); }
851	.p-axis-view-label { font-size: 0.62rem; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.08em; font-weight: 700; margin-bottom: 0.35rem; }
852	.p-axis-bull .p-axis-view-label { color: var(--ok); }
853	.p-axis-bear .p-axis-view-label { color: var(--danger); }
854	.p-axis-nvs { background: var(--rule-soft); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 6px; padding: 0.75rem; font-size: 0.84rem; line-height: 1.6; }
855	.p-axis-nvs-label { font-size: 0.62rem; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.08em; font-weight: 700; color: var(--muted); margin-bottom: 0.3rem; }
856	@media (max-width: 720px) { .p-axis-views { grid-template-columns: 1fr; } }
857	```
858	
859	**Accessibility.** Each card contains a unique `<h3>` that remains in the TOC navigation. The view labels (`Bull` / `Bear`) carry meaning as text, not colour alone. The NVS strip's purpose is stated in its label text.
860	
861	**When to use.** Playbook §4 "Sell-Side Disagreement Axes" sections with 2–8 numbered or lettered axes, each having a question, bull view, bear view, and optional NVS falsifiability criterion. ASML §4 and GOOG §4 are the canonical cases. Do not apply to generic sections that happen to mention "bull" or "bear" inline — the H3-level structure with explicit `**Bull:**` / `**Bear:**` markers is the required signal.
862	
863	---
864	
865	## P22 — Failure-mode bullets → layer-tagged cards
866	
867	**Detect.** Section H2 matches `/failure modes?/i` AND the section body is a bullet list where ≥50% of items end with a `[xxx layer]` tag (regex `\[[\w ]+layer\]` at the end of the trimmed item text). P17 takes precedence when the failure-modes content is a markdown table rather than a bullet list.
868	
869	**HTML.** Any intro paragraph renders as prose above the list. Each bullet item becomes a `.p-fm-card`. The `[xxx layer]` suffix is extracted from the item text, lowercased, and " layer" is stripped to produce the tag slug (e.g. `[demand layer]` → `demand`, `[FX layer]` → `fx`). The slug drives the CSS modifier class and the chip label text. The `[xxx layer]` suffix is removed from the rendered card body. Unknown slugs fall back to `.p-fm-tag-other`. Bullets without a `[xxx layer]` tag render as plain `<li>` items inside a `<ul>` fallback.
870	
871	```html
872	<p>These are the thesis-breaking scenarios that the DCF does not capture…</p>
873	<div class="p-fm-list">
874	  <div class="p-fm-card">
875	    <span class="p-fm-tag p-fm-tag-demand" aria-label="demand layer">demand</span>
876	    <strong>Customer concentration shock:</strong> TSMC + Samsung + Intel represent the dominant share…
877	  </div>
878	  <div class="p-fm-card">
879	    <span class="p-fm-tag p-fm-tag-regulatory" aria-label="regulatory layer">regulatory</span>
880	    <strong>China service/spare-parts export controls:</strong> Current restrictions primarily cover new EUV…
881	  </div>
882	  <!-- one .p-fm-card per tagged bullet -->
883	</div>
884	```
885	
886	**CSS.**
887	
888	```css
889	.p-fm-list { display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 0.6rem; margin-top: 0.75rem; }
890	.p-fm-card { border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 6px; padding: 0.7rem 0.9rem 0.7rem 3.75rem; font-size: 0.88rem; line-height: 1.6; position: relative; }
891	.p-fm-tag { position: absolute; left: 0.65rem; top: 0.7rem; font-size: 0.58rem; text-transform: uppercase; font-weight: 700; padding: 0.15rem 0.45rem; border-radius: 4px; white-space: nowrap; }
892	.p-fm-tag-demand       { background: var(--danger-soft); color: var(--danger); }
893	.p-fm-tag-interface    { background: var(--danger-soft); color: var(--danger); }
894	.p-fm-tag-regulatory   { background: var(--warn-soft);   color: var(--warn); }
895	.p-fm-tag-monetisation { background: var(--warn-soft);   color: var(--warn); }
896	.p-fm-tag-technology   { background: var(--accent-soft); color: var(--accent); }
897	.p-fm-tag-cost         { background: var(--accent-soft); color: var(--accent); }
898	.p-fm-tag-ecosystem    { background: var(--info-soft);   color: var(--info); }
899	.p-fm-tag-fx           { background: var(--info-soft);   color: var(--info); }
900	.p-fm-tag-accounting   { background: var(--rule-soft);   color: var(--muted); }
901	.p-fm-tag-other        { background: var(--rule-soft);   color: var(--muted); }
902	```
903	
904	**Accessibility.** Each chip carries `aria-label="[slug] layer"` so screen readers announce the category before the body text. Layer distinctions are conveyed by both the text label and colour.
905	
906	**When to use.** Playbook §5 "Failure Modes" sections where bullets are tagged with `[xxx layer]` per the playbook convention. Do not apply to untagged bullet lists (fall through to prose) or table-format risk registers (P17 handles those).
907	
908	---
909	
910	## P23 — Scenario-axes shape plot
911	
912	**Detect.** P9 (frontmatter parser) has already produced a `scenario_axes` field with at least one of `bear` or `bull` sub-keys containing `growth_ceiling` or `terminal_margin` overrides. The section H2 matches `/overrides?|rationale/i`. Insert the plot immediately below the H2, before the existing prose.
913	
914	**Extract from frontmatter.**
915	| Symbol | Source | Fallback when absent |
916	|---|---|---|
917	| `gc_base` | `growth_ceiling` frontmatter | (required — always present) |
918	| `tm_base` | `terminal_margin` frontmatter | (required — always present) |
919	| `gc_bear` | `scenario_axes.bear.growth_ceiling` | `gc_base × 0.60` |
920	| `tm_bear` | `scenario_axes.bear.terminal_margin` | `tm_base × 0.85` |
921	| `gc_bull` | `scenario_axes.bull.growth_ceiling` | `gc_base × 1.30` |
922	| `tm_bull` | `scenario_axes.bull.terminal_margin` | `tm_base × 1.07` |
923	
924	The fallback multipliers match the DCF skill's own scenario-spread formulas so that the shape plot and the model agree on the meaning of absent overrides.
925	
926	**SVG coordinate formulas.** Plot area: `x ∈ [55, 470]`, `y ∈ [20, 220]`. Axis ranges: x = growth ceiling 0–25%, y = terminal margin 15–40%.
927	- `cx = 55 + (gc / 0.25) × 415`
928	- `cy = 220 − ((tm − 0.15) / 0.25) × 200`
929	
930	Clamp any computed coordinate to the plot area before rendering.
931	
932	**HTML.**
933	```html
934	<figure class="p-ax-fig" aria-label="Scenario-axes shape: bear gc={{gc_bear*100}}% tm={{tm_bear*100}}%; base gc={{gc_base*100}}% tm={{tm_base*100}}%; bull gc={{gc_bull*100}}% tm={{tm_bull*100}}%">
935	  <svg class="p-ax-svg" viewBox="0 0 500 260" role="img" aria-hidden="true">
936	    <!-- grid lines -->
937	    <line x1="55" y1="220" x2="470" y2="220" class="p-ax-axis"/>
938	    <line x1="55" y1="20"  x2="55"  y2="220" class="p-ax-axis"/>
939	    <line x1="193" y1="20" x2="193" y2="220" class="p-ax-grid"/>
940	    <line x1="332" y1="20" x2="332" y2="220" class="p-ax-grid"/>
941	    <line x1="470" y1="20" x2="470" y2="220" class="p-ax-grid"/>
942	    <line x1="55" y1="140" x2="470" y2="140" class="p-ax-grid"/>
943	    <line x1="55" y1="60"  x2="470" y2="60"  class="p-ax-grid"/>
944	    <!-- axis labels -->
945	    <text x="193" y="238" text-anchor="middle" class="p-ax-lbl">10%</text>
946	    <text x="332" y="238" text-anchor="middle" class="p-ax-lbl">20%</text>
947	    <text x="470" y="238" text-anchor="middle" class="p-ax-lbl">25%</text>
948	    <text x="48"  y="144" text-anchor="end"    class="p-ax-lbl">25%</text>
949	    <text x="48"  y="64"  text-anchor="end"    class="p-ax-lbl">35%</text>
950	    <text x="262" y="255" text-anchor="middle" class="p-ax-lbl">Growth ceiling</text>
951	    <text x="14"  y="120" text-anchor="middle" class="p-ax-lbl" transform="rotate(-90,14,120)">Terminal margin</text>
952	    <!-- scenario polygon -->
953	    <polygon points="{{bear_cx}},{{bear_cy}} {{base_cx}},{{base_cy}} {{bull_cx}},{{bull_cy}}"
954	             class="p-ax-poly"/>
955	    <!-- scenario dots -->
956	    <circle cx="{{bear_cx}}" cy="{{bear_cy}}" r="6" class="p-ax-bear"><title>Bear: gc={{gc_bear*100}}% tm={{tm_bear*100}}%</title></circle>
957	    <circle cx="{{base_cx}}" cy="{{base_cy}}" r="6" class="p-ax-base"><title>Base: gc={{gc_base*100}}% tm={{tm_base*100}}%</title></circle>
958	    <circle cx="{{bull_cx}}" cy="{{bull_cy}}" r="6" class="p-ax-bull"><title>Bull: gc={{gc_bull*100}}% tm={{tm_bull*100}}%</title></circle>
959	    <!-- point labels -->
960	    <text x="{{bear_cx}}" y="{{bear_cy - 10}}" text-anchor="middle" class="p-ax-pt-lbl">bear</text>
961	    <text x="{{base_cx}}" y="{{base_cy - 10}}" text-anchor="middle" class="p-ax-pt-lbl">base</text>
962	    <text x="{{bull_cx}}" y="{{bull_cy - 10}}" text-anchor="middle" class="p-ax-pt-lbl">bull</text>
963	  </svg>
964	</figure>
965	```
966	
967	**CSS.**
968	```css
969	.p-ax-fig { margin: 0.75rem 0 0; }
970	.p-ax-svg { width: 100%; max-width: 500px; height: auto; display: block; }
971	.p-ax-axis { stroke: var(--fg); stroke-width: 1.5; }
972	.p-ax-grid { stroke: var(--rule); stroke-width: 1; stroke-dasharray: 3 3; }
973	.p-ax-lbl  { fill: var(--muted); font-size: 11px; }
974	.p-ax-poly { fill: var(--accent-soft); stroke: var(--accent); stroke-width: 1.5; fill-opacity: 0.5; }
975	.p-ax-bear { fill: var(--danger); opacity: 0.85; }
976	.p-ax-base { fill: var(--accent); opacity: 0.85; }
977	.p-ax-bull { fill: var(--ok);     opacity: 0.85; }
978	.p-ax-pt-lbl { fill: var(--fg-soft); font-size: 10px; }
979	```
980	
981	**Accessibility.** The `<figure>` carries a full `aria-label` naming each scenario's coordinates in prose. SVG is `aria-hidden`. If the shape degenerates to a line (one axis is identical across all three scenarios), the polygon still renders — just as a line, which is the correct representation of a one-dimensional spread.
982	
983	**When to use.** Playbook §6 "Ticker-Specific Overrides" sections whose frontmatter includes a `scenario_axes` block. Both GOOG §6 and ASML §6 match. Do not fire if `scenario_axes` is absent from frontmatter — fall through to prose.
984	
985	---
986	
987	## P24 — Historical reset-and-recover scatter
988	
989	**Detect.** Section H2 matches `/historical reset|reset[- ]and[- ]recover|recovery prior/i` AND the section contains a markdown table with headers matching `Episode`, `Drawdown`, and `Recovery` (case-insensitive, partial match).
990	
991	**HTML.** Inline SVG scatter (`viewBox="0 0 500 260"`) rendered immediately before the source table. Plot area: x ∈ [55, 470], y ∈ [20, 220] (width 415, height 200). X-axis: recovery months, default max 18 (extend to `maxMonths + 2` if any value exceeds 16). Y-axis: drawdown magnitude %, default max 50 (extend to `maxPct + 5` if any value exceeds 45). Coordinate formulas: `cx = 55 + months / maxMonths × 415`; `cy = 220 − pct / maxPct × 200`. Parse drawdown from `~−XX%` or `~−XX–YY%` (midpoint of range). Parse recovery months from `~N months` or `~N–M months` (midpoint). Dot colour: drawdown ≤ 20% → `.p-sc-warn`; > 20% → `.p-sc-danger`. Episodes with non-numeric recovery (e.g. "Partial", "Ongoing") render as hollow circles at `cx = 478` with class `.p-sc-partial`. Each circle has a `<title>` child naming the episode. The `<figure aria-label>` summarises all plotted episodes; the SVG is `aria-hidden="true"`.
992	
993	```html
994	<figure class="p-sc-fig" aria-label="Reset-and-recover: 2022 ad recession −45% recovered 12 months; 2023 Bing scare −15% recovered 6 months; 2024 DOJ partial recovery not plotted">
995	  <svg class="p-sc-svg" viewBox="0 0 500 260" role="img" aria-hidden="true">
996	    <!-- horizontal grid lines at 10/20/30/40% drawdown levels -->
997	    <line x1="55" y1="180" x2="470" y2="180" class="p-sc-grid"/>
998	    <line x1="55" y1="140" x2="470" y2="140" class="p-sc-grid"/>
999	    <line x1="55" y1="100" x2="470" y2="100" class="p-sc-grid"/>
1000	    <line x1="55" y1="60"  x2="470" y2="60"  class="p-sc-grid"/>
1001	    <!-- axes -->
1002	    <line x1="55" y1="20"  x2="55"  y2="220" class="p-sc-axis"/>
1003	    <line x1="55" y1="220" x2="470" y2="220" class="p-sc-axis"/>
1004	    <!-- Y axis labels (drawdown %) -->
1005	    <text x="50" y="221" class="p-sc-lbl" text-anchor="end">0</text>
1006	    <text x="50" y="181" class="p-sc-lbl" text-anchor="end">10</text>
1007	    <text x="50" y="141" class="p-sc-lbl" text-anchor="end">20</text>
1008	    <text x="50" y="101" class="p-sc-lbl" text-anchor="end">30</text>
1009	    <text x="50" y="61"  class="p-sc-lbl" text-anchor="end">40</text>
1010	    <text x="50" y="24"  class="p-sc-lbl" text-anchor="end">50%</text>
1011	    <!-- X axis labels (months) -->
1012	    <text x="55"  y="237" class="p-sc-lbl" text-anchor="middle">0</text>
1013	    <text x="193" y="237" class="p-sc-lbl" text-anchor="middle">6</text>
1014	    <text x="332" y="237" class="p-sc-lbl" text-anchor="middle">12</text>
1015	    <text x="470" y="237" class="p-sc-lbl" text-anchor="middle">18 mo</text>
1016	    <!-- axis titles -->
1017	    <text x="262" y="254" class="p-sc-lbl" text-anchor="middle">Recovery (months)</text>
1018	    <text x="14"  y="120" class="p-sc-lbl" text-anchor="middle" transform="rotate(-90 14 120)">Drawdown</text>
1019	    <!-- data points (cx/cy from formulas above; example uses GOOG data) -->
1020	    <circle cx="332" cy="40"  r="7" class="p-sc-danger"><title>2022 ad recession: −45%, ~12 months</title></circle>
1021	    <circle cx="193" cy="160" r="7" class="p-sc-warn"><title>2023 Bing scare: −15%, ~6 months</title></circle>
1022	    <!-- partial recovery: hollow circle beyond right axis edge -->
1023	    <circle cx="478" cy="132" r="7" class="p-sc-partial"><title>2024–25 DOJ: ~−22%, partial recovery</title></circle>
1024	  </svg>
1025	  <p class="p-sc-note">† Open circles beyond axis edge = recovery not yet complete. Source table below is the canonical record.</p>
1026	</figure>
1027	```
1028	
1029	**CSS.**
1030	
1031	```css
1032	.p-sc-fig { margin: 0.75rem 0 0; }
1033	.p-sc-svg { width: 100%; max-width: 500px; height: auto; display: block; }
1034	.p-sc-axis { stroke: var(--fg); stroke-width: 1.5; }
1035	.p-sc-grid { stroke: var(--rule); stroke-width: 1; stroke-dasharray: 3 3; }
1036	.p-sc-lbl    { fill: var(--muted); font-size: 11px; }
1037	.p-sc-danger { fill: var(--danger); opacity: 0.8; }
1038	.p-sc-warn   { fill: var(--warn);   opacity: 0.8; }
1039	.p-sc-partial { fill: none; stroke: var(--muted); stroke-width: 2; stroke-dasharray: 3 2; }
1040	.p-sc-note { font-size: 0.75rem; color: var(--muted); margin: 0.3rem 0 0; }
1041	```
1042	
1043	**Accessibility.** The `<figure aria-label>` summarises all episodes; the SVG is `aria-hidden="true"`. Each `<circle>` has a `<title>` child for hover tooltips. The source table that follows is the canonical record.
1044	
1045	**When to use.** Playbook §7 "Historical Reset-and-Recover Priors" tables with `Episode | Drawdown | … | Recovery | …` headers. Does not fire without a parseable drawdown column.
1046	
1047	---
1048	
1049	## Fallback
1050	
1051	A section that matches no pattern renders with the shell's section-header treatment (`<section class="s"><header><span class="ix">NN</span><h2>…</h2></header>`) and the shell's prose defaults (`<p>`, `<ul>`, `<table>`, `<pre>`, `<blockquote>`). The fallback is "well-typeset doc," not raw HTML — false-positive pattern matches degrade review trust faster than missing treatments.
1052	
1053	## Adding a new pattern
1054	
1055	When a new pattern earns its slot:
1056	
1057	1. Add a section here following the same six-part structure: heading, **Detect**, **HTML**, **CSS**, **Accessibility**, brief rationale.
1058	2. Number it sequentially (`P13 — …`) so cross-references stay stable.
1059	3. Confirm the CSS uses shell colour tokens (`--accent`, `--ok`, `--warn`, `--info`, `--danger`, `-soft` variants) — no ad-hoc hex values.
1060	4. Confirm the CSS only adds new selectors prefixed `p-` — it must not override any rule in `html-skeleton.md`.
1061	5. If the detection signal could overlap an existing pattern, document the precedence rule explicitly. The first-match-wins ordering of this file is the resolution mechanism.
1062	
```

This doc is the design — what gets built, why, in what order — and is the input to <code>shape-planning-and-task-breakdown</code> when the initiative is sliced into issues.</p>\n    </div>\n\n    <section class=\"s\" id=\"context\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">01</span><h2>Context</h2></header>\n      <p>Today <code>drain-cycle</code> only tests against a real Linear cycle. Anything past happy-path becomes expensive: failure modes need deliberately broken issues, resume needs an actual halt-and-restart, and usage tracking needs real Claude sessions. Unit tests cover individual modules but stop at the function boundary — they monkey-patch <code>linear._post</code> and swap <code>claude -p</code> for marker-touching shell scripts (<code>tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py:22</code>, <code>tests/test_linear_pending_issues.py:65</code>).</p>\n      <p>We need <strong>scenario-level integration tests</strong> that drive the orchestrator end-to-end against a controllable Linear surface, covering:</p>\n      <ul>\n        <li><strong>Best-case drains</strong> — dependency-ordered execution, runlog shape, state transitions</li>\n        <li><strong>Per-issue failure paths</strong> — every halt condition in <code>orchestrator._drain_one_issue</code> (repo resolution, worktree setup, <code>set_state</code> failure, breach kill, not-Done exit)</li>\n        <li><strong>Cycle-level halts</strong> — <code>DependencyCycleError</code>, cycle-cap breach propagation</li>\n        <li><strong>Resume semantics</strong> — halt mid-cycle, re-run, observe pickup of in-flight issues reverted to Todo</li>\n        <li><strong>Session/usage tracking</strong> — <code>_UsageAccumulator</code> totals, cycle aggregates in runlog, per-issue caps triggering breaches</li>\n      </ul>\n      <p>The drain has no native retry (deliberate, per <code>docs/design-decisions.md</code>); \"retry\" here means <strong>resume</strong> — a re-invocation against the same cycle.</p>\n    </section>\n\n    <section class=\"s\" id=\"system-context\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">02</span><h2>System </g>\n\n          <path d=\"M240 56 L340 56\" marker-end=\"url(#arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n          <text x=\"290\" y=\"48\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-edge-label\">populate</text>\n\n          <path d=\"M140 90 L140 150\" marker-end=\"url(#arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n          <text x=\"160\" y=\"125\" class=\"p-edge-label\">run()</text>\n\n          <path d=\"M240 175 L340 70\" marker-end=\"url(#arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n          <text x=\"298\" y=\"118\" class=\"p-edge-label\">GraphQL HTTP</text>\n\n          <path d=\"M140 210 L140 270\" marker-end=\"url(#arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n          <text x=\"160\" y=\"245\" class=\"p-edge-label\">spawn</text>\n\n          <path d=\"M240 300 L340 300\" marker-end=\"url(#arr-accent)\" class=\"p-edge-async\"/>\n          <text x=\"290\" y=\"292\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-edge-label\">MCP stdio</text>\n\n          <path d=\"M440 270 L440 90\" marker-end=\"url(#arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n          <text x=\"460\" y=\"185\" class=\"p-edge-label\">GraphQL POST</text>\n\n          <path d=\"M540 70 L620 170\" marker-end=\"url(#arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n          <text x=\"565\" y=\"115\" class=\"p-edge-label\">writes</text>\n\n          <path d=\"M540 290 L620 195\" marker-end=\"url(#arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n          <text x=\"565\" y=\"255\" class=\"p-edge-label\">writes</text>\n        </svg>\n        <p class=\"p-diagram-caption\">Solid = control / data flow over HTTP or subprocess. Dashed (accent) = MCP stdio between the child and the shim. The dashed boundary marks what's mocked: every byte that would otherwise hit <code>api.linear.app</code> is intercepted, but <code>claude -p</code> itself is the production binary every run. Both the parent's HTTP calls and the child's MCP calls land in one shared store, so scenarios assert against one unified call log.</p>\n      </div>\n    </section>\n\n    <section class=\"s\" id=\"workflow\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">03</span><h2>Workflow</h2></header>\n      <ol>\n        <li><strong><code>/shape-initiative</code></strong> — done. Linear project created with the six-field OKR (linked above).</li>\n        <li><strong><code>shape-planning-and-task-breakdown</code></strong> — convert the <em>Slices</em> section below into Linear issues under the new initiative; assign slices 1–4 to the current cycle, 5–12 to the backlog.</li>\n        <li><strong><code>agent-skills:test-driven-development</code></strong> during build — each slice ships a failing scenario before the supporting infra.</li>\n        <li><strong><code>agent-skills:code-review-and-quality</code></strong> per slice before commit, per <code>AGENTS.md</code>.</li>\n      </ol>\n    </section>\n\n    <section class=\"s\" id=\"approach\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">04</span><h2>Approach</h2></header>\n      <p><strong>Parent HTTP mock + Linear-MCP shim + real <code>claude -p</code> + in-process driver.</strong> The spawned session is real claude every time; only the Linear surface is mocked. Three pillars.</p>\n      <p><strong>Alternatives rejected</strong> (draft for the ADR slice):</p>\n      <ul>\n        <li><strong>Scripted/fake <code>claude -p</code>.</strong> Cheaper, faster, deterministic. Rejected because a scripted child stops being an integration test — it cannot validate the real session's usage stream, MCP tool calls, prompt-to-completion behaviour, or production OTel spans against actual data.</li>\n        <li><strong>Hybrid: real claude only on happy-path; scripted child for failure determinism.</strong> Two patterns to maintain; weaker default signal; gives up real-claude coverage on usage-tracking edge cases.</li>\n        <li><strong>Function-injection / <code>LinearClient</code> protocol on the parent.</strong> Cheaper than HTTP but skips the HTTP boundary and adds an abstraction layer that earns nothing else. The existing module-level functions in <code>linear.py</code> drive easily against an HTTP fake once <code>LINEAR_API_URL</code> is overridable.</li>\n        <li><strong>VCR-style recorded fixtures.</strong> Realistic but awkward for failure injection, expensive to maintain under schema drift.</li>\n      </ul>\n      <p>Slice 1 produces a short ADR at <code>docs/adrs/0001-integration-test-architecture.md</code> recording the chosen approach, these rejected alternatives, and revisit conditions.</p>\n\n      <h3 id=\"cost-runtime-and-determinism\">Cost, runtime, and determinism</h3>\n      <p>Real claude makes the suite costly, slow, and non-deterministic on numbers.</p>\n      <ul>\n        <li><strong>Cost / runtime budget</strong>: ~$0.05–$0.20 per spawning scenario, ~15–30 seconds per scenario. Full suite of ~30 scenarios: ~$1–$3 and ~15 minutes. Run on demand, not as part of the default suite.</li>\n        <li><strong>Default <code>pytest</code> run</strong> covers only the pre-spawn halt scenarios (no claude spawned) — fast and deterministic, suitable for repeated local invocation while iterating.</li>\n        <li><strong>Determinism strategy</strong> — split assertions:\n          <ul>\n            <li><strong>Exact</strong> — exit codes, final Linear state, halt reason class, runlog file presence, structural shape of runlog JSON, set of MCP tool calls observed.</li>\n            <li><strong>Predicate</strong> — <code>tokens &gt; 0</code>, <code>0 &lt; cost_usd &lt; per_issue_cost_usd</code>, <code>num_turns &gt;= 1</code>, <code>model is not None</code>. No exact values.</li>\n            <li><strong>Tolerant snapshot</strong> — <code>dirty-equals</code> matches keys and types, ignores values.</li>\n          </ul>\n        </li>\n        <li><strong>Cost guard</strong>: scenarios that spawn claude carry <code>@pytest.mark.real_claude</code>. Default <code>pytest</code> invocation excludes the marker; the <code>real_claude</code> slice opts in via <code>pytest -m real_claude</code> and runs on demand. <code>ANTHROPIC_API_KEY</code> required.</li>\n      </ul>\n\n      <h3 id=\"pillar-1-mocklinear\">Pillar 1 — <code>MockLinear</code>: an in-process fake GraphQL server</h3>\n      <p>A FastAPI/Starlette app on a random localhost port, started per-test via a pytest fixture. It speaks the five GraphQL operations <code>linear.py</code> uses (<code>current_cycle</code>, <code>pending_issues</code>, <code>get_issue</code>, <code>set_state</code>, <code>resolve_state</code> / WorkflowState lookup) plus an <code>IssueUpdate</code> mutation the scripted child hits to flip an issue to Done.</p>\n      <p>State lives in a typed in-memory store: <code>Cycle</code>, <code>Issue</code>, <code>Label</code>, <code>WorkflowState</code>. The store records every call (operation, variables, timestamp) so tests can assert call sequence and counts.</p>\n      <p>Fault injection is <strong>per-operation, per-call-index</strong>: <code>mock.fail_after(op=\"set_state\", call=2, mode=\"http_500\")</code>. Modes: <code>http_500</code>, <code>http_429</code>, <code>graphql_error</code>, <code>malformed_json</code>, <code>slow_response(seconds)</code>, <code>connection_reset</code>. The drain has no retry, so \"fail\" means \"this single call breaks\" — tests then verify the halt path.</p>\n      <p><strong>Production change required</strong>: <code>linear.py:18</code> hard-codes <code>https://api.linear.app/graphql</code>. Replace with <code>os.environ.get(\"LINEAR_API_URL\", \"https://api.linear.app/graphql\")</code>. No CLI flag — env var only.</p>\n\n      <h3 id=\"pillar-2-linear-mcp-shim\">Pillar 2 — <code>linear_mcp_shim</code>: a Linear MCP server backed by the mock</h3>\n      <p>A small Python stdio MCP server (built on the official <code>mcp</code> SDK) that exposes the two tools the drain-cycle prompt uses today: <code>save_issue</code> (state transitions) and <code>save_comment</code>. Each tool translates its call into a GraphQL POST against <code>MockLinear</code>, sharing the same backing store as the parent's HTTP surface.</p>\n      <p>The harness writes a custom <code>.mcp.json</code> into each test worktree registering the shim under the name <code>linear</code> (matching the existing tool prefix <code>mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*</code>). The orchestrator already symlinks <code>.mcp.json</code> from the configured source into the worktree (<code>orchestrator.py:257-259</code>); the harness arranges for the test source to be the shim-configured file.</p>\n      <p>The shim records every tool call (tool name, arguments, timestamp) on the shared store, so scenarios assert against both parent GraphQL calls and child MCP calls through one call log.</p>\n      <p><strong>Why an MCP shim and not just <code>LINEAR_API_URL</code> in the child env.</strong> MCP is a separate protocol from raw GraphQL; the official Linear MCP server speaks MCP over stdio to claude and HTTPS to Linear. Setting <code>LINEAR_API_URL</code> in the child has no effect — there's no way to redirect the official server without forking it. A purpose-built shim is the smallest seam.</p>\n\n      <h4 id=\"happy-path-sequence\">Happy-path sequence</h4>\n      <p>The seam closes end-to-end. One issue, picked up and driven to Done:</p>\n\n      <div class=\"p-diagram\">\n        <svg viewBox=\"0 0 860 500\" xmlns=\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\" role=\"img\" aria-label=\"Sequence diagram of a single-issue happy-path drain showing messages between Harness, orchestrator, MockLinear, claude-p, and linear_mcp_shim.\">\n          <defs>\n            <marker id=\"seq-arr\" viewBox=\"0 0 10 10\" refX=\"9\" refY=\"5\" markerWidth=\"7\" markerHeight=\"7\" orient=\"auto-start-reverse\">\n              <path d=\"M0,0 L10,5 L0,10z\" fill=\"currentColor\"/>\n            </marker>\n            <marker id=\"seq-arr-accent\" viewBox=\"0 0 10 10\" refX=\"9\" refY=\"5\" markerWidth=\"7\" markerHeight=\"7\" orient=\"auto-start-reverse\">\n              <path d=\"M0,0 L10,5 L0,10z\" fill=\"var(--accent)\"/>\n            </marker>\n          </defs>\n\n          <g class=\"p-node\">\n            <rect x=\"20\" y=\"20\" width=\"120\" height=\"44\" rx=\"8\"/>\n            <text x=\"80\" y=\"47\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-node-title\">Harness</text>\n          </g>\n          <g class=\"p-node\">\n            <rect x=\"180\" y=\"20\" width=\"140\" height=\"44\" rx=\"8\"/>\n            <text x=\"250\" y=\"47\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-node-title\">orchestrator</text>\n          </g>\n          <g class=\"p-node\">\n            <rect x=\"360\" y=\"20\" width=\"140\" height=\"44\" rx=\"8\"/>\n            <text x=\"430\" y=\"47\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-node-title\">MockLinear</text>\n          </g>\n          <g class=\"p-node\">\n            <rect x=\"540\" y=\"20\" width=\"120\" height=\"44\" rx=\"8\"/>\n            <text x=\"600\" y=\"47\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-node-title\">claude -p</text>\n          </g>\n          <g class=\"p-node\">\n            <rect x=\"700\" y=\"20\" width=\"140\" height=\"44\" rx=\"8\"/>\n            <text x=\"770\" y=\"47\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-node-title\">linear_mcp_shim</text>\n          </g>\n\n          <line x1=\"80\"  y1=\"64\" x2=\"80\"  y2=\"475\" stroke=\"var(--rule)\" stroke-width=\"1.5\" stroke-dasharray=\"4 4\"/>\n          <line x1=\"250\" y1=\"64\" x2=\"250\" y2=\"475\" stroke=\"var(--rule)\" stroke-width=\"1.5\" stroke-dasharray=\"4 4\"/>\n          <line x1=\"430\" y1=\"64\" x2=\"430\" y2=\"475\" stroke=\"var(--rule)\" stroke-width=\"1.5\" stroke-dasharray=\"4 4\"/>\n          <line x1=\"600\" y1=\"64\" x2=\"600\" y2=\"475\" stroke=\"var(--rule)\" stroke-width=\"1.5\" stroke-dasharray=\"4 4\"/>\n          <line x1=\"770\" y1=\"64\" x2=\"770\" y2=\"475\" stroke=\"var(--rule)\" stroke-width=\"1.5\" stroke-dasharray=\"4 4\"/>\n\n          <path d=\"M80 95 L430 95\" marker-end=\"url(#seq-arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n          <text x=\"255\" y=\"88\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-edge-label\">populate(Cycle, Issue)</text>\n\n          <path d=\"M80 130 L250 130\" marker-end=\"url(#seq-arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n          <text x=\"165\" y=\"123\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-edge-label\">run(repos, limits)</text>\n\n          <path d=\"M250 170 L430 170\" marker-end=\"url(#seq-arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n          <text x=\"340\" y=\"163\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-edge-label\">pending_issues</text>\n          <path d=\"M430 195 L250 195\" marker-end=\"url(#seq-arr)\" class=\"p-edge\" stroke-dasharray=\"4 4\"/>\n          <text x=\"340\" y=\"210\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-edge-label\">[Issue]</text>\n\n          <path d=\"M250 240 L430 240\" marker-end=\"url(#seq-arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n          <text x=\"340\" y=\"233\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-edge-label\">set_state(In-Progress)</text>\n\n          <path d=\"M250 280 L600 280\" marker-end=\"url(#seq-arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n          <text x=\"425\" y=\"273\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-edge-label\">spawn</text>\n\n          <path d=\"M600 320 L770 320\" marker-end=\"url(#seq-arr-accent)\" class=\"p-edge-async\"/>\n          <text x=\"685\" y=\"313\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-edge-label\">get_issue (MCP)</text>\n          <path d=\"M770 345 L430 345\" marker-end=\"url(#seq-arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n          <text x=\"600\" y=\"338\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-edge-label\">GraphQL get_issue</text>\n\n          <path d=\"M600 385 L770 385\" marker-end=\"url(#seq-arr-accent)\" class=\"p-edge-async\"/>\n          <text x=\"685\" y=\"378\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-edge-label\">save_issue(Done) · save_comment</text>\n          <path d=\"M770 410 L430 410\" marker-end=\"url(#seq-arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n          <text x=\"600\" y=\"403\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-edge-label\">GraphQL set_state(Done)</text>\n\n          <path d=\"M250 455 L430 455\" marker-end=\"url(#seq-arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n          <text x=\"340\" y=\"448\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-edge-label\">get_issue (verify Done)</text>\n        </svg>\n        <p class=\"p-diagram-caption\">The parent (left half) talks to <code>MockLinear</code> directly over HTTP. The child (right half) reaches the same store indirectly, via <code>linear_mcp_shim</code> over MCP stdio. Both write into the in-memory store, so the test's call log records the full conversation across both surfaces — the parent's <code>pending_issues</code> / <code>set_state</code> calls and the child's <code>save_issue</code> / <code>save_comment</code> calls interleave in one ordered list that scenarios assert against.</p>\n      </div>\n\n      <h3 id=\"how-scenarios-steer-claude\">How scenarios steer claude</h3>\n      <p>Scenarios drive child behaviour through three knobs the harness sets per-scenario:</p>\n      <ul>\n        <li><strong>Issue body</strong> — written into <code>MockLinear</code> and read by claude via <code>mcp__claude_ai_Linear__get_issue</code>. The body is the natural prompt seam (e.g. \"create <code>&lt;id&gt;.txt</code> containing 'hello'; then mark this issue Done\"). Library lives in <code>tests/integration/prompts/*.md</code>.</li>\n        <li><strong>Per-issue limits</strong> — <code>limits.yml</code> overrides (e.g. <code>per_issue_tokens=2000</code>, <code>per_issue_seconds=30</code>) used with verbose prompts to provoke breach scenarios deterministically.</li>\n        <li><strong>Claude flags</strong> — the harness may inject <code>--max-turns 1</code> for \"exit without Done\" cases (claude exits before completing the workflow), via the <code>_CLAUDE_CMD</code> patch point at <code>orchestrator.py:25</code>.</li>\n      </ul>\n      <p>The drain-cycle prompt (<code>drain_cycle/prompt.py:39-42</code>) stays unchanged — same prompt the real drain uses.</p>\n\n      <h3 id=\"pillar-3-integrationharness\">Pillar 3 — <code>IntegrationHarness</code>: a fixture that drives <code>orchestrator.run()</code> in-process</h3>\n      <p>A pytest fixture that:</p>\n      <ul>\n        <li>Creates a temp <code>HOME</code> so <code>~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml</code>, <code>~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml</code>, and <code>~/.drain-cycle/runs/</code> redirect to a tmpdir (<code>monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", ...)</code> plus any path-resolution helpers in <code>cli.py</code> / <code>runlog.py</code>).</li>\n        <li>Initialises one or more real git repos in tmpdir (existing pattern in <code>test_orchestrator_iteration.py</code>).</li>\n        <li>Writes a <code>repos.yml</code> mapping label names to those repo paths.</li>\n        <li>Writes a <code>.mcp.json</code> in each repo registering the Linear-MCP shim under <code>linear</code>.</li>\n        <li>Starts <code>MockLinear</code> and populates it with the scenario's cycle, issues (with prompt body), and labels.</li>\n        <li>Sets <code>LINEAR_API_URL</code>, <code>LINEAR_API_KEY=test</code>, <code>MOCK_LINEAR_URL=http://localhost:&lt;port&gt;</code>, <code>OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true</code> env vars. Verifies <code>ANTHROPIC_API_KEY</code> is set for scenarios that spawn claude.</li>\n        <li>Optionally appends extra flags to <code>_CLAUDE_CMD</code> (e.g. <code>--max-turns 1</code>) for scenarios that need to coerce claude's behaviour.</li>\n        <li>Calls <code>orchestrator.run(repos, limits)</code> directly and returns a <code>Result</code>: exit code, runlog JSON, parent GraphQL call log, child MCP call log, final mock state.</li>\n      </ul>\n      <p>Tests assert against <code>Result</code>. One scenario per <code>test_*</code> function. Helpers (<code>make_cycle</code>, <code>make_issue</code>, <code>expect_runlog</code>, <code>expect_calls</code>) keep scenarios short.</p>\n      <p><strong>Per-scenario contract.</strong> Every scenario declares:</p>\n      <ol>\n        <li><strong>Setup</strong> — <code>Cycle</code>, <code>Issue[]</code>, <code>Label[]</code>, <code>WorkflowState[]</code>, repos.yml mapping, limits.yml overrides, transcript fixture name(s), any fault injection.</li>\n        <li><strong>Expected exit code</strong> — orchestrator return.</li>\n        <li><strong>Expected runlog assertions</strong> — a list of (a) structural shape (keys, types) matched via <code>dirty-equals</code>, (b) predicate assertions on numeric fields (<code>tokens &gt; 0</code>, <code>cost_usd &lt; limits.per_issue_cost_usd</code>), (c) exact assertions on deterministic fields (<code>final_linear_state</code>, <code>halt_reason</code>).</li>\n        <li><strong>Expected final mock state</strong> — issue states the mock should end in (e.g. <code>{ \"ABA-1\": \"Done\", \"ABA-2\": \"Todo\" }</code> after a halt on issue 2).</li>\n        <li><strong>Expected call sequence</strong> — ordered <code>(surface, operation, predicate-on-args)</code> tuples across both parent GraphQL and child MCP calls. Strict by default; <code>expect_calls(strict=False)</code> for tolerant cases where claude's tool-call order varies between runs.</li>\n      </ol>\n      <p>A collection-time lint test fails any scenario missing (3), (4), or (5).</p>\n    </section>\n\n    <section class=\"s\" id=\"production-code-changes-required\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">05</span><h2>Production code changes required</h2></header>\n      <p>Minimal. Every test-only seam reuses existing structure.</p>\n      <ol>\n        <li><strong><code>drain_cycle/linear.py:18</code></strong> — <code>_GRAPHQL_URL</code> reads from <code>LINEAR_API_URL</code> env var, default unchanged. One-line change.</li>\n        <li><strong><code>drain_cycle/runlog.py</code></strong> — verify the run-log directory honours <code>HOME</code> re-resolution; slice 1 greps for <code>Path.home</code>, <code>expanduser</code>, and <code>HOME</code>. If the path caches at module load, add an accessor (e.g. <code>_run_log_dir()</code>) so the harness re-resolves after <code>monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", ...)</code>.</li>\n        <li><strong><code>drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:25</code></strong> — <code>_CLAUDE_CMD</code> is already module-level. Tests patch it.</li>\n      </ol>\n      <p>No new abstraction layers, no <code>LinearClient</code> Protocol — the existing module-level functions drive easily against an HTTP fake once the URL is configurable.</p>\n    </section>\n\n    <section class=\"s\" id=\"test-infra-correctness-checks\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">06</span><h2>Test-infra correctness checks (one-time pre-slice work)</h2></header>\n      <p>Risks the plan-review surfaced. Each lands before dependent slices.</p>\n      <ul>\n        <li><strong>Telemetry isolation</strong> — drain-cycle wires httpx OTel auto-instrumentation in <code>telemetry.py</code>. The harness sets <code>OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true</code> to keep tests from exporting spans. Confirmed in slice 2.</li>\n        <li><strong>MCP-shim correctness</strong> — the shim must implement the same tool schemas the official Linear MCP exposes for <code>save_issue</code> and <code>save_comment</code>, otherwise claude's tool calls land with wrong shapes. Slice 3 spike: capture the official schemas (from the Linear MCP docs or by running the real one briefly), implement matching ones in the shim, verify a single happy-path drain reaches Done end-to-end. <strong>Kill-switch</strong>: if the schemas drift between Linear MCP versions, pin the shim to a captured snapshot and add a periodic check.</li>\n        <li><strong>Breach scenario reproducibility</strong> — with real claude, breach scenarios depend on prompts that reliably exceed tight per-issue caps. Slice 8 spike: confirm a verbose prompt (e.g. \"write a 2000-line poem\") plus <code>per_issue_tokens=1000</code> reliably triggers token breach across 5 consecutive runs. <strong>Kill-switch</strong>: if not reliable, fall back to time-breach only (tight <code>per_issue_seconds</code> plus a \"sleep 60s\" prompt is robust because the breach loop polls wall-clock).</li>\n      </ul>\n    </section>\n\n    <section class=\"s\" id=\"failure-mode-coverage\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">07</span><h2>Failure-mode coverage</h2></header>\n      <p>The matrix below names every halt path in the production code and the scenario that covers it. KR 2's halt-coverage guard fails the suite when a new halt slug lands without a matching row.</p>\n      <table>\n        <thead>\n          <tr><th>Halt path</th><th>Source</th><th>Scenario</th><th>Spawns claude?</th><th>Slice</th></tr>\n        </thead>\n        <tbody>\n          <tr><td>Missing <code>repo:</code> label</td><td><code>orchestrator.py</code> repo-resolve</td><td><code>test_pre_spawn_halts::test_missing_repo_label</code></td><td>no</td><td>7</td></tr>\n          <tr><td><code>repo:</code> label → nonexistent path</td><td><code>orchestrator.py</code> repo-resolve</td><td><code>test_pre_spawn_halts::test_unknown_repo_path</code></td><td>no</td><td>7</td></tr>\n          <tr><td>Worktree <code>add</code> fails</td><td><code>orchestrator.py</code> worktree</td><td><code>test_pre_spawn_halts::test_dirty_worktree</code></td><td>no</td><td>7</td></tr>\n
